**Regressions, recorded not hidden:** none in any metric. After the edits the same four reruns write all 50 files byte for byte as committed (`baseline_reproduction.json`, label `post_edit`, each run started after the last edit to the code it imports), so every Net3, Net2, persistent stress-test and synthetic-pilot number in the README and in the sections above is unchanged. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun, so their numbers are not rechecked by this task. Two behaviour changes, both outside the default numbers. (1) A grid build now stops with an error when less than 1.5 GB is free, where before it would start and could fail partway through (a planning probe hit EPANET error 308 when its temporary directory filled). (2) A chloramine condition now raises NotImplementedError until task 12.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.checks` (about 20 s at task 8; about 75 to 80 s with the 39 checks of task 10b, whose wall-bank nesting check alone takes about 22 s; `--quick` a few seconds then, about 15 s now), and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.checks --reproduce <scratch dir> --label <name>` to rerun and compare the committed experiments (7.3 minutes for the four runs, plus a few seconds for the app demo).

### Task 9, water age and where chlorine is lost

**Asked:** answer the water-age part of the manager's question with something a judge can see, and split each junction's chlorine loss between demand in the water (organics and other dissolved reactants) and demand at old pipe walls. (1) A new `residualmap/age.py`: the water age of the grid's 9 hydraulic settings (demand x0.85, x1, x1.15 times roughness x0.9, x1, x1.1) with the min, median and max per junction and hour; an age weighted by a fitted SimGP24's posterior over those settings; and the wall-versus-water split for the calibrated member. (2) The truth's own water age (`build_scenario(truth_age=True)`, built in task 8) as the test. (3) `python -m residualmap.experiment_chem age <net> <seeds>`: the operator's age against the truth's, the coverage of the 9-setting range and of the posterior band after 8 random daytime samples, and the chlorine error by nominal-age group for today's SimGP24 at those samples, which task 13 compares against. Net3 and Net2 with 8 seeds and ky4 with 2, each under the default truth and the persistent structural stress test; junctions whose true age passes 160 h excluded and counted. (4) In the app, a water-age panel and a 'where chlorine is lost' panel, the water age in the route reasons, and an 'oldest water' line in the PDF. Acceptance: the outputs exist with the listed keys; the range is called a 90% band only if `band_coverage` is at least 0.85; the checks pass with the anchors unchanged; the app's default demo numbers do not move, and the new panels render on Net3, on Net2 and with the demo off.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/age.py`, new. `hydraulic_age_band(sc)` runs EPANET AGE on the operator's nominal model at the 9 settings, in the grid's own order, each run in a temporary directory; the (x1, x1) member is the nominal age bit for bit (a saved check). It also runs the nominal model once more for its initial-water share (below). `posterior_age(model)` sums the model's joint member-by-dose posterior over the dose and decay axes to get weights on the 9 settings, and returns the weighted age per junction and hour and the central 90% of that 9-point posterior on the daily-mean age. `loss_split(model)` runs the calibrated (MAP) member four times: as calibrated, with wall decay off, with bulk decay off, and with no decay. With L the mean over the scored day's 24 hours of ln(C_ref / C), the bulk share is L(wall decay off) / L(as calibrated) and the wall share is 1 minus that; (L(wall off) + L(bulk off)) / L(as calibrated) is reported as a non-additivity diagnostic. The split is exact along a single plug-flow path, because EPANET's first-order bulk term and its mass-transfer-limited first-order wall term are both linear in C (Rossman, Clark & Grayman 1994, J Environ Eng 120(4):803), and approximate where flows of different ages mix. Shares are left undefined where the loss is under 2% (junctions next to a source). It refuses a model fitted under a chemistry condition, which SimGP24 cannot have yet, because its runs use the 20 C rates. Field context: wall loss is reported at up to 97% of the total in old pipes and bulk loss at up to 35% in newer ones (Maleki et al. 2023, Water Supply 23(2):657).
- `simulate.initial_water_share(wn, age)`: the share of each junction's water on the scored day that is still the water the 7-day run started with in its pipes, junctions and tanks. Water age mixes linearly, so a second AGE run with every junction's and tank's starting age raised by 1000 h raises each junction's age by exactly 1000 h times that share. Where the share is above 5% (`age.INITIAL_SHARE_MAX`), the run's age is a lower bound: that water is older than the run. A saved check compares it with an independent measure (one minus a no-decay chlorine run with every source at 1 and the starting water at 0). `build_scenario(truth_age=True)` now also gives the truth's own share (`sc.truth_initial_share`), from one more AGE run after all draws.
- A deviation from the plan, on purpose. The plan writes L with C_src, the source dose. The reference here is the member's own no-decay run, because of finding 6: on the scored day some junctions still receive water from the file's initial tank contents, and ln(C_src / C) would count that dilution as chlorine loss. Where the water has been replaced the two are the same. This costs four runs instead of the plan's two extra (the grid stores ln C floored at 0.02 mg/L, so the as-calibrated run is repeated rather than read from it).
- `simulate.build_scenario(truth_loss_split=False)`: when True, the truth's own perturbed network is rerun after all draws with wall decay off, bulk decay off and no decay, with every coefficient restored afterwards; `age.truth_loss_split(sc)` then gives the truth's own split, so the model's split can be scored. No new random draws: the chlorine truth and a following `truth_age` run are bit-identical to the runs without it (a saved check). The chemistry path records its quality scale, so the corrected-unit truth gives the same split as the legacy one.
- `residualmap/experiment_chem.py`, new, subcommand `age`. The 8 samples are drawn exactly as the app's demo draws them (8 distinct junctions, hours 07:00 to 17:00, 0.03 mg/L noise, readings rounded to 0.01 mg/L), so Net3 seed 0 under the default truth is the app's default demo. Junctions whose true daily-max age passes 160 h are excluded from the age metrics and counted, as the plan says; the same metrics are repeated on the junctions where neither the operator's model nor the truth holds more than 5% starting water (`converged_only` in the JSON, `conv_*` in the CSV). Outputs: `outputs/chem/water_age_<net>.csv` (one row per truth and seed), `water_age_<net>.json` (pooled over seeds; top-level numbers for the default truth, both truths under `by_truth`, the chlorine error by nominal-age tercile, the truth's nominal rates and dose under `settings.truth_settings`), `water_age_junctions_<net>.csv` (per junction of the operator's file: daily-mean and daily-max age, the 9-setting range, the initial-water share and whether the age is a lower bound), `water_age_<net>.png` (age map, range-width map, wall-share map, true daily-minimum chlorine against age coloured by `path_wall_index`), and `error_by_age_<net>.csv` (by nominal-age decile, per seed and pooled, with each bin's share of lower-bound ages; written for ky4 too).
- `route.plan_route(with_age=False)`: with True, the route gains a `water_age_h` column and each reason names the water age at that junction and hour in the operator's model ('at least' where more than 5% of the water then is starting water), plus a `why` column for the PDF. It is opt-in because `experiment.main` writes the default reasons into `summary_<net>.json`; the default text is unchanged, so the committed experiments still reproduce (a saved check compares the Net3 route with the committed one). The route's P(daily min < threshold) now comes from the model's own draws at the route's threshold, so it is right even for a model built for another threshold; at a matching threshold it is the same number as before. The new reason text has no em dash.
- `app.py`. A 'Water age' panel: the daily-maximum water age of the operator's model, and how far it moves across the 9 settings, called a 'range across demand and roughness errors' and quoting the simulated coverage of the daily-max and daily-mean age on the example networks (read from `by_truth.default` in `outputs/chem/water_age_<net>.json`; an uploaded file gets the same label and no number). The caption says every age is a 7-day-simulation age and counts the junctions whose age is a lower bound. A 'Where chlorine is lost' panel: the calibrated member's wall share per junction, with its median, the non-additivity range and its simulated accuracy on the example networks in the caption; with no samples it asks for one, and with no junction losing 2% it says there is nothing to split. The route table and reasons carry the water age. The PDF header gains the 'oldest water' line; where that junction's water at its oldest hour is more than 5% starting water it reads 'at least X h' and says why. The 10 age runs are cached once per network and the four split runs once per calibrated member.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: seven new checks, 30 in all, in 8 groups. The band's member order, its bit-identity with the nominal age and its initial-water share against the independent no-decay run; the posterior age as the weighted member age (one-hot weights return the member, uniform weights the mean); the split's exactness on the 12-pipe plug-flow chain; the truth's split leaving the truth unchanged; the default route reasons equal to the committed ones, the age opt-in, and the route's P at a threshold of 0.5; the committed water-age outputs (keys, the band-label rule, the lower-bound counts, the Net3 seed 0 row recomputed exactly, which is the app's demo); and an AppTest of the new panels on Net3, Net2 and with the demo off.
- After review, also in the app: the model is built at the threshold the operator sets (before, the map's daily-minimum P, the PDF headline, the demo reveal and the route's P stayed at 0.2 while the route labelled them with the operator's threshold); the PDF route table wraps its 'why' text instead of cutting it at 110 characters; the loss caption picks 'junction' or 'junctions' by count; and the example-network labels no longer name a utility or carry dashes.

**Found** (all simulated; every number is from `outputs/chem/water_age_<net>.json`, `.csv` or `water_age_junctions_<net>.csv`):
1. Every age here is a 7-day-run age, and many are lower bounds. Each run starts with the file's water in the pipes and tanks, at age 0, and where some of that water is still arriving on the scored day the age EPANET reports is lower than the real age. In the operator's file, more than 5% of the water on the day's average is starting water at 19 of Net3's 92 junctions, 28 of Net2's 35 and 745 of ky4's 959; at their oldest hour, at 87, 29 and 799. The truths are the same (on average 18.4, 27.25 and 743 junctions). The plan's 160 h cut catches only the extreme cases: it excludes nothing on Net3 and Net2 and 59 of 1918 junction-seeds on ky4. So the comparisons below are between 7-day transients, and the converged-only numbers are given beside them.
2. How far the file's age is from the truth's. RMSE of the file's daily-mean age against the simulated truth's: 5.82 h on Net3, 5.62 h on Net2 and 8.11 h on ky4. That is large next to the ages themselves: on Net3 it is more than half the median junction's age (10.50 h); Net2's median is 49.29 h and ky4's 58.06 h. Per seed the Net3 RMSE runs from 2.03 to 10.27 h, Net2's from 3.40 to 7.76 h. On the junctions where neither age is a lower bound the RMSE is 3.10 h on Net3 (571 of 736 junction-seeds), 1.02 h on Net2 (54 of 280) and 6.72 h on ky4 (418 of 1918). Under the persistent stress test the file says Net2's water is 12.39 h older on average than the truth's (RMSE 15.77 h): a tank with half the volume the file says turns over faster. Most of that is at junctions whose ages are lower bounds on both sides: where neither is, the stress-test RMSE is 1.96 h (56 junction-seeds). Net3 moves less (6.24 h). On ky4 the stress test drew one tank and no closed pipe in both seeds and barely moved the ages (8.10 h).
3. Whether the 9 settings make a 90% band. The truth's daily-mean age lies inside the range across the 9 demand and roughness settings at 0.766 of junctions on Net3, 0.579 on Net2 and 0.868 on ky4; for the daily-maximum age the app maps, at 0.791, 0.779 and 0.811; under the stress test at 0.717, 0.182 and 0.868 (daily mean). ky4's daily-mean coverage, 0.868 (0.876 and 0.860 per seed), passes the plan's own bar of 0.85; its daily-max coverage, 0.811, does not. The rule used here is stricter than the plan's: '90% band' only if every coverage in the file (daily mean and daily max, both truths) is at least 0.85. So all three networks use the label 'range across demand and roughness errors', with the numbers, ky4 included. ky4's 0.868 is also measured mostly over ages that are lower bounds; on the junctions where neither is, its coverage is 0.837 (daily max: 0.870 on 262 junction-seeds), and Net3's 0.737, Net2's 0.426. The cause is the one the plan expected: the truth perturbs each junction's demand (log-normal, 15%) and each pipe's roughness (10%), and the 9 settings only scale them globally. Per seed the Net3 coverage runs from 0.511 to 0.957.
4. Eight chlorine samples improve the pooled age estimate, but not every time. Weighting the 9 settings by the posterior after the 8 samples cuts the pooled age RMSE from 5.82 to 4.58 h on Net3 (bias from +1.73 to +0.14 h) and from 8.11 to 5.20 h on ky4; on Net2 it moves little (5.62 to 5.41 h), and under Net2's stress test it cannot fix the tank (15.77 to 14.81 h). It is worse than the file's own age on 2 of 8 seeds on Net3 (seeds 5 and 7: 2.83 h against 2.32, 2.65 against 2.03) and 2 of 8 on Net2 (seeds 1 and 7: 8.94 against 6.99, 4.05 against 3.95). The posterior's own 90% band is narrower (8.90 h wide against 11.57 h on Net3) and holds the truth less often (0.717, 0.539 and 0.768 on Net3, Net2 and ky4): a 9-point posterior is overconfident about age, for the same reason as finding 3.
5. Where the chlorine error sits by water age (SimGP24, 8 random daytime samples, unsampled junctions, daily minimum; the baseline for task 13). On Net3 every miss and most false alarms are in the middle third of nominal ages (9.0 to 14.3 h), where junctions cross 0.2 mg/L: 51 of 53 violations found there and 19 of the 31 false alarms, with the lowest 90% coverage (0.825); the oldest third (14.6 to 73.3 h) has the smallest error (RMSE 0.045 mg/L) and all 191 violations found. On Net2 the misses are also all in the middle third (16 of 22 found, 37.8 to 61.5 h); its false alarms and its lowest coverage are not (13 of the 32 false alarms are in the youngest third, and the lowest coverage, 0.845, is in the oldest). On ky4 the oldest third's band is too narrow (coverage 0.742). The bins are by the file's 7-day-run age, so in the older bins they order junctions rather than measure them: 61% of Net3's oldest third, all of Net2's middle and oldest thirds and 93% and 90% of ky4's middle and oldest thirds are lower bounds. Pooled: daily-minimum RMSE 0.078, 0.061 and 0.067 mg/L; 242 of 244, 84 of 90 and 764 of 780 violations found; 31, 32 and 151 false alarms on 672, 216 and 1902 unsampled junction-seeds (Net3, Net2, ky4). These are not the README's daily-minimum numbers: the samples here follow the app's draw and are rounded, not the experiment's sampling rules. Net3 seed 0 is the app's default demo exactly: 36 of 36 unsampled violations found, 1 false alarm.
6. Where chlorine is lost: mostly at the walls in these simulated truths. The truth's median wall share is 0.859 on Net3, 0.818 on Net2 and 0.624 on ky4; the calibrated member's is 0.869, 0.768 and 0.538, with a per-junction mean absolute difference of 0.064, 0.061 and 0.090 and a correlation of 0.966, 0.985 and 0.989 (means over seeds). The model puts less of the loss at the walls than the truth on Net2 (bias -0.046, and -0.085 under the stress test) and on ky4 (bias -0.081, and -0.143 under the stress test), where it calls 0.437 of junctions wall-dominated against the truth's 0.696: with 8 samples, bulk and wall decay trade off along the grid. Per scenario the calibrated median can be far off: by 0.053, 0.066 and 0.091 on average over seeds, and by up to 0.097, 0.265 and 0.177 (Net2 seed 1: 0.562 against 0.827; ky4 seed 0: 0.448 against 0.625; Net3 seed 0, the app's demo: 0.788 against 0.843). So the app calls it an estimate from the single most likely decay rates and quotes these numbers. The split is close to additive even where flows mix: the non-additivity stays within 0.962 to 1.028 on Net3 and 0.995 to 1.044 on ky4 under the default truth (up to 1.153 for ky4's stress-test truth). On the plug-flow chain it is exact (bulk rate recovered as 0.8002 /day for 0.8; non-additivity off by 0.00045). The wall dominance is a property of these synthetic truths (on Net3 wall decay 0.70 m/day with roughness and per-pipe factors, against bulk decay 0.40 /day; `settings.truth_settings`), not a field result.
7. A start-up transient in every 7-day run, recorded and not fixed here, because fixing it would move every committed Net2 and ky4 number. It is the same cause as finding 1, seen through chlorine: every run starts the file's tanks and pipes with no chlorine, and on the scored day some of that water has still not been replaced. In the calibrated member's no-decay run, the median junction's water is 1.3% initial contents on Net3 (worst junction 28.7%), 13.7% on Net2 (worst 27.4%) and 15.9% on ky4, where some junctions get no source water at all within the 7 days. The truth shares it: on average 4.6 of Net3's 92 junctions, 14.1 of Net2's 35 and 532.5 of ky4's 959 get less than 95% of the lowest source dose with no decay at all (Net2's stress-test truth, with its smaller tank, 1.0). The model's grid starts from the same state, so model-against-truth scores stay consistent, but absolute counts of low junctions on Net2 and ky4 are partly this transient. Proposed fix, for a later task: a longer warm-up for slow networks, or tanks started at source quality; either would also make the ages converge. It is also why the split's reference is the no-decay run.
8. The oldest water in each file, all lower bounds: junction 243 on Net3, at least 141 h at its oldest hour (62 to 141 h across the 9 settings), when 58% of its water is still starting water; junction 34 on Net2, at least 121 h (111 to 128 h), 34% starting water then; on ky4, junction J-247 at least 167 h, all of it starting water, and 26 junctions pass 160 h. The app and the PDF say 'at least' for these, and the README and this journal do not quote them as ages.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:** none in the default numbers. After the edits, the four committed reruns (`python -m residualmap.checks --reproduce`, labels `post_task9` and, after the review fixes, `post_task9_review` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`) write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs, so every Net3, Net2, persistent stress-test and synthetic-pilot number in the README and the sections above is unchanged; ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun. The app's default demo still flags 42 of 92 junctions and reads 41 violations, 36 of 36 unsampled found, 1 false alarm, sample mean 0.53 mg/L. Against the plan: (1) the plan's 160 h exclusion does not find the ages that have not converged; most ages on Net2 and ky4, and a fifth of Net3's, are lower bounds (finding 1), so the headline age numbers compare 7-day transients and the converged-only numbers sit beside them. (2) The '90% band' label rule here is stricter than the plan's; under the plan's literal rule ky4 (daily-mean coverage 0.868) would earn the label, under this one it does not (finding 3). (3) The posterior band is narrower than the truth needs, and the posterior is worse than the file on 2 of 8 seeds on Net3 and Net2 (finding 4). (4) The model's wall share is biased low on Net2 and ky4 and can be off by up to 0.265 in a single scenario (finding 6). (5) The plan's split formula was changed to a no-decay reference (four runs, not two extra), for the reason in finding 7. One behaviour change outside the default numbers: at a threshold other than 0.2, the app's daily-minimum flags, PDF headline, demo reveal and route P now use the model's own P at that threshold (before, the map used a normal approximation and the rest stayed at 0.2). Costs: the app runs 10 more EPANET simulations once per network and four per new calibrated member, both cached. Still open, for task 14: `experiment.NET_DESC` names a utility in the figure titles of the committed iteration-3 outputs; renaming it would change those figures, so it was left.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.experiment_chem age Net3 8`, `age Net2 8`, `age ky4 2`; outputs go to `outputs/chem/`. In the last run they took about 9 s, 5 s and 136 s of wall-clock time (from the run's own log, not stored in `outputs/`).

### Task 10, temperature: a seasonal model that nests today's model, tested out of season

**Asked:** make the plant's monthly water temperature an input. Each grab sample is explained at its own month's temperature, and any month, including a coming summer, is predicted at that month's temperature. The mechanism is a bank: for each distinct month temperature, the committed 675-member grid is re-run with its bulk and wall rates scaled by the Arrhenius factor f(T; E/R) for E/R = 5000, 8000 and 12000 K, plus EPANET's viscosity and diffusivity at that temperature; these blocks are stacked next to today's grid, which is kept as hypothesis H0, with a prior of 1/4 on each of the four hypotheses. A 12-month synthetic test on Net3 (16 seeds) and Net2 (8 seeds; ky4 is left out because one bank would take about 5 hours there, the plan's estimate, not measured): hidden truths with their own temperature sensitivity (V1), a 15 C control (V2) and in-network warming the model cannot see (V3); today's temperature-blind model (B0, previous 3 months), the same with 1 month (B1), the bank (M, 3 months; M6, up to 6 months), bulk-only Arrhenius (M1b), two sensitivity priors, persistence, the network mean and the best single member of the month's bank (oracle); a rolling test (April to December), an extrapolation test without refit (January to March predicts July to September; July to September predicts December), and a plus or minus 2 C error in the logged temperature. Also: `pilot.validate(plant=...)` and `--plant`, the docs/pilot_protocol.md fix of the unsourced "roughly doubles per 10 C", saved checks for direction and bank block order. The water temperature schedule, the soil curve, the warming time constants and the wall temperature factor are assumptions, not measurements.

**Pre-registered before the first scored run.** Written on 6 October 2026 at 22:15 PDT, after the code was written and before the seasonal model was run on any data; the bars below are not changed afterwards. Definitions: "M" is the bank with the uniform prior and a 3-month window; "pooled" means sums over seeds and target months (RMSE = square root of the summed squared error over the summed count; recall = violations flagged over violations; false alarms are counts); the daily-minimum map is scored at every junction not sampled in the 3-month window before the target month (the same set for every model), a junction flagged when P(daily min < 0.2 mg/L) > 0.5; held-out readings are scored at their own junction and hour, a reading flagged when its predicted median is below 0.2 mg/L; warming months are April to August and cooling months September to December. Every number is read from `outputs/chem/summary_season_<net>.json`, key `acceptance`.

- A1. `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`: every check passes, the new task-10 checks included.
- A2. V2 control (15 C every month), rolling test, each network: |RMSE_M / RMSE_B0 - 1| <= 0.05 for held-out readings and for the daily-minimum map, and |recall_M - recall_B0| <= 0.03 for held-out readings and for the daily-minimum map.
- A3. V1 seasonal, rolling test, each network: pooled held-out-reading RMSE_M <= RMSE_B0. The README claims a gain on a network only if the upper end of the paired bootstrap 90% interval of RMSE_M / RMSE_B0 (seeds resampled 2000 times) is below 1.0; otherwise it says "no measurable gain on <network>".
- A4. V1, Net3, extrapolation without refit, fit January to March and predict July, August and September, daily-minimum map pooled over seeds and the three months: RMSE_M <= 0.85 x RMSE_B0; recall_M >= recall_B0 + 0.10; coverage90_M >= 0.80. Reverse, fit July to September and predict December: false alarms_M <= false alarms_B0. (Computed for Net2 too and reported; the bar is set for Net3.)
- A5. V1, rolling, each network: the median over seeds of the standard deviation of M's kb20 across the 9 rolling windows is below the median over seeds of the standard deviation of B0's most likely (MAP) kb across the same windows. (A like-for-like comparison, B0's posterior-mean kb, is reported beside it and is not the bar.)
- A6. In the A4 test on Net3, with the logged temperature off by +2 C and by -2 C, in the forecast month only and in every logged month (four cases): M's daily-minimum recall falls by at most 0.10 from its value with the correct temperatures, in every case.
- A7. Stop rule. Rolling test, each network, each variant (V1, V2, V3) and each season class (warming, cooling): if RMSE_M > 1.10 x RMSE_B0, or recall_M < 0.90 x recall_B0, for held-out readings or for the daily-minimum map, stop and report before task 11. False alarms are printed beside recall; a rise of more than 10% (false alarms_M > 1.10 x false alarms_B0) is listed under "Regressions, recorded not hidden".
- Also recorded, not a bar: M's held-out-reading 90% coverage on V1 should fall in the pilot bands, 0.85 to 0.95 for taps seen in the window and 0.80 to 0.95 for new taps; a miss is recorded. Failing A3 or A4 is a legitimate outcome and is written up with its numbers.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** The stop rule A7 triggered on both networks, so this task stops here and task 11 waits for a decision; the model was not changed after the scored run. The stop is carried by Net3's warming-month map RMSE and Net2's cooling-month map recall, whose bootstrap intervals lie wholly on M's worse side of 1.0; four of Net2's six triggers are within count noise, one of them in the 15 C control (finding 1). On held-out readings the temperature-aware model (M) is no better than today's temperature-blind model (B0): RMSE ratio 0.974 on Net3 (90% interval 0.937 to 1.013) and 1.007 on Net2, so the README says "no measurable gain" on both. Fitted in winter and asked for summer it is worse where it matters most: from January-to-March samples it predicts the July-to-September map (pooled over the three months) with RMSE 0.137 mg/L against B0's 0.101 on Net3, and finds every violation (recall 0.996 against 0.753) only by raising 554 false alarms against B0's 18. The other way round, fitted on July to September and asked for December, it has the better RMSE (0.104 against 0.151) but the lower recall (0.790 against 1.000). The main cause is the wall. M scales the pipe-wall decay rate by the same temperature factor as the bulk rate (an assumption the plan flagged), while the simulated truth's wall decay moves much less with temperature; on these wall-dominated networks M therefore expects summer water to lose chlorine far faster than it does. Checks run after the stop confirm the wall as the main cause, though not the only one (finding 2). The bulk-only variant (M1b), which leaves the wall chemistry at 20 C, was never more than 10% worse than B0 in any class on Net3 and much better out of season (July-to-September map RMSE 0.077), but on Net2 it would also have triggered the stop rule; it was a guard, not the pre-registered model, and how strongly real pipe walls respond to temperature is not known from the literature either. Today's default model and every committed number are unchanged.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/seasonal.py`, new. `plant_schedule(variant)`: the monthly plant water temperature the operator logs (V1 and V3: 10.5, 10, 10.5, 12.5, 15, 17.5, 19.5, 20, 19.5, 17.5, 15, 12.5 C, January to December; V2: 15 C every month) and, for V3, the soil temperature the truth's water warms toward (16 + 6 sin(2 pi (m - 5)/12) C); all three are assumptions, labelled so in code, outputs and figures. `covariate_bank(sc, temps)`: hypothesis H0 is the committed grid; for each temperature T and E/R in {5000, 8000, 12000} K the committed 675 members are re-run with bulk and wall rates times f(T; E/R) and EPANET's viscosity and diffusivity at T; every block still to build, of every condition, goes into one process-pool call; each block is cached in `outputs/cache` under its `chemistry.cache_tag` name, or built in memory only (`cache="off"`, used for the plus or minus 2 C blocks and by the saved checks, `cache="read"` reads but never writes). At 20 C every factor is exactly 1, so the H_E blocks there are the committed grid itself. `Bank.stack(T)` puts the blocks side by side, H0 first, each in the grid's own (decay-major, hydraulic-minor) order, so `grid_dose_weights`, `posterior_moments`, `local_hydraulic_var`, `check_grid_order` and the dose offset work unchanged.
- `SeasonalSimGP24`, a subclass of `SimGP24` (which is not touched). `fit(samples, target_temp_C)`: each sample, with its month's logged temperature, is scored on the block of its own temperature; the posterior is SimGP24's Student-t likelihood (nu 3, scale 0.35) over all 2700 members and 5 doses, with a prior of 1/4 on each hypothesis (`uniform`) or, for the sensitivity runs only, `cejas` (H0 1/4, the H_E in proportion to N(E; 8000, 2000)) or `h0_0.1`. The discrepancy GP is fitted on the residuals against each sample's own month mean, with SimGP24's inputs and kernel. `set_target(T)` predicts any month from the same fit: moments, local hydraulic variance and daily-minimum Monte Carlo from the blocks at T. A plant dose per month (`dose_ratio`) is an exact ln-offset, because the model is still first order. Reported: `kb20()` (posterior mean of kb over the H_E members, a 20 C rate) and `hypothesis_posterior()`.
- `chemistry.Warming` and `simulate.build_scenario(warming=...)` (truth only, variant V3): every pipe gets its own bulk coefficient and wall factor at T_p = T_soil + (T_plant - T_soil) exp(-a_p / 12 h), with a_p the nominal daily-mean age at its downstream node; every tank its own bulk coefficient with a 72 h time constant; the viscosity and diffusivity options at the flow-weighted mixed temperature (`simulate.warming_temperatures`). It makes no random draw, so V3 is paired draw for draw with V1, and with the soil at the plant temperature it is V1 bit for bit (a saved check). `_truth_loss_runs` now also switches the per-pipe and per-tank bulk coefficients off and restores them.
- `pilot.synthetic_log(schedule=..., return_truth=...)`: with a schedule, 12 months under `Chemistry(temp_C=T_m)` (plus warming for V3), month seeds 1000 + 100 seed + m on this path only (the committed 100 + 10 seed + m repeats after ten months: seed 0's month 10 is seed 1's month 0), the logged temperature on every reading, and the plant log. Without a schedule the committed six-month log is unchanged, draw for draw. `pilot.load_plant`, `pilot.validate(plant=...)` and `python -m residualmap.pilot ... --plant plant_log.csv` (`month,temp_C,toc_mgL,dose_mgL`; `docs/example_plant_log.csv`, assumed values): the summary gains the month's temperature, kb20 and the hypothesis posterior; TOC is carried for task 11 and not used.
- `python -m residualmap.seasonal <net> <seeds> [--workers N]`: the experiment. One task per seed and variant in a process pool, one BLAS thread per worker (so the numbers do not depend on threading), each worker in its own scratch directory; the plus or minus 2 C blocks go to a temporary file in `outputs/cache` for the workers and are deleted at the end. Outputs: `outputs/chem/season_<net>.csv` (one row per variant, test, temperature error, seed, month and model, with the sums every pooled number is built from), `summary_season_<net>.json` (pooled metrics by variant, test and season class, paired bootstrap intervals, the `acceptance` key A1 to A7, the hypothesis posterior against the truth's E/R) and three figures.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: six new checks in a `seasonal` group (36 in all): the bank's block order and its blocks against direct builds; the model at 20 C against SimGP24; the warming truth and the 12-month log; the direction (warmer is lower, every junction); the pilot's plant-log path; and the committed task-10 outputs, with Net3 seed 0's April rows recomputed exactly.
- `simgp.py` needed no edit: the plan listed "bank cache tags only", and `grid_cache_path` already tags every chemistry condition, the bulk-only ablation included (label `M1b`).
- `docs/pilot_protocol.md`: the plant log in section 1's table (marked as tested and not adopted) and in the run line, and section 5's unsourced "roughly doubles per 10 C" replaced by the cited literature span, about 1.8 to 4.3 times per 10 C (E/R about 4660 to 12,100 K; the model's 5000 to 12000 K give 1.8 to 4.2). The real utility it named as the target pilot is replaced by "a local district", as in the iteration-3 CHANGELOG entry.
- After the stop, not pre-registered and never used to choose a model: `seasonal.mechanism_checks` (`python -m residualmap.seasonal Net3 8 --mechanism`, `outputs/chem/task10_mechanism_checks.json`, deterministic, no timings in it): the counts and paired bootstrap interval of every A7 trigger (`a7_triggers`, finding 1; `bootstrap_recall_ratio` is the recall companion of `bootstrap_ratio`, on the same resampled seeds), the same experiment code in three worlds that differ only in the truth's wall temperature law (`wall_worlds`), the oracle-wall bank on seed 0 (`oracle_wall_seed0`) and two numbers read from the committed rows (finding 2). For these only: `simulate.build_scenario(truth_wall_law=...)` ('theta_w' is the pre-registered truth and the default; 'arrhenius' gives the truth's wall its own bulk factor f(T; E_true); 'none' gives it no temperature factor) and `run_task(temp_errors=..., truth_wall_law=...)`; every default is unchanged, and the pre-registered world reproduces the 312 committed B0, M and M1b rows of seeds 0 to 7 exactly. The summer-forecast figures' title now says that its pooled numbers cover July, August and September; both figures were redrawn by rerunning both experiments, which rewrote every other task-10 output byte-identical.

**Found** (all simulated; every number is from `outputs/chem/summary_season_<net>.json` or `season_<net>.csv`, Net3 with 16 seeds and Net2 with 8, pooled over seeds and target months, except where a finding names `outputs/chem/task10_mechanism_checks.json`, written after the stop, or labels a number as a reviewer check not in `outputs/`):
1. The stop rule triggered, on both networks. On Net3, in the warming months (April to August) M's daily-minimum map RMSE is 1.229 times B0's under the seasonal truth V1 (0.093 against 0.076 mg/L) and 1.208 times under in-network warming V3 (0.092 against 0.076); in the cooling months (September to December) M flags 0.646 of the held-out readings below 0.2 mg/L against B0's 0.758 (V1, ratio 0.853) and 0.682 against 0.804 (V3, 0.849). On Net2 six class and metric pairs trigger: V1 warming map RMSE 1.134; V1 cooling reading recall 0.286 against 0.357 (4 against 5 of 14 low readings) and map recall 0.714 against 0.879; V2 warming reading recall 0.161 against 0.226 (5 against 7 of 31); V3 cooling reading recall 0.353 against 0.529 (6 against 9 of 17) and map recall 0.771 against 0.936. Several Net2 triggers rest on a handful of readings. The pre-registered rule makes no allowance for small counts, so they stand, but they do not carry the same weight. A paired bootstrap over seeds (2000 resamples, 90% intervals, the seeds resampled as for the RMSE intervals; computed after the stop, `outputs/chem/task10_mechanism_checks.json`, key `a7_triggers`) separates them. Map counts are low junction-months (junctions not sampled in the window, summed over seeds and target months); for Net2's reading recall, the 40, 8 and 7 of 2000 resamples in which B0 flags no low reading are dropped.

   | network | trigger (rolling) | M / B0 | counts, M / B0 | ratio (90% interval) | weight |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Net3 | V1 warming, map RMSE | 0.093 / 0.076 mg/L | 5867 junction-months scored | 1.229 (1.112 to 1.352) | carries the stop: wholly past the 1.10 bar |
   | Net3 | V3 warming, map RMSE | 0.092 / 0.076 mg/L | 5867 scored | 1.208 (1.079 to 1.348) | carries the stop: excludes 1.0 |
   | Net3 | V1 cooling, reading recall | 0.646 / 0.758 | 64 / 75 of 99 low readings | 0.853 (0.780 to 0.925) | a loss: excludes 1.0, but reaches above 0.90 |
   | Net3 | V3 cooling, reading recall | 0.682 / 0.804 | 73 / 86 of 107 | 0.849 (0.774 to 0.934) | a loss: excludes 1.0, but reaches above 0.90 |
   | Net2 | V1 cooling, map recall | 0.714 / 0.879 | 100 / 123 of 140 low junction-months | 0.813 (0.760 to 0.866) | carries the stop: wholly past the 0.90 bar |
   | Net2 | V3 cooling, map recall | 0.771 / 0.936 | 121 / 147 of 157 | 0.823 (0.753 to 0.884) | carries the stop: wholly past the 0.90 bar |
   | Net2 | V1 warming, map RMSE | 0.083 / 0.073 mg/L | 672 scored | 1.134 (0.971 to 1.335) | within noise: reaches 1.0 |
   | Net2 | V1 cooling, reading recall | 0.286 / 0.357 | 4 / 5 of 14 | 0.800 (0.667 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2 | V2 warming, reading recall | 0.161 / 0.226 | 5 / 7 of 31 | 0.714 (0.000 to 1.000) | within count noise, and in the 15 C control, where temperature cannot be the cause |
   | Net2 | V3 cooling, reading recall | 0.353 / 0.529 | 6 / 9 of 17 | 0.667 (0.571 to 1.000) | within count noise |

   So the stop rests on Net3's warming-month map RMSE and Net2's cooling-month map recall. Net3's cooling-month reading recall is a real loss but not clearly past the bar, and four of Net2's six triggers, the V2 control's included, are within count noise.
2. Why: mostly the wall. M multiplies each member's wall decay rate by the same Arrhenius factor as its bulk rate. The hidden truth multiplies its wall rate by theta_w^(T - 20), with theta_w drawn per seed between 1.00 and 1.07; the seeds drawn here have theta_w from 1.0015 to 1.0631, so the truth's wall decay is 1.02 to 1.84 times faster at 20 C than at 10 C, while M's three wall hypotheses make it 1.83, 2.62 and 4.24 times faster. Both truths lose most of their chlorine at the walls (task 9: a median wall share of 0.859 on Net3 and 0.818 on Net2). So M expects warm water to lose chlorine much faster than it does, and cold water much slower. Out of season this is plain: fitted on January to March and asked for July to September, M flags almost every violation on Net3 (recall 0.996) but with 554 false alarms against B0's 18 (precision 0.699 against 0.982) and a map RMSE of 0.137 against 0.101; asked the other way, from July to September to December, M is better on RMSE (0.104 against 0.151), coverage (0.971 against 0.393) and false alarms (59 against 163) but misses more (recall 0.790 against 1.000). The rolling test shows the same pattern in miniature: in warming months M raises more map false alarms (385 against 185 on Net3), in cooling months it flags fewer low readings.

   Checked directly after the stop, with the same code (`python -m residualmap.seasonal Net3 8 --mechanism`, `outputs/chem/task10_mechanism_checks.json`; not pre-registered; Net3, V1, seeds 0 to 7, B0, M and M1b, no plus or minus 2 C test):

   - Three worlds that differ only in the truth's wall temperature law (`wall_worlds`). The pre-registered world reproduces the 312 committed rows exactly. Where the truth's wall follows f(T; E_true), the structure M assumes, M beats B0: January to March predicting July to September, map RMSE 0.092 against 0.185 mg/L and recall 601 against 318 of 611 low junction-months (though with 127 false alarms against 0); rolling, warming-month reading RMSE 0.103 against 0.142 and cooling-month map RMSE 0.103 against 0.135. Where the truth's wall has no temperature factor, M loses (out-of-season map RMSE 0.161 against 0.067) and M1b has the best rolling map (0.079 against B0's 0.085). So the pipeline does not penalise M; the stop comes from the mismatch between M's wall law and the truth's.
   - The oracle-wall bank on seed 0 (`oracle_wall_seed0`): M's bank rebuilt in memory with every block's wall rate times the truth's own theta_w^(T - 20) (theta_w 1.0631), the bulk still f(T; E). M's July map RMSE falls from 0.154 to 0.098 mg/L and its bias from -0.137 to -0.083 (E8000 block -0.102 to -0.047, E12000 -0.169 to -0.058), but M stays worse than B0 (0.082) and M1b (0.049) on that seed: even with the right wall every E/R block decays too fast (bias -0.037, -0.047 and -0.058 at 5000, 8000 and 12000 K).
   - From the committed rows, over 16 Net3 seeds: the correlation between theta_w and M's out-of-season map RMSE minus B0's is -0.58; the weaker the truth's wall response, the worse M does.

   Hence "mostly": the wall is the main cause, not the only one. The smaller contributors: E/R is not identified (finding 6), so even with the oracle wall 0.42 of the posterior sits on 12000 K against the truth's 8581 K; and a reviewer found the Monte Carlo daily minimum of B0, M and M1b already biased low inside the fit window (-0.021 to -0.038 mg/L in March, 8 seeds; a reviewer check, not in `outputs/`), while the discrepancy GP moved M's July bias by only 0.005 (also a reviewer check, not in `outputs/`).

   The truth's wall factor is itself an assumption (no published wall temperature factor exists; Lee et al. 2014 give only the direction), so this measures how M behaves when the wall responds weakly to temperature, not how real pipes behave.
3. The bulk-only ablation M1b (the plan's guard: wall chemistry left at 20 C, the wall moving only through the mass-transfer coefficient) did what M was meant to do on Net3, without being the pre-registered model. Rolling, V1: held-out-reading RMSE ratio to B0 0.952 (90% interval 0.935 to 0.970), daily-minimum map 0.931 (0.907 to 0.952). January to March predicting July to September: map RMSE 0.077 against B0's 0.101, recall 0.870 against 0.753, coverage 0.876, 58 false alarms against 18. July to September predicting December: RMSE 0.124 against 0.151, 109 false alarms against 163. On Net3 it is never more than 10% worse than B0 in any variant and season class (lowest recall ratio 0.953, highest RMSE ratio 1.007), so it would not have triggered A7 there. On Net2 it would have: reading recall ratios 0.800, 0.857 and 0.889 in three classes, on 14, 31 and 17 low readings, while its RMSE ratios stay between 0.908 and 1.012 (map recall 0.904 against 0.494 in the July-to-September forecast). This is a post hoc reading of one guard, against a truth whose wall factor sits in M1b's favour; adopting M1b, or adding weak-wall hypotheses to the bank, needs a decision and a fresh pre-registered test on new seeds.
4. What passed. On Net3, the 15 C control behaves (A2): RMSE ratio 0.988 for readings and 0.982 for the map, recall within 0.016 and 0.006. M is not worse on readings (A3: 0.109 against 0.112 mg/L) but the bootstrap interval reaches 1.013, so there is no measurable gain. The judge figure works as the plan described: B0's most likely kb rides with the seasons (median 0.10 /day in the windows ending March to June, then 0.25 to 0.325) while M's kb20 stays between 0.323 and 0.362 (A5: median standard deviation across windows 0.053 against 0.132; against B0's posterior-mean kb, 0.072). A flat kb20 is not a good forecast, though: finding 2 is about the wall, which kb20 does not show. A 2 C error in the logged temperature costs almost nothing (A6: at most 0.007 of recall on Net3 and 0.013 on Net2), because recall out of season is already near 1; it moves false alarms instead (+2 C in the forecast month: 741 against 554 on Net3).
5. Net2 fails more. Control (A2): the RMSE ratios are 0.993 and 0.995, but the reading recall is 0.159 against 0.205, 7 against 9 of 44 low readings, a difference of 0.045 against the 0.03 bar. A3: 0.126 against 0.125 mg/L (ratio 1.007, interval 0.957 to 1.066). A5: B0's most likely kb does not move at all across the 9 windows in 7 of 8 seeds, and its median is the grid's lowest value (0.10 /day) in every window, because Net2's true bulk rate (0.10 /day at 20 C, times 0.8 to 1.2 and the temperature factor) sits at or below the grid's floor all year. M's kb20 moves instead (median standard deviation 0.064): in cold windows the slow decay can be matched by a high kb20 with a large E/R (median kb20 0.272 for the January-to-March fit against a 20 C truth of about 0.10), which is the kb20 and E/R confounding the plan predicted for one-temperature windows.
6. E/R is not identified, as expected. The correlation between M's posterior-mean E/R and the truth's is 0.005 on Net3 and 0.144 on Net2; the mean posterior under V1 on Net3 is 0.223 on H0, 0.353 on E/R 5000 K, 0.285 on 8000 K and 0.139 on 12000 K, with no trend across the truth's E/R terciles. In the 15 C control, H0 keeps only 0.136 (Net3) and 0.108 (Net2), so the mass on H0 says nothing about seasonality.
7. Bands. M's 90% band on held-out readings is better calibrated than B0's (V1, rolling, Net3: 0.85 against 0.74 for all readings), but taps seen in the window come in under the pilot band of 0.85 to 0.95: just under on Net3 (0.8495), and by about 0.04 on Net2 (0.812) (recorded miss); new taps are inside their band (0.872 and 0.851).
8. The floor. The oracle, the best single member and dose of the target month's bank against the full daytime truth, has a reading RMSE of 0.071 mg/L and a map RMSE of 0.053 on Net3 (0.085 and 0.041 on Net2), against 0.109 to 0.112 and 0.093 to 0.094 for M and B0. Most of the remaining error is calibration from 39 readings, not the first-order form.
9. The truth's seasons are real in this test: the share of junctions below 0.2 mg/L at the daily minimum under V1 rises from 0.206 in January to 0.387 in August on Net3, and from 0.114 in January (0.111 in February and March) to 0.525 in August on Net2.
10. Two saved-check tolerances, set before any measurement, were wrong and were changed after the first full checks run, both on numerical grounds. The plant-log path at 20 C reproduces the temperature-blind pilot path to 1.3e-8 mg/L, not the 1e-9 first written (the two paths sum the same numbers in a different order, and the GP's hyperparameter search enlarges that last-bit difference); the bar is now 1e-6. The recomputation of the committed Net3 rows matched B0 exactly but M only to the last bit, because numpy here uses Apple's Accelerate, whose thread count `threadpoolctl` cannot set; the experiment's workers run single-threaded through environment variables set before they start, so the check now recomputes in a subprocess started the same way, and matches all 102 values exactly.
11. Limitations, recorded. Every truth, every bank member and every model share the committed 7-day run, so the start-up transient found in task 9 is in all of them (on Net2 the median junction's water on the scored day is still 13.7% the run's starting contents): comparisons between models are internally fair and comparable with the committed numbers, but absolute counts of low junctions on Net2 partly reflect it. The temperature schedule, the soil curve, the warming time constants and the truth's wall factor are assumptions. Seasonal demand is not in the truth (summer demand shortens water age and partly offsets warm-water decay). ky4 is not in this test (the plan estimates about 5 hours for one bank there; not measured); for ky4-sized systems the plan's advice stands: bin temperatures to 1 C and use E/R 8000 K only, or the 75-run decay grid. The grid's kw axis is read as 20 C rates, so in winter the high-E/R blocks cannot reach the truth's wall: on seed 0 the January-to-March fit puts 0.975 of the E12000 block's mass and 0.43 of the E8000 block's at kw20 = 1.5 m/day, the top of the grid (`oracle_wall_seed0` in `task10_mechanism_checks.json`), where the E12000 block's wall rate at 10 C is 1.5 x 0.236 = 0.35 m/day against the truth's nominal winter 0.70 x 1.0631^(T - 20) = 0.38 to 0.39 m/day. Lifting the cap would make E12000 scale the wall even more in July, so it does not bias the comparison against M. And H0 keeps little mass after a winter fit: a mean of 0.104 over 16 Net3 seeds (0.012 to 0.21; `from_committed_rows`), where the plan's risk section expected it to keep about its prior 1/4, so out of season M is mostly its temperature-scaled hypotheses, not something close to today's model.
