**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The pre-registered model failed its own bars: A7 triggered on both networks (finding 1), A4 failed on Net3, and A2, A3 and A5 failed on Net2. Seen-tap reading coverage is under the pilot band on both networks: just under on Net3 (0.8495), by about 0.04 on Net2 (0.812).
- False alarms rose by more than 10% in every warming class where seasons are in the truth: Net3 V1 readings 49 against 14 and map 385 against 185, V3 readings 55 against 12 and map 376 against 170; Net2 V1 readings 48 against 14 and map 145 against 54, V3 readings 53 against 15 and map 126 against 44. Over all months on Net3 (V1) the map false alarms are 605 against 547 (+11%), the size the prototype saw; out of season they are far larger (554 against 18).
- None in the default numbers. After all the edits the four committed reruns (`python -m residualmap.checks --reproduce`, label `post_task10` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`) write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs, and the app's default demo still flags 42 of 92 junctions and reads 41 violations, 36 of 36 unsampled found, 1 false alarm, sample mean 0.53 mg/L. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun. Nothing in the app or the default model uses the temperature path.
- Against the plan: the plus or minus 2 C test was run in two modes (the forecast month only, and every logged month), and A6 was pre-registered on the worse of the four cases; the daily-minimum map is scored on the 3-month window's unsampled junctions for every model, so M6 (and B1) see a slightly different share of them; `simgp.py` needed no edit; two check tolerances were changed after measurement (finding 10). A first launch of the full run was stopped after one minute of bank building and restarted with 6 workers instead of 11, because the machine was already using 6 GB of swap, which shares the disk with the data volume; no scored output came from the first launch. A 1-seed Net2 smoke run (seed 0, one of the scored seeds), after the pre-registration and written to a scratch folder, found one bug (the forecast-month temperature error asked M's bank for a temperature only the plus or minus 2 C blocks hold; M now uses the merged bank, whose blocks are identical at every M temperature). Two more edits to `seasonal.py` followed it before the scored run, both to reporting, not to any model: A1 is recorded as null in the summary instead of being read from `checks_report.json` at run time (that file's `season_outputs_reproduce` check reads the summary, so the verdict cannot be written there), and seen-tap and new-tap coverage is reported at 90% only, where the plan listed 50, 80, 90 and 95% (all four are still reported for all readings). The pre-registration says it was written after the code was written; in fact `seasonal.py` (twice), `pilot.py` and the task-10 checks in `checks.py` were edited in the three minutes after it (22:17 to 22:19), before the smoke run, and after the scored run (22:28 to 22:39) `seasonal.py` and `pilot.py` had docstring-only edits and `checks.py` the tolerance changes of finding 10 (times from the session's edit history, read by a reviewer). None of this changes M: the saved check recomputes Net3 seed 0's April rows exactly, and rerunning both experiments with the final code writes every scored output byte-identical. A7 was pre-registered on the rolling test only, narrower than the plan's "any network, variant or season class"; applied to the extrapolation tests it would also trigger on Net3 (January to March predicting July to September: map RMSE ratio 1.364; July to September predicting December: map recall 0.790 against 1.000). The verdict is the same. Also after the stop and not pre-registered: the trigger intervals of finding 1 and the mechanism checks of finding 2, with the two optional keywords they need (defaults unchanged), and the corrected summer-forecast title.
- Costs: the banks add 285 MB of git-ignored files to `outputs/cache` (measured with du; Net3 and Net2, M and M1b; the plus or minus 2 C blocks are deleted after each run). The Net3 banks took 466 s to build (38,475 EPANET runs on 11 processes) and the 48 seed and variant tasks about 100 s on 6 workers; Net2 took 111 s in all; the mechanism checks take about 100 s (the timings and run counts are from the runs' own logs; none of these numbers is stored in `outputs/`). The tracked season CSVs are the largest outputs in the repository, `season_Net3.csv` 2.3 MB and `season_Net2.csv` 1.2 MB; they keep full float precision because `season_outputs_reproduce` compares committed rows exactly.

**What needs a decision before task 11** (nothing below was done): (a) keep today's model and record temperature as tested and not adopted, with the plant log kept as an input for later; (b) make the bulk-only variant the model and test it again, pre-registered, on fresh seeds (for example 16 to 31 on Net3, 8 to 15 on Net2), knowing that its advantage here rests on the truth's assumed weak wall factor; (c) widen the hypotheses so the wall's temperature response is calibrated rather than assumed (for example wall E/R in {0, 5000} K crossed with the bulk E/R), at about twice the bank size, then test that pre-registered; in that test, define the kw axis per hypothesis or at the window temperature, so high-E/R blocks are not stuck at the grid edge (finding 11), and report H0's posterior mass after the out-of-season fits next to the control's 0.136. Task 13's two-reactant audit with in-pipe warming would also say how much of the summer error is the first-order shortcut rather than the wall.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal Net3 16 --workers 6` and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal Net2 8 --workers 6` (outputs to `outputs/chem/`, banks to `outputs/cache/`; a build is refused below 1.5 GB free disk); `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal Net3 8 --mechanism --workers 6` for the checks run after the stop (reads the committed `season_<net>.csv` files, writes `outputs/chem/task10_mechanism_checks.json`); `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.pilot --inp <file> --samples <log> --taps <map> --plant <plant log>` for the real-data path.

### Task 10b, the model learns the wall's temperature response

**Asked:** task 10's pre-registered model M stopped at its stop rule A7. Reviewers confirmed the stop is real and mostly caused by M's wall assumption: M scales each member's pipe-wall decay rate by the same Arrhenius factor as its bulk rate, while task 10's truth has a weak wall response (theta_w between 1.00 and 1.07, itself an assumption, since no published wall temperature response exists). In a world where the truth's wall follows its own bulk factor, M beat B0 widely; with the weak wall, M lost and the bulk-only ablation M1b won on Net3. Devansh chose to have the model learn the wall's response instead of assuming it. So: a model M2 whose hypotheses cross the bulk E/R with a wall E/R of its own, with today's grid nested as H0 and with M (wall E/R equal to bulk) and M1b (wall E/R 0) nested in it; a wall E/R grid from 0 to at least 12000 K; every task-10 bank block reused; the new cache within the disk budget; and the reviewer's note on the kw axis checked (read as 20 C rates, the grid may hold a steep wall hypothesis below what the winter data want) and recorded. Then a test, pre-registered here, on fresh seeds, in two truth worlds, with task 10's bars and two new ones.

**Pre-registered before the first scored run.** Written on 7 October 2026 at 00:08 PDT, after the code was written and run once on an unscored seed (Net2 seed 100, written to a scratch folder, to check that it runs end to end), and before M2 was run on any scored seed; the bars below are not changed afterwards. A copy of this block is kept outside the repository.

Definitions:
- **M2**: hypothesis H0 (today's grid, the same every month) plus 15 pairs, bulk E/R in {5000, 8000, 12000} K crossed with wall E/R in {0, 2500, 5000, 8000, 12000} K. Pair (b, w) is the committed 675-member grid re-run at the month's temperature T with every bulk rate times f(T; b), every wall rate times f(T; w), and EPANET's viscosity and diffusivity at T. The pairs (b, b) are M's blocks and (b, 0) are M1b's, the same cached files. The wall grid's 20 C to 10 C rate ratios are 1.00, 1.35, 1.83, 2.62 and 4.24. Prior: 1/4 on H0, as in M, and 0.05 on each pair, so each bulk E/R keeps M's 1/4 and every wall E/R within it gets the same share. Likelihood, discrepancy GP, daily-minimum Monte Carlo and the 3-month window are M's.
- **M** and **M1b**: task 10's, unchanged (1/4 on each of their four hypotheses). **B0**: today's temperature-blind model, previous 3 months. **Oracle**: the best single member and dose of M2's bank for the target month against the full daytime truth.
- **Worlds**: W1 is task 10's truth (the wall factor theta_w^(T - 20), theta_w ~ U(1.00, 1.07) per seed); W2 gives the truth's wall its own bulk factor f(T; E_true), with the same E_true draw. Nothing else differs between them (`simulate.build_scenario(truth_wall_law='theta_w' or 'arrhenius')`).
- **Seeds**: Net3 16 to 31, Net2 8 to 15, fresh (task 10 scored Net3 0 to 15 and Net2 0 to 7; the code refuses those). Every seed runs in both worlds and all three variants.
- **Same as task 10**: schedules (assumptions), V1 seasonal, V2 15 C control, V3 in-network warming, 12-month logs of 10 route taps and 3 rotating taps, rolling test April to December, the two out-of-season tests without refit (January to March predicting July, August and September, pooled; July to September predicting December), the plus or minus 2 C test, scoring of readings and of the daily-minimum map, flags, warming (April to August) and cooling (September to December) classes, the paired bootstrap over seeds (2000 resamples).
- Every number is read from `outputs/chem/summary_season2_<net>.json`, key `acceptance.<world>`, computed from `season2_<net>.csv` and `season2_windows_<net>.csv`.

Bars, in each world (W1 and W2) and on each network unless a bar says otherwise:
- A1. `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`: every check passes, the new task-10b checks included.
- A2. V2 control, rolling: |RMSE_M2 / RMSE_B0 - 1| <= 0.05 and |recall_M2 - recall_B0| <= 0.03, for held-out readings and for the daily-minimum map.
- A3. V1, rolling: pooled held-out-reading RMSE_M2 <= RMSE_B0. A gain is claimed only if the upper end of the bootstrap 90% interval of RMSE_M2 / RMSE_B0 is below 1.0; otherwise "no measurable gain on <network> in <world>".
- A4. Net3, V1, January to March predicting July to September, daily-minimum map: RMSE_M2 <= 0.85 x RMSE_B0; recall_M2 >= recall_B0 + 0.10; coverage90_M2 >= 0.80. July to September predicting December: false alarms_M2 <= false alarms_B0. (Computed for Net2 too and reported; the bar is set for Net3.)
- A5. V1, rolling: the median over seeds of the standard deviation of M2's kb20 across the 9 windows is below the same for B0's most likely kb.
- A6. Net3, in the A4 test, with the logged temperature off by +2 C and by -2 C, in the forecast month only and in every logged month: M2's daily-minimum recall falls by at most 0.10, in every case.
- A7. Stop rule, M2 against B0, each network, each variant (V1, V2, V3) and each class (rolling warming, rolling cooling, January to March predicting July to September, July to September predicting December): stop if RMSE_M2 > 1.10 x RMSE_B0 or recall_M2 < 0.90 x recall_B0, for held-out readings or for the daily-minimum map. This follows the plan's wording ("any network, variant or season class"); task 10 pre-registered the rolling test only, which its journal lists as a deviation, and the rolling-only verdict is reported beside this one. For every trigger the counts and a paired bootstrap 90% interval over seeds are reported: a trigger whose interval lies wholly on M2's worse side of 1.0 is called robust, one whose interval reaches 1.0 is called within count noise. Both count toward the stop: the rule makes no allowance for small counts. False alarms up by more than 10% (M2 against B0) are listed under "Regressions, recorded not hidden".
- R1. Robustness, in each world, network, variant and A7 class: M2's daily-minimum map RMSE is at most 1.10 x the smaller of M's and M1b's, and its map recall at least 0.90 x the larger of M's and M1b's.
- R2. Reported, no bar: whether M2's posterior over the wall E/R moves toward the truth's own wall response (W2: E_true; W1: ln(theta_w) x 293.15 x 283.15 K, the E/R with the same 10 C to 20 C ratio, 0 to 5616 K), for all rolling windows, for the January-to-March and July-to-September windows (0.5 C of contrast each) and for the windows spanning 4 C or more; and P(H0) after the out-of-season fits next to the 15 C control's.
- Adoption. M2 becomes the temperature model (it would replace M behind `pilot --plant`; the app has no temperature input yet) only if A7 passes in both worlds on both networks. Otherwise it is recorded as "tested, not adopted", with the numbers and the framing that the deciding unknown is the wall's real temperature response, which real seasonal data would settle.
- Also recorded, not bars: M2's held-out-reading 90% coverage against the pilot bands (0.85 to 0.95 for seen taps, 0.80 to 0.95 for new taps); the kw-axis check (`kw_cap` in the summary: where the grid's top kw, read as a 20 C rate, falls below each world's nominal wall rate at each month's temperature, and the posterior share each wall hypothesis puts at that top).

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** The stop rule A7 triggered, so M2 is tested and not adopted, and the model was not changed after the scored run. It triggered on Net3 in both worlds and on Net2 in the weak-wall world W1; on Net2 in W2 it did not. Under task 10's narrower rolling-only rule it would also have triggered in the same three places (finding 1). M2 does what it was built to do in part. Its held-out readings are measurably better than today's model in all four network and world pairs (RMSE ratio 0.774 to 0.916, every 90% interval below 1.0), and in the test fitted on January to March and asked for July to September it has the smallest worst case of the four models across the two worlds: its map RMSE on Net3 is 0.112 mg/L in W1 and 0.083 in W2, where today's model has 0.113 and 0.180, M 0.144 and 0.104, and M1b 0.090 and 0.138 (finding 2, a reading after the fact, not a bar). But in the weak-wall world it still expects summer water to lose more chlorine at the walls than it does (Net3 warming-month map RMSE 1.124 times today's, interval 1.056 to 1.191, with 272 false alarms against 142); fitted on July to September in that world it flags 8 of the 12 low December readings where today's model flags all 12 (ratio 0.667, interval 0.400 to 0.889, the one trigger whose interval lies wholly past the 0.90 bar); and in cooling months it flags fewer low readings than today's model in both worlds, with reading-recall losses in the 15 C control too (finding 1). It also misses the robustness bar R1 on every network and in every world: somewhere it is more than 10% worse than the better of M and M1b, in W1 mostly against M1b and in W2 mostly against M (finding 3). From the same seeds its posterior mean wall E/R is 1539 K higher in W2 than in W1 on Net3, but against the 15 C control, which has the same truths and no temperature contrast, the move is one-sided: in W1 it goes toward the weak-wall truth, in W2 it does not go toward the steep one, so on Net3 the seasons teach M2 that a weak wall is weak but not that a steep wall is steep (finding 4). On Net2 the floors of the bulk-rate and wall-rate grids, not only the data, push its winter wall posterior toward steep walls (finding 5, found after the review). The deciding unknown is still how real pipe walls respond to temperature, which a year of real grab samples with plant temperatures would settle. Today's default model and every committed number are unchanged.

**Built:**
- `chemistry.Chemistry(wall_er_K=...)`: a model condition's own wall E/R. Under wall mode 'arrhenius' the wall rate is scaled by f(T; wall_er_K) and the bulk rate by f(T; er_K); None (the default) keeps the wall on er_K, as before. It is stored in canonical form, so a condition that is physically a task-10 condition IS that condition: a wall E/R equal to the bulk E/R is stored as None (task 10's M block) and a wall E/R of 0 as wall mode 'mass_transfer_only' (task 10's M1b block). `cache_tag` leaves an unset wall E/R out of its hash, so every task-10 tag and cache name is unchanged (the committed T12.5/E8000 tag, 72f9c19f47, is a saved check); a new pair gets its own tag and the label part `W<e>`, for example `free_T10_E8000_W2500`.
- `residualmap/seasonal.py`, a task 10b section. `WALL_ER_HYPOTHESES_K = (0, 2500, 5000, 8000, 12000)` K: 0 is M1b's structure, 5000 to 12000 are the bulk hypotheses (M's structure on the diagonal), 2500 fills the gap; their 20 C to 10 C wall ratios are 1.00, 1.35, 1.83, 2.62 and 4.24, about even steps on a log scale. `WallBank`, a `Bank` whose hypotheses are (bulk, wall) pairs; `nested('M')` and `nested('M1b')` give task 10's banks over the same arrays, so a worker holds them once. `wall_bank(sc, temps)`: the 15 pairs at every temperature, the pairs (b, b) and (b, 0) read from task 10's cached files, the other 9 per temperature built in one process-pool call and cached under their own tags; the plus or minus 2 C blocks built in memory only. `nested_prior` (H0 1/4, each pair 0.05). `WallSeasonalSimGP24`, a `SeasonalSimGP24` subclass for M2: its prior, or any `prior_mass` (zeros allowed), and the posterior by wall E/R and by bulk E/R, the posterior mean wall E/R, and the share of each wall hypothesis's mass at the top of the kw grid. `SeasonalSimGP24.posterior_columns()` (the experiment rows' P columns, unchanged for M and M1b); `Bank.merged` keeps the subclass.
- `run_task(bank_m2=...)`: with M2's bank, M2 is fitted beside M and M1b in every test, the plus or minus 2 C test runs on M2 instead of M, and the oracle is the best member of M2's bank (which holds M's and M1b's). Without it every row is task 10's: rerunning task 10's Net2 experiment with the new code wrote all five task-10 Net2 outputs byte-identical, and the saved check recomputes Net3 seed 0's April rows exactly (both before the scored run).
- `python -m residualmap.seasonal <net> <n> --wall [--first-seed N] [--worlds W1,W2]` (`run_wall`): one task per world, seed and variant, 6 workers, each holding M2's bank and its plus or minus 2 C blocks (about 1.2 GB on Net3, from the arrays' sizes); seeds default to Net3 16 and Net2 8 onward, and any seed task 10 scored is refused. Outputs, kept compact: `outputs/chem/season2_<net>.csv` (the sums every pooled number is built from, summed over the target months of each world, variant, test, temperature error, seed, season class and model, 6 significant digits), `season2_windows_<net>.csv` (one row per rolling fit: the fitted rates, M's and M2's posteriors, the truth's E_true, theta_w and wall response as an E/R), `summary_season2_<net>.json` (pooled metrics, paired bootstrap intervals, the pre-registered acceptance key per world, R2, P(H0), the kw-axis check), computed from the two CSVs as written; figures `season2_<net>.png` (map RMSE and recall of every model in the four A7 classes, both worlds) and `wall_posterior_<net>.png` (R2).
- `kw_cap_table`: per wall E/R and plant temperature, the largest nominal wall rate the grid can represent (1.5 m/day at 20 C times f(T; wall E/R)) against each world's nominal wall rate range at T; `kw_top_observed`: the posterior share each wall hypothesis puts at the top of the kw grid after the January-to-March fit and over all rolling fits.
- After the review, with no rerun (the scored CSVs, the figures and every bar unchanged): `_a7_entry` lists false-alarm rises from zero, which the scored summary had left out (ratio null, `from_zero` true); `kw_cap_table` also reports where the bottom of the kw grid (0.10 m/day times f(T; wall E/R)) and of the kb grid (0.10 per day times f(T; bulk E/R)) lies above the truth's nominal range (`where_the_floor_binds`, `kb_floor`); `resummarise_wall` (`--wall --resummarise`) rewrites `summary_season2_<net>.json` from the committed CSVs, and both summaries were rewritten with it (diffed against the scored ones: the only differences are three added rises, a `from_zero` flag on each listed rise and the floor fields; the same code without the two changes rewrote both byte-identical); `WallSeasonalSimGP24.edge_shares()` and `grid_edge_checks` (`--wall --grid-edges`) refit M2 on every V1 rolling window of the scored seeds in both worlds and write the posterior mass at the edges of both grids to `outputs/chem/task10b_grid_edges_<net>.json`; the refits match the committed windows rows in every M2 column (5184 values on Net3, 2592 on Net2, identical to 6 significant digits).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: three checks in a new `seasonal_wall` group: the canonical forms and tags (quick); the wall bank against direct builds and M2 reduced to M and to M1b by its prior (decay grid, in memory); and the committed task-10b outputs, with Net3 seed 16's W2 July-to-September-to-December rows and October fit recomputed in a single-threaded subprocess.

**Found** (all simulated; every number is from `outputs/chem/summary_season2_<net>.json`, computed from `season2_<net>.csv` and `season2_windows_<net>.csv`, except finding 5's posterior shares at the grid's floors and today's kb at the floor, which are from `outputs/chem/task10b_grid_edges_<net>.json`, added after the review; Net3 with seeds 16 to 31 and Net2 with seeds 8 to 15, each in both worlds; pooled over seeds and target months):
1. The stop rule triggered, in three of the four network and world pairs. Every trigger, with its counts and the paired bootstrap 90% interval over seeds (`acceptance.<world>.A7.triggered`); "robust" means the interval lies wholly on M2's worse side of 1.0, as pre-registered, and both kinds count:

   | network, world | trigger | M2 / B0 | counts, M2 / B0 | ratio (90% interval) | weight |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Net3, W1 | V1 rolling warming, map RMSE | 0.088 / 0.078 mg/L | 5874 junction-months scored | 1.124 (1.056 to 1.191) | robust |
   | Net3, W1 | V3 rolling warming, map RMSE | 0.087 / 0.078 mg/L | 5874 scored | 1.115 (1.036 to 1.189) | robust |
   | Net3, W1 | V1 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.675 / 0.762 | 54 / 61 of 80 low readings | 0.885 (0.787 to 0.969) | robust |
   | Net3, W1 | V3 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.667 / 0.789 | 60 / 71 of 90 | 0.845 (0.769 to 0.908) | robust |
   | Net3, W1 | V1 July to September predicting December, reading recall | 0.667 / 1.000 | 8 / 12 of 12 | 0.667 (0.400 to 0.889) | robust, wholly past the 0.90 bar |
   | Net3, W1 | V2 January to March predicting July to September, reading recall | 0.500 / 0.568 | 22 / 25 of 44 | 0.880 (0.739 to 0.969) | robust, in the 15 C control |
   | Net3, W1 | V3 July to September predicting December, reading recall | 0.833 / 1.000 | 10 / 12 of 12 | 0.833 (0.643 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net3, W2 | V1 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.647 / 0.750 | 44 / 51 of 68 | 0.863 (0.766 to 0.944) | robust |
   | Net3, W2 | V2 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.519 / 0.596 | 27 / 31 of 52 | 0.871 (0.724 to 0.974) | robust, in the 15 C control |
   | Net3, W2 | V3 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.697 / 0.776 | 53 / 59 of 76 | 0.898 (0.837 to 0.952) | robust |
   | Net3, W2 | V1 July to September predicting December, reading recall | 0.857 / 1.000 | 6 / 7 of 7 | 0.857 (0.667 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net3, W2 | V3 July to September predicting December, reading recall | 0.857 / 1.000 | 6 / 7 of 7 | 0.857 (0.667 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net3, W2 | V2 July to September predicting December, map RMSE | 0.110 / 0.094 mg/L | 1175 scored | 1.171 (0.951 to 1.451) | within noise, in the 15 C control |
   | Net2, W1 | V1 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.333 / 0.381 | 7 / 8 of 21 | 0.875 (0.734 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2, W1 | V3 rolling cooling, reading recall | 0.500 / 0.567 | 15 / 17 of 30 | 0.882 (0.733 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2, W1 | V1 July to September predicting December, map recall | 0.333 / 1.000 | 2 / 6 of 6 low junction-months | 0.333 (0.000 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2, W1 | V3 July to September predicting December, map recall | 0.429 / 1.000 | 3 / 7 of 7 | 0.429 (0.200 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2, W2 | none | | | | |

   Intervals of recall ratios drop the bootstrap resamples whose seeds hold no low reading (or junction-month) or give today's model a recall of 0: 212 of 2000 for each of Net2 W1's two December map-recall triggers, 13 for its V1 rolling cooling reading recall, 4 for each of Net3 W2's two December reading-recall triggers, and none for the others (`bootstrap.n_resamples_dropped`).

   So three kinds of trigger carry the stop on Net3. In the weak-wall world, the warming-month map: M2 still expects warm water to lose more chlorine at the walls than this truth does, less than M did (map RMSE 0.088 against M's 0.096 and M1b's 0.076) but enough to raise false alarms (272 against B0's 142, V1). In both worlds, cooling-month reading recall in the rolling test: M2 flags 10 to 16% fewer low readings than today's model (ratios 0.845 to 0.898), the same pattern task 10 found for M (0.853 there). And December reading recall after a July-to-September fit: in W1 M2 flags 8 of the 12 low readings that today's model flags all of (0.667, interval 0.400 to 0.889), the only trigger whose interval lies wholly past the 0.90 bar; with in-network warming (V3) 10 of 12, and in W2 6 of 7 (V1 and V3), all within count noise. Reading-recall losses also appear in the 15 C control V2 in both worlds (W1, the January-to-March fit asked for July to September, 22 against 25 of 44, robust; W2, rolling cooling months, 27 against 31 of 52, robust), where the temperature schedule cannot be the cause, so part of the recall loss comes from the bank itself, whose blocks at 15 C are not today's grid, rather than from the seasons. On Net2 all four triggers are within count noise. Under task 10's rolling-only rule the verdict is the same: it triggers on Net3 in W1 (4 pairs) and W2 (3) and on Net2 in W1 (2), not on Net2 in W2 (`A7.rolling_only_as_task10`).
2. Where M2 helps. On held-out readings it is measurably better than today's model everywhere: rolling V1 reading RMSE 0.109 against 0.119 on Net3 in W1 (ratio 0.916, interval 0.875 to 0.950), 0.114 against 0.147 in W2 (0.774, 0.721 to 0.820), 0.119 against 0.132 on Net2 in W1 (0.902, 0.846 to 0.951) and 0.127 against 0.158 in W2 (0.807, 0.754 to 0.862). In W2 it passes A4 on Net3 by a wide margin: fitted on January to March, its July-to-September map RMSE is 0.083 against 0.180, recall 0.922 against 0.570, coverage 0.979, at 80 false alarms against 4. Read across the two worlds (after the fact, not a bar), M2 is the model whose worst world is least bad: January to March predicting July to September, the worse of its two map RMSEs is 0.112 on Net3 and 0.097 on Net2, against 0.180 and 0.192 for today's model, 0.144 and 0.123 for M, and 0.138 and 0.126 for M1b; rolling over all months (V1), 0.091 and 0.085, against 0.112 and 0.110, 0.094 and 0.086, and 0.100 and 0.095 (ahead of M by 0.003 and 0.001 mg/L; on Net2 within noise, the paired M2/M map RMSE ratio in its worse world, W2, being 0.990, interval 0.960 to 1.017). In the other out-of-season test (July to September predicting December) M has the smaller worst case (0.125 on Net3 against M2's 0.146). Its reading bands are calibrated: seen-tap 90% coverage 0.877 and 0.871 on Net3 and 0.854 on Net2 in W1, inside the pilot band, and 0.839 on Net2 in W2, under it.
3. R1, the robustness bar, failed on every network and in every world; every failure (`acceptance.<world>.R1.failures`):
   - Net3, W1 (4): against M1b on map RMSE in the rolling warming months (V1 0.088 against 0.076, ratio 1.158; V3 0.087 against 0.074, 1.180) and January to March predicting July to September (V1 0.112 against 0.090, 1.240; V3 0.108 against 0.092, 1.170).
   - Net3, W2 (3): on December map RMSE after the July-to-September fit, against M (V1 0.146 against 0.125, 1.166; V3 0.156 against 0.133, 1.170) and, in the 15 C control, against M1b (V2 0.110 against 0.090, 1.225).
   - Net2, W1 (7): against M1b on map RMSE in the rolling warming months (V1 0.084 against 0.074, 1.137) and January to March predicting July to September (V1 0.097 against 0.075, 1.303; V3 0.087 against 0.075, 1.156); in the December test against M on map RMSE (V1 0.0995 against 0.0885, 1.124; V3 0.097 against 0.084, 1.152) and against M1b on map recall (V1 2 against 4 of 6 low junction-months, ratio 0.500; V3 3 against 5 of 7, 0.600).
   - Net2, W2 (5): against M on January-to-March-to-July-to-September map recall (V1 0.704 against 0.916, ratio 0.769; V3 0.672 against 0.916, 0.734) and map RMSE (V3 0.087 against 0.077, 1.135), and on December map RMSE (V1 0.157 against 0.137, 1.139; V3 0.167 against 0.149, 1.120).

   The two Net2 W1 December recall failures rest on 6 and 7 low junction-months. The Net2 W2 December failures are RMSE failures, scored on every unsampled junction-month; that test has 1 low junction-month, which every model flags. So M2 sits between M and M1b: outside the 15 C control it never loses as badly as the worse of them, but it does not match the better one. In the control it is marginally worse than both in two classes (Net3 W1: rolling warming map RMSE 0.0880 against M's 0.0880 and M1b's 0.0858; December test 0.0878 against 0.0872 and 0.0866). The Net2 numbers carry the grid-floor confound of finding 5.
4. R2, the wall posterior (reported, no bar; `r2_wall_posterior`). On the same seeds and windows, M2's posterior mean wall E/R is higher in W2 than in W1 in 94% of the V1 rolling fits on Net3 (by 1539 K on average; 95% and 2220 K in the windows spanning 4 C or more) and in 92% on Net2 (751 K; 100% and 1232 K in the spanning windows). In the 15 C control the two worlds differ by only 385 K on Net3 and -53 K on Net2. But the posterior stays near the middle: on Net3 its mean is 4525 K in W1 against the truth's 2881 K (the truth's wall response as an E/R, mean over seeds), and 6064 K in W2 against 7441 K; the per-fit correlation with the truth's is 0.335 and 0.350; within the pairs, the steepest wall (12000 K) keeps 0.109 of the mass in W1 and the flattest (0 K) 0.146 in W2. Read against the 15 C control, which has the same truths, seeds and grid but no temperature contrast, the move on Net3 is one-sided. In W1 the posterior goes from the control's 5818 K (closer to the truth than the prior in 13% of fits) to 4525 K (72%), toward the weak-wall truth. In W2 it does not move toward the steep truth: 6064 K over all rolling fits (closer than the prior in 59%) against the control's 6204 K (85%), and 6304 K against the control's 6199 K in the windows spanning 4 C or more (55% against 86%). So most of the 1539 K gap between the worlds is W1 moving down: on Net3 the seasons teach M2 that a weak wall is weak, not that a steep wall is steep. Near 20 C nothing is learnt: after the July-to-September fit (0.5 C of contrast) the posterior sits at about the prior (5404 K in W1, 5482 K in W2, each wall E/R holding 0.19 to 0.21 of the mass; correlation with the truth -0.011 and -0.146). On Net2 in W1 it does not move toward the truth (5682 K against 2695 K; closer than the prior in 32% of fits; the control 6106 K, 0%); in W2 its mean moves toward the truth from the control's (6433 K against 6053 K; truth 8673 K), though fewer fits are closer than the prior (65% against 75%); both Net2 worlds are shaped by the grid's floors (finding 5). Two more cautions. The January-to-March window has only 0.5 C of contrast, yet on Net3 its posterior already tracks the truth (correlation 0.749 in W1); that is mostly the top of the kw grid (finding 5), not temperature information. And where the data carry no temperature contrast, the posterior is shaped by the grid and the prior, as the controls show.
5. The kw axis, the reviewer's note (`kw_cap`). Read as 20 C rates, the grid's top wall rate (1.5 m/day) times f(T; wall E/R) is, at 10 C, 0.572 m/day for a wall E/R of 8000 K and 0.353 m/day for 12000 K. On Net3 (nominal wall rate 0.70 m/day) it is below some weak-wall (W1) truths' winter wall rates for 8000 K at 10 and 10.5 C and for 12000 K at 10 to 12.5 C, and below every W1 truth for 12000 K at 10 C, where W1's range is 0.356 to 0.70 m/day; in W2 it binds only for 12000 K at 10 and 10.5 C, and only for the truths with the weakest response. The cap binds only for wall hypotheses steeper than the truth's own response, so it never stops the right hypothesis from fitting, but it removes the wrong steep ones for a reason that is the grid's range, not the data: after the January-to-March fit on Net3, 0.960 of the 12000 K hypothesis's mass sits at the top of the kw grid in W1 (0.502 in W2), and 0.579 of the 8000 K hypothesis's (0.066 in W2).

   On Net2 (0.20 m/day) the top never binds, but the bottom does. The first version of this finding said nothing binds on Net2, because `kw_cap_table` then tested only the top of the grid; a reviewer found the floor, and it was checked after the review (`kw_cap.where_the_floor_binds`, `kw_cap.kb_floor`, and the refits in `outputs/chem/task10b_grid_edges_<net>.json`; none of it pre-registered). Read as 20 C rates, the grid's lowest wall rate, 0.10 m/day times f(T; wall E/R), lies above some W2 truths' winter wall rates (0.047 to 0.114 m/day at 10 C) for wall E/R 0 and 2500 K at 10 to 12.5 C, for 5000 K at 10 and 10.5 C and for 0 K at 15 C, and just below the slowest W1 truth (0.10 against 0.102 m/day at 10 C). Net2's nominal bulk rate, 0.10 per day, is the kb grid's lowest value, so the kb floor lies above some truths at every temperature for every bulk E/R. The posterior shows both. After the January-to-March fit, 0.54 of the pairs' mass in W1 (0.55 in W2) sits at kb20 = 0.10, and the flat wall hypotheses pile up at kw20 = 0.10, in W1 too: 0.432 of the 0 K hypothesis's mass and 0.188 of the 2500 K one's, against 0.003 of the 12000 K one's (W2: 0.892, 0.745 and 0.541 for 0, 2500 and 5000 K). A hypothesis held at the grid's edge is held back by the grid's range, not the data, so on Net2 the floors push the winter posterior toward steep walls, and in W1, where the flat walls are right, away from the truth: its January-to-March posterior mean is 6398 K against the truth's 2695 K, closer than the prior in 0 of 8 fits, with 0.266 of the pairs' mass on 12000 K against a prior of 0.20. This is a confound for R2 on Net2 (finding 4) and plausibly for Net2's out-of-season numbers, which rest on the winter fit (121 false alarms against today's 4 in W1); it was not tested further. Today's model sits at the kb floor too: its most likely kb is the grid's lowest in 89 to 99% of Net2's rolling windows, as task 10 found on Net2 (and in 31 to 45% of Net3's). On Net3 the kw floor holds almost no mass (at most 0.022 of a hypothesis's after the January-to-March fit), and the kb floor's 0.24 of the pairs' mass is spread over every wall hypothesis (0.16 to 0.26 of each one's), so it does not single out flat walls. The robust triggers are all on Net3, where the kw floor holds almost no mass and the kb floor does not tilt the wall posterior; whether the kb floor moves them otherwise was not tested.

   Neither axis was extended: that changes every block (a new grid), and the task reused task 10's blocks as asked. Recorded as a limitation: in a future bank the kw axis should be defined per hypothesis or at the window temperature, and both axes should reach below 0.10 for a network like Net2.
6. P(H0). After the January-to-March fit H0 keeps 0.117 (W1) and 0.105 (W2) on Net3 and 0.060 and 0.050 on Net2, against 0.145 and 0.133 (Net3) and 0.103 and 0.118 (Net2) in the 15 C control, from a prior of 0.25. After the July-to-September fit it keeps about its prior (0.252, 0.245; 0.224, 0.221), because near 20 C every block is close to today's grid.
7. The other bars. A2 (15 C control) fails on Net3 in both worlds on reading recall (0.658 against 0.692 in W1, a difference of 0.033; 0.516 against 0.570 in W2, 0.054; RMSE ratios 0.970 to 1.001) and passes on Net2. A3 passes everywhere with a measurable gain (finding 2). A4 (Net3 bar) fails in W1 on RMSE alone (0.112 against 0.113, ratio 0.987 where 0.85 was needed; recall 0.985 against 0.758, coverage 0.922 and December false alarms 69 against 160 pass) and passes in W2. A5 passes on Net3 (median SD of kb20 0.059 and 0.064 against 0.128 and 0.146) and fails on Net2, where today's most likely kb barely moves (median SD 0.025 and 0.000 against M2's 0.049 and 0.044) because Net2's true bulk rate sits near the grid's floor, as in task 10. A6 (Net3 bar) passes: a 2 C error costs at most 0.030 of recall in W1 and 0.072 in W2; on Net2, not a bar, it costs up to 0.202 in W2 (-2 C in the forecast month).
8. The floor. The oracle, the best single member and dose of M2's bank, has a V1 rolling map RMSE of 0.052 and 0.055 on Net3 and 0.040 on Net2, against 0.090 and 0.091 and 0.081 and 0.085 for M2: most of the remaining error is calibration from about 39 readings, not the bank.
9. Limitations, recorded. The truths' wall laws are both assumptions (W1's theta_w range has no published source; W2 copies the bulk law); real walls may respond differently, which is the point of learning it. Every truth and bank member share the committed 7-day run and its start-up transient (task 9). E/R is still not identified within one window. The schedules, the soil curve and the warming time constants are assumptions; seasonal demand is not in the truth; ky4 is not tested.
