**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The pre-registered model failed its own bars: A7 triggered on Net3 in both worlds and on Net2 in W1 (finding 1), R1 failed on both networks in both worlds (finding 3), A2 failed on Net3 in both worlds, A4 on Net3 in W1, A5 on Net2 in both worlds (finding 7). Seen-tap reading coverage on Net2 in W2 is 0.839, under the pilot band. On Net2 in W2, where A6 is not a bar, a logged temperature 2 C too low in the forecast month costs 0.202 of map recall.
- False alarms rose by more than 10% against today's model in every warming class with seasons in the truth and in every January-to-March-to-July-to-September test: Net3 W1 V1 warming map 272 against 142 and out of season 267 against 20; Net3 W2 229 against 129 and 80 against 4; Net2 W1 138 against 39 and 121 against 4; Net2 W2 86 against 17 and 62 against 2 (`A7.false_alarm_rises_over_10pct`). Three rises from none were missing from that list in the scored summary, because a ratio to zero was not computed; they are listed now (`from_zero`): Net3 W2 V3, January-to-March-to-July-to-September reading false alarms 11 against 0; the same test's map on Net2, 91 against 0 in W1 and 44 against 0 in W2.
- None in the default numbers. After the task's edits (the review's later edits touch no code that the reruns or the app import) the four committed reruns (`python -m residualmap.checks --reproduce`, label `post_task10b` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`) write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs, and the app's default demo still flags 42 of 92 junctions and reads 41 violations, 36 of 36 unsampled found, 1 false alarm, sample mean 0.53 mg/L. Task 10's own outputs are unchanged too: rerunning both task-10 experiments with the new code rewrote all 10 of them byte-identical. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun. Nothing in the app or the default model uses the temperature path.
- Against the plan and the process: a one-seed smoke run on an unscored seed (Net2 seed 100, both worlds, all variants, written to a scratch folder) checked the code end to end before the pre-registration. Its printout included that seed's stop-rule verdict (triggered in both worlds), which was seen; no model change followed. After it and before the pre-registration, two edits, neither to a model: the compact CSV now keeps only the five models of this task (`run_task` also scores persistence and the network mean out of season, which the smoke run's CSV included), and the figure layout (a two-line title, a note in an empty panel, room for a legend). The Net2 cached blocks the scored run read were built by the smoke run (model blocks, the same whoever builds them). After the scored run (00:08 to 00:40 PDT), one docstring-only edit at the top of `seasonal.py` (checked by diff against the copy taken before the run); every check and rerun above used the final code. A7 covers the rolling and the two out-of-season tests, as the plan's wording asks (task 10 pre-registered the rolling test only), and the rolling-only verdict is reported beside it; the wall grid adds 2500 K inside the span the addendum asked for; the oracle is the best member of M2's bank, larger than task 10's M bank; B1, M6, the sensitivity priors, persistence and the network mean are not run (the addendum lists B0, M, M1b, M2 and the oracle); the CSVs are summed per seed and season class at 6 significant digits, so per-month rows are kept only for the rolling fits (`season2_windows_<net>.csv`), and the summary is computed from the files as written. The kw axis was checked and not extended (finding 5). After the review, with no rerun: the false-alarm list gained the rises from zero, `kw_cap` gained the floors of both grids, both summaries were rewritten from the committed CSVs, and the grid-edge refits were added (Built, last item); no scored number, interval or bar changed, and the pre-registration block above is untouched. These edits touch only task 10b's functions in `seasonal.py`, which neither the four committed reruns nor the app imports, and the checks' docstring; the full checks were rerun after them (39 of 39 pass in 75.8 s, `checks_report.json` unchanged). Finding 5's first version said the kw grid never binds on Net2, which was wrong (its check looked only at the top of the grid), and finding 3 now lists every R1 failure.
- Costs: 448 MB of new git-ignored cache in `outputs/cache` (108 files, 54 per network, from their byte sizes). Net3's banks took 1235 s (54 cached and 105 in-memory conditions, 107,325 EPANET runs on 11 processes) and its 96 world, seed and variant tasks 12 to 19 s each on 6 workers, 1481 s in all; Net2 took 401 s (tasks 6 to 9 s; its cached blocks came from the smoke run, 540 s). From the arrays' sizes, not measured: a Net3 block is 5.96 MB, so the plus or minus 2 C blocks written to disk for the workers are about 0.63 GB (105 blocks) and each Net3 worker holds about 1.2 GB of bank (91 distinct cached arrays and the 105 plus or minus 2 C blocks). Free disk was logged at 2.82 GB just after Net3's banks were built, before those blocks were written, so it was about 2.2 GB during the Net3 tasks (an estimate, not a reading); the lowest reading in the logs is 2.7 GiB, after the run. The timings and readings are from the runs' logs, df and du, not stored in `outputs/`. The new tracked outputs are 0.40 MB and 0.25 MB of CSV on Net3, 0.19 MB and 0.13 MB on Net2, two summaries of 0.38 and 0.37 MB, four figures, and, after the review, two grid-edge files of 25 KB and 15 KB (their refits take about 85 s on Net3 and 28 s on Net2).

**What needs a decision before task 11** (nothing below was done): (a) keep today's model and record temperature as tested and not adopted, for both M and M2, with the plant log kept as an input for later; (b) the deciding unknown is how real pipe walls respond to temperature: with a year of real grab samples and plant temperatures, M2's wall posterior could be read directly, but in simulation on Net3, measured against the 15 C control, it moves toward a weak wall when the truth's wall is weak and not toward a steep wall when the truth's is steep (finding 4), and on a network whose rates sit near the grid's floor (Net2) the floors shape it (finding 5), so it would be read with those limits; (c) the cooling-month reading-recall loss appears in the 15 C control too, so a check of how the temperature blocks at a constant temperature move the reading flags against today's grid would separate a bank effect from a seasonal one; (d) any future bank should define the kw axis per hypothesis or at the window temperature, and reach below 0.10 on both the kb and kw axes for a network like Net2 (finding 5), which needs a new grid for every block.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal Net3 16 --wall --workers 6` and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal Net2 8 --wall --workers 6` (seeds 16 to 31 and 8 to 15 by default; outputs to `outputs/chem/`, banks to `outputs/cache/`; about 25 minutes for Net3 the first time, mostly bank building, and refused below 1.5 GB free disk). After the review: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal <net> 0 --wall --resummarise` rewrites `summary_season2_<net>.json` from the committed CSVs, and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.seasonal <net> 0 --wall --grid-edges` writes `outputs/chem/task10b_grid_edges_<net>.json` (both read the committed outputs; the seeds argument is ignored).

### Task 11, organics: TOC as a logged input, tested against chlorine-organics chemistry first order cannot represent

**Asked:** make "organics that impart chlorine demand" an explicit input whenever the utility logs TOC: the plant's monthly TOC scales the bulk decay rate, and the model stays first order on purpose, so the dose stays an exact ln-offset. The plan built this on top of an adopted temperature model; neither task 10's nor task 10b's was adopted, so the plan's addendum 2 recasts the test. The primary comparison is today's model (B0) against M_TOC, today's model plus a TOC axis on the bulk decay with today's grid nested at TOC_ref = 2.0 mg/L, on hidden truths at a fixed 20 C (so temperature cannot confound the TOC test), including the first-storm month, when the assumed plant TOC doubles from 1.5 to 3.0 mg/L in November. The honest test is a hidden truth built from EPANET's own second-order kinetics with a limiting concentration (Clark's chlorine-plus-reactive-organics form), on the cheap EPANET path through task 8's unit recipe, with no MSX: its decay slows as the water ages, the residual responds nonlinearly to the dose, and the bulk demand can run out. The plan's bars, recast against B0, a stop rule, a bootstrap claim rule, pre-registration and fresh seeds. Secondary, reported with no bar: task 10b's M2 plus TOC on the seasonal truth. If TOC passes its bars, it is wired into the pilot path and the app as an optional logged input (default off, default numbers unchanged). The TOC schedule, phi and k2 are assumptions, not measurements.

**Pre-registered before the first scored run.** Written on 7 October 2026 at 02:03 PDT, after the code was written and run on unscored seeds into a scratch folder (Net2 seed 200 with every truth under schedules S and C; Net3 seed 200 with truth O2 under schedules S and D; the secondary test on Net2 seed 200), and before M_TOC was run on any scored seed; the bars below are not changed afterwards. A copy of this block is kept outside the repository. The smoke runs' outputs were looked at to check the code, and two things in them were seen: the Net2 run's exit code said its one seed triggered the stop rule, and its figure showed M_TOC behind B0 in November on that seed, under the matched-structure truth too. That was checked for a bug before writing this block: on that seed the truth's own November chlorine rose (mean daily minimum +0.037 mg/L against October) although TOC alone lowers it by 0.042 mg/L (the same month's truth rerun at TOC 1.5), because the month's other draws (bulk factor, demand, dose) moved it more; M_TOC predicted a drop of 0.026 mg/L, in the right direction; and both models' maps were biased low in every class on that seed. No bug was found and no model change followed: M_TOC's hypotheses and prior were fixed before the first smoke run. Edits after the smoke runs, none to a model or a bar: the dose-step summary now compares the same months, one figure label, and the saved checks.

Definitions:
- **Truths**, all at a fixed 20 C (the truth's E/R and wall-temperature draws are made and multiply by exactly 1), with the committed per-pipe wall decay, demand, dose and monthly bulk-factor draws: **O1**, matched structure, a debug check: first order, kb_m = kb_net u_m TOC_m / 2.0 (u_m the committed U(0.8, 1.2) monthly draw). **O2**, Clark (Clark 1998, J Environ Eng 124(1):16): EPANET bulk and tank order 2 with a limiting potential, dC/dt = -k2 u_m C (C - CL_m), CL_m = d_m - phi TOC_m (d_m the mean of the month's drawn source doses), phi = 0.85 mg Cl2 per mg C, k2 = kb_net / (phi TOC_ref), 0.235 L/mg/day on Net3 and 0.059 on Net2, so at TOC 2.0 the initial apparent rate k2 (d - CL) equals today's truth's kb; the wall stays first order. **O3**, low demand (like membrane-filtered water): phi = 0.5, k2 = kb_net / 1.0, so CL = +0.2 mg/L at TOC 2.0 and dose 1.2 and the bulk demand runs out. phi, k2 and the schedules are assumptions.
- **Schedules**: **S**, the assumed plant TOC (2.5 mg/L January to March, 2.0 April, 1.5 May to October, 3.0 November, 2.5 December; inside Powell et al. 2000's 1 to 3 mg/L); **C**, a control at 2.0 every month; **D** (truth O2 on Net3 only, no bar), S with the plant dose stepped from 1.2 to 1.5 mg/L in July, logged.
- **M_TOC** (`residualmap/organics.py::TocSimGP24`): hypotheses H0 (today's 675-member grid, the same every month) and H_TOC (the same grid re-run with every bulk rate times TOC_m / 2.0, the AWWARF and Powell form at a fixed temperature, its constant absorbed into the calibrated kb, read as the rate at TOC_ref; the wall rate is not scaled), each grab sample explained at its own month's TOC; prior H0 1/4, H_TOC 3/4 (H0 keeps the share it had in tasks 10 and 10b); likelihood, discrepancy GP, daily-minimum Monte Carlo and the 3-month window are today's. In schedule D it is told the logged dose, as an exact ln-offset. At TOC 2.0 every month it is today's model by construction.
- **Other models**: **B0**, today's TOC-blind model on the previous 3 months; sensitivity priors on H0 of 1/2 and 0 (schedule S; reported, never used to choose); M_TOC not told the dose (schedule D); persistence and the network mean (readings, schedule S); the **oracle**, the best single member and dose of M_TOC's bank at the target month's TOC against the full daytime truth (the floor for any model on this first-order grid).
- **Seeds**: Net3 32 to 47 (16), Net2 16 to 23 (8), fresh (tasks 10 and 10b scored Net3 0 to 31 and Net2 0 to 15; the code refuses those). Net3 runs O1, O2 and O3 under S and C, and O2 under D; Net2 runs O1, O2 and O3 under S and C.
- **Tests**: 12-month logs as in task 10 (10 route taps and 3 rotating taps a month, hours 07:00 to 17:00, N(0, 0.03) mg/L noise, month seed 1000 + 100 seed + month). Rolling: fit the previous 3 months, predict April to December, in four classes by the TOC's course: falling (April to July), steady (August to October), first storm (November, fitted on August to October) and after the storm (December). Extrapolation without refit: fit January to March (TOC 2.5), predict July, August and September (TOC 1.5), pooled.
- **Scores**, as task 10: held-out readings at their own junction and hour (RMSE, MAE, coverage 50/80/90/95, recall below 0.2 mg/L with the predicted median below 0.2, false alarms) and the daily-minimum map at every junction not sampled in the 3-month window (the same set for every model; RMSE, MAE, coverage90, recall with P(daily min < 0.2) > 0.5, false alarms, precision), plus the map's RMSE and bias by nominal-age tercile. Pooled means sums over seeds and target months; paired bootstrap over seeds (2000 resamples, 90% intervals). Every number is read from `outputs/chem/summary_organics_<net>.json`, key `acceptance`, computed from `organics_<net>.csv` and `organics_windows_<net>.csv`.

Bars:
- C1. `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`: every check passes, the new task-11 checks included; the four committed reruns write all 50 files byte-identical, and tasks 10's and 10b's outputs are unchanged.
- T1 (the plan's O1, a debug check; the bar is set for Net3, Net2 is reported). Truth O1, schedule S, rolling test, all target months: pooled daily-minimum map RMSE_M_TOC < RMSE_B0. Otherwise it is treated as a bug and debugged before reporting.
- T2 (the plan's O2; the bar is set for Net3, Net2 is reported). Truth O2, schedule S, the first-storm month: daily-minimum map RMSE_M_TOC <= 0.85 x RMSE_B0 and map recall_M_TOC >= recall_B0. A failure is recorded.
- N (nesting, every network). Schedule C, rolling test, every truth: |RMSE_M_TOC / RMSE_B0 - 1| <= 0.02 and |recall_M_TOC - recall_B0| <= 0.03, for held-out readings and for the daily-minimum map. M_TOC is today's model there by construction (only its daily-minimum Monte-Carlo draws differ), so a failure is a bug.
- T3 (the plan's stop rule, recast against B0; every network). Truths O2 and O3, schedule S, each of the five classes (rolling falling, steady, first storm and after the storm; January to March predicting July to September): stop if RMSE_M_TOC > 1.10 x RMSE_B0 or recall_M_TOC < 0.90 x recall_B0, for held-out readings or for the daily-minimum map. For every trigger the counts and a paired bootstrap 90% interval over seeds are reported; a trigger whose interval lies wholly on M_TOC's worse side of 1.0 is called robust, one whose interval reaches 1.0 within count noise; both count toward the stop. False alarms up by more than 10% are listed under regressions. The control C is covered by N instead, because there M_TOC is today's model and any difference is Monte-Carlo noise.
- D (every network). M_TOC's posterior mass on the dose axis's edges (multipliers 0.90 and 1.10) is below 0.5 in at least 90% of its rolling fits (every truth, schedules S and C).
- G. A gain is claimed only where the upper end of the paired bootstrap 90% interval of RMSE_M_TOC / RMSE_B0 is below 1.0.
- L. The ladder B0 to M_TOC (with the oracle, and the sensitivity priors under S) is reported for every truth, schedule and test, whatever the direction; the dose step (D) and the secondary test are reported with no bar.
- Adoption. M_TOC becomes an optional logged input (pilot `--plant` with `toc_mgL`, and the app, default off) only if T1 and T2 pass on Net3, and T3 does not stop and N and D pass on every network. Otherwise it is recorded as "tested, not adopted".
- Secondary (addendum 2; reported, no bar): Net3, seeds 32 to 47, truth O2 on task 10's seasonal truth (the V1 plant temperatures, the truth's own bulk E/R, task 10's weak wall, W1) with schedule S; models B0, task 10b's M2 (temperature, TOC-blind), M_TOC (TOC, temperature-blind), M2_TOC (M2's 15 bulk and wall pairs with every bulk rate also times TOC_m / 2.0) and the oracle of M2_TOC's bank; the same rolling classes and extrapolation.

**Note added after the review, outside the block above.** The smoke-run figures quoted in the block (Net2 seed 200: the truth's November +0.037 mg/L against October, -0.042 from TOC alone, M_TOC's predicted -0.026) were read from the smoke runs' outputs, which went to a scratch folder and were not kept, so they cannot be traced to `outputs/`; the same holds for the bank-build times in Costs. No scored number depends on them.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** The stop rule T3 triggered, so M_TOC is tested and not adopted, and the model was not changed after the scored run (02:03 to 02:06 PDT on Net3, 02:06 to 02:07 on Net2). On Net3 one pair triggered, and it is robust: under the second-order (Clark) truth, fitted on January to March and asked for July to September, M_TOC flags 44 of the 61 low held-out readings where today's model flags 49 (recall ratio 0.898, interval 0.840 to 0.952). On Net2 three reading-recall pairs triggered, each one flag apart on 14 to 22 low readings, all within count noise. The first storm, the case the model was built for, missed its bar T2: under the Clark truth M_TOC's November map RMSE is 0.938 times today's (interval 0.874 to 1.016; the bar was 0.85), although it finds more of the low junctions (0.917 against 0.861 of 483 junction-months) with more false alarms (38 against 27). The dose-axis bar D failed on Net3 (0.769 of fits below half their mass at the dose grid's edges, against 0.90), but today's model has the same share (0.773), so that is the dose axis, not TOC. Where M_TOC does help: held-out readings measurably better than today's under all three truths on Net3 (RMSE ratio 0.975 to 0.989), and the daily-minimum map measurably better after the TOC falls (0.949 to 0.959 from April to July; 0.898 to 0.910 for July to September from January-to-March samples); T1, the matched-structure debug bar, passes (map RMSE 0.099 against 0.101, ratio 0.976, interval 0.966 to 0.987), and in the 2.0 mg/L control M_TOC is today's model (N passes). Not all of these gains are the TOC's: a grid-only control run after the review (M_TOC told TOC 1.5 every month, so it knows nothing about TOC) shows that the reading gains under O1 and O2, the falling-month and out-of-season map gains and the first-storm reading gains need the logged TOC, while part of the others, most of O3's rolling map gain among them, comes from the TOC block's bulk rates reaching below today's grid floor of 0.10 per day (finding 2). In December, just after the storm, its map is worse (1.043 times today's under the Clark truth on Net3, interval 1.001 to 1.082); split after the review into bias and spread, its spread equals today's and the whole difference is the bias term (finding 5). Both models' maps are biased low in these simulations by about 0.05 to 0.08 mg/L on Net3 (about 0.03 on Net2; the best single grid member is unbiased); the July-to-September trigger is where M_TOC, told the summer water carries less organic matter, raises its summer predictions toward the truth and so flags fewer of the low readings today's pessimism happens to catch (finding 4, a reading after the fact). That trigger rests on 5 low readings on 5 seeds, all one way, and a correct TOC law can fire it: under the matched-structure truth the same test loses reading recall too (finding 4). Today's default model, the pilot path, the app and every committed number are unchanged; TOC stays a logged column that nothing uses.

**Built:**
- `chemistry.Chemistry(phi=...)`: the Clark truth's demand per mg of TOC (mg Cl2 per mg C). Kinetics 'clark' needs `toc_mgL` and `phi`, and `phi` goes with 'clark' only; an unset `phi` is left out of `cache_tag`, so every earlier tag and cache name is unchanged (the committed T12.5/E8000 tag is a saved check).
- `simulate._chem_truth`, kinetics 'clark': EPANET bulk and tank order 2 with a limiting potential, dC/dt = -k2 C (C - CL), CL = d - phi TOC (d the mean of the month's drawn source doses; one CL per run, so one plant dose is assumed) and k2 = kb_net u f(T; E_true) / (phi TOC_ref), both rounded to the 4 decimals WNTR's .inp writer keeps so the record is what EPANET ran; the wall stays first order; corrected units (task 8's recipe); no new draw. In-network warming with Clark kinetics is refused. The plan gave k2 without the committed monthly bulk factor u; it is included here, so the Clark truth keeps the committed month-to-month variation and differs from O1 only in its kinetics. The plan gave O3's k2 as 0.40 L/mg/day, which is kb_net / (phi TOC_ref) on Net3; the same rule gives 0.10 on Net2.
- `pilot.synthetic_log`: a schedule month may carry `toc_mgL`, `kinetics`, `phi` and `dose_mgL`; readings and the plant log then carry the month's TOC and dose. Task 10's schedules give the same log, draw for draw (a saved check).
- `seasonal.SeasonalSimGP24`: the column that names a sample's bank condition and its key are now class attributes (`condition_column`, `_condition_key`), 3 lines, so a subclass can read TOC or a condition label; for temperature both are as before, and the saved checks recompute task 10's and task 10b's committed rows identically.
- `residualmap/organics.py`, new, instead of a section in `seasonal.py` as the plan said (that file is 2,478 lines and holds tasks 10 and 10b, which are left as they were): `TocBank` and `toc_bank` (the committed grid re-run with every bulk rate times TOC / 2.0 at 1.5, 2.5 and 3.0 mg/L, cached in `outputs/cache` under tagged names, 3 blocks per network); `TocSimGP24`, model M_TOC (a `SeasonalSimGP24` over the TOC bank; prior H0 1/4, H_TOC 3/4, or any prior; `fit(samples, target_toc_mgL, target_dose_ratio)`, `set_target(toc)`); the truths and schedules (`toc_schedule`); `run_task` (every model and test for one network, seed, truth and schedule); the experiment `python -m residualmap.organics <net> <n>` (fresh seeds enforced; 6 workers); compact outputs (`organics_<net>.csv`, the sums per truth, schedule, test, seed, class and model at 6 significant digits; `organics_windows_<net>.csv`, one row per rolling fit), the summary with the pre-registered acceptance key computed from the two files as written, and two figures; `--resummarise` (the summary from the committed CSVs) and `--replot`. The secondary test: `KeyedWallBank` and `M2TocSimGP24` (task 10b's M2 over blocks keyed by a (temperature, TOC) label) and `m2toc_bank` (at TOC 2.0 a block is M2's own cached block, at 20 C the TOC bank's, and the other 105 Net3 blocks are built in memory and never cached), run by `--seasonal`.
- After the scored run, with no rerun and no number changed: the class figure's title names each stop-rule trigger (redrawn from the committed summary with `--replot`).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: five new checks in an `organics` group (below).
- After the reviews, with no scored rerun and no scored number changed, in `organics.py` only: the stop-rule entries' count keys, `flagged_<model>`, held the true positives, so they are now `low_flagged_<model>`, with `n_flagged_<model>` (true positives plus false alarms) beside them, and both summaries were rewritten with `--resummarise` (checked key by key against the earlier files: only these keys changed); the first-storm figures' subtitle now gives the ratio, its interval, the bar and the false alarms (`--replot-first-storm`, which refits the first seed's November; the panels are unchanged, Net3 seed 32 flags 30 and 35 of 46 with no false alarm); and `--review` writes `outputs/chem/task11_review_<net>.json`: a grid-only control (M_TOC fitted and read with every month labelled 1.5 mg/L), the January-to-March fit read in July to September at the fitting months' TOC as well as at the logged one, with the readings' signed error, and splits computed from the committed CSVs alone (the map's bias and spread, the stop trigger's seeds, the out-of-season reading recall under every truth and prior, today's bulk rate against its grid floor). It recomputes B0, M_TOC and M_TOC_linear on every scored seed and log, and their sums equal the committed CSV in 5808 of 5808 values on Net3 and 2904 of 2904 on Net2 at 6 significant digits (key `recomputed_against_committed_csv`).
- Not built, because of the stop: TOC in the pilot path and the app. `pilot.py`'s docstring now says the column is carried and not used.

**Found** (all simulated; every number is from `outputs/chem/summary_organics_<net>.json`, computed from `organics_<net>.csv` and `organics_windows_<net>.csv`, except where a finding names `organics_windows_<net>.csv` directly, the secondary's files or `task11_review_<net>.json` (written after the review); Net3 with seeds 32 to 47, Net2 with seeds 16 to 23; pooled over seeds and target months):
1. The stop rule triggered on both networks. Every trigger, with its counts and the paired bootstrap 90% interval over seeds (`acceptance.T3.triggered`):

   | network | trigger | M_TOC / B0 | counts, M_TOC / B0 | ratio (90% interval) | weight |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Net3 | Clark (O2), January to March predicting July to September, reading recall | 0.721 / 0.803 | 44 / 49 of 61 low readings | 0.898 (0.840 to 0.952) | robust, not wholly past the 0.90 bar |
   | Net2 | O2, rolling, falling months, reading recall | 0.227 / 0.273 | 5 / 6 of 22 | 0.833 (0.600 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2 | O2, January to March predicting July to September, reading recall | 0.071 / 0.143 | 1 / 2 of 14 | 0.500 (0.000 to 1.000) | within count noise |
   | Net2 | O3 (low demand), rolling, falling months, reading recall | 0.125 / 0.188 | 2 / 3 of 16 | 0.667 (0.000 to 1.000) | within count noise |

   Recall intervals drop the bootstrap resamples whose seeds hold no low reading or give today's model a recall of 0: 9, 194 and 56 of 2000 for the three Net2 triggers, none for Net3's (`bootstrap.n_resamples_dropped`). No map trigger fired, and no RMSE trigger: the worst RMSE ratio in any stop-rule class is 1.053 on Net3 (O3, December map) and 1.082 on Net2 (O2, December map).
2. Where M_TOC helps (bar G, claims only where the interval's upper end is below 1.0). Held-out readings, rolling, all months, on Net3: 0.989 (0.981 to 0.997) under O1, 0.988 (0.982 to 0.997) under O2, 0.975 (0.968 to 0.982) under O3; on Net2 only under O2 (0.979, 0.960 to 0.995). Readings at the first storm: 0.903, 0.936 and 0.919 on Net3 (each interval below 1.0), and 21 of 31 low readings flagged under O2 against today's 18 (O3: 20 against 13 of 28). The daily-minimum map after the TOC falls: April to July 0.949 (O1), 0.959 (O2) and 0.951 (O3) on Net3, 0.904, 0.919 and 0.937 on Net2; January to March predicting July to September 0.909, 0.898 and 0.910 on Net3, 0.905, 0.863 and 0.946 on Net2, every interval below 1.0. Rolling over all months, Net3's map is measurably better under every truth (0.969 to 0.976). So the logged TOC moves the prediction in the right direction when the TOC falls, under the second-order truth as much as under the matched one.

   Not all of it needs the TOC (checked after the review, `outputs/chem/task11_review_<net>.json`, key `grid_only_control`, with the summary's bootstrap). The control is M_TOC fitted and read with every month labelled 1.5 mg/L: it knows nothing about TOC and differs from today's model only by H_TOC's 1.5 mg/L block, whose bulk rates reach 0.075 per day, below today's grid floor of 0.10. On the same seeds and logs it gives, against today's model: under O1 readings 0.998 and map 0.996 (no measurable change), so O1's gains are the TOC's; under O2 readings 0.999 (the reading gain is the TOC's) but map 0.987 (0.977 to 0.999), about half of M_TOC's 0.972; under O3 readings 0.991 (0.985 to 0.996) and map 0.976 (0.966 to 0.984), about a third of M_TOC's reading gain (0.975) and most of its map gain (0.969); on Net2 under O2 readings 0.986 (0.976 to 0.995), about two thirds of M_TOC's 0.979. In the steady months every window and target is at 1.5 mg/L, so M_TOC is the control there, and its steady-month gains under bar G (Net3 O2 map 0.982, O3 readings 0.983 and map 0.966; Net2 O2 map 0.992) carry no TOC contrast. What needs the logged TOC: the falling-month map gains under O1 and O2 (control 0.998 and 0.991 on Net3, 0.989 and 0.991 on Net2; under O3 the control's 0.973 is about half of M_TOC's 0.951), the first-storm reading gains (control 0.993 to 1.011), and the out-of-season map gains: the same January-to-March fit read in July to September at the fitting months' 2.5 mg/L instead of the logged 1.5 gives today's numbers or worse (map 1.012, 1.007 and 1.017 times today's under O1 to O3 on Net3, 1.010, 0.974 and 1.056 on Net2; key `jan_mar_to_jul_sep_read_at_fitting_toc`). At the first storm under O2 the control's map is 0.965 (0.926 to 1.002), so the TOC information itself is worth about 0.938 / 0.965 = 0.972 there. The control helps where today's grid floor binds: today's most probable bulk rate is the floor, 0.10 per day, in 60% (O2) and 72% (O3) of Net3's schedule-S windows (34% under O1) and in 93% to 100% on Net2, whose truth's bulk rate is 0.10 (key `csv_only.kb_grid_floor`, from `organics_windows_<net>.csv`), as task 10b found for Net2 (finding 5 there). That is a finding about today's grid, not about TOC.
3. The first storm (bar T2, set for Net3) missed on RMSE. November map, fitted on August to October (TOC 1.5) and told November's TOC is 3.0: under O2, RMSE 0.068 against 0.073 mg/L (0.938, interval 0.874 to 1.016), recall 0.917 against 0.861 (443 against 416 of 483 low junction-months), false alarms 38 against 27; under O1 0.985 (0.902 to 1.062), under O3 1.018 (0.957 to 1.087). On Net2, not a bar: 0.993 under O2, recall 0.963 against 0.815 (78 against 66 of 81), false alarms 16 against 3. The truth's share of junctions below 0.2 mg/L at the daily minimum rises from 0.296 in October to 0.409 in November under O2 on Net3 (from 0.362 to 0.433 under O1), and M_TOC moves its map with it: its November map bias is -0.004 mg/L against today's +0.015, so today's model, which predicts no change, is slightly too high at the first storm and M_TOC is about centred, and M_TOC's extra false alarms (38 against 27) come with that downward move (the spread of its November map errors is 0.956 times today's, `task11_review_Net3.json`, key `csv_only.map_bias_and_spread`); most of what remains is scatter that a better TOC law alone would not remove (the best single member of the bank still has a November map RMSE of 0.048). By nominal-age tercile (youngest, middle, oldest) under O2 in November, M_TOC's RMSE is 0.077, 0.067, 0.059 against today's 0.086, 0.067, 0.062.
4. The July-to-September trigger, read after the fact (not pre-registered). Both models' daily-minimum maps are biased low in these simulations, by about 0.05 mg/L on Net3 over the rolling months under every truth (about 0.03 on Net2) and by 0.084 (today's) and 0.070 (M_TOC) mg/L in the July-to-September test under O2, while the best single grid member is unbiased (-0.004): the calibrated model is pessimistic about unsampled junctions' daily minimum. Told the summer water carries less organic matter (1.5 against the fitting months' 2.5 mg/L), M_TOC raises its summer predictions toward the truth, which cuts its map RMSE (0.099 against 0.111) and its reading false alarms (19 against 23), and flags 5 fewer of the 61 low readings (44 against 49). Checked after the review (`outputs/chem/task11_review_Net3.json`): the readings are biased the same way as the map, with a mean signed error in July to September of -0.017 mg/L for today's model and -0.004 for M_TOC; the same fit read at the fitting months' TOC (2.5) flags exactly today's 49 with today's 23 false alarms; the 5 come one each from 5 of the 16 seeds and no seed goes the other way, which is why the interval lies below 1.0 (key `csv_only.jan_mar_to_jul_sep_reading_recall`); and under the matched-structure truth O1, where M_TOC's TOC law is the truth's, the same test loses reading recall too (56 against 59 of 79, 0.949, interval 0.896 to 1.000; with the prior's whole mass on the TOC law 52 against 59, 0.881, interval 0.793 to 0.965, which would count as a robust trigger). So the trigger says more about today's low bias, which a correct TOC law partly removes, than about M_TOC finding low readings worse; it stands as recorded. The bias is a property of today's model, not of TOC: task 9's error-by-age file shows the same sign after 8 random samples on a single week (every Net3 decile negative, -0.016 to -0.082 mg/L, about -0.045 weighted by count; Net2 -0.054 to +0.001, `outputs/chem/error_by_age_<net>.csv`), and this task adds the signed error to the 12-month outputs.
5. December, just after the storm, M_TOC's map is worse: 1.066 (O1), 1.043 (O2, interval 1.001 to 1.082) and 1.053 (O3) times today's map RMSE on Net3, and 1.096 (1.015 to 1.177), 1.082 (1.032 to 1.159) and 1.045 on Net2, with more false alarms (Net3 O2 81 against 72, O3 124 against 99), inside the stop rule's 1.10 but recorded. The fitting window, September to November, holds one first-storm month at 3.0 and two at 1.5, and December is at 2.5. Split after the review into bias and spread (spread is the square root of RMSE squared minus bias squared; `outputs/chem/task11_review_<net>.json`, key `csv_only.map_bias_and_spread`, from `organics_<net>.csv`), the whole difference is the bias term: M_TOC's December spread is 1.015, 1.003 and 1.009 times today's on Net3 and 0.998, 0.991 and 0.933 on Net2. In the 2.0 mg/L control both models are biased low in December by the same amount (Net3 -0.048, -0.055 and -0.074 mg/L under O1 to O3, Net2 -0.041, -0.039 and -0.041; the oracle's -0.001 to -0.015 on Net3). Under the TOC schedule M_TOC carries about that bias (Net3 -0.048, -0.052 and -0.065), while today's model, which does not know that December's TOC is above its window's, predicts more chlorine and so partly cancels it (-0.039, -0.046 and -0.058); the same holds under the matched-structure truth O1, whose TOC law is M_TOC's. So the December loss is the shared low bias showing once the TOC error is gone, not the TOC law misfiring; where the bias comes from was not investigated.
6. The dose axis (bar D) failed on Net3: 0.769 of M_TOC's 864 rolling fits have less than half the posterior at the dose grid's edges (multipliers 0.90 and 1.10), against a 0.90 bar; on Net2 0.991, a pass. Today's model, fitted on the same windows, has 0.773 on Net3 and 0.986 on Net2 (`organics_windows_<net>.csv`, column `B0_dose_edge`; computed after the run, not a bar). So on Net3 the samples often want a dose outside plus or minus 10% whether or not TOC is in the model; the axis spans exactly the truth's own dose draw (plus or minus 10% per source), and the other monthly draws (bulk factor plus or minus 20%, demand) push on it too. Not changed.
7. The control (bar N) passes: with TOC 2.0 every month M_TOC's posterior equals today's in every window (posterior-mean kb identical in all 432 Net3 and 216 Net2 control fits, `organics_windows_<net>.csv`, columns `B0_post_kb` and `M_TOC_post_kb`), the reading RMSE ratio is 1.000 on Net3 and the map's within 0.001; on Net2 the largest difference is a reading RMSE ratio of 0.995 under O2, from one seed's falling months (seed 21, `organics_Net2.csv`), where the discrepancy GP's hyperparameter search settled on a different optimum from last-bit differences in its residuals (the posterior is identical).
8. The samples barely weigh the TOC hypothesis. P(H0) after the rolling fits stays near its prior of 0.25: 0.245 to 0.250 in the falling months, whose windows hold TOC contrast, and 0.19 to 0.24 elsewhere on Net3 (0.15 to 0.25 on Net2), where the windows hold one TOC (1.5 mg/L from May to October) and the difference is the two blocks' different kb grids (today's floor is 0.10 per day, H_TOC's at 1.5 mg/L is 0.075), not evidence about TOC. With 39 readings a window the TOC contrast inside a window does not separate the two hypotheses, so M_TOC predicts with about the prior's three quarters on the TOC law. The sensitivity priors (reported, never used to choose) move it as expected: with all the mass on H_TOC the July-to-September map is 0.095 under O2 (0.099 with the pre-registered prior, 0.103 with 1/2 on H0), and the first-storm map 0.0678 (0.0680, 0.0688).
9. The oldest water is not where first order misfits the second-order truth. Within a month the first-order grid fits the Clark truth about as well as the first-order truth: the oracle's rolling map RMSE is 0.048 mg/L under both O1 and O2 on Net3 (0.052 under O3) and 0.039, 0.041 and 0.044 on Net2, the same finding the plan's scratch evidence gave (Powell 2000 and Monteiro 2014). By nominal-age tercile under O2 the oracle's map RMSE is 0.058, 0.042 and 0.042 (youngest, middle, oldest) and both models' errors are largest in the youngest third (today's 0.111, 0.084, 0.092; M_TOC's 0.109, 0.082, 0.087), with a negative bias in every third. Under Clark the TOC effect on aged water is steeper than linear (at 1.5 mg/L and dose 1.2 the limiting concentration is about -0.08 mg/L, at 3.0 it is -1.37 mg/L, from `truth_k2_and_limiting`), but across the months it did not show up as an oldest-third misfit.
10. The dose step (schedule D, truth O2 on Net3, no bar). From July the plant dose is 1.5 mg/L, logged. In the truth the mean daily minimum per unit dose is 1.024 times higher at 1.5 than at 1.2 mg/L over the same 96 seed-months (0.302 against 0.295; first order would give exactly 1), so the exact ln-offset under-predicts by about 2% at the higher dose. Told the dose, M_TOC's rolling map RMSE is 0.111 mg/L against today's 0.136 and 0.133 for M_TOC not told it; January to March predicting July to September, 0.136 against 0.219 and 0.206. In this test the logged dose matters far more than the logged TOC; today's default path does not take a plant log at all, and the plant log's dose column is used only by task 10's temperature model, which was not adopted.
11. The secondary test (addendum 2; no bar; `outputs/chem/summary_organics_seasonal_Net3.json`, from `organics_seasonal_Net3.csv`; Net3, seeds 32 to 47, truth O2 on task 10's seasonal truth with the weak wall, W1). The ladder B0 to M2 to M2_TOC on held-out readings, rolling: 0.103, 0.097 and 0.096 mg/L (M2_TOC against today's 0.932, interval 0.895 to 0.974); on the rolling map 0.098, 0.100 and 0.098 (0.995, 0.968 to 1.024). Adding TOC to M2 lowers its map RMSE in the falling, steady and July-to-September classes (0.111 to 0.106, 0.092 to 0.090, 0.127 to 0.115) and leaves it about the same in November and December (0.072 to 0.073, 0.104 to 0.107); against today's model, though, M2_TOC's falling-month map is still worse (0.106 against 0.0955 mg/L, 1.110 times, interval 1.068 to 1.155), and its first-storm readings are worse (1.133, interval 1.006 to 1.257). On this truth the first storm is also a cooler month (15 C after 20, 19.5 and 17.5 C in the fitting months), and the TOC-only model M_TOC, which ignores temperature, is worse than today's there (November map 0.092 against 0.0805 mg/L, ratio 1.143, interval 1.096 to 1.186): colder water slows the decay that the extra organics speed up, and M_TOC sees only the organics. On this truth M_TOC's held-out readings are also measurably worse than today's, the reverse of its gain at a fixed 20 C: rolling 1.015 (interval 1.007 to 1.024), falling months 1.016 (1.007 to 1.028), December 1.025 (1.016 to 1.036, with its December map 1.059, 1.025 to 1.107), and January to March predicting July to September 1.060 (1.039 to 1.078), flagging 15 of the 58 low readings against today's 20; only its falling- and steady-month maps are better (0.953 and 0.983, intervals below 1.0). M2_TOC's November map is 0.073 (0.905 of today's, interval 0.813 to 1.023), M2's 0.072. Out of season, January to March predicting July to September, M2_TOC carries M2's weak-wall problem from task 10b: map RMSE 0.115 against today's 0.089 (1.292, interval 1.105 to 1.505), 492 false alarms against 184, while its readings are better (0.112 against 0.152). So on a seasonal truth a TOC input without temperature can hurt at the first storm, because the storms come with cooler water, and on the held-out readings, and the temperature model it would need is not adopted.
12. Limitations, recorded. phi, k2, the TOC schedule and its first-storm jump are assumptions; TOC-proportional laws are water-specific; one CL per run assumes one plant dose (Net3 has two sources with their own dose draws); the walls consume chlorine but not organics, which breaks Clark's invariant (acceptable for a truth generator); single-reactant Clark is not invariant across doses (Fisher, Kastl & Sathasivan 2011), so task 13's two-reactant truth is the stronger test; every truth and bank member share the committed 7-day run and its start-up transient (task 9); ky4 is not tested.
