  - Free disk stayed at 3.3 GiB or more throughout (df readings).

**What needs a decision** (nothing below was done):
- (a) Both disinfectants' bands are too narrow under the straddle rule. A mixed route, as already proposed for free chlorine (README), would apply here too.
- (b) The 300 s quality step moves fronts by up to about 0.18 mg/L against a 60 s step in EPANET itself. A finer step would change committed numbers, so that is Devansh's call.
- (c) The nitrification stress acts mostly through tanks that start empty. A rerun with tanks started at the source's quality, or a longer warm-up, would show the watch against nitrification proper.
- (d) For an uploaded file, the app's chloramine demo truth uses a wall rate of 0.20 m/day, which no rule sets.
- (e) Task 13 can reuse `residualmap/msx.py` as fixed after the review (before the fix, its uncompiled fallback failed every time).
- (f) Whether the single-species MSX check's move to a 60 s step stands (Regressions), or the plan's order-2 fallback truth should be built and run as a second check of the result.
- (g) Model (c)'s prior was centred on exact plant values; a rerun with a logging-error draw (for example pH plus or minus 0.1 from the truth's own generator) before k_hat is computed would test how much that flatters it.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.experiment Net3 --disinfectant=chloramine --workers 6` and `... Net2 --disinfectant=chloramine --workers 6` (fresh seeds 300 to 307, plus 308 to 311 at low pH on Net3; outputs to `outputs/chloramine/`; the chloramine grid and the free grid at 2.0 mg/L go to `outputs/cache/`; about 2 minutes and 30 s here once the grids exist). `--calibrate` reruns the two settings rules and rewrites `calibration_chloramine.json` and `kb_prior_table.csv`. `--resummarise` rewrites the summary from the committed CSVs. `--replot` redraws the figures, recomputing the first seed's rows and comparing them with the CSV. The pilot on a total chlorine log: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.pilot --inp <file.inp> --samples docs/example_grab_log_total.csv --taps docs/example_tap_map.csv --disinfectant chloramine --dose 2.0 --ph 8.0 --cl2n 4.5` (model (c); without `--ph` and `--cl2n`, model (b)). EPANET-MSX needs Homebrew's libomp and, for compiled reactions, the Xcode command-line tools; without a compiler it runs uncompiled, much slower, and says so.

### Task 13, what first-order chemistry costs: today's model against two-reactant chlorine-organics chemistry

**Asked:** the strongest honesty test of the iteration. Audit today's free-chlorine model (B0: SimGP24 on the committed 675-member first-order grid, every setting as committed) against chemistry its structure cannot represent: Fisher's two-reactant chlorine-organics model (2RA, a fast and a slow pool of organic reactants; Fisher, Kastl & Sathasivan 2012, doi:10.1016/j.watres.2012.03.017), run in EPANET-MSX on the committed draws. In the 2RA water the apparent first-order decay rate falls as the water ages, and the residual responds nonlinearly to the dose. The plan's tests at fixed conditions: the pre-registered materiality test (is B0 more than 10% worse on the 2RA truth than on the first-order truth, at 8 and 15 samples, under the random and straddle-on-the-daily-minimum rules), the dose-step test and the bias by water-age decile. A two-rate model assembled from today's grid ('full2r') is built only if the cost is material. Addendum 3 adds a pre-registered opt-in variant: B0 with its bulk-rate grid extended below today's floor of 0.10 per day, under its own cache name, on both truths, reported plainly; adopting it into the default path would move committed numbers, which is Devansh's decision. Addendum 2 drops the plan's seasonal part (the 2ra_warm truth with in-pipe warming, its temperature and TOC claims, and the cost decomposition), because no temperature model was adopted, and task 11's TOC model was not adopted either, so the audit runs against B0 only. ky4 (optional in the plan) is not run.

**Pre-registered before the first scored run.** Written on 7 October 2026 at 06:07 PDT, after the code was written and run on an unscored seed, and before any model was run on a scored seed; the bars below are not changed afterwards. A copy of this block and of the code is kept outside the repository. What was run before it: the five experiment runs (below) on Net3 and Net2 seed 990 into a scratch folder, the audit, the summary and the gated two-rate model on that seed, and the new saved checks. What was seen in them, recorded here: the smoke summary called the cost material on seed 990, from a single cell (Net2, random rule, 8 samples, daily-minimum RMSE); the two-rate model, run on that seed, did not recover half of that cell's gap; and the Net3 figure was looked at to check its layout (its curves for that one seed). One code fault was found by a new saved check and fixed before this block, in the shared runner `msx.py` (MSX computes its Reynolds number with water's 20 C viscosity whatever the file says; it does not touch any truth here, all of which run at the files' own viscosity). No truth, grid, model or bar was changed after the smoke runs. Edits after them: the saved checks, that runner fix, the summary's two context sections and docstrings.

Definitions:
- **Truths**, each on the committed draws in their committed order (monthly bulk factor u ~ U(0.8, 1.2); per pipe the wall factor and the roughness error; global and per-junction demand; one dose factor per source), with NET_TRUTH's rates (Net3 kb 0.40 per day and kw 0.70 m/day; Net2 0.10 and 0.20) and the 1.2 mg/L nominal dose. **first_order**: the committed truth itself (`build_scenario`, `chem=None`), EPANET first order with the mass-transfer-limited wall. **2ra** (`msx.two_reactant_truth`): dC/dt = -kF C F - kS C S - (4/d) kw kf / (kw + kf) C, dF/dt = -kF C F, dS/dt = -kS C S, with Greenvale's kF = 0.141 and kS = 0.00366 L/mg/h at 20 C (Fisher 2012 via Walski's Bentley blog, a secondary source), F = 1.13 s u and S = 2.87 s u mg/L at every source (proportional to u is an assumption, the analogue of the committed bulk factor), the committed per-pipe wall coefficients through EPANET's own mass-transfer-limited form, zero initial quality, the 300 s quality step, RK5, compiled reactions. s is solved so that the 2RA batch's apparent first-order rate at 24 h, dose 1.2 mg/L and 20 C equals the network's committed bulk rate (truth tuning, disclosed): 0.304 on Net3 and 0.083 on Net2 (`outputs/chem_2ra/scaling.json`). In that batch the apparent rate falls from 1.07 per day at 2 h to 0.12 at 144 h on Net3 (0.29 to 0.030 on Net2), and the local rate in water 4 to 7 days old is about 0.05 per day on Net3 and 0.012 on Net2, below today's grid floor of 0.10. **2ra_m96** (Net2 only, the plan's sensitivity, no bar): s matched at 96 h, 0.208.
- **Seeds**: Net3 400 to 407 and Net2 400 to 407, fresh (no free-chlorine experiment scored them; the code refuses seeds below 400, and the smoke seeds 990 and 991 outside a scratch folder). The plan paired against "the committed first-order-truth outputs", which are on seeds 0 to 7, the seeds today's settings were developed on. Following the lead's instruction to use fresh seeds, the paired reference is the committed truth rerun by the committed code on the same fresh seeds, and the committed seeds-0-to-7 values are reported beside it for context, with no bar.
- **Runs**: `python -m residualmap.experiment <net> 8 --chemistry=first_order`, `--chemistry=2ra` and, on Net2, `--chemistry=2ra --match=96`: the committed experiments (14:00 snapshot, time-aware, routes), models and sampling rules exactly as committed, outputs to `outputs/chem_2ra/` (`first_order/`, `match96/`), CSVs and summaries only. Then `python -m residualmap.chemexp audit <net> 8`: the oracle, the age deciles, the dose step (Net3) and the opt-in grid on every truth, then `summarise`.
- **Pairing**: the same seed gives the same draws under every truth; the random rule's samples are the same junctions, hours and reading errors under both truths, and the 3 first samples are the same for every rule (a saved check); the straddle rules choose their own samples.
- **Scores**: the daily minimum at the unsampled junctions. RMSE, MAE and coverage 50/80/90/95 are means over seeds of per-seed values (as committed and in the README); recall, precision and F1 come from counts pooled over seeds (found of the low junction-days; a seed with none adds nothing), false alarms are summed, and the committed mean-of-seeds recall (1.0 for a seed with nothing to find) is reported too. Baselines from the runs: the time-blind model, the mean of samples and the decay-only grid; plus the oracle, the best single member and dose of a grid against the full daytime truth, scored on every junction's daily minimum with no discrepancy GP. Paired bootstrap over seeds: 2000 resamples, generator seed 2026, 90% intervals.

Bars and readings:
- **M, materiality** (the plan's). Sixteen cells: Net3 and Net2 (24 h match) x the random and straddle_min rules x n = 8 and 15 x {daily-minimum RMSE, recall}. The cost is material if in any cell B0's 2RA value is more than 10% worse than its paired first-order value: an RMSE ratio above 1.10 or a pooled recall ratio below 0.90 (point ratios). Every trigger is reported with its counts and interval, and called robust (interval wholly on the worse side of 1.0) or within seed noise; both count. Net2's 96 h match: the same eight cells, reported, no bar. If no cell triggers, the journal records "first order costs at most 10% at fixed conditions", with the cells.
- **G, the gated two-rate model** (only if M triggers). Built for every network with a material cell, from that network's committed grid with no new EPANET run: today's 675 members, plus, for each of the 10 pairs k1 > k2 of today's bulk rates and f in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75}, C = f C(k1) + (1 - f) C(k2) at every (kw, gamma, demand, roughness) (exact for EPANET's linear first-order operator; Vieira, Coelho & Loureiro 2004, doi:10.2166/aqua.2004.0036; a saved check runs two chlorine parts through MSX against the mixture of two EPANET runs); 4725 members, uniform prior, everything else as B0; run under the committed rules on that network's first-order and 2RA truths (same seeds). The plan said Net3 only; its reason, about 620 MB on ky4, does not apply to Net2, where it needs no run and 16 MB of memory. Per network: G1, in every material cell of that network, full2r on the 2RA truth recovers at least half of B0's gap (RMSE at most B0_2RA - 0.5 (B0_2RA - B0_first_order); recall at least B0_2RA + 0.5 (B0_first_order - B0_2RA)); G2, its random-rule coverage90, averaged over n = 3, 8 and 15, lies in [0.85, 0.97] on both truths; G3, on the first-order truth at n = 8 and 15 under both rules, its RMSE ratio against B0 is at most 1.10 and its pooled recall ratio at least 0.90. All three on every gated network: "a candidate for the default model"; otherwise "tried and rejected". Either way this task changes nothing in the default path: wiring a candidate in would move committed numbers, Devansh's decision.
- **AGE** (the plan's). 2RA truth, B0 after 8 demo samples (task 9's draw, the app's demo draw), the daily-minimum map's bias at unsampled junctions by decile of the nominal daily-mean age (task 9's bins), pooled over seeds. If the oldest decile's |bias| exceeds 0.05 mg/L, the journal records it as the first-order limit and names, without building, the next step: an EPANET order-2 grid with a simulated dose axis (about 5 times the runs). Expected sign: negative (first order over-decays old water under 2RA). Reported beside it: the paired first-order truth's deciles (same seeds and samples) and task 9's committed baseline (seeds 0 to 7). Stated in advance: if the first-order truth's oldest decile is also beyond 0.05 mg/L in the same direction, the journal says so, and only the paired difference is attributed to first-order chemistry.
- **DOSE** (Net3, no bar, an expectation). B0 fitted on 15 demo samples at 1.2 mg/L; its daily-minimum map moved to 0.9 and 1.6 mg/L by the exact ln-offset first order implies (median and band times the dose ratio; P(daily min < 0.2) from the model's own draws at 0.2 over the ratio); scored at the unsampled junctions against each truth rerun at that dose with every draw the same (on the first-order truth the offset is exact, so it is the twin). Expectation, from the batch's ln shift of 0.311 to 0.403 between 1.2 and 1.6 mg/L against first order's 0.288 (`scaling.json`): under 2RA, relative to the twin, the map moved up a dose is conservative (biased low) and the map moved down optimistic (biased high). Reported: RMSE, bias, coverage90, recall and false alarms at each dose for both truths, and the paired bias difference with its interval.
- **LOWKB, the opt-in grid** (addendum 3). B0 on `simgp.GRIDS['full_lowkb']`: bulk rates 0.0125, 0.025, 0.05 and 0.075 per day added below today's 0.10 to 0.70 (1215 runs per network, cache file `grid24_full_lowkb_<net>_924eb8b02a.pkl`; today's grid, its file and every committed number untouched); everything else B0's; the committed rules on every truth and seed. 0.0125 was chosen below the 2RA water's local rate in Net2's oldest water. Reported plainly: the README columns at n = 3, 8 and 15, its ratios against B0 at n = 8 and 15 with intervals, the share of fits whose most probable bulk rate sits at each grid's floor, its age deciles and its oracle. The reading for Devansh, with no change made here: R1, on the first-order truths of both networks, at n = 8 and 15 under both rules, its RMSE ratio against B0 is at most 1.10 and its pooled recall ratio at least 0.90; R2, its random-rule coverage90 averaged over n = 3, 8 and 15 lies in [0.85, 0.97] on every truth; R3, a measurable RMSE gain (interval's upper end below 1.0) in at least one 2RA cell (24 h match). All three: "a candidate for the default grid; adopting it would move committed numbers, which is Devansh's decision"; otherwise "tested, not recommended".
- **C, checks.** `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`: every check passes, the task-13 group included; the four committed reruns (`--reproduce`) write all 50 files byte-identical; the app's default demo is unchanged.
- What counts as a stop. This is the plan's measurement task: a worse score on the harder truth is a finding, not a regression, and M is a materiality test, not a stop rule. The plan's 10% rule for a new model against its own baseline on the same truth appears here as G3 and R1: a failure means the model or grid is rejected or not recommended, and nothing is adopted. The default path changing (C) would be a stop.

**Notes added after the block, outside it.** The scored runs started at 06:07:43 PDT. At that time the code differed from the copy taken when the block was written only by one saved check added later (checked by diff afterwards). Later, with no scored number changed, `chemexp.py` gained the false-alarm section (`false_alarm_cells`) and its figure code was changed, and the module docstring of `checks.py` was edited (all listed below). The five experiment runs ran in parallel from 06:07:43 to 06:11:14 and the audit from 06:07:50 to 06:09:36 (from the runs' logs, kept outside the repository); the gated two-rate model on Net2 wrote `results_full2r_Net2.csv` at 06:12:01 (its file time; its run log was not kept). After the scored runs, with no scored number changed: the figures were redrawn (`chemexp replot`: the cost figure now names the material cells per network and gained a false-alarm panel, and two titles that were cut off were fixed), the summaries gained a section `false_alarms_no_bar` (B0's false alarms on the two truths, with intervals; it reads the same CSVs, and every other key of both summaries is unchanged, checked key by key), and one saved check was added (`two_reactant_outputs_reproduce`).

**Corrections to the block, found by the review** (the block above is left as written):
- Its LOWKB item says 0.0125 per day was chosen below the 2RA water's local rate in Net2's oldest water. That is inexact: `outputs/chem_2ra/scaling.json` gives that local rate as 0.0138, 0.0126 and 0.0115 per day over 96 to 120, 120 to 144 and 144 to 168 h, so 0.0125 lies inside it, and the new floor does not bracket the truth there. That fits finding 6: on Net2 the new floor holds the most probable rate in 53% of the two-reactant fits.
- Its Asked paragraph says addendum 2 drops the 2ra_warm truth, the seasonal ladder and the cost decomposition. The addendum's own words drop only the seasonal 2ra_warm claims, because no temperature model was adopted. Not building the 2ra_warm truth, the ladder or the decomposition is this task's reading of it: the ladder's other rungs were temperature and TOC models that were not adopted, and the decomposition's bar (25% of M_TT's RMSE) is defined against a model that no longer exists. A seasonal cost of first order for B0 alone could still be run; that is the lead's call (What needs a decision, f).
- Its G item calls the two-rate mixture exact for EPANET's linear first-order operator, and the plan said its members are masked where C is below the grid's floor. They are not masked: the committed grid stores ln max(C, 0.02 mg/L), so the mixture is of floored values and is exact only above the 0.02 mg/L floor (where the fast part sits at the floor and the slow part does not, the mixture is overstated by at most f x 0.02 mg/L, far below the 0.2 mg/L threshold). The model was rejected on G3 either way.
- Its AGE item's attribution clause (only the paired difference is attributed to chemistry) applies if the first-order truth's oldest decile is also past 0.05 mg/L. It was not (-0.031 on Net3), so the clause did not fire, and the bar fired on the raw bias, as the rule says. Finding 3 reports the paired difference with its interval beside it.

**After the review** (three independent reviewers; fixes made on 7 October from 06:50 PDT; no scored number changed):
- Code. The gated model's in-process grid cache is keyed on the committed grid's cache file (whose name carries the plant-dose tag) and that file's time, not only the network, so a second scenario at another dose can no longer be served the first one's mixtures (no committed run was affected: the gated model ran only at the nominal dose). The audit's parent process now builds the grids and reads the age bins inside a temporary working directory, so EPANET's `temp.inp`, `temp.rpt` and `temp.bin` are no longer left in the folder it starts from; three such git-ignored files from the scored two-rate run were deleted from the repository root. Rerun into a scratch folder with the fixed code, `chemexp full2r Net2 8` and `chemexp audit Net2 8` wrote `results_full2r_Net2.csv`, `audit_Net2.csv`, `error_by_age_2ra_Net2.csv` and `results_lowkb_Net2.csv` byte-identical to the committed files, and left nothing in the folder they started from.
- New in the summaries, each reported with no bar: `near_threshold_no_bar` (from `chemexp near`, which rebuilds every truth on the audit's seeds and counts junction-days by daily minimum just above 0.2 mg/L, `outputs/chem_2ra/near_threshold_<net>.csv`); the oldest decile's paired difference with its bootstrap interval (`difference_lo90` and `difference_hi90` in `acceptance.age_decile`); and every RMSE cell's ratio with the per-seed RMSEs pooled as a root mean square (`ratio_root_mean_square_no_bar`). Every other key of both summaries is unchanged (checked key by key), and the five figures were redrawn byte-identical.
- The saved check of first-order MSX against EPANET at 10 and 25 C now reruns the uncorrected runner too and records the gap the Reynolds-number fix closes (`before_fix_max_abs_diff_mgL`), and asserts that the uncorrected runner fails the bar at 10 C.
- A comment in `simgp.py` (the 0.0125 rationale above) and docstrings in `chemexp.py` and `msx.py` were corrected, so the four committed reruns were repeated after the last edit (label `post_task13_review`).

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** By the rule fixed in advance, first-order chemistry's cost at fixed conditions is material, from one cell of 16 that sits inside seed noise; the pre-registered oldest-water bar also triggered on Net3, and, with no bar, false alarms doubled there. The one cell is Net2 at 15 straddle-chosen samples: B0's daily-minimum RMSE is 0.0940 mg/L on the two-reactant truth against 0.0852 on the first-order truth with the same seeds and draws (ratio 1.102, interval 0.838 to 1.463; 1.089 if the per-seed RMSEs are pooled as a root mean square instead of averaged as committed). In the other 15 cells B0's daily-minimum RMSE on the two-reactant truth is 0.914 to 1.056 times its first-order value and its recall 0.936 to 1.033 times; one of them is a measurable loss inside the 10%: recall on Net2 at 15 random samples, 67 of 77 low junction-days against 93 of 100 (ratio 0.936, interval 0.847 to 0.991). The material cell opened the gate, so the gated two-rate model was built for Net2. It recovered that cell's gap on the two-reactant truth (RMSE 0.0708 against a target of 0.0896) and kept its coverage, but it was more than 10% worse than today's model on the first-order truth under the straddle rule (RMSE ratio 1.132 at 8 samples, interval 1.046 to 1.255; 1.193 at 15) and measurably worse under the random rule (1.072 and 1.077), so it is tried and rejected. Two costs show where the materiality test does not look. First, false alarms (no bar): on Net3, B0 flags about twice as many junction-days that are not low on the two-reactant truth (65 against 32 at 8 random samples, ratio 2.03, interval 1.53 to 2.57; precision 0.785 against 0.887), on every cell, with intervals wholly above 1; on Net2 the rise (1.17 to 1.50 times) is within seed noise. Second, the oldest water (the AGE bar): on Net3 the oldest tenth of junctions by water age is predicted 0.076 mg/L too low on average under the two-reactant truth (interval 0.055 to 0.095 too low), past the pre-registered 0.05 mg/L, against 0.031 on the first-order truth. The bar fires on that raw bias, as written, so the journal records it as first order's limit here and names the next step without building it; the part the paired truths attribute to the chemistry, a difference of 0.045 mg/L (interval 0.032 to 0.061), is itself inside the bar, and B0 under-predicts this decile by 0.031 on the first-order truth too. The dose step behaved as expected: moved from 1.2 to 1.6 mg/L by the exact ln-offset, the map is 0.021 mg/L more conservative under the two-reactant truth than on its first-order twin (interval 0.010 to 0.032), and moved to 0.9 mg/L it is 0.006 mg/L more optimistic (0.0004 to 0.011). The opt-in grid with bulk rates below 0.10 per day met all three of its pre-registered readings, so it is a candidate for the default grid, but adopting it would move committed numbers and is Devansh's decision; it trades some recall for fewer false alarms on Net3's first-order truth (recall 0.926 against 0.981 at 8 random samples, interval 0.920 to 0.969 of today's, false alarms 18 against 32). Today's default model, the app and every committed number are unchanged.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/msx.py`, the two-reactant section (the plan put the 2RA builder here): Greenvale's constants; `two_reactant_batch` (the batch solution, scipy LSODA at rtol 1e-10), `two_reactant_apparent_rate` and `two_reactant_scale` (s by brentq); `two_reactant_model` (CL2, FAST and SLOW in mg/L, rates per second, RK5 at the network's quality step, EPANET's mass-transfer-limited wall through `add_mass_transfer_terms`, bulk-only tanks; the constants are named kFast and kSlow because MSX names are case-insensitive and kF would collide with the term Kf); `TwoReactantTruth` (match age, compiler); and `two_reactant_truth`, the hidden truth on the committed draws in their committed order, with no new draw. A fault in the shared runner was found by a new saved check and fixed before the block: MSX computes its Reynolds number with water's 20 C viscosity whatever the file says, while EPANET uses the file's relative VISCOSITY, so at a 10 C viscosity the two engines' first-order wall terms differed by up to 0.086 mg/L on Net3 (0.018 at 25 C; the saved check now reruns the uncorrected runner and records these as `before_fix_max_abs_diff_mgL` in `checks_report.json`). `add_mass_transfer_terms` now divides MSX's Re by the file's relative viscosity when that is not 1 (0.0061 and 0.0055 mg/L after the fix). At relative viscosity 1, which every committed MSX run used (Net3, Net2 and ky4 all are), its expressions are written exactly as before (a saved check), so no committed number moves.
- `chemistry.py`: kinetics `2ra`, a free-chlorine truth only, at 20 C with no TOC input (the seasonal variant is refused with a message saying it was dropped).
- `simulate.py`: `build_scenario(two_reactant=...)` and the `2ra` branch of `_chem_truth`; warming, another wall law and the loss split are refused for it.
- `simgp.py`: the opt-in grid `GRIDS['full_lowkb']` (`KB_LOWKB`: 0.0125, 0.025, 0.05 and 0.075 added below today's five bulk rates, 1215 runs) and `COMMITTED_GRID_NAMES`; a grid outside that list always carries a hash of its axes in its cache name (`grid24_full_lowkb_<net>_924eb8b02a.pkl`), and the committed names, axes and condition tags are unchanged.
- `experiment.py`: `run_scenario_time(make_model=None, baselines=True, extra=False)` (an opt-in model in place of SimGP24; the random-rule baselines can be skipped, which draws nothing from the sampling generator; `_metrics_time(extra=True)` adds the daily minimum's MAE, bias and the counts behind its rates) and `main(chemistry=None, match_h=24, figures=True)` with `--chemistry=first_order|2ra`, `--match` and `--seed0`/`--outdir` for unscored runs (`NET_CHEM`, `CHEM_FIRST_SEED`, `chem_outdir`). A chemistry run writes the committed CSVs and summary (with a `chemistry` block of each seed's truth settings) to `outputs/chem_2ra/`, and its figures to a temporary folder that is deleted. Every default is the committed run, file for file.
- `residualmap/chemexp.py`, new: the audit (`scaling`, the oracle, the age deciles in task 9's format, the dose step, the opt-in grid under the committed rules, a process pool with one thread per worker and private working directories), the gated two-rate model (`two_rate_members`, `two_rate_grid`, `TwoRateSimGP24`; the plan put a grid 'full2r' in `simgp.py`, it lives here, built in memory from the cached committed grid with no new run), the summary with its pre-registered acceptance key, three figures, `summarise` and `replot`; after the review, `near` (the near-threshold counts) and the two fixes listed under After the review.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: nine checks in a new `two_reactant` group (Checked, below).
- Not built: the plan's 2ra_warm truth and seasonal ladder, and the cost decomposition (dropped by addendum 2); ky4 (optional, not run).

**Found** (all simulated; numbers from `outputs/chem/summary_audit_<net>.json`, computed from `outputs/chem_2ra/**/results_time_<net>.csv`, `results_lowkb_<net>.csv`, `results_full2r_Net2.csv`, `outputs/chem/audit_<net>.csv` and `error_by_age_2ra_<net>.csv`; the truth tuning from `outputs/chem_2ra/scaling.json`; Net3 and Net2 seeds 400 to 407; B0 is today's model):
1. The materiality test (`acceptance.materiality`). Every cell, B0 on the two-reactant truth against the paired first-order truth:

   | network, rule, samples | daily-minimum RMSE, 2RA / first order (mg/L) | ratio (90% interval) | recall, 2RA / first order (found of low junction-days) | ratio (90% interval) |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | Net3, random, 8 | 0.0759 / 0.0729 | 1.041 (0.964 to 1.152) | 0.975 (238 of 244) / 0.981 (252 of 257) | 0.995 (0.974 to 1.019) |
   | Net3, random, 15 | 0.0703 / 0.0695 | 1.010 (0.925 to 1.121) | 0.974 (222 of 228) / 0.970 (230 of 237) | 1.003 (0.984 to 1.032) |
   | Net3, straddle_min, 8 | 0.0700 / 0.0740 | 0.945 (0.851 to 1.084) | 0.988 (242 of 245) / 0.992 (249 of 251) | 0.996 (0.988 to 1.000) |
   | Net3, straddle_min, 15 | 0.0710 / 0.0673 | 1.056 (0.950 to 1.150) | 0.987 (229 of 232) / 0.991 (222 of 224) | 0.996 (0.980 to 1.014) |
   | Net2, random, 8 | 0.0563 / 0.0555 | 1.015 (0.952 to 1.071) | 0.950 (96 of 101) / 0.962 (126 of 131) | 0.988 (0.938 to 1.031) |
   | Net2, random, 15 | 0.0543 / 0.0528 | 1.028 (0.858 to 1.205) | 0.870 (67 of 77) / 0.930 (93 of 100) | 0.936 (0.847 to 0.991) |
   | Net2, straddle_min, 8 | 0.0738 / 0.0807 | 0.914 (0.637 to 1.298) | 1.000 (96 of 96) / 0.968 (120 of 124) | 1.033 (1.000 to 1.073) |
   | Net2, straddle_min, 15 | **0.0940 / 0.0852** | **1.102 (0.838 to 1.463), material, within seed noise** | 0.955 (63 of 66) / 0.944 (85 of 90) | 1.011 (0.931 to 1.075) |

   One cell of 16 is past the bar, by 0.002 of the ratio, with an interval that reaches 0.84. The rule written in advance counts it, so the cost is material and the gate opened. Without that cell the reading would have been "first order costs at most 10% at fixed conditions"; the record keeps both facts. The trigger also depends on how the per-seed RMSEs are aggregated (added after the review, `ratio_root_mean_square_no_bar`): pooled as a root mean square instead of averaged as committed, this cell is 1.089 and no cell of the 16 is above 1.10 (the highest are this one and Net3 at 15 straddle samples, both 1.089). Inside the 10%, one cell is a measurable loss: recall on Net2 at 15 random samples, 0.936 (interval 0.847 to 0.991, wholly below 1). The Net2 sensitivity with s matched at 96 h (no bar) has no cell past the bar: RMSE ratios 0.743 to 0.942 and recall ratios 0.989 to 1.028, so on Net2 the two-reactant truth matched at 96 h is easier for B0 than the first-order truth. For context only, B0's committed numbers on seeds 0 to 7 (`outputs/results_time_<net>.csv`) at the same cells are RMSE 0.068 to 0.086 on Net3 and 0.053 to 0.091 on Net2, a similar range to the fresh seeds' first-order values (0.067 to 0.074 and 0.053 to 0.085).
2. False alarms are where first order costs most here (added after the run, no bar; `false_alarms_no_bar`). On Net3 B0 flags about twice as many junction-days that are not low on the two-reactant truth, in every cell: 65 against 32 at 8 random samples (ratio 2.03, interval 1.53 to 2.57, more under 2RA in 6 of 8 seeds), 55 against 30 at 15 (1.83, 1.30 to 2.62), 56 against 27 and 32 against 13 at 8 and 15 straddle samples (2.07 and 2.46, intervals from 1.50 and 1.78 upward). Precision falls from 0.887 to 0.785 at 8 random samples. On Net2 the rise is 1.17 to 1.50 times, every interval reaching 1.0. Read after the run: B0's map is biased low on both truths and a little more on the two-reactant one (-0.043 against -0.036 mg/L at 8 random samples on Net3), and the two-reactant water keeps more chlorine in old water, so more junction-days sit just above 0.2 mg/L, where a low-biased map flags them. Counted after the review (`near_threshold_no_bar`; every junction, 8 seeds): on Net3 the two-reactant truth has 84 junction-days with a daily minimum from 0.20 to 0.25 mg/L against 53 under first order (more on 6 of the 8 seeds) and 59 against 47 from 0.25 to 0.30; on Net2, 61 against 44 and 27 against 11. That supports the reading, but how much of the doubling is the bias and how much the kinetics is not separated (What needs a decision, c). The pre-registered metrics (RMSE and recall) did not look here; the materiality rule would not have caught this.
3. The oldest water (`acceptance.age_decile`; Net3 triggers the plan's rule). After 8 demo samples, B0's bias in the oldest decile of junctions by nominal water age (39.6 to 73.3 h on Net3; 90% of them are lower-bound ages, task 9) is -0.076 mg/L on the two-reactant truth (interval -0.095 to -0.055) against -0.031 (-0.047 to -0.012) on the first-order truth, on the same seeds and samples: a paired difference of -0.045 mg/L (interval -0.061 to -0.032; `difference_lo90` and `difference_hi90`, added after the review). The bar fires on the raw bias, as the rule says. The attribution clause written in advance (if the first-order truth's oldest decile were also past 0.05 mg/L, only the paired difference would be attributed to chemistry) did not apply, since that decile is at -0.031; the part the paired truths attribute to the chemistry, 0.045 mg/L, is measurable but itself inside the 0.05 bar. The expected sign held (under-predicted), and the bias grows with age only under the two-reactant truth (deciles 9 and 10: -0.055 and -0.076, against -0.024 and -0.031); the 90% band held the oldest decile's true daily minimum at 0.710 of its junction-seeds under the two-reactant truth against 0.884 on the first-order truth (`error_by_age_2ra_Net3.csv`). By the plan's rule this is recorded as the first-order limit, and the next step is named and not built: an EPANET order-2 grid with a simulated dose axis (about 5 times the runs). On Net2 the oldest decile's bias is -0.028 mg/L under the two-reactant truth (-0.016 first order; paired difference -0.012, interval -0.016 to -0.008), inside the bar. Task 9's committed baseline (first-order truth, seeds 0 to 7) has -0.026 on Net3 and -0.053 on Net2: on those seeds Net2's first-order truth alone is past 0.05, so an oldest-decile bias of that size is not by itself evidence of chemistry.
4. The gated two-rate model, Net2 (`acceptance.full2r`): tried and rejected. G1 met: in the one material cell its two-reactant RMSE is 0.0708 against B0's 0.0940 and a target of 0.0896. G2 met: random-rule coverage90 0.922 (two-reactant) and 0.902 (first order). G3 missed: on the first-order truth its RMSE is 1.072 (interval 1.028 to 1.119) and 1.077 (1.004 to 1.145) times B0's at 8 and 15 random samples, and 1.132 (1.046 to 1.255) and 1.193 (0.792 to 1.783) times at 8 and 15 straddle samples, the last two past the 1.10 bar; its recall ratios were 0.983 to 1.047. Elsewhere on the two-reactant truth it was no better than B0 (RMSE ratios 0.981 to 1.019) except in the material cell itself (0.754). Its most probable member was a two-rate one in 48% of the first-order truth's fits and 20% of the two-reactant truth's (column `map_kb`, negative codes), so the extra hypotheses were used more where they are wrong. Read after the run: Net2's chlorine loss is mostly at the walls (task 9), and splitting the bulk rate did not buy accuracy there.
5. The dose step (Net3, no bar; `dose_step`). B0 fitted on 15 demo samples at 1.2 mg/L and moved by the exact ln-offset: against the first-order twin, where the offset is exact, the two-reactant map at 1.6 mg/L is biased 0.021 mg/L lower (interval 0.010 to 0.032 lower), and at 0.9 mg/L 0.006 higher (0.0004 to 0.011): conservative going up, optimistic going down, as expected. The truth itself moved by a mean ln ratio of 0.348 between 1.2 and 1.6 mg/L (0.288 to 0.561 by junction) against first order's 0.288, and by -0.348 going down (-0.563 to -0.288). In mg/L both truths gave about the same RMSE at 1.6 mg/L (0.172 and 0.172) and the two-reactant truth a lower one at 0.9 (0.085 against 0.097); the moved map's coverage90 fell to 0.677 at 1.6 mg/L under the two-reactant truth (0.739 on the twin). Read after the run: B0 with 15 demo samples is biased low on both truths at the nominal dose (-0.068 and -0.071 mg/L), most of it from two seeds: on seed 403 the map is 0.207 mg/L too low on the first-order truth and 0.192 on the two-reactant truth (`audit_Net3.csv`), and seed 404 is 0.12 and 0.11 too low; looked at after the run, on seed 403 the discrepancy GP fitted a large amplitude with a short length scale on water age. That is today's model under this sampling draw, the same on both truths, and the paired differences above remove it.
6. The opt-in grid with lower bulk rates (`lowkb`, `acceptance.lowkb_recommendation`): all three readings met, so it is a candidate for the default grid, Devansh's decision. R1 (first-order truths, no loss over 10%): its RMSE ratios against B0 are 0.913 to 1.079 and its recall ratios 0.935 to 1.008. R2: random-rule coverage90 averaged over n is 0.912 to 0.942 on every truth (B0 0.891 to 0.926). R3: measurable RMSE gains on the two-reactant truth at 8 and 15 random samples on Net3 (0.915, interval 0.862 to 0.967, and 0.881, 0.840 to 0.919) and at 8 random and 8 straddle samples on Net2 (0.841, 0.759 to 0.933, and 0.719, 0.580 to 0.920). Its costs, recorded: on Net3's first-order truth its recall at 8 random samples is 0.926 against B0's 0.981 (ratio 0.944, interval 0.920 to 0.969, a measurable loss within the 10%), with 18 false alarms against 32; at 15 straddle samples 0.964 against 0.991 (0.972, 0.953 to 0.991); on Net2's first-order truth 0.870 against 0.930 at 15 random samples and 0.906 against 0.968 at 8 straddle samples (ratios 0.935 and 0.936, intervals reaching 1.0); at the 96 h match its RMSE at 15 random samples is 1.073 times B0's. Its most probable bulk rate sits below 0.10 per day in 32% (first order) and 38% (two-reactant) of Net3's fits and 63% and 80% of Net2's, where B0's sits at its floor of 0.10 in 35% and 45% of Net3's fits and 79% and 96% of Net2's; the new floor of 0.0125 holds the most probable rate in 21% to 23% of Net3's fits and in 36% (first order) and 53% (two-reactant) of Net2's, so on Net2 even this grid is often at its edge. It also shrinks the oldest-decile bias under the two-reactant truth on Net3 from -0.076 to -0.049 mg/L.
7. The oracle (`oracle`): the best single member and dose of today's grid represents the two-reactant truth at least as well as the first-order truth: daily-minimum RMSE 0.051 against 0.059 mg/L on Net3 (daytime RMS ln error 0.161 against 0.174) and 0.044 against 0.046 on Net2. Within a week at a fixed dose, a first-order member absorbs the fast initial demand into its rate and dose, the finding of Powell et al. (2000) and Monteiro et al. (2014) that the plan expected. The truths differ in their low counts: 267 against 282 low junction-days on Net3 and 135 against 173 on Net2 (every junction, 8 seeds).
8. The other experiments of the paired runs (no bar; `snapshot_and_routes_no_bar`): the 14:00 snapshot model's RMSE at 8 random samples is 0.062 on the two-reactant truth against 0.071 on Net3 (Net2 0.076 and 0.076), and the optimised 8-site route's daily-minimum recall 0.948 against 0.966 on Net3 (0.901 against 0.931 on Net2).
9. The engines agree (Checked). The two-reactant reactions in MSX match the LSODA batch solution on a 12-pipe chain within 1.3e-5 mg/L at 10, 20 and 25 C; first-order MSX matches EPANET within 0.0061 mg/L at 10 and 25 C after the Reynolds-number fix; two chlorine parts through MSX equal the mixture of two EPANET runs within 0.0055 mg/L, the exactness the two-rate model rests on (above the grid's 0.02 mg/L floor; see the corrections after the block).
10. Limitations, recorded. The Greenvale constants are from a secondary source and one water; the reactant loads proportional to the monthly bulk factor, and s matched at one age (24 h), are assumptions and truth tuning; the walls consume chlorine but not organics; everything runs at 20 C with one TOC, so this says nothing about seasons or storms; the truths start their pipes and tanks with no chlorine and no organics, and the oldest decile's ages are mostly lower bounds (task 9's start-up transient); the dose step shares B0's own low bias at 15 demo samples; ky4 was not run; 8 seeds per network.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- None in the default numbers. After the task's code edits, the four committed reruns write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs (`outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`, label `post_task13`; the reruns ran from 06:14 to 06:22, after the last edit to the code they import), and again after the review's edits (label `post_task13_review`, from 07:03 to 07:10, after the last edit at 07:00), and the app's default demo is unchanged both times. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun.
- In the audit, which is a measurement and changes nothing: the pre-registered materiality rule fired on one cell (Net2, 15 straddle samples, RMSE 1.102 times, within seed noise); the gated two-rate model was rejected (more than 10% worse than today's model on the first-order truth under the straddle rule, and measurably worse under the random rule); today's model raises about twice the false alarms on Net3 under the two-reactant truth, and under-predicts Net3's oldest tenth of junctions by 0.076 mg/L there, past the 0.05 bar (0.045 of it attributable to the chemistry by the paired truths); its 90% band holds that decile's truth at 0.710; and its recall on Net2 at 15 random samples is a measurable 0.936 times the first-order truth's (interval 0.847 to 0.991), inside the 10%. These are findings about first-order chemistry on a harder truth, not changes to the default path.
- In the opt-in grid (not adopted): measurable recall losses on Net3's first-order truth (0.944 and 0.972 times today's at 8 random and 15 straddle samples), recall ratios of 0.935 and 0.936 on Net2's first-order truth (intervals reaching 1.0), and an RMSE of 1.073 times today's at 15 random samples under the 96 h match; its most probable bulk rate sits at its own new floor in up to 53% of Net2's fits.
- The dose step at 15 demo samples shows today's model biased low by about 0.07 mg/L at the nominal dose on Net3 under both truths, most of it from two seeds (0.21 and 0.19 mg/L too low on seed 403 under the first-order and two-reactant truths, 0.12 and 0.11 on seed 404), with coverage90 0.74 and 0.75; this is today's model under that sampling draw, reported, not investigated further.
- Against the plan and the process:
  - The plan paired against the committed first-order outputs (seeds 0 to 7); the lead asked for fresh seeds, so the committed truth was rerun with the committed code on seeds 400 to 407 as the paired reference, and the seeds-0-to-7 values are reported for context only.
  - The plan's materiality test is per cell and the rule was applied as written: one cell inside seed noise opened the gate. The bar uses point ratios, so it counts noise; the intervals are reported beside it.
  - The gated model was built for Net2, where the cell fired; the plan said Net3 only (its reason was ky4's disk) and Net3 had no material cell. It lives in `chemexp.py`, built in memory from the committed grid, not as a cached grid 'full2r' in `simgp.py`.
  - Addendum 2 dropped the seasonal 2ra_warm claims. Reading that, this task did not build the 2ra_warm truth or run the seasonal ladder or the cost decomposition: the ladder's other rungs were models that were not adopted, and the decomposition's bar (25% of M_TT's RMSE) rests on one. That is this task's reading, not the addendum's words (corrections after the block); a B0-only seasonal cost of first order is the lead's call. The plan's figure `cost_of_first_order_Net3.png` now shows the fixed-condition comparison instead of the decomposition. ky4 (optional) was not run.
  - The plan said the two-rate members are masked where C is below the grid's floor; they are not: the mixture is of the committed grid's floored values, so it is exact only above 0.02 mg/L (corrections after the block).
  - The block's 0.0125 per day rationale (below Net2's oldest-water local rate) was inexact; that rate is 0.0115 to 0.0138 per day (corrections after the block).
  - Added beyond the plan's list: a recall bar read from pooled counts (the committed per-seed convention counts a seed with nothing to find as recall 1.0, which flatters the truth with fewer low junctions), the paired-sample check, the Net2 96 h sensitivity's own folder, `audit_<net>.csv`, `error_by_age_2ra_<net>.csv` and `results_lowkb_<net>.csv`.
  - After the scored runs, with no scored number changed: the figures were redrawn, the summaries gained `false_alarms_no_bar`, one saved check was added, and the module docstring of `checks.py` was edited (after the full check run). After the review, also with no scored number changed: the fixes and additions listed under After the review (two code fixes, three summary sections with no bar, two new CSVs, the uncorrected runner in one saved check). The smoke runs and what was seen in them are described in the block.
  - Left by the scored runs and deleted after the review: EPANET's `temp.inp`, `temp.rpt` and `temp.bin` in the repository root (git-ignored), written by the gated model's parent process (fixed, After the review).
- Costs:
  - Two new git-ignored grid files in `outputs/cache`, the low-bulk-rate grids: 10.8 MB (Net3) and 4.1 MB (Net2), built during the smoke runs in under a minute together (byte sizes; build time from the smoke logs, not kept).
  - The scored runs: the five experiment runs in parallel took 2.9 to 3.5 minutes each and the audit's pool 53 s (Net3) and 48 s (Net2) on 4 workers (from the runs' logs, kept outside the repository); one 2RA truth takes about 2 s in compiled MSX. The gated model's scored log was not kept; its rerun after the review took 24 s on 4 workers, and `chemexp near` about 8 s per network.
  - New tracked outputs: `outputs/chem_2ra/` about 2.3 MB (20 CSVs of at most 0.21 MB, two of them the near-threshold counts added after the review, 5 summaries, `scaling.json`); in `outputs/chem/` two audit CSVs (0.01 MB each), two decile CSVs (0.04 and 0.05 MB), two summaries (0.07 and 0.09 MB) and five figures.
  - Free disk stayed at 3.5 GiB throughout (df readings).

**What needs a decision** (nothing below was done):
- (a) The opt-in low-bulk-rate grid met its readings. Making it the default would move every committed number and trade some recall for fewer false alarms on Net3's first-order truth; it would also remove the floor that today's fit sits on in most Net2 and second-order-truth fits (tasks 10b, 11 and this one). Devansh's call.
- (b) First order's limit in the oldest water is recorded. The named next step is an EPANET second-order grid with a simulated dose axis (about 5 times the runs: some 3,400 EPANET runs per network instead of 675).
- (c) The false alarms: today's map is biased low (about 0.02 to 0.04 mg/L at 8 and 15 random samples here, as in tasks 9 and 11), and a low-biased map flags the two-reactant truth's junctions that sit just above 0.2 mg/L (on Net3, 84 junction-days from 0.20 to 0.25 mg/L against 53 under first order; `near_threshold_no_bar`). How much of the doubled false alarms is that bias and how much the kinetics was not separated, and where the bias comes from (the discrepancy GP, the daily-minimum Monte Carlo, or the grid's floor) is still open.
- (d) The materiality rule is per cell on point ratios, so one noisy cell opened the gate; a future rule could require the bootstrap interval to clear the bar. The trigger also depends on the aggregation: with the per-seed RMSEs pooled as a root mean square instead of averaged, that cell is 1.089 and no cell is past 1.10 (`ratio_root_mean_square_no_bar`).
- (e) `msx.py` now corrects MSX's Reynolds number for a file whose relative viscosity is not 1; nothing committed used one, and a seasonal MSX truth would need it.
- (f) Whether a seasonal cost of first order for B0 alone is wanted. This task read addendum 2 as dropping the 2ra_warm truth, the ladder and the decomposition with the seasonal claims (corrections after the block); a B0-only run on a warming two-reactant truth is possible with the runner as it stands, but it has no pre-registered bar left to judge it.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.chemexp scaling`, then `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.experiment Net3 8 --chemistry=first_order`, `... Net3 8 --chemistry=2ra`, the same for Net2 and `Net2 8 --chemistry=2ra --match=96` (about 3 minutes each; fresh seeds 400 to 407; outputs to `outputs/chem_2ra/`, figures not kept), then `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.chemexp audit Net3 8 --workers 4` and `audit Net2 8` (about a minute each; the low-bulk-rate grids go to `outputs/cache/`), `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.chemexp full2r Net2 8 --workers 4` (the gated model, about 30 s), `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.chemexp near Net3 8` and `near Net2 8` (the near-threshold counts, added after the review, about 10 s each), and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.chemexp summarise Net3` and `summarise Net2` (summaries and figures; `replot` redraws the figures from the committed summary). Runs on other seeds need `--seed0` and a scratch `--outdir` (experiment) or `--root` (chemexp). EPANET-MSX needs Homebrew's libomp and, for compiled reactions, the Xcode command-line tools.

### Task 14, the app's capability table, the PDF's chemistry lines, the journal's summary and the numbers script

**Asked:** close the iteration for a reader (plan task 14, narrowed by addenda 2 and 3 and the lead's brief). In the app: a 'What this model accounts for' table with one row per point the general manager raised (water age, organics and chlorine demand, chlorine type, temperature), each saying what is modelled and what was tested and not adopted; PDF lines for the disinfectant, the measured species, the threshold and its source, and 'simulation only', with no kb20 or temperature field, because no temperature model was adopted, saying instead that the seasons are absorbed by refitting. The plan's monthly temperature and TOC inputs and its 'next month's temperature' outlook went with the temperature models (addendum 2). In this journal: this section, a capabilities table answering the four points from `outputs/`, an iteration-4 summary, code-map lines, bug-risk rows and how-to-run lines. Elsewhere: a top-level summary in the README, the CHANGELOG, the pilot protocol, the feature dictionary, and a script that checks every iteration-4 number in the README and this journal against `outputs/`. The plan's seven headless app runs, with its two outlook runs replaced (below). Defaults byte-identical, the app's default demo unchanged, no em or en dashes, no real utility named.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/capabilities.py`, new.
  - `SOURCES` names the file and JSON key of every result number the app's table quotes, and `numbers()` reads them. It also derives a few: the audit cells within 10%, the low junction-days found (recall times the low count), the range of task 10b's held-out ratios and the world at its low end, the count of task 10b's stop triggers, the junction counts, and the adoption flags.
  - `accounts_for()` returns the four rows; `report_lines()` returns the PDF's chemistry lines; `threshold_source()` gives the default's note or 'set by you'; `grid_edge_warning()` writes the app's grid-edge warning. In chloramine mode the PDF's season line says that this month's water temperature is used only by the nitrification watch and that no seasonal chloramine test has been run. The default notes are 'a common operating minimum' for 0.2 mg/L free chlorine and 'a common utility operating target' for 0.5 mg/L total chlorine, each 'not a California rule (California requires a detectable residual)'.
  - The wording follows the plan's rule: 'accounts for' only where there is a term and a test, 'absorbed into the calibrated decay rates' where there is not, and 'not modelled' for nitrification as a process, blending and pH.
  - The adoption words come from the outputs (task 10b's `adoption`, task 11's `adoption`, task 12's `experimental`), so a rerun that changed an outcome would change the table. Every result number in the table is read from `outputs/`; settings and bars (the 0.85 bar, the 5% starting-water rule, the 0.10 per day floor) are fixed text.
  - The temperature and organics rows name the assumptions behind their numbers: the assumed temperature and TOC schedules, the assumed wall response and constants of the simulated truths, and which of task 10b's worlds gives the low end of its range.
- `app.py`:
  - the table, under 'What this model accounts for', with a caption that every test is a simulation; if `outputs/` cannot be read, the app says so instead of failing;
  - four chemistry lines in the PDF header: the disinfectant and measured species (in chloramine mode also the prior's pH and Cl2:N and the nitrification-watch count); the minimum residual and its source; how the seasons are handled; 'Validated in simulation only'. They are wrapped, and the header is taller;
  - help text on the threshold: the default's note, plus Texas as an example (0.2 mg/L free chlorine, or 0.5 mg/L total chlorine in chloraminated systems, 30 TAC 290.110);
  - a grid-edge warning (`capabilities.grid_edge_warning`) when more than half of the calibration sits on the lowest or highest bulk or wall decay rate of the grid. For free chlorine at the bulk floor it points to tasks 10b, 11 and 13 and the low-rate grid;
  - a fallback for the chloramine demo truth: if EPANET-MSX cannot run, the demo uses task 12's first-order twin (EPANET first-order decay at the rate EPA's chemistry gives for the water, on the same hidden draws). A warning says so, and says this flatters the model. The failure is cached like a result, so MSX is tried once per network, dose and scenario in a server process, not on every widget change, and the hidden truth cannot switch between the two within a session;
  - `prepare()`'s in-memory cache key carries the chloramine grid's `chemistry.cache_tag`;
  - `workers_skip_this_app()`, a fix for a fault this task's upload test found (finding 1).
- `residualmap/numbers.py`, new (`python -m residualmap.numbers`, or `python -m residualmap.checks --numbers`): a registry of task 14's headline numbers, each tied to a file and key, and a scan of every iteration-4 number in the README and this journal (finding 3).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: six new checks in a `task14` group, and a way for a check to be skipped with a reason (`SkipCheck`, recorded as skipped, never as a pass):
  - `capabilities_table_reads_outputs`;
  - `grid_edge_warning_text`, the warning on the real free-chlorine and chloramine grids with made-up posteriors (silent below half of the calibration on an edge, the rate named above it);
  - `app_task14_runs`, the headless app runs;
  - `numbers_trace_to_outputs`;
  - `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text`;
  - `no_listed_names_in_tracked_files`. It reads a private list of names from a CSV kept outside the repository (`RESIDUALMAP_NAMES_CSV`) and fails if any tracked text file names one. The one exception is the public provenance of EPA's Net3 example in `experiment.NET_DESC`, which also appears in committed figure titles and was left as it is. Without the variable the check is skipped, and it records counts only, never a name. Its result goes to the git-ignored run log only (`RUN_LOG_ONLY`), so the committed report is the same with or without the list; a failure still fails the run.
- Docs:
  - in this journal: this section, 'Capabilities after iteration 4', 'Iteration 4 summary', the iteration-4 lines in the code map, the bug-risk rows (marked 'iteration 4') and the how-to-run lines;
  - the README: 'Iteration 4 in one page', the limitations, the app section, the run lines and the layout;
  - the CHANGELOG;
  - the pilot protocol: section 1 asks for the plant's monthly water temperature and TOC with the reason; section 3 lists the new table and PDF lines; section 5 gains an organics bullet and a note on chloraminated pilot candidates; the checklist asks for the temperature and TOC logs;
  - the feature dictionary: a section on the chemistry settings.
- Not built, on purpose:
  - the plan's monthly temperature and TOC inputs in the app, its kb20 and kb(T) PDF lines and its outlook: no temperature or TOC model was adopted;
  - the optional `temp_C` column in `docs/example_grab_log.csv`: the plant log already carries the monthly temperature, and nothing adopted would read a per-sample one;
  - any change to `route.py`: its reasons already use the threshold.

**Found:**
1. The app could not build a grid inside itself, a fault from iteration 3 that this task's upload test exposed.
   - The cause: process pools start their workers with multiprocessing's spawn method, which re-runs the main module's file in each worker. Under Streamlit the main module is `app.py`.
   - What happened: every worker of the grid build re-ran the whole app (the default Net3 demo) before its job. The parallel copies collided on EPANET's temporary files in the shared working directory, and the build died with `BrokenProcessPool`.
   - When it bites: an uploaded `.inp`, or any network whose grid is not yet cached (ky4 at the default dose, a fresh machine).
   - The chloramine demo truth, a pool of one worker, ran the app once before each MSX run and did not fail.
   - The fix: while a pool starts, `sys.modules['__main__']` is an empty stand-in, so workers import only `residualmap`. With it, the upload test builds its fresh 675-run grid inside the app.
   - Rechecked by running a copy of the app with the fix removed and an empty grid cache: the default Net3 demo stopped with `BrokenProcessPool`. The same copy with the fix built the grid inside the app and flagged the default 42 of 92 junctions.
   - No committed number used this path: the experiments run from the command line, where the main module is the experiment.
2. The seven app runs, by widget label (`app_task14_runs` in `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`).
   - The free chlorine default demo is unchanged and carries the table and the PDF lines.
   - The chloramine demo on Net3 runs EPA's chemistry in EPANET-MSX, calibrates on the chloramine grid, flags junctions at 0.5 mg/L total chlorine, and on the reveal scores them against the hidden truth.
   - The upload of the same Net3 file gives the same map as the example network. The check moves any earlier upload of that file and its cached grid aside first and requires the run to create the grid's cache file, so it always exercises the fix in finding 1.
   - ky4 with the chloramine demo is refused with its message before any simulation.
   - With EPANET-MSX made to fail, the chloramine demo falls back to the reduced truth and says so.
   - The plan's runs (3) and (5), the outlook's direction and the outlook disabled on ky4, went with the outlook, and were replaced by the ky4 refusal and the MSX fallback.
   - No grid-edge warning appeared in these runs.
3. What the numbers script can and cannot show.
   - It reads every number in the iteration-4 parts of the README and this journal, the CHANGELOG excluded.
   - It looks each one up at its printed precision: first among the committed output files its paragraph cites, then its section's, then all of `outputs/chem`, `outputs/chem_2ra` and `outputs/chloramine`, then all of `outputs/`.
   - A number found in none of them must be a time, a date, a citation, an identifier, a run cost, a count derived from a stored rate, a value written in the code, or an allowlisted literature value with its reason (one, Blokker's E/R, in task 10's section). None is unexplained.
   - The spans blanked as dates, identifiers, times, citations and seed ranges are guarded on both sides, so they never take part of a decimal; a blanked span that touches a digit is reported as unexplained. Before the review they had no right-hand guard: 'July' or 'model' followed by a decimal took its integer part, and the rest was never read, so 11 numbers in the text were not checked (for example the 0.949 after 'April to July'). With the guard, a wrong digit put in any of those places is caught.
   - Its limit: the outputs hold over a million values, so a printed three-digit decimal usually matches some value by chance, even inside the files its paragraph cites. Changing the last printed digit of a traced decimal by one would be caught only about one time in ten, and of a traced integer about one time in seven, since an integer is matched at plus or minus 0.5 and an 'X of Y' count is accepted when X/Y is a stored rate (the script prints the exact shares). So the scan shows a number exists in `outputs/`, not that it came from the right key.
   - The registry is the exact check: each headline number of task 14's own text (the capabilities table, the summary's key numbers and its list of regressions, the README's one-page section) is tied to one file, one key or one rule over a file, one format and the words around it, and all of them match. Two wrong entries (another network's value, a coarser format) are run as controls and fail, as they should.
   - The scan leaves its own entry in `checks_report.json` out of the values it searches. Without that, its counts fed back into the next run, and a full run on an unchanged tree changed the committed report (seen on this task's first full run). It still searches the rest of the report, so after a change to any other check's result the first full run settles the scan's counts and the next full run leaves the report unchanged.
4. The capability table's wording rule held without exceptions: water age and chlorine type have a term and a test; organics and temperature are 'absorbed into the calibrated decay rates', with what was tested and not adopted stated beside it. Gas chlorine and hypochlorite are not modelled separately: both give the same free chlorine, and they differ mainly through pH, which is absorbed into the calibrated rates.
5. No utility on the private list is named in any tracked text file except the provenance string above, which the lead asked to keep; the check found it on 1 line of `residualmap/experiment.py`. One other real utility is named in a tracked file: iteration 3's prompt (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`) names a pilot target that is not on the list. That file is Devansh's own prompt, so it was left as it is and flagged for him.

**After the review** (three reviewers; each issue was checked against the files before it was fixed):
- Water age, the one major issue: the capabilities row quoted the daily-maximum coverage (0.791, 0.779, 0.811) beside the daily-mean lower-bound counts. For the daily-maximum ages it is scored on, the counts are 87 of 92, 29 of 35 and 799 of 959, so that coverage is measured mostly on lower bounds. The journal row, the README's one-page row and the app's row now say so, with the converged-only coverage beside it in this journal.
- The free-chlorine settings' 0.60 of the posterior on their lowest rate is a 15-sample value (pooled over both sampling rules), but the app's sentence tied it to 8 random samples. The app, this journal and the README now say 'fitted to 15 samples', and the 8-sample false alarms have their own clause.
- The TOC adoption words now follow task 11's own adoption flag on both networks, not its first-storm bar alone (they read the same today).
- The temperature and organics rows name the assumptions behind their numbers (Built, above), here, in the README and in the app.
- The numbers scan's blanking patterns took the integer part of 11 decimals next to a month name, a word such as 'model', or a colon, so those numbers were never read (finding 3). They are guarded now, a blanked span that touches a digit fails the scan, and the scan's power on integers is measured beside its power on decimals.
- The registry now covers the numbers in the summary's list of regressions too (0.710, up to 53%, about 0.07 mg/L, 10 to 14%, 7 to 10%, 2 of 8 seeds, among others), and the README's full list, with a rule over a file where the text quotes a range or a share. It found one rounding: task 12's low-pH prior cost was quoted as 10 to 15%, but its largest excess rounds to 14%, so it now reads 10 to 14% in the task 12 section, the summary, the README and task 12's CHANGELOG entry.
- The README's list of regressions left out items that were only in this journal (seen-tap coverage under the pilot band, the 160 h cut, the 60 s MSX quality step and others). It now has every item of the summary's list, task by task, and its 'Unchanged' line says which default numbers were rerun, as do the summary's key-numbers row for task 14 and its closing line (ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun).
- The upload run now always builds its grid (above), and the EPANET-MSX failure is cached (above). The reduced truth's docstring said it ran EPANET in a temporary directory; it writes EPANET's temporary files to the app's working directory, as the free-chlorine demo truth does, and now says so.
- The names check's result goes to the run log only, so a full run without the private list no longer changes the committed report. `pypdf` is in `requirements.txt`, and without it the app check still requires the PDF and skips only its PDF-text assertions, which the run log records (`pdf_text_checked`), instead of failing.
- Smaller: the grid-edge warning no longer says the map 'tends to read low' at the floor as if that were established (it says today's map was biased low in those simulations, cause not settled); the table's note no longer names `outputs/chem_2ra`, which it does not read, and says every result number (not settings or bars) comes from `outputs/`; the app's docstring says chloramine mode asks for the water temperature for the nitrification watch; the PDF title has no em dashes.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- None in the default numbers. After the edits, the four committed reruns write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs (label `post_task14` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`; no module they import was edited), and the app's default demo is unchanged. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun.
- Behaviour changes outside the default numbers: the PDF header is taller and carries four more lines; the threshold inputs have help text; a grid-edge warning can appear; a failing MSX demo truth now falls back instead of stopping the app; the app builds grids for new networks instead of crashing (finding 1).
- Against the plan:
  - The app has no temperature or TOC input, no kb20 or kb(T) line and no outlook (addendum 2, the lead's brief). The plan's runs (3) and (5) were replaced.
  - The optional `temp_C` grab-log column was not added.
  - The journal keeps its 'Iteration 4, chemistry (tasks 8 to 14)' header; the plan's '(October 2026)' header was not added, because the numbers script and the README find the section by that name.
  - The plan asked that every digit be found in `outputs/`. Times, dates, citations, identifiers, run costs, code settings and one literature value are classified instead, and counted by the script.
  - The scan's power is low (finding 3), so it is not proof of provenance; the registry covers task 14's own text only (the capabilities table, the summary and the README's one-page section), not the task sections of tasks 8 to 13.
  - Two of the plan's doc audits were not automated: that the word 'simulated' appears with every chemistry claim (checked only for the app table's 'tested' cells, which must say simulation), and that no scratch probe number is quoted. The scratch figures the plan names were searched for by hand in the README, the CHANGELOG and the iteration-4 journal, and none is quoted; the scan cannot catch a scratch number that happens to equal some output value.
  - The plan's 'pilot protocol: a note on the named pilot target' became a note on chloraminated pilot candidates, without a name (decision 7).
- Two checks lean on things beyond the code: the PDF check reads the PDF with `pypdf` (added to `requirements.txt` after the review; it was only in `.venv`; without it only the PDF-text assertions are skipped, and the run log says so), and the names check needs the private CSV and is skipped without it. Its result is kept in the git-ignored run log (`outputs/chem/checks_run.json`), not in the committed report, so a full run with or without the CSV leaves the report unchanged; the run still fails if the names check fails.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.numbers` (about 2 s; `--list` prints every number that is not traced to a file, with its class), and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.checks`. The task-14 checks are part of the full run; `app_task14_runs` takes about a minute, and `no_listed_names_in_tracked_files` runs only with `RESIDUALMAP_NAMES_CSV` set.

### Capabilities after iteration 4

The manager's four points, answered line by line. Every number is simulated, from the file named, and nothing here has been validated on a real system.

| point | what the model does now | how it was tested (simulated) | the number | what it does not do |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| water age | accounts for it: EPANET moves the water through every pipe and tank of the operator's file hour by hour, so age is inside every prediction; since task 9 the app shows the age map with its range across demand and roughness errors, and splits each junction's chlorine loss between the water and the pipe walls (adopted) | against the simulated truth's own water age and loss split, Net3 and Net2 8 seeds, ky4 2 (task 9) | the range holds the true daily-maximum age at 0.791, 0.779 and 0.811 of junctions (Net3, Net2, ky4), so it is a range, not a 90% band; the file's daily-mean age is off by 5.82, 5.62 and 8.11 h RMSE; the calibrated wall share is off by 0.064 per junction on Net3 (`outputs/chem/water_age_<net>.json`) | the ages come from a 7-day run, so they are lower bounds wherever the run's starting water is still more than 5% of a junction's water. For the daily-maximum ages the coverage is scored on, that is at their oldest hour 87 of 92 junctions on Net3, 29 of 35 on Net2 and 799 of 959 on ky4 (on the day's average 19, 28 and 745), so the coverage is measured mostly on lower bounds; where neither the file's nor the truth's daily-maximum age is one, the range holds the truth at 0.975 on Net3 (40 junction-seeds), 0.500 on Net2 (48) and 0.870 on ky4 (262); a longer warm-up is not built |
| water temperature | absorbed into the calibrated decay rates, refitted from the last three months of samples, so the model follows the seasons with a lag; temperature is not an input | two temperature-aware models on 12 simulated months, fitted in one season and asked for another (tasks 10 and 10b), against an assumed coastal temperature schedule (10 to 20 C) and an assumed wall response in the truth: task 10's weak wall, an assumption, and in task 10b also a constructed world where the wall follows the water's temperature response | task 10: July-to-September map from January-to-March samples 0.137 against today's 0.101 mg/L on Net3 (`summary_season_Net3.json`); task 10b: held-out readings 0.774 to 0.916 of today's RMSE (the low end in the constructed world), but its stop rule triggered in 3 of 4 network and wall-response cases (`summary_season2_<net>.json`); tested, not adopted | the deciding unknown is how real pipe walls respond to temperature; a year of real grab samples with monthly plant temperatures would settle it |
| organics (TOC) that impart chlorine demand | absorbed into the calibrated bulk decay rate; TOC is not an input | a TOC-aware model on 12 simulated months at a fixed 20 C, against chlorine-organics chemistry first order cannot represent, with an assumed TOC schedule (the first storm is an assumed November doubling) and assumed Clark constants in the truth (task 11); today's model against two-reactant chlorine-organics chemistry, with the Greenvale constants from one water via a secondary source (task 13) | task 11, Net3: held-out readings 0.988 of today's RMSE, but the first-storm map 0.938 against a 0.85 bar, and the stop rule triggered (`summary_organics_Net3.json`); tested, not adopted. Task 13: within 10% of the first-order truth in 15 of 16 cells; Net3's oldest tenth of junctions 0.076 mg/L too low (`summary_audit_<net>.json`) | TOC is not an input; part of the TOC model's gain came from bulk rates below today's grid floor of 0.10 per day, and a grid with lower rates is a candidate default, not adopted |
| free chlorine or chloramine | accounts for it: two separate modes, never mixed; chloramine reads total chlorine with its own decay ranges, dose axis, likelihood scale, prior from the plant's pH and Cl2:N, a 0.5 mg/L default threshold and a nitrification watch (adopted as a separate mode, task 12) | against EPA's chloramine chemistry in EPANET-MSX with this task's own assumed wall term, Net3 and Net2, fresh seeds (task 12) | Net3, 8 straddle samples: recall 0.890 (97 of 109 low junction-days); random-sample 90% coverage 0.896 (Net3) and 0.887 (Net2); free-chlorine settings on the same network, fitted to 15 samples: 0.60 of the posterior on their lowest rate; at 8 random samples, 94 false alarms against the mode's 37 (`summary_chloramine_<net>.json`) | under the straddle rule its band is too narrow (0.726 at 8 samples, Net3); breakpoint where chloraminated and chlorinated water blend, and nitrification itself, are not modelled; no seasonal chloramine test |

Related chemistry, for completeness (no new numbers; the task sections hold them):

| item | status after iteration 4 |
|---|---|
| chlorine form (gas or hypochlorite) | not modelled separately: both give the same free chlorine (HOCl and OCl-), they differ mainly through pH, and the effect is absorbed into the calibrated rates (Liu, Reckhow and Li 2014 model pH explicitly) |
| old-pipe wall decay | accounts for it: the old-pipe factor on the grid, and since task 9 the loss split; tested against the simulated truth (task 9) |
| plant dose | accounts for it: a dose axis, exact for first order; task 13's dose step shows the shortcut's direction under two-reactant chemistry (conservative going up, optimistic going down) |
| nitrification | a watch list with literature thresholds in chloramine mode, not validated; not modelled as a process (task 12) |
| blending and breakpoint | not modelled |
| warming in the pipes | only inside task 10's simulated truth; the temperature models that would use it were not adopted |
| pH | free chlorine: not modelled, absorbed into the calibrated rates; chloramine: the prior on the bulk rate from the plant's logged pH and Cl2:N (task 12) |

### Iteration 4 summary

**What was asked.** A water-district general manager asked whether the model accounts for water age, water temperature, organics that impart chlorine demand, and the type of disinfectant. Before this iteration the honest answer was: water age yes, exactly, through EPANET, but not shown; temperature and organics only through decay rates refitted from the last three months of samples; free chlorine only. Iteration 4 (tasks 8 to 14) made each point explicit and tested it in simulation, against hidden chemistry the model does not assume, with bars written down before the scored runs.

**What was adopted.**
- Water age shown, with its range and with each junction's chlorine loss split between the water and the walls (task 9). The ages are marked as lower bounds where the 7-day run has not replaced its starting water.
- A separate chloramine mode (task 12). It met its pre-registered bars where they apply, so it is not labelled experimental. Its recorded weaknesses are in the list of regressions below.
- The chemistry plumbing and the saved checks (task 8).
- This task's table, PDF lines, numbers script and the app fix (task 14).

**What was tested and not adopted.**
- Temperature, two ways (task 10 assumed the wall's temperature response, task 10b learned it). Both stopped at their pre-registered stop rules. The deciding unknown is the real wall response. Today the model absorbs the seasons by refitting on the last three months.
- Organics as a logged TOC input (task 11). It stopped at its stop rule, and part of its gain came from the bulk-rate grid floor.
- The gated two-rate model of task 13, rejected for losing more than 10% on the first-order truth.
- The low-bulk-rate grid of task 13: it met its readings and is a candidate default, awaiting Devansh's decision.
- The two-reactant audit itself (task 13) changed nothing in the model: it measures what the first-order shortcut costs against chemistry it does not assume.
