**Regressions and missed bars, all of them in one list** (each task section has the detail):
- Task 9:
  - The water-age range is not a 90% band on any network, and its daily-maximum coverage is measured mostly on lower bounds (87 of 92, 29 of 35 and 799 of 959 junctions at their oldest hour).
  - The age weighted by the samples' posterior was worse than the file's own age on 2 of 8 seeds on Net3 and on Net2.
  - The calibrated wall share is biased low on Net2 and ky4, and was off by up to 0.265 in one Net2 scenario.
  - The plan's 160 h cut misses most unconverged ages, and the plan's loss-split formula was changed to a no-decay reference.
  - Every 7-day run carries a start-up transient (below).
- Task 10:
  - The stop rule triggered on both networks.
  - Bars missed: A4 on Net3; A2, A3 and A5 on Net2.
  - False alarms rose by more than 10% in every warming class with seasons in the truth, and far more out of season.
  - Seen-tap reading coverage is under the pilot band on both networks.
- Task 10b:
  - The stop rule triggered in 3 of the 4 network and world cases.
  - The robustness bar R1 was missed in every case.
  - Also missed: A2 on Net3 in both worlds, A4 on Net3 in the weak-wall world, A5 on Net2 in both worlds.
  - False alarms rose by more than 10% in every warming class and every out-of-season test, and seen-tap coverage is under the pilot band on Net2 in the steep-wall world.
  - On Net3, against the 15 C control, the wall posterior moved toward the weak wall but not toward the steep one.
- Task 11:
  - The stop rule triggered: one robust pair on Net3, three within count noise on Net2.
  - The first-storm bar was missed, and the December map was worse than today's.
  - The dose-axis bar failed on Net3, as it would for today's model.
  - Part of the gain came from the grid floor, not the TOC.
  - False alarms rose by more than 10% in the November and December classes, and the TOC model's seen-tap coverage is just under the pilot band on Net3 and under it on Net2.
- Task 12:
  - Under the straddle rule the chloramine band is too narrow.
  - At 15 straddle samples on Net3 the free-chlorine settings had the lower RMSE.
  - Net2's random-sample coverage is in its band only at 8 samples.
  - The prior used the simulated plant's exact pH and Cl2:N.
  - The maps are biased low.
  - The single-species MSX check met its bar only at a 60 s quality step.
  - At low pH the pH and Cl2:N prior made the mode 10 to 14% worse in RMSE than a uniform prior under the random rule.
  - First-order kinetics cost 7 to 10% of RMSE on Net2 against the mechanistic truth at every random sample size.
- Task 13:
  - The materiality rule fired on one cell inside seed noise.
  - The two-rate model was rejected.
  - Today's model doubled false alarms on Net3 and under-predicted Net3's oldest water past the bar, on the raw bias.
  - Net2's recall at 15 random samples was a measurable 0.936 times the first-order truth's.
  - The low-rate grid loses some recall on Net3's first-order truth, and its most probable bulk rate sits at its own new floor in up to 53% of Net2's fits.
  - Today's 90% band holds the oldest tenth's truth at 0.710 on Net3 under the two-reactant truth.
  - At 15 demo samples on Net3 today's map is biased low by about 0.07 mg/L at the nominal dose under both truths, mostly from two seeds (the dose step).
  - The seasonal part was not run, on this task's reading of addendum 2.
- Task 14:
  - The numbers scan's power is low, about one time in ten.
  - The plan's temperature and TOC inputs and its outlook were not built.
- Not one of these moved a default number that was rerun: every task ended with the four committed reruns (Net3, Net3 under the persistent stress test, Net2, the synthetic pilot) writing their files byte-identical to the committed outputs (`outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`). ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun.

**Open decisions for Devansh** (nothing below was done):
1. The low-bulk-rate default grid (task 13). It met its pre-registered readings, and would remove the 0.10 per day floor that today's fit sits on in most Net2 and second-order-truth fits. It trades some recall for fewer false alarms on Net3's first-order truth. Adopting it would move every committed number.
2. The 7-day warm-up transient (task 9). Every run starts the file's tanks and pipes with no chlorine and with water of age 0, so the ages at most Net2 and ky4 junctions are lower bounds, and absolute counts of low junctions there partly reflect the start-up. A longer warm-up, or tanks started at source quality, is the proposed fix. It would move every committed Net2 and ky4 number.
3. Real seasonal data. A year of grab samples with the plant's monthly water temperature and TOC is what would settle the wall's temperature response, which decided tasks 10 and 10b, and would replace the assumed schedules.

Also open, from task 13's list: a second-order grid with a simulated dose axis for the oldest water; where the low bias of today's map comes from; whether the materiality rule should need its interval to clear the bar; and whether a seasonal cost of first order for today's model alone is wanted.

## Iteration 5, inputs and access (tasks 15 onward)

Started 18 October 2026. Devansh asked for more ways to bring a utility's network and samples into ResidualMap, so that a town can start from the files it already has, and for a way to use it without installing Python. The plan, kept outside the repository with its research, splits the work into tasks 15 to 21: read any model file safely (15), make an imported model mean what the model assumes (15b), a grab-log upload and a Get started page (16), tap mapping by coordinates (16b), zone extracts (16c), GIS and CAD importers (17 and 18), an operator form with the measured cost of each missing input (19), a no-install route (20), and the input and output contract (21). The format is iteration 4's: Asked / Built / Found / Numbers / Regressions. Every number is read from a file under `outputs/` (new: `outputs/intake/`). Importer results are converter self-consistency on public models (WNTR's bundled networks, rewritten by our own writers), never a test on a utility's own files, and no real data was used.

**Sources.** Paper Lantern was not available in this session, so there is no Paper Lantern attribution. The file signatures follow the formats' public specifications (EPANET's binary output, the OLE compound file, SQLite and GeoPackage, the ESRI shapefile and dBASE table, DXF, the STEP header of IFC) and the iteration-5 research notes. The realistic quirks come from that research's review, which ran a first prototype on files as they really arrive and found most of them misread.

### Task 15, read any model file safely

**Asked:** (plan task 15, category a) identify an uploaded file by its content, not its name. That covers an EPANET model as modelling packages and Windows save it (a byte-order mark, comment lines first, UTF-16, a Windows code page), renamed or kept as a backup, or inside an emailed zip, and every other kind of file a utility might send (CAD drawings, MicroStation, BIM, vendor model databases, GIS layers, spreadsheets, a PDF map book, an EPANET results file), each with plain advice on what to send instead. Decode the text before WNTR sees it. Catch duplicate and over-long IDs in the text before WNTR parses, because WNTR keeps one of two duplicate lines without a word, loads a junction that shares a tank's ID and then fails in the simulator, and stops on a long ID with an AssertionError. Give a readiness report that says what came from the file, what was derived, what is assumed and what is missing. Read zips in memory only, with limits. In the app, an uploader that takes any file; in the pilot, a private, content-named copy of the model and a git-ignored results folder; Streamlit listening on this laptop only. Seven saved checks. Defaults byte-identical, no new package.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/importers.py`, new (the standard library and WNTR only):
  - `sniff(data, name)` reads the first 64 KB, and the whole file for zips, SQLite, OLE and JSON, all in memory; also the whole text when the first 64 KB starts like an EPANET file (a `[TITLE]`, a `[PATTERNS]` block or `;` comment lines) but holds none of its node or link sections. It returns a kind, a plain label, a detail, a confidence, whether the file name decided anything (`by_extension`), whether the name marks a backup copy, and advice. It never raises: a file whose reading fails comes back as 'unknown', with advice.
  - What it recognises: EPANET text by its section headers, whatever the line ends and in each header form WNTR reads (a singular name such as `[PIPE]`, a form feed before the bracket), after a byte-order mark, UTF-16, or leading ';' comment lines; a SWMM sewer or storm model and an EPANET-MSX reaction file, which share some EPANET headers, by their own sections, before the EPANET test; ASCII DXF by its group codes, after any 999 comment groups; binary DXF by its sentinel; DWG by its version code; DGN V7 by its header; IFC by its STEP header and schema; PDF; EPANET binary results by their magic number; OLE files by their stream names (Workbook or Book is a legacy Excel file, Dgn~ is DGN V8); Access databases (Jet or ACE); SQLite typed by the GeoPackage application id or a `gpkg_contents` table, otherwise 'a SQLite database that is not a GeoPackage', which is what a WaterGEMS or WaterCAD model database is; XML by its root element (KML, the WaterCAD or WaterGEMS `.wtg` settings file, GML, GPX); JSON by its keys (GeoJSON with its `crs` member and its coordinates tested against the lon/lat range, Esri JSON, WNTR's model JSON); zips by their member list without unpacking (a workbook, an EPANET model, KML, shapefile layers in nested folders, a file geodatabase, vendor model files), leaving out the files macOS and Windows add (`__MACOSX/`, `._` copies, `.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`); delimited text by `csv.Sniffer` on a bounded sample with its quote characters taken out, with a consistency test.
  - The content decides the kind. The file name either adds a note (a `.wtg.mdb` or `.wtg.sqlite` name says WaterGEMS and WaterCAD keep models in such files, at high confidence) or breaks a tie the content cannot settle (at low confidence). When it is used, `by_extension` is true and the detail ends '(from the file name)'. A `.bak` name is reported as 'a backup copy of' whatever the content is.
  - `decode_text`: a UTF-8 byte-order mark first, then a UTF-16 one, then UTF-16 without one (NUL bytes on every other position), then strict UTF-8, then cp1252, saying which. Any of the five bytes cp1252 leaves undefined is read as latin-1 and every other byte as cp1252 (the first version read the whole file as latin-1 then, which turned a euro sign or a curly quote into a control character).
  - `scan_inp_ids`: IDs in `[JUNCTIONS]`, `[RESERVOIRS]` and `[TANKS]`, and in `[PIPES]`, `[PUMPS]` and `[VALVES]`, read from the text. A duplicate within either group, or an ID longer than EPANET allows, blocks with 'Export the model with IDs, not labels'. The length is counted in bytes of the UTF-8 copy (EPANET's 31; WNTR counts characters), so an accented ID that fits in a Windows code page can be too long; pattern and curve IDs are held to it too. IDs that differ only by case are a warning. The text is read the way WNTR reads it: a singular header such as `[PIPE]` counts as `[PIPES]`, lines end at LF, CR or CR-LF only, and `[END]` ends the file.
  - `Readiness`: rows tagged 'from your file', 'derived', 'assumed' or 'missing, please supply', blocking problems, fixes, warnings and the run cost (EPANET runs per dose and the grid file's size), with `to_markdown()` and `to_json()`. Its summary for a model that passes says what was checked, 'WNTR reads it and EPANET runs its first hour without an error; the full runs come next', and an 'assumed' row says the physics settings (headloss formula, reaction orders, per-pipe and per-tank BULK lines, LIMITING POTENTIAL, START CLOCKTIME) are used as the file has them and not checked yet; a formula other than Hazen-Williams, or a reaction order other than 1, gets a warning that the decay rates do not mean what they say on that file. The checks themselves are task 15b.
  - `prepare_inp(src, out_dir, disinfectant)`: a clean UTF-8 file given as a path comes back as the same path, untouched; clean bytes are written as `<sha1 of the bytes>[:8]_<name>`, the app's upload name since iteration 3; a fixed file is written as `<sha1 of the new text>[:8]_<stem>.inp`; the original is never changed. A file that is not a model, or has a blocking problem, gives no path and leaves nothing behind: a new copy is written under a temporary name, WNTR reads it there (after the ID scan, never before), and it is moved to its name only when WNTR read it, the model has an entry point and EPANET ran its first hour (a test run set up as the app's first run, in a temporary folder that is removed). WNTR's message names the file, never its folder. An upload name too long once the digest is added (over 160 bytes) is shortened to the safe stem; one that fits is kept as it is. Nothing in a file makes `prepare_inp` raise: a failure after the file is read (a zip that breaks on reading, a copy that cannot be saved) is a blocking problem, with nothing kept.
  - Zips: read in memory only; a member with an absolute path or a '..' part is refused; the members may add up to 200 MB unpacked; the member that is read (the model) may not be packed more than 100 to 1 when it is over 10 MB unpacked. Members that are only listed are not held to the ratio, because a city's shapefile table packs past it (the first version applied it to every member, and so refused a city-size mains layer, and a model zipped with one). A member is read with a cap, so a zip that lies in its directory cannot fill memory; a member that cannot be read (a size that does not match, a bad checksum, encryption) refuses the zip instead of raising. An emailed zip that holds one model is read in memory and prepared; one that holds several is blocked.
  - `python -m residualmap.importers FILE` says what each file is and what to do; `--prepare` prints the readiness report.
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`, new: every test file, built from WNTR's bundled Net1 and Net3 and from byte literals for the header-only files (DWG, DGN V7, Jet, the EPANET results header). Nothing binary is committed. The writers share no code with the readers they test: the OLE compound file, the zips, the R12 DXF, the shapefile and dBASE table, the GeoPackage geometry, the PDF and the workbook are written by hand with the standard library. No fixture names a real utility, and the coordinates are invented. `--record` writes `outputs/intake/task15_import.json`; `--build DIR` writes every file.
- `app.py`:
  - the uploader takes any file (`type=None`) with the help 'any file: it is identified by its content'. Its label and the 'Upload my .inp' radio are unchanged, so the saved checks still find them.
  - an upload goes through `prepare_inp`, cached per file, name and disinfectant. A model shows a 'Model readiness report' expander at the top of the main area, opened when there is a fix, a warning or a blocking problem. A blocking problem shows an error and stops the page. A file that is not a model shows its advice and stops the page before any simulation, and nothing is written for it. Any error during an upload that `prepare_inp` did not turn into a plain problem is caught too, and shown as plain advice.
  - a clean UTF-8 `.inp` is stored exactly as before (`outputs/app_uploads/<sha1>[:8]_<name>`), so its grid's cache name does not change. No new main-area selectbox, uploader or download button.
- `residualmap/pilot.py`: `--inp` goes through `prepare_pilot_inp`, which runs `prepare_inp` and copies the prepared model to `outputs/private/inputs/<sha1 of the file>[:8]_<stem>.inp` (a model that needed a fix, or came in a zip, is already written there by `prepare_inp` under that kind of name and is not copied again). The name of a network bundled with WNTR (`--inp Net3`) runs by that name, as before task 15, with its report and no private copy. The default `--out` for a real run is `outputs/private/pilot`; `--synthetic` keeps `outputs/pilot`. A real run writes `import_report_<tag>.json` beside its results, prints the report, and stops on a blocking problem.
- `.gitignore` gains `outputs/private/`. `.streamlit/config.toml`, new: `server.address = "localhost"` and `browser.gatherUsageStats = false`.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: seven checks in a `task15` group (Numbers below), and `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text` now also covers the new files whole and the iteration-5 sections of this journal and the CHANGELOG.
- `residualmap/numbers.py`: scans the iteration-5 sections of this journal (they follow iteration 4's) and of the README, looks numbers up in `outputs/intake/` too, and has registry entries for this section's headline numbers.
- Docs: this section, code-map lines, bug-risk rows, how-to-run lines; the README's app section, limitations, run lines and layout; the CHANGELOG.

**Found** (converter self-consistency on public models):
1. The realistic files (`sniff_identifies_realistic_files`, `outputs/intake/task15_import.json`).
   - All 36 of the fixture table come back as their kind, across 25 kinds, and none is 'unknown'.
   - The file name decided something for 2 of them, the Access database named `.wtg.mdb` and the SQLite database named `.wtg.sqlite`: the content says what they are, and the name adds that WaterGEMS and WaterCAD use such files for the model. Both say so in their detail.
   - The 3 `.bak` files come back as backup copies of an EPANET model, a text DXF and a DWG.
   - Two OLE headers with no directory after them cannot be typed by their content. The extension breaks the tie (a legacy Excel file for `.xls`, DGN V8 for `.dgn`), with low confidence, and the detail says '(from the file name)'.
   - As a cross-check by hand, the review's own realistic files, as they arrived, and the prototype's samples all come back typed. The review's header-only `.xls` is typed by its extension, flagged as such.
2. What WNTR does with ID problems when nothing checks first (the control in `inp_duplicate_ids_block`):
   - a junction listed twice: it loads 92 junctions from 93 lines, keeping the later line, without a word;
   - a pipe listed twice: 117 pipes from 118 lines, without a word;
   - a junction that shares a tank's ID: it loads, and the simulator then raises AttributeError;
   - a 32-character ID: WNTR raises AssertionError.
   With the scan, all 4 block before WNTR is called (its model class and reader are traps during those calls, and are never touched), each with 'Export the model with IDs, not labels'. A 31-character ID runs.
3. Encodings (`inp_encodings_normalized`). WNTR reads UTF-8 only: on the files as they arrived it raises ENSyntaxError at line 1 for the byte-order mark, and UnicodeDecodeError for the cp1252 and the UTF-16 text. Each of the 3 prepared copies loads with Net3's nodes and links, and its report lists the fix. Net3 as WNTR ships it already has Windows (CRLF) line ends, and it, a CRLF copy and an LF copy pass through as the same path with the same bytes; nothing is written for them.
4. Zips (`zip_uploads_are_safe`). A member that climbs out of the zip (`../../evil.inp`), one that starts at the root (`/tmp/evil.inp`) and a model of 21 MB packed 1029 to 1 are refused before any member is opened (zipfile's open is counted: 0 times): the first two at the listing, the third when the model is about to be read. A zipped shapefile whose padded table packs 155.4 to 1 but unpacks to 0.894 MB passes: that is why the ratio rule starts at 10 MB. The file counts of the checks' scratch folder and of `outputs/` do not change, and the only files written are the prepared copies of the zipped models (the emailed one, and two of the review's cases in item 9).
5. The stale-grid bug in the pilot (`pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private`). The grid cache is keyed by the model's file name, so before this task a revised model sent again as `zone.inp` was served the first file's grid. Two different models both named `zone.inp` now get two private copies and two grids, whose values differ.
6. Where Streamlit listens, measured on this Mac. A small Streamlit page started from a folder with no config file listened on every network interface and printed a Network URL and an External URL besides the local one. The app, with `.streamlit/config.toml`, listened on 127.0.0.1 only and printed one localhost URL. The file is read when a server starts: a server started before it existed keeps listening on every interface until it is restarted, and one such server, not started by this task, was still running on this Mac during the review; it was left for Devansh to restart. Streamlit reads the file next to `app.py` as well as in the working folder, so it applies however the app is started (`streamlit_config_private` checks this with Streamlit's own config loader, started from another folder). FileVault is on (`fdesetup status`), so the uploads, prepared copies and private results are encrypted at rest.
7. One real-browser upload of a `.bak`, because AppTest's `set_value` bypasses the browser. Headless Chromium through Playwright (already in `.venv` for the walkthrough screenshots; not a project dependency, and the browser build already on this Mac, because the newer one Playwright asks for would need a download) opened the local app, chose 'Upload my .inp' and set `net3_backup.inp.bak` on the page's own file input. It was done again after the review's fixes, on the final code. The input carries no accept attribute and nothing was refused; the readiness report said 'Reads and runs' (the wording then; item 10 changed it to say what was checked), called the file an EPANET model identified by its content whose name marks it as a backup copy, and listed the physics settings as not checked yet; the map flagged 42 of 92 junctions, the Net3 default. The server listened on 127.0.0.1 only. The run's copy and grid were deleted afterwards.
8. Names. The names check passes with the outreach list plus the large-utility contact's organisation, put together in a private CSV outside the repository (the outreach list itself was not edited). Adding the named target pilot, which the outreach list leaves out on purpose, flags the one line of iteration 3's prompt (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`) that task 14 found and left for Devansh.
9. The review (three reviewers, after the first build). Every issue below was reproduced first, then fixed, and each case is now a fixture in the check it belongs to (the `REVIEW_*` tables in `fixtures.py`, added after the pre-registered tables, which did not change; `outputs/intake/task15_import.json`, the `review` entries of each part):
   - A run of XML comments with no element after it made the root-element pattern backtrack exponentially: on the review's file of 26 comments it ran for seconds, the time doubling with each extra comment, and the matcher holds the interpreter lock, so every app session froze while it ran. The root is now found by a linear scan. The 26-comment file, a 6,000-comment file and a run of processing instructions each finish within the 2 s bound.
   - Two more inputs had no time limit. `csv.Sniffer` is quadratic in its sample (a quote-heavy text of two 30 KB lines took seconds); it now sees the first 4 KB, and the consistency test reads the rest (not enough on its own: item 10). A 4 KB GeoPackage whose `gpkg_contents` is a view over a recursive count would have run for hours; only a plain `gpkg_contents` table is read now, 50 rows at most, and every SQLite query runs under a step budget. Both are timed cases in the sniff check.
   - Malformed JSON raised instead of giving advice (a `crs` member of the wrong shape, `features` that is not a list, an Esri spatial reference written as a bare code, geometry nested 3,000 deep, a list nested 100,000 deep): each member is now type-checked, the coordinate walk is iterative with a depth cap, and `sniff` catches any error as 'unknown'. All come back typed, none raises.
   - A model zipped by Finder's Compress was blocked as two models, because the `._` copy under `__MACOSX/` ends in `.inp` too, and a Mac-zipped shapefile was listed as two layers. The files macOS and Windows add are left out now, and both zips hold one model or one layer.
   - The ratio rule refused a city's mains layer: 15,000 records with three blank 254-character fields unpack to 11.595 MB at 159.4 to 1, and a zip of the model with that layer was refused whole. The rule now applies to the member that is read, so the layer is listed and the model runs.
   - A SWMM sewer model and an EPANET-MSX reaction file were typed as EPANET models (both use `[JUNCTIONS]`, `[PIPES]` or `[TANKS]`), and the MSX file was then blocked with 'export with IDs, not labels'. Both have their own kind and advice now.
   - Valid models were called 'not a model': CR-only and CR-CR-LF line ends, a `[TITLE]` past 64 KB, a large `[PATTERNS]` block first and a 70 KB comment header. All 5 are typed by content now and pass through untouched; WNTR reads each as Net3.
   - A 31-character ID with two accented letters, saved in cp1252, passed the scan and then made EPANET fail (33 bytes in the UTF-8 copy); a 33-character pattern ID reached WNTR's AssertionError. Both block before WNTR now, with the plain message.
   - A model blocked after the ID scan (WNTR could not read it, or no entry point) left its prepared copy behind, although the docs said nothing was written. The copy is now written under a temporary name and kept only when the model is accepted; the check's case (a byte-order mark and a pipe to a missing node) leaves nothing.
   - The pilot: `--inp Net3` (a bundled network's name) had stopped working, because the name was opened as a path; it runs by name again: on the example grab log its results are byte-identical to the committed `outputs/pilot/validation_Net3.json` and `validation_Net3.csv` (rmse 0.077 and coverage90 0.923), as before task 15, and since the final verification `pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private` compares them. A zipped `--inp` was copied twice; once now.
   - Wording: the readiness report said 'Ready to run' with no word that the physics settings are unchecked; the File row said 'identified by its content' for a backup copy whose name, not content, says so; the emailed zip's fix said nothing was unpacked to disk just before the copy was saved; the coordinates row counted lines, not nodes; the InfoWater menu path came from a third-party guide and is no longer given; the cp1252 description did not match the code; the uploader check recorded two values as constants, now measured; the pilot protocol still named `outputs/pilot` for a real run's metrics. All corrected.
   - Found in passing and not part of task 15: uploading Net1 (9 junctions) stops the app in the route planner, at HEAD too, because the route asks for more sites than there are free junctions. It is left for a separate fix.

10. The final verification (three verifiers, after the review's fixes; converter self-consistency on public models, as above). Each issue was reproduced on the tree the review left, then fixed, and each case is a fixture in the check it belongs to (new rows in the `REVIEW_*` tables, new `review_*` entries in `outputs/intake/task15_import.json`):
   - The summary 'Reads and runs' claimed more than was checked. `prepare_inp` never ran EPANET: Net3 with one junction that no pipe connects (common in an exported zone model) read fine, got 'Reads and runs', and then stopped the app with EPANET's Error 200 as a traceback. The app before task 15 crashed the same way, without a report saying the model runs. `prepare_inp` now runs EPANET on the model's first hour, set up as the app's first run, in a temporary folder; a failure is a blocking problem that names any node no pipe, pump or valve connects. The summary says what was checked ('WNTR reads it and EPANET runs its first hour without an error; the full runs come next'), with a 'Test run' row. The case blocks given as a path and as bytes, and leaves nothing either way.
   - A clean model uploaded under a 250-character name (legal on APFS) was accepted, then raised OSError ('File name too long') at the rename and left its temporary copy behind: a traceback in the app. A prepared name over 160 bytes is now shortened to the safe stem (the case gives a 93-byte name, one file, no exception), a failed save removes the temporary copy and is a blocking problem, and the app has a last guard that turns any other error during an upload into plain advice. A name that fits is kept, so the app's upload and grid cache names do not change.
   - A zip whose end record puts its members at negative offsets lists fine, and reading its model raised ValueError ('negative seek value') out of `prepare_inp` (found by fuzzing; zipfile alone raises the same, the control). Any failure while a zip is listed or read now refuses the zip, with nothing written.
   - `csv.Sniffer` was still super-linear inside the 4 KB sample: a 263-byte text with a line of 250 quote marks took seconds, holding the interpreter lock, so every app session waited. Sniffer now gets the sample with its quote characters taken out, which leaves it only its linear count of each delimiter; the consistency test, with csv.reader's quoting, still decides. Both quote-heavy texts are timed against their own bound of 0.25 s, because the first one's slow version took 2 to 3 s and could pass the general 2 s bound on a fast machine.
   - Valid models that WNTR runs were refused as 'plain text with no EPANET sections': singular core headers (`[JUNCTION]`, `[PIPE]`, `[TANK]`, `[RESERVOIR]`, which WNTR's reader takes for the plural sections) and a form feed before a header. The sniff now matches header names the way WNTR does, after any whitespace. The same gap let a duplicated pipe under `[PIPE]` through the ID scan to 'Reads and runs' while WNTR kept the later line without a word; the scan now reads names, line ends and `[END]` as WNTR does, and blocks it.
   - Found in the verifiers' probe of variants, not in their list, and fixed because it was cheap: Net1 padded with 200 NUL bytes after its `[END]` was typed as binary, although WNTR runs it. Trailing NUL bytes no longer count in the binary test, and the file passes through and reads as Net1.
   - The pilot claim in item 9 had no citation; it now names the committed files, and a saved check compares them byte for byte.
   - After the fixes, the verifiers' fuzzing (mutated files behind every signature, two seeds) gives no exception, no case over 2 s and nothing left behind, and their probes of the variants give no exception and leave nothing behind. Noted there, not fixed: a large SWMM model whose SWMM-only sections all come after the first 64 KB is typed as EPANET and then blocked with WNTR's syntax error rather than the SWMM advice; a zip that holds a model renamed `.txt` is not recognised as holding a model; a UTF-8 file with one stray cp1252 byte is decoded as cp1252, so its accented IDs change and it is blocked with EPANET's error rather than a plain word on the mixed encoding.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- None in the default numbers. After the edits the four committed reruns write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs (label `post_task15` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`), and the app's default demo is unchanged. That first rerun started before the last edits to the task's modules, so it was repeated on the final tree, after the review's fixes (label `post_task15_review`), with the same result. The example networks never go through `prepare_inp`, and nothing new runs inside `simulate.load`. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun.
- Behaviour changes outside the default numbers:
  - the uploader takes any file; a file that is not a model stops the page with advice instead of WNTR's error;
  - a model that needs a fix runs from a copy whose name differs from the upload, so its grid is built under the copy's name;
  - every uploaded model is parsed once more by WNTR, for the report (cached per file in the app);
  - the pilot's default `--out` for real data moved from `outputs/pilot` to `outputs/private/pilot`, its result names use a stem made safe for a file name (a space becomes an underscore), and a real run prints the readiness report first and stops on a blocking problem;
  - after the review: the readiness summary for a model that passes is 'Reads and runs' (it was 'Ready to run'), with an 'assumed' row for the unchecked physics settings and warnings for a headloss formula other than Hazen-Williams or a reaction order other than 1; the prepared copies are written through a temporary file, so they get owner-only permissions (before, the app's uploads got the default ones); a text whose first 64 KB starts like an EPANET file without its node or link sections is searched whole, up to 50 MB; `csv.Sniffer` sees the first 4 KB only.
- After the final verification: every prepared model gets one more EPANET run, of its first hour, in a temporary folder; a model that WNTR reads but EPANET cannot run is blocked at the readiness report instead of failing later in the app; the summary for a model that passes says 'WNTR reads it and EPANET runs its first hour without an error; the full runs come next', with a 'Test run' row; an upload name over 160 bytes gives a shortened prepared name (and so a different grid cache name); `csv.Sniffer` gets its sample without quote characters; singular core headers and a form feed before a header are read as EPANET sections; the ID scan stops at `[END]`, as WNTR does, so an ID after `[END]` is no longer checked (WNTR never reads it); a small text padded with NUL bytes at its end is no longer called binary.
- Broken by the first version of this task and fixed after the review: `--inp` with a bundled network's name (`--inp Net3`) stopped with FileNotFoundError, and the valid models of item 9 (CR-only line ends, sections after 64 KB) were refused as 'not a model', although the app before task 15 ran them. Broken until the final verification and fixed then: models with singular core headers, a form feed before a header, or NUL padding after `[END]` were refused the same way, although the app before task 15 ran them. The long upload name and the isolated junction also crashed the app before task 15; the zip with broken offsets could not be uploaded then.
- Against the plan:
  - The drawing, GIS and vendor-model answers are identification and advice only; nothing reads those files yet (tasks 17 and 18). The advice says a skeleton model from a drawing or a GIS layer is planned, not built.
  - The header-only fixtures (DWG, DGN V7, the Access database, the EPANET results file) test the signature logic, not real files. The DGN V8 stream names (Dgn~) follow the plan's research; no real DGN V8 file was at hand to confirm them.
  - The review's own files were checked by hand, once; they live in a scratch folder, so no saved check reads them.
  - The plan's emailed zip is identified, and its single model is also read and prepared; a zip with several models is blocked. That reading is a small step past the plan's 'identify'.
  - `prepare_inp` loads the prepared file with WNTR for the report's counts, after the ID scan, and since the final verification runs EPANET on its first hour. The plan did not ask for either; they cost one parse and one short run per upload.
  - The plan says delimited text is typed 'by csv.Sniffer'. Sniffer still runs, but on a sample with its quote characters taken out, so it only orders the delimiters tried; the consistency test with csv.reader decides.

  - The two readiness warnings (headloss formula, reaction orders) read values WNTR already parsed; they are a small step into task 15b, which converts or blocks.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.importers <file>` (add `--prepare` for the readiness report), `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.fixtures --record`, and `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.checks`. The task-15 checks are part of the full run; `uploader_accepts_any_type` and `pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 15b, make an imported model mean what the model assumes

**Asked:** (plan task 15b, category a) make `prepare_inp` check the physics settings that WNTR reads without a word and that the decay model cannot use as they are, and turn each into a fix in the copy, a blocking problem or a warning, each a row of the readiness report: reaction orders other than 1; per-pipe and per-tank BULK lines and a LIMITING POTENTIAL; the Darcy-Weisbach headloss formula, converted to Hazen-Williams behind a pre-registered water-age gate (Chezy-Manning always blocks); the START CLOCKTIME and weekly patterns; pressure-control valves with made-up elevations; whether the coordinates are to scale; sources and entry points; pumps without controls, missing patterns, zero demand, disconnected parts and export connectors; one C value on every pipe, where the old-pipe factor cannot be learned (a new 225-run grid); and the run cost with a size gate. Ten saved checks, two new committed records under `outputs/intake/`, defaults byte-identical. Lead decisions for this task: the Darcy-Weisbach gate's bars as proposed (a median water-age change of at most 1 h and a 95th percentile of at most 4 h); boosters already in a file get a warning only; a Net1 upload must run end to end in the app (the route planner stopped it in task 15); task 15's leftovers fixed where cheap.

**Built** (its numbers are converter self-consistency on public models):
- `residualmap/importers.py`:
  - `fix_reactions_text` edits the text of the copy and nothing else: an ORDER other than 1 (bulk, wall or tank) becomes 1, per-pipe BULK and per-tank TANK lines become comments, a LIMITING POTENTIAL other than 0 becomes 0, each with a `;ResidualMap:` comment giving what the file had. Per-pipe WALL lines and a ROUGHNESS CORRELATION are reported as ignored, because the grid sets every pipe's wall rate itself.
  - `dw_to_hw` converts a Darcy-Weisbach model: each pipe's C is the one that gives its Darcy-Weisbach headloss at its reference velocity (the median of its absolute velocity over the file's own scored day, at least 0.3 m/s), with the plan's formula, Swamee-Jain's friction factor and the kinematic viscosity from the file's VISCOSITY option (EPANET's 1.1e-5 ft2/s times it). The converted copy is run too, and water age is compared at every junction-hour of the scored day; the gate passes when the median change is at most 1 h and the 95th percentile at most 4 h, bars fixed before any gate run. Chezy-Manning blocks.
  - `_physics_report` adds rows and warnings: the start clock in hours (recorded, the file not changed; since the review a start other than midnight also warns that the app's and the pilot's hours are model hours), weekly patterns (the weekday the scored day falls on is asked for) and patterns longer than a day; with a PRV, PSV or PBV every junction at elevation 0 is flagged, and one elevation everywhere blocks with 'Elevations needed at these valves', quoting `outputs/intake/elevation_prv_ky10.json`; the coordinate scale from drawn over declared pipe length (schematic above a spread of 1.5, else feet or metres from the median); sources at entry points (overridden) and elsewhere (boosters), more than one inlet; pumps with no control, rule action or speed pattern, no patterns, zero demand, disconnected parts (networkx), and pipes of 1 ft and 99 in or elements tagged AddedForINPConversion.
  - The old-pipe factor: one roughness value on every pipe, read from the file before any conversion, sends the file to `full_fixed_gamma` (free chlorine) and the row says the factor is not identifiable.
  - `run_cost` gives the grid's runs, its cache file (64.8 KB per junction for free chlorine and 138.2 KB for chloramine, per dose), the temporary EPANET files of one run per worker, and a time estimate (`estimate_seconds`: one timed 7-day grid member times the runs, over an assumed effective speedup of 4). More than 3,000 junctions is a hard warning that needs an explicit confirm, and `size_gate` refuses a grid that would leave less than 1.5 GB free.
  - `control_scan` reads what the controls and rules set and read; `coordinate_scale`, `grid_for`, `size_gate` and `disk_free_gb` are small helpers the checks call directly.
  - Task 15's four leftovers: a SWMM model whose SWMM-only sections come after 64 KB is typed as SWMM; a zip holding a model renamed `.txt` is typed by its content; UTF-8 with a stray cp1252 byte keeps its UTF-8 characters (`_mixed_utf8`) and warns; and a model blocked now leaves no content-named copy that an earlier version accepted.
- `residualmap/simgp.py`: the grid `full_fixed_gamma`, the `full` grid's gamma 0 slice (225 runs) under its own cache name. `COMMITTED_GRID_NAMES` is unchanged.
- `residualmap/route.py`: a route never asks for more sites than there are junctions left (Net1's 9 junctions with the app's 8 demo samples stopped the app with 'attempt to get argmax of an empty sequence'); an empty route keeps its columns.
- `app.py`: the 'Build the grid anyway' checkbox in the readiness report for a large model, the disk gate before a grid build, the fixed-gamma grid, 'old-pipe factor: not identifiable from one C value' on the page and in the PDF, the demo's sample count capped at the junction count, and a placeholder row in the PDF for an empty route.
- `residualmap/pilot.py`: `--allow-large`, the disk gate, and the fixed-gamma grid for a uniform-C file; a bundled `--inp` name that needs a fix (ky10, Net6) runs from the fixed copy.
- `residualmap/intake_exp.py`, new: writes `outputs/intake/readiness_bundled.json` (the reports of WNTR's six bundled networks, run times and the worker count left out), `elevation_prv_ky10.json` and `task15b_physics.json` (each check's result, part by part), and, timed and not part of any check, `run_cost_timing.json`.
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: 19 fixtures for this task (the physics cases, task 15's leftovers and the review's three), and 10 more for the second review's cases (item 14) with its encoding fuzz, built from WNTR's bundled networks by the fixtures' own writers, among them `net3_dw.inp`, Net3 written in Darcy-Weisbach by the inverse of the stated formula at Net3's own reference velocities, so the converter must give Net3's C back.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: the `task15b` group of 13 checks, the plan's 10 plus `task15_leftovers_fixed`, `route_small_network` and `app_small_network_upload`, and since the second review a 14th, `numbers_scan_settles`, with the full run's `settle_numbers_entry`. Two task-15 assertions now look for the new rows, with their committed details unchanged.
- `residualmap/numbers.py`: registry entries for this section's headline numbers.

**Found** (converter self-consistency on public models):
1. Reaction orders (`inp_reaction_orders_fixed`). ky10 declares order 0 for bulk, wall and tank, and then the bulk rate has no effect: its grid member's mean residual is 0.5748 mg/L at a bulk rate of 0.2 and of 0.8 per day. The prepared copy, with order 1, gives 0.5714 and 0.4561. Net6 declares 0 too and gets the same fix; with 3,323 junctions it also gets the large-model warning. Both now run from a fixed copy, so their grids are cached under the copy's name.
2. Per-pipe bulk lines (`inp_bulk_lines_cleared`). Net3 with BULK lines on 10 pipes, a TANK line on one tank and a LIMITING POTENTIAL of 0.5: the prepared copy's grid member at kb 0.40, kw 0.70 and gamma 1 equals Net3's at all 2208 junction-hours (largest difference 0.0); the file as it came differs by up to 0.1909 mg/L.
3. Headloss (`inp_headloss_formula`). On `net3_dw.inp` the gate passes with a median water-age change of 0.0447 h and a 95th percentile of 1.1313 h. The 114 pipes a positive roughness can match all recover their C within 0.5 (largest error 0.331). Net3's 3 pipes at C 199 cannot be matched by any positive roughness at their reference velocity, so the fixture gives them a tiny roughness; they come back at 153.76, 45.24 below, reported and not scored. `net3_cm.inp` is blocked. The review's case, Net3 in Darcy-Weisbach with one roughness of 0.26 mm on every pipe, converts to 68 distinct C values (the gate passes, 0.0641 h and 2.4698 h); before the review those would have been read as pipe ages, and now the file's one roughness sends it to the fixed-gamma grid.
4. Start clock and pattern length (`inp_clock_and_weekly`). Net2 starts at 8 h and its own source at node 1 is overridden by the dose; `net3_clock6.inp` starts at 6 h; a weekly pattern gets the weekday question. The warning on patterns longer than a day found something about the committed Net2 example: its three demand patterns are 55 h long, with a pattern step of 1 h (`outputs/intake/readiness_bundled.json`). EPANET repeats each over its own length, so Net2's scored day, hours 144 to 167 of the 7-day run, is pattern hours 34 to 54 and then 0 to 2, not one daily cycle, and every Net2 statement by hour of day (the README's worst hours, 04:00 to 08:00 and 18:00) is in model hours of that 55 h cycle, which starts at 08:00. No committed number changes; a caveat now sits next to the README's Net2 paragraph. Converting hours to clock time is task 16, with lead decision 2's caption.
5. Elevations (`inp_prv_elevations`). With every ky10 junction at the mean elevation, water age moves by more than 2 h at some hour of the scored day at 90 of 920 junctions, by up to 47.41 h (`outputs/intake/elevation_prv_ky10.json`); the review probe had seen the same. ky10's one junction at elevation 0 (J-16) and Net6's three are flagged; `ky10_const_elev.inp` is blocked and quotes that file; Net3, with 8 junctions at 0 and no pressure-control valve, is not flagged.
6. Coordinates (`inp_coordinate_scale`). Net3, Net2 and Net6 are schematic (spreads 6.71, 2.33 and 13.03); ky4 and ky10 are to scale in feet (spread 1.01, median ratios 3.2809 and 3.2808); a file without coordinates says so, and since WNTR then puts every node at one point, the row says the maps will show them there.
7. Structure (`inp_controls_and_structure`). Net3 with its controls removed is flagged, and with speed patterns instead it is not; zero demand and a second part no pipe joins are flagged; a model with no entry point is blocked; a WaterGEMS-like export's 2 connector pipes and 2 tags are reported. On the bundled networks the pump warning fires on ky4 (1 of 2 pumps), ky10 (10 of 13) and Net6 (1 of 61); Net3 and ky10 get the two-inlet warning.
8. One C value (`uniform_c_fixes_gamma`). Net1, C 100 on every pipe, gets `full_fixed_gamma`; its 225 members are each bit-identical to the matching gamma 0 member of Net1's `full` grid. The committed networks have more than one C value (Net3 5, Net2 2, ky4 2), so no default moves. A Net1 upload now runs end to end in the app (`app_small_network_upload`): 8 of 9 junctions flagged, a route of 1 site, the PDF made, the old-pipe factor shown as not identifiable.
9. Size gate (`run_cost_gate`). Net6 gets the hard warning; a 10,000-junction chloramine grid (1382.4 MB, plus 1089.7 MB of temporary files on 11 workers) is refused with 3 GB free and accepted with 6 GB. One run's EPANET files measure 9.5 MB on ky4, 9.2 MB on ky10 and 31.6 MB on Net6 (`outputs/intake/task15b_physics.json`), which is what the temporary-file term scales.
10. **The run cost's time estimate missed its pre-registered bar.** The bar: Net3's estimate within a factor of 2 of the fresh grid time. In the scored run the estimate was 5.40 s (one run of 0.032 s, times 675, over 4) and the fresh grid took 11.34 s, a ratio of 2.10 (`outputs/intake/run_cost_timing.json`, `scored_run`). The task stopped there and was not tuned. Measured afterwards, as characterizations and not rescorings (same file): the review's full run gave 1.783; five repeats on the final tree gave 1.437 to 1.838, an effective speedup of 2.18 to 2.78 on Net3; ky10's grid took 194.73 s against an estimate of 303.92 s, a ratio of 0.641 and a speedup of 6.24. So on an unchanged tree Net3's ratio falls on either side of the bar, and the plan's speedup of 4 sits between a small model's, where starting the workers takes most of the time, and a large one's. Decision taken at the review, open to Devansh: the miss stands and is recorded here; the formula is not restated, because restating it after seeing these timings would be tuning (a restated estimate, for example with a start-up term, needs its own pre-registered bar and one scored run); the saved check asserts only what does not depend on timing (that the product reports the stated formula from the unrounded run time, Net6's hard warning, the mocked disk gate and the temporary files), and each run's ratio goes to the git-ignored run log only, where a ratio inside the bar does not count as a pass. The readiness row no longer says the runs go 4 times faster: it says the speedup is assumed and grows with the model's size.
11. The bundled networks (`readiness_bundled_reproduces`): all six are accepted. ky10 and Net6 get one fix each (the reaction orders); Net2 gets 3 warnings (the start clock, its 55 h patterns, its own source), Net3 1 (two inlets), Net6 3 and ky10 3. The app never prepares the bundled examples, so this is a record, not a step of any run.
12. Task 15's leftovers (`task15_leftovers_fixed`), each reproduced on task 15's code first: the SWMM model is typed as SWMM with its advice (its first 64 KB alone still reads as EPANET, the control); the zipped `.txt` model is prepared as Net3; the UTF-8 file with one stray byte keeps its four accented IDs and warns (read as cp1252 throughout, WNTR raises UnboundLocalError, the control); a stale copy is removed and an unrelated file kept.
13. The review (after the first build). Each issue was checked first, then fixed or answered:
    - The mixed-encoding test misread a file in cp1252 throughout, which task 15 decoded right, whenever an accented capital is followed by a cp1252 quote, dash, degree sign or no-break space: each such pair is valid two-byte UTF-8, so 'CITÉ’15' loaded as another ID. The test then needed at least as many valid UTF-8 characters as stray bytes; the review's file (4 pairs, 9 stray bytes) decoded as cp1252 again with its IDs kept, and it is a second control in `task15_leftovers_fixed`. That count was replaced after the second review (item 14).
    - The mocked disk gate used this machine's worker count, so on a 2-core machine the 3 GB case was accepted; it now uses 11 workers, recorded.
    - A pump that a rule only reads ('IF PUMP 10 STATUS IS OPEN THEN PIPE 20 STATUS IS OPEN') counted as controlled, so a pump closed for 7 days got no warning; only a rule's actions set a link now (a new fixture).
    - In chloramine mode a uniform-C file's row said the factor 'is held at 0', while the 1440-run chloramine grid still varies it; the row now says so, and no chloramine fixed-gamma grid exists yet (Later).
    - A Darcy-Weisbach file's old-pipe row was tagged 'from your file' for C values the conversion made, and the uniform test ran on them (item 3).
    - The Net2 finding of item 4, stated here and in the README rather than left in a JSON file.
    - The start time printed as '05:60' within 30 s of an hour; it is rounded once now.
    - Wording: the coordinate rows described tap mapping as refused (it does not exist yet, task 16b); the Elevations rows said elevations never change the flows without valves (tank elevations do) and counted 'junctions downstream' that were the valves' two end nodes; a blocked report listed 'fixes applied to a copy' with no copy kept; the booster rows said both 'kept' and 'left out'; Net6's one pipe of 1 ft and 99 in was presented as a WaterGEMS export (it is counted now, and reported only with a tag or three such pipes).
    - The two constants the code called measured are now cited: the speedup of 4 is the plan's assumption (its feasibility review measured 2.3 and 4.9, copied since the second review into `run_cost_timing.json`, `feasibility_review`), and the temporary-file sizes are in the record.
    - The implementer's timing summary left out two standalone builds as slow as the check run's (9.35 to 11.31 s across both build logs, kept outside the repository and copied since the second review into `run_cost_timing.json`, `standalone_builds`), and quoted repeats it had not saved; the timings above are only those saved in `outputs/intake/run_cost_timing.json`.
14. The second review (after the fixes). Each issue was reproduced first, then fixed, and each case is now in a saved check:
    - The mixed-encoding count of item 13 counted occurrences, and an ID is written on its own line, at each pipe end and in the coordinates. So one ID with a pair that is valid UTF-8 by chance outweighed the stray bytes of a file in cp1252 throughout: Net3 with 'CITÉ’15' and a degree sign in the title (3 valid characters against 1 stray byte) loaded the ID as another one. `_mixed_utf8` now weighs each valid UTF-8 character by what it would be. A pair that a cp1252 file makes by chance (an accented capital before a quote, a dash, a degree sign or a no-break space, which in UTF-8 is a C1 control, a Latin Extended-B, IPA or combining character, or a lone Cyrillic to NKo letter) is evidence for cp1252, and in a mixed file it is read as cp1252 too. A lowercase accented letter, a curly quote or dash, or the euro sign settles UTF-8, because in cp1252 those bytes are an 'Ã' or 'â€' before a symbol, which no file writes. Otherwise lines are counted, not occurrences. On the review's fuzz (seed 11: 150 files in cp1252 throughout, 150 in UTF-8 with stray bytes) no ID changes now, against 1 and 12 with item 13's count and 0 and 150 with task 15's decoding (`task15b_physics.json`, part `leftovers`, `encoding_fuzz`). Six controls on both sides of the old count are in `task15_leftovers_fixed`, the review's one-ID file among them. Left as at task 15: a file in cp1252 throughout whose only non-ASCII bytes are chance pairs is valid UTF-8 and is read as UTF-8 (the review's case B), because changing strict UTF-8 would misread UTF-8 files with IPA letters, such as names written with ɛ or ɔ.
    - The committed checks report was not stable. The numbers scan reads that report as a source, every entry but its own, so the run that added task 15b's entries committed a scan of the previous run's report, and the next run on the same tree rewrote it. The full run now writes the report with its pass counts up to date (they are numbers in the pool too), rescans it and puts that result in the entry before the final write, repeating while the entry changes (`checks.settle_numbers_entry`); since the scan leaves its own entry out, the first pass settles it unless the entry's own pass or fail moves the counts. The first version updated the counts after the scan, and the full run caught it: its new check failed once the journal quoted the new count. Fixed, and the full run repeated. `numbers_scan_settles` reproduces the case on a copy of the report with a probe entry. Leaving the report out of the scan instead would leave three iteration-4 numbers that only the report holds unexplained.
    - The start clock row and the model-hours warning went by the unrounded start, so a start within 30 s of midnight was shown as nearly 24 h, at 00:00, with a warning that the model starts at 00:00, 'not midnight'. Both go by the rounded minute now: 23:59:50 and 0:00:20 are '0 h (00:00)' with no warning, and the offset the warning quotes is the rounded one (`inp_clock_and_weekly`).
    - The uniform test rounded roughness in metres to 6 decimals, so two Darcy-Weisbach roughness values of plastic pipe, 0.0015 mm and 0.0024 mm, were one value, and the file got the fixed-gamma grid with a row naming a roughness no pipe has. It compares 6 significant digits now (`net3_dw_plastic.inp` in `inp_headloss_formula`: 2 values, the `full` grid).
    - The uniform test counted an export's connector pipes, which the same report recognises: Net1 written as an export, with a 1 ft connector of C 130 at its pump, got the `full` grid and 'learned from your file's 2 C values'. Recognised connectors (tagged AddedForINPConversion, or of 1 ft and 99 in once the Export connectors row is shown) are left out of the test and of the count now, and the row says so (`net1_export_like.inp` in `uniform_c_fixes_gamma`).
    - Four numbers in item 13 came from the private plan and from build logs outside the repository. They are copied into `run_cost_timing.json` (`feasibility_review`, `standalone_builds`; `python -m residualmap.intake_exp timing --fixed-only` rewrites only such copied records and times nothing) and registered in `numbers.py`.
    - Bug-risk row 8 said a cp1252 file with 'many' chance pairs could be misread; one ID was enough. It now says what the new rule still misreads.
