**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- None in the default numbers. On the final tree the four committed reruns write all 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs (label `post_task15b_review` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`, and `post_task15b_review2` after the second review's fixes; the label `post_task15b_stopped` is the same rerun on the tree the stop left), and the app's default demo is unchanged. The example networks never go through `prepare_inp`, and nothing new runs inside `simulate.load`. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun.
- A missed pre-registered bar: the run cost's time estimate on Net3 (item 10). Recorded, not tuned; the saved check no longer asserts it.
- Behaviour changes outside the default numbers (uploads and the pilot's real-data path only):
  - every prepared model gets one more 7-day EPANET run, timed for the run cost, and a Darcy-Weisbach file two more;
  - Net3's readiness report opens with a warning (two inlets), Net2's with three;
  - ky10 and Net6 run from a fixed copy (their reaction orders), so their grid cache names differ, in the pilot's `--inp ky10` too;
  - a file with one C value, or one Darcy-Weisbach roughness, gets the 225-run fixed-gamma grid, in the app and the pilot;
  - a model of more than 3,000 junctions needs the checkbox, or `--allow-large` in the pilot;
  - a blocked model removes its old content-named copy;
  - the app's demo sample count is capped at the junction count, and an empty route gets a placeholder PDF row;
  - since the review, a file in cp1252 throughout decodes as task 15 decoded it, a model whose rules only read a pump gets the pump warning, and a start other than midnight gets the model-hours warning;
  - since the second review, a file that fails strict UTF-8 is decoded by the new rule, so a file the first fix misread decodes differently (on the review's fuzz, the 1 file in cp1252 throughout and the 12 in UTF-8 it misread), and a chance pair inside a mixed file is read as cp1252; a start within 30 s of midnight gets no model-hours warning and its row says 0 h; Darcy-Weisbach roughness values are compared to 6 significant digits; an export's recognised connector pipes are left out of the uniform-C test and the count of C values; and the full run rescans the numbers on the report it writes before writing it.
- Against the plan:
  - The plan asked for 10 checks; there are 13, with the leftovers, the route and the app upload the lead asked for.
  - The timing bar is no longer asserted by the saved check, a decision taken after the miss (item 10), so the saved run's pass or fail does not depend on the machine's load.
  - The temporary-file term uses ky4's 9.5 MB per run scaled by junctions; ky10 measures 0.1 MB above that scaling and Net6 1.3 MB below it.
  - Two task-15 check assertions changed on purpose (`uploader_accepts_any_type` looks for the 'Reaction orders' and 'Run cost' rows, `pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private` expects the fixed-gamma grid names for Net1); their committed details did not change.

**Later** (each with its reason):
- A chloramine grid with the old-pipe factor held at 0: until then a uniform-C file in chloramine mode is searched over a factor it cannot learn, and the app shows the factor as not identifiable.
- A seasonal model (`--plant`) on a uniform-C file keeps its grid, for the same reason.
- Grid cache files keyed to a removed stale copy stay in `outputs/cache` (git-ignored) until deleted.
- The app's disk gate is not exercised by AppTest; `size_gate` itself is tested with the free disk stood in.
- Restating the time estimate (a start-up term, or a speedup that depends on size): a decision for Devansh, then a new pre-registered bar.
- Boosters already in a file stay at about a thousandth of their strength (open decision 4).
- Hiding the maps for a file without coordinates, instead of saying they show every node at one point.
- A file in cp1252 throughout whose only non-ASCII bytes are chance pairs is valid UTF-8 and is read as UTF-8, as at task 15 (the second review's case B); telling it from UTF-8 with IPA letters needs more than the bytes.
- The decoding rule's remaining misreads (bug-risk row 8) are constructed cases; a real export from the contact will show whether any occur.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.intake_exp` writes the three records (about 1 minute); `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.intake_exp timing --ky10` writes the timed record (about 5 minutes); `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.importers --prepare <file>` prints a readiness report. The task-15b checks are part of the full run; `inp_reaction_orders_fixed`, `inp_prv_elevations`, `inp_coordinate_scale`, `uniform_c_fixes_gamma`, `run_cost_gate`, `readiness_bundled_reproduces` and `app_small_network_upload` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 16, grab-log upload, templates and the Get started page

**Asked:** (plan task 16, category b) let an operator go from the grab-sample log the lab or field system already exports, CSV or Excel, and a tap map by ID, to the chlorine map without typing a junction ID. The log as it really arrives (critique 19): one datetime column or a date and a time, US or European dates, headers such as 'Total Chlorine (mg/L)' or 'Sample Point', semicolons and decimal commas, a Windows code page or a byte-order mark, '<0.02' and 'ND', typed Excel cells with tap IDs that lost their leading zeros, and online analyzers' 15-minute series. Guess the columns and let the operator confirm them; refuse a date that reads two ways with a plain question; keep censored readings, list them and show them on the map, never fit them; never mix free and total chlorine. Convert clock time on the way in and out (critique 10): a sample's model hour is its time of day minus START CLOCKTIME, mod 24 h, minutes truncated as today (lead decision 1), in the app and the pilot; an uploaded file that does not start at midnight shows its route hours, worst hour, hour picker and hour of the daily minimum as times of day, with the route's 07:00 to 17:00 window by the clock; demo and typed hours never converted; the bundled Net2 keeps model hours with a caption (lead decision 2). Use the last 3 months of a long log and say so; list every row that is not used with its reason; a per-junction results table with a plain-text equivalent (critique 25), keeping one download button. A Get started page: each package's export steps, templates, and 'Check a file'. One new package, openpyxl 3.1.5 with et-xmlfile 2.0.0 (lead decision 6). Eleven saved checks; defaults byte-identical.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/importers.py`, a grab-log section (the standard library, pandas, and openpyxl for Excel):
  - `read_table(data, name)`: the file's kind by its content (`sniff`), then delimited text in any encoding `decode_text` reads, its separator found the way the sniff finds it (comma, semicolon, tab or vertical bar, by a consistency test over the first 25 lines; since the review, past a title, a 'Lab:/Exported' block or Excel's 'sep=;' line before the header, by each separator's most common cell count), or an Excel workbook, its first sheet that holds a table, typed cells kept, after the zip limits of task 15 (since the review through openpyxl in read-only mode, at most 200 columns and the row limit read; `pandas.read_excel` before). Each row's number is its line in the file, a quoted field over two lines included. A legacy `.xls`, a PDF, a model or a drawing is refused with the sniff's advice; without openpyxl an Excel log is refused with 'save the sheet as CSV', and the sniff's advice says the same.
  - `guess_log_columns`: the tap, reading, date and time columns from the header (the template's names, 'Sample Point', 'Sample Date/Time', 'Total Chlorine (mg/L)', 'Cl2 Free' and the like), the reading columns sorted by the species their header names. `guess_tap_columns` does the same for a tap map.
  - `parse_reading`: decimal commas, a trailing 'mg/L' or 'ppm', '<0.02', '< 0.05', 'ND', 'BDL' and 'not detected' (censored, below detection), '>5' (above the range, kept apart since the review: kind 'above'), blanks, negatives and text, each with its reason.
  - `read_grab_log`: the rows of the log joined to the tap map, with the pilot's columns (junction, model hour, reading, month, date, tap). A column of numeric dates is month first or day first when one date settles it (a number above 12); when none does and some date reads two ways, the log is refused with the question, until a date format is given (MDY, DMY, YMD or a strptime format). A log with a free-chlorine and a total-chlorine column is refused until its reading column is chosen, and then only that column is read (since the review); a reading column that names the other species is refused; one that names neither is read as the disinfectant chosen, with a warning. Since the review, a long-format export's analyte column (found by its header, 'Analyte', 'Parameter', 'Test', or by its values) keeps only the rows of the chosen species, and a units column or the reading's header gives its unit (µg/L divided by 1000, with a warning); a reading above 10 mg/L is listed, not used. A tap that Excel turned into a number is matched to the tap map by number, with a warning. A tap with 12 or more readings a day at a median step of at most 30 minutes on 2 or more days, or with 12 or more a day on 7 days or more, is an online analyzer and keeps one reading a day, the one nearest 10:00 (or the hour given), if one lies within an hour of it, unless the operator keeps it whole (before the review, 12 or more on its median day was enough). Most grab samples outside 06:00 to 18:00 warns of a UTC export. Every row that is not used, an analyzer's thinned readings included, is kept in a list with its reason; `dropped_lines` groups them by reason, with one example for a reason such as 'date not read', and lists every row, a run of consecutive rows as a range, up to 20 runs per reason and then the count. A file the csv module cannot read is refused, and the app and the Get started page turn any other error while reading a log into plain advice.
  - `model_hour`, `clock_label`, `clock_minute_of`, `daytime_model_hours`, `shows_clock`: the conversion both ways. The start is taken to the minute once, as the readiness row shows it; `readiness.flags['start_clock_h']` stays unrounded.
  - `GrabLog.only_junctions` (rows at a junction the model does not have move to the not-used list) and `GrabLog.window` (the last 3 calendar months that hold a reading, as the pilot's `--window` counts months; since the review it also lists the rows before the window, censored ones included, with their reason).
  - The readiness report's start-clock warning now says what is converted and what is not (typed hours), and the advice for a table or workbook points to the grab-log upload.
- `residualmap/pilot.py`: `load_log` reads through `read_grab_log` and keeps its six columns and its rows for the committed example logs; `--date-format`, `--analyzer-hour` and, past the plan, `--columns` (an explicit mapping, since the review with `analyte=` and `unit=`) and `--keep-every-reading`; a real run converts the log's times with the model's START CLOCKTIME from the readiness report (`--synthetic` logs are model hours already) and, for a file that does not start at midnight, says that its outputs' hours are model hours; a refused log stops the run with its message instead of a traceback; readings below detection and above the range are noted apart.
- `app.py`:
  - with the demo off, '2. Your samples' adds 'Grab log (CSV or Excel)' and 'Tap map (CSV)', after the model uploader, and an expander 'Columns of your grab log' with the guesses in selectboxes (tap, reading, date, time, date format; since the review also the analyte column, the units column and a typed date format, the reading starting at '(choose a column)' for a log with both species, and the expander open when a guess is not a template name) and, for a log with an analyzer, the hour to keep or, since the review, 'Keep every reading of these taps'. All of them sit in the sidebar after the example picker, because AppTest lists main-area widgets before the sidebar's, so `selectbox[0]` and `file_uploader[0]` keep their labels.
  - above the sample table: 'Grab log: using N samples from <date> to <date>', the last 3 months of a longer log and the months it runs over, and that within the window every sample is treated as one simulated day; a warning listing every row that is not used, grouped by reason with its row numbers; the reader's warnings. A refused log stops the page with its message (the question, for a date that reads two ways). The rows prefill the sample table, whose key holds a hash of them, so a new upload or a new mapping replaces the table; the operator can still edit it. `samples.dropna()` became `dropna(subset=["junction", "hour", "mg/L"])`.
  - junctions with a reading below detection are marked on the chlorine map (and the PDF's) with a red x, 'measured below detection (not fitted)'; since the review, a reading above the measuring range gets a blue plus, 'measured above the range (not fitted)'.
  - clock time: for an uploaded file whose start, to the minute, is not midnight, the route is planned over the model hours of 07:00 to 17:00 by the clock (`plan_route(hours=..., hour_label=...)`), and the route's hours, the worst hour, the hour picker, the hour of each junction's daily minimum, the maps' tags, the worst-hour bars and the PDF show times of day, with a caption; the sample table's hour column stays the model hour, with a caption that says so. The bundled examples keep model hours; Net2 gets the caption 'model hour (this file starts at 08:00)'.
  - 'Every junction: the daily minimum', a dataframe of every junction (junction, median, lo90 and hi90 in mg/L, P(daily min < threshold), the hour of the daily minimum), whose toolbar downloads the CSV, and a plain `st.table` of the 20 junctions most likely below the threshold, which screen readers read more easily. No WCAG claim.
  - the advice for a file that is not a model, and the foot of the page, link to the Get started page.
- `pages/1_Get_started.py`, new, 'Bring your data': step 1, 'Which software holds your network?', shows each package's section of `docs/how_to_export_your_model.md` (new; WaterGEMS and WaterCAD, InfoWater Pro, InfoWorks WS Pro, KYPipe, EPANET, AutoCAD and Civil 3D, MicroStation, ArcGIS, QGIS, Google Earth, paper maps, not sure; GIS and CAD marked 'coming, task 17' or 'task 18'); step 2, the templates (`docs/templates/`, new: the grab log in free and total chlorine, the tap map by ID, the plant log) and the grab log as an Excel workbook written on the fly by openpyxl; step 3, 'Check a file', which gives any file its sniff, a model its readiness report (from a copy in a temporary folder under the git-ignored `outputs/private/`, deleted at once; the map's grid is never built), a log its reading without a tap map (rows, months, every row that would not be used) and a tap map its taps.
- `residualmap/route.py`: `plan_route(hour_label=...)`, the label the reason prints for an hour; without it the reason is as before.
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: 15 task-16 fixtures written by hand (string formatting and the codecs, never the reader), the workbook by openpyxl (same-library self-consistency), with invented readings at Net3's junctions, and 16 more for the review's cases (two of them workbooks, same-library). `residualmap/intake_exp.py samples` writes `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json` (new).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: the `task16` group of 12 checks, the plan's 11 and, since the review, `grab_log_review_cases` (Numbers below); `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text` covers the new page, guide and templates.
- `residualmap/numbers.py`: registry entries for this section's headline numbers.
- `requirements.txt`: `openpyxl==3.1.5` and `et-xmlfile==2.0.0`, installed into `.venv` from PyPI (`pip install openpyxl==3.1.5`, which brought et-xmlfile 2.0.0), nothing else.
- After the final verification (Found 14): in `residualmap/importers.py` the species words read inside brackets (`reading_species`, `names_chlorine`, `is_reading_header`), the reading-column question for any two reading columns not of one named species, the analyte-value question and `analyte_value` (with `*` for every row), `header_unit` on a compact form of the unit, the line count before a text is split and the record limit (`_iter_lines`), the separator and Excel header rules for a title, the Excel 00:00 rule by cadence, the time of a full datetime in a time column, ASCII-only number matching, the two-column tap map rule, and grouped and capped not-used reasons (`dropped_lines`); in `app.py` 'Analyte value' and the Net2 upload caption; in the pilot `analyte_value=` and at most 20 unknown taps named; the final verification's fixtures in `residualmap/fixtures.py`, part `final_review` (and the probes' numbers, part `final_review_probes`) in `residualmap/intake_exp.py`, and the check `grab_log_final_review_cases`; the guide's grab-sample section names the analyte value.

**Found:**
1. Column mapping (`grab_log_column_mapping`, `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json`, part `mapping`). The LIMS export, with a sample ID per row, 'Sample Point', one 'Sample Date/Time' column of US dates with AM/PM times and 'Total Chlorine (mg/L)', maps by the guessed columns: 40 of 40 rows, as its own table says, with MDY and with the strptime format. Without a format it is refused with the question, because every one of its dates reads two ways. The censored log gives its 3 readings (one with a decimal comma, one with 'mg/L'), lists 4 censored readings ('<0.02', '< 0.05', 'ND', 'BDL') as kept and not fitted, and drops 1 blank with its reason. The semicolon and tab logs give their 4 rows, guessed and with an explicit mapping. `ambiguous_dates.csv` is refused with 'Which is it?' and read as 3 rows once 'day first' is given.
2. Encodings (`grab_log_encodings`). The cp1252 log (Windows line ends, a tap 'Église_3' that the UTF-8 tap map also holds) gives its 3 rows, the accented tap matched; the log with a byte-order mark gives its 2 rows, its first header read as 'date'. As a control, task 15b's `load_log` (git HEAD) on the same files: it raised UnicodeDecodeError on the cp1252 log and read the one with the mark (pandas strips it); it refused the semicolon, LIMS and censored logs for want of a `free_chlorine_mgL` column, raised UnicodeDecodeError on the workbook, and read the analyzer file with every 15-minute reading as a sample.
3. Excel (`excel_log_equals_csv_log`). `grab_log_typed.xlsx`, with typed date and time cells and the tap IDs 007 and 012 stored as the numbers 7 and 12, gives the same 5 rows and the same S as its CSV twin, with the warning that 2 taps were matched by number; the CSV gets no warning. Same-library self-consistency: openpyxl wrote it and reads it.
4. Analyzers (`analyzer_thinning`). Two analyzers at 15-minute steps for 30 days, 5,760 readings, keep 60, one per site per day, each at 10:00, and the other 5,700 are listed as thinned; the 3 grab samples are kept; with the hour set to 8 the kept readings are at 08:00.
5. UTC (`utc_warning`). Six daytime samples written 8 h late warn ('6 of 6 sample times fall outside 06:00 to 18:00'); the semicolon log and the committed example log do not.
6. Clock time (`clock_conversion`). With the start at 06:00, a 09:00 sample is model hour 3 (09:59 too: minutes truncated), the daytime window is model hours 1 to 11, shown as 07:00 to 17:00, and a start at midnight gives `simgp.DAY_HOURS` and changes no hour (all 1,440 minutes of the day checked). In the app, the Net3 example with a log whose times of day equal its model hours, and `net3_clock6.inp` (Net3 starting at 06:00) with the same readings written 6 h later, put the same rows in the sample table, so the fits are the same: the worst hour is 00:00 on Net3 and 06:00 on the clock file, every junction's hour of the daily minimum moves by 6 h, the clock file's map at 09:00 equals Net3's at 03:00 (4 of 92 junctions flagged), and the clock file's route hours all lie within 07:00 to 17:00. A probe during the build found net3_clock6's 675-run grid bit-identical to Net3's (Net3's controls are by elapsed time, and EPANET's patterns do not read the clock), which is why the two match exactly. The Net2 example keeps model hours, with its caption. ky4 was not run in the app here (its free-chlorine grid at 1.2 mg/L is not cached); it starts at midnight, so its hours are unchanged by construction.
7. The upload in the app (`app_grab_log_upload`, AppTest, the demo off, the Net3 example). The committed example log with its tap map gives 'Samples used' 39, the number of `load_log`'s 78 rows that fall inside the window (its last 3 of 6 months), and the page says 'using 39 samples from 2026-04-03 to 2026-06-27' and that within the window every sample is one simulated day. Uploading the 9-month log in its place changes the table and uses its last 3 months, 12 rows, and says so. The unknown tap is listed in a warning with its row; the mixed-species log is refused before any map; the workbook gives its CSV twin's table, with the leading-zero warning; the censored log lists its 4 censored rows and marks their 4 junctions on the map (counted through matplotlib); the ambiguous log stops with the question and runs once 'day first' is chosen; a log with no tap map asks for it. The demo path has no grab-log uploader, and every page has one download button.
8. One real-browser run (headless Chromium through Playwright, as in task 15, against the app started on this laptop; first on the tree before the first build's last fix, which did not touch the table, then again after the review's fixes, at 08:41, before a last edit to `residualmap/importers.py`, and since the final verification once more, on the app's final code (no file the app runs was edited after it), whose result is copied into `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json`, part `browser_check`): with the demo on, and again with the demo off after the example log and tap map were set on the page's own file inputs ('Samples used' 39), the 'Every junction' grid's toolbar shows 'Download as CSV', and clicking it wrote 92 rows with the 6 columns. Chrome's route for that button is its save-file dialog, which a headless browser cannot answer, so the dialog was stood in by a stub that keeps what the page writes; the button and the CSV are the page's own. The Get started link opened 'Bring your data' with its 5 template buttons. So the CSV stays on the main page's table, as the plan wanted, and the main page keeps exactly one download button.
9. The table (`app_per_junction_table`): 92 rows and 6 columns, the plain table 20 rows sorted by the probability, with the default demo (still 42 of 92 flagged) and with the example log.
10. The Get started page (`get_started_page_runs`): 12 software choices, each showing its own section of the guide (5 marked 'coming'), 5 template downloads, and every template parses (part `templates`: the free-chlorine grab log gives 2 readings and 1 censored, the total-chlorine one 3, the tap map 3 taps, the plant log 3 months, the workbook the free log's rows). 'Check a file' gives the GeoPackage its advice, the `.bak` copy of Net3 its readiness report, the censored log its rows and reasons, the tap map its taps and the ambiguous log the question; no grid is built and nothing is left in `outputs/private/check_a_file` or `outputs/app_uploads`.
11. Widget order (`app_widget_order_unchanged`): in 12 scenarios replayed from `app_task14_runs`, `app_chloramine_mode` and `app_water_age_panels` plus the new one, `selectbox[0]` is 'Example' in the 10 that show the picker, `file_uploader[0]` is 'EPANET .inp file' in both upload scenarios, and the new upload with the demo off lists the model, grab-log and tap-map uploaders in that order. The demo truth's EPANET-MSX run was stood in by the reduced truth there, which changes no widget.
12. The pilot (`pilot_clock_and_log`): the LIMS log on Net3 in chloramine mode runs with `--date-format MDY` and stops with the question without it; the clock log on `net3_clock6.inp` and the same readings 6 h earlier on Net3 by name write byte-identical predictions and validation files, the first's hours being its times of day minus 6 h. The committed demonstration (`synthetic_pilot_net3`) and the example log's run by name (`pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private`, byte-identical to `outputs/pilot`) are unchanged.
13. The review (after the first build). Each issue was reproduced with the review's probe first, then fixed; each case is now a fixture (part `review` of `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json`) in the new check `grab_log_review_cases`, or a new scenario of an AppTest check:
    - 'Check a file' crashed with a traceback on a junction list (a GIS or EPANET junction export, 'Node ID,Elevation,Base Demand'): a junction column was found and no tap column, and the page looked up the missing column. It now says the table looks like the model's list of junctions, not a tap map, and the table branch has the main page's last guard (`get_started_page_runs`, `junction_list.csv`).
    - A reading above the measuring range ('>2.2') went into the censored list with the readings below detection, so the map and the PDF marked a high-chlorine site 'measured below detection' and the pilot counted it so. Censored rows now carry a kind, 'below' or 'above': on `log_overrange.csv` the one '<0.02' junction gets the red x and the two '>' junctions a blue plus, 'measured above the range', and the pilot notes 1 reading below detection and 2 above the measuring range.
    - No reading had a unit or a plausibility check: a log in µg/L was fitted as mg/L, every reading a thousand times too high, so the review's map flagged no junction where its mg/L twin flagged many, and a LIMS missing code of 9999 was fitted. The unit is now read from the reading's header or a units column (µg/L, ug/L, mcg/L or ppb divided by 1000, with a warning; mg/L, ppm or none read as mg/L), a reading above 10 mg/L is listed and not used, and a median above 5 mg/L warns. `log_ugL.csv` gives the rows of its mg/L table; `log_implausible.csv` lists its 9999 and 450.
    - A long-format LIMS export (one row per analyte: Analyte, Result, Units) was fitted with every analyte in it, pH and temperature included, once 'Result' was chosen as the reading, and a log whose analyte column named free and total chlorine mixed them, so the claim that free and total chlorine are never mixed was false for that layout. The analyte column is now found by its header (analyte, parameter, test, determinand and the like) or by its values, the reading guess is its result column, and only the rows of the chosen species are read; the others are listed as another analyte or as the other chlorine ('both species', the plan's row-level reason). `lims_long.csv` gives its 3 free chlorine rows, and its 3 total chlorine rows in chloramine mode; in the app it gives 'Samples used' 3, with the analyte column guessed.
    - A log with a free and a total column was refused even after the operator picked one as the reading, so neither the app nor `--columns` could resolve it. Now the bare guess is refused with the question (which column is the reading?), the app's 'Reading column' starts at '(choose a column)' for such a log, and once one is chosen only that column is read, with a note that the other is not; `mixed_species_log.csv` reads 2 free chlorine readings, or 2 total chlorine ones in chloramine mode, and runs in the app once its reading column is chosen.
    - A title line, a 'Lab:/Exported' block or Excel's 'sep=;' line before the header made the separator test refuse the file. A 'sep=;' line now names the separator, and when the first line does not fit any separator, each separator's most common cell count over the first 50 records finds the header; the lines before it are skipped and named in the notes. Every file the first build read is read as before (that test runs first). `log_title_line.csv`, `log_lab_preamble.csv` and `log_sep_hint.csv` give their 4 rows.
    - 'chlor' matched inside 'Chloride', so a chloride column before the chlorine one was taken as the reading. The match is now by word, never chloride, chlorate, chlorite, chlorophyll or chlorine dioxide, and a header that names chlorine comes before one that says only 'residual' (`log_chloride_first.csv`); the column expander opens when a guess is not a template name.
    - Month-name dates ('10-Mar-26') and European dotted times ('09.15') were not read, and each row's reason held its raw date, so the not-used list grew by one line per date. Both are read now (`log_month_names.csv`, its 4 rows), the app's 'Date format' takes a typed strptime format, and 'date not read' and 'no time of day' are one reason each, with one example.
    - An Excel datetime at exactly 00:00 was taken as a date with no time, so an analyzer's series in Excel lost its midnight reading each day. Midnight is now a time when another datetime cell of the column carries one: `analyzer_midnight.xlsx` (same-library self-consistency) reads all 192 readings its CSV twin reads, with no 'no time of day' row.
    - An Excel log had no size limit before parsing: `pandas.read_excel` padded every row to the widest one, so a few-KB file with one stray cell far down and far right took memory in proportion to rows times columns. The workbook is now read with openpyxl in read-only mode, at most 200 columns and the row limit, and a cover sheet before the data is passed over (`log_cover_and_stray.xlsx`: its 2 rows, the stray cell cut).
    - The not-used list named every row, so an analyzer export interleaved by timestamp gave a warning of hundreds of KB. Each reason now gives at most 20 runs of rows and then its count: ten analyzers interleaved by time for two days (`analyzer_interleaved.csv`, 1,920 readings, 20 kept) give 10 lines, 2,307 characters, and the count still covers all 1,900 thinned rows.
    - After a quoted comment over two lines every later row was reported one line early; a row's number is now its first line in the file (`log_multiline_comment.csv`: the unknown tap on line 4).
    - The analyzer rule (12 or more readings on the tap's median day) also caught a diurnal grab-sampling study, the readings the page's 'any hour of the day' rests on, and the first build did not say so. A tap is now an analyzer by its cadence: 12 or more readings a day at a median step of at most 30 minutes on 2 or more days, or 12 or more a day on 7 days or more (an hourly historian export). `grab_study_24h.csv` (T1 hourly on two days, T3 two-hourly on one, three route samples) keeps all 63 readings; `analyzer_15min.csv` is thinned as before; 'Keep every reading of these taps' in the app and `--keep-every-reading` in the pilot keep an analyzer whole (5,763 rows of that file). The note no longer says a thinned series cannot outweigh the grab samples (thinning leaves about 30 readings a month per analyzer, more than the fixture's grab samples); it says each series counts once a day and how many analyzer readings and grab samples are kept.
    - Rows before the 3-month window were neither listed nor counted, although the page said every row not used is listed. The window now lists them, censored ones included, with the reason 'dated before the window': the 9-month log's 24 rows before 2026-07 are counted on the page and in the not-used list. 'Check a file' said a long log's readings 'would be used'; it now gives the main page's window too.
    - With the demo on, an uploaded file that does not start at midnight drew its 'daytime' demo samples at the model hours 7 to 17 and showed them as times of day (13:00 to 23:00 for a 06:00 start). The demo now draws at the model hours of 07:00 to 17:00 by the clock for such a file (`clock_conversion`, net3_clock6 with the demo on); every bundled example starts at midnight, so the default demo draws exactly as before.
    - The pilot's outputs for a converted file are in model hours with nothing to say so; the run now prints that every hour in its outputs is a model hour, with the start (`pilot_clock_and_log`). Its files are unchanged, so the acceptance wording is now: input converted in the app and the pilot, display converted in the app, the pilot's outputs in model hours with a printed note.
    - Words corrected: the stored start-clock warning for the bundled Net2 said the app converts 'for this file', though the bundled Net2 keeps model hours; it now says the conversion applies when the file is uploaded or run with `--inp` (`outputs/intake/readiness_bundled.json` rewritten, Net2's warning the only line changed). The guide said Darcy-Weisbach headloss is fixed with no condition (it is converted only when the water age moves little), and the pilot protocol still said a date-only reading gets the usual hour (the code lists it as 'no time of day'). The checks' docstring run times were stale.
    - Found 8's browser run was on the tree before the first build's last fix; it was rerun after these fixes (item 8; that rerun was not on the final tree after all, item 14).
14. The final verification (after the review's fixes). Each issue was reproduced first with the verification's own scripts, run again on the live tree before any edit (the reader cases, the sizes and lists, the superscript tap, the Net2 hours, and the AppTest cases), then fixed; each case is a fixture (part `final_review` of `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json`) in the new quick check `grab_log_final_review_cases`, or a new scenario of `app_grab_log_upload`, `get_started_page_runs` or `clock_conversion`. What the probes measured before and after is copied into part `final_review_probes` (the probes' logs are kept outside the repository):
    - The claim that free and total chlorine are never mixed was false again, for the species written in brackets. The species was read after the brackets were stripped, so 'Chlorine (Free)', 'Chlorine (Total)', 'Cl2 (free)' and 'Cl2 (total)' all read as plain chlorine: the committed example log written as one row per analyte (Chlorine (Free), Chlorine (Total) and pH rows) gave 'Samples used' 78 and 20 of 92 flagged in the app, against 39 and 35 of 92 for the log itself; a wide log with both columns did not ask which was the reading, and in chloramine mode read the free column as total chlorine (87 of 92 flagged); and 'Chloramine (mg/L)' was read as free chlorine. The only warning said the column did not say free or total chlorine, which was false. The species is now read with the bracket contents kept and the unit words left out; 'chloramine' and 'chloramines' are total chlorine ('combined' is never free, and still never a reading); and the reading column must be chosen whenever two reading columns are not both of one named species, a column with no species beside another included (`_species_question`). The same long-format log now gives 39 and 35 of 92, as the log itself; `long_paren.csv` gives its 3 free chlorine rows (3 total chlorine rows in chloramine mode) with no species warning; `wide_paren.csv` in both modes, its twin with the total column first, and `wide_residual_and_free.csv` stop with 'Which column is the reading?' and read only the one chosen; in the app's chloramine mode its 'Reading column' starts at '(choose a column)'; `chloramine_header.csv` is refused in free mode as total chlorine and read in chloramine mode.
    - A long-format export whose analytes the reader did not recognise was fitted whole. 'Free Cl', 'Total Cl', FCL2 and TCL2 named no chlorine, so the analyte column was dropped with a warning and every row was read, pH included ('Free Cl', 'Total Cl' and pH with no units column: 0.45, 1.2, 7.6, 0.38, 1.05, 7.8). 'Cl' with a species word ('Cl' alone may be chloride) and the codes FCL2, CL2-F, TCL2 and CL2-T now name chlorine and their species (`long_short_names.csv`, `long_lims_codes.csv` under 'Parameter Code': 3 free chlorine rows each, 3 total in chloramine mode). And the review's fallback, which was not built, is: an analyte column whose values cannot pick the species (codes the reader does not know, CHLF and CHLT; two chlorine analytes that name no species; one with no species beside the other species) is refused with 'Which value is the chlorine reading?', and a plain 'Result' column beside an analyte-like column that is not chosen ('Parameter Code', which the guess passes over) is refused naming that column. The answer is 'Analyte value' in the app (the column's values, '(the chlorine chosen, found by its name)' by default, and '(every row: this column does not pick the rows)' for a column that names something else) and `analyte_value=` in the pilot's `--columns` (`*` for every row): `long_unknown_codes.csv` is refused, then reads its 3 CHLF rows with a warning that CHLF names no species; in the app it stops with the question and gives 'Samples used' 3 once 'Analyte value' is CHLF.
    - Past the issues, cheap and in the same rule: a detection or reporting limit ('Free Chlorine MDL', 'Free Chlorine (RL)'), a pressure or a temperature column is no reading column (the verification's probe read 'Free Chlorine MDL' as the reading of a log with 'Free Chlorine (mg/L)' after it, and 'Residual Pressure (psi)' as the reading of a free-chlorine log whose only chlorine column was total chlorine); a log with two columns of one named species says which it read.
    - A CSV of many short rows was split into a list of every line and every record before the row limit was applied: 1 million rows of '1,' (3 MB) took 454 MB and 2.05 s, 3 million (9 MB) 1,036 MB and 7.66 s before the refusal, 6 million 1,930 MB and 443.5 s while the Mac swapped. The text's line breaks are now counted before it is split (more than 2,000,050 is refused unread), the lines are read one at a time, and records stop at the row limit plus 51: 3 million rows are refused in 0.01 s at 56 MB, 33 million (99 MB) in 0.11 s at 236 MB, 2 million (inside the line count) after 0.59 s at 247 MB; a file at the limit, 1 million rows, still reads (388 MB, 1.15 s).
    - Reasons that hold a value were not grouped, so the not-used list grew again with the values: a lab qualifier on every reading ('0.452 J', 20 analyzers interleaved over 30 days, 57,600 rows) gave 1,451 lines and 324,067 characters, and 2,000 taps from another system's tap map 2,000 lines and 476,796. A reason that names a value (not a number, a tap not in the tap map, a junction not in the model, a unit, another analyte, the other chlorine, censored and above the range) is now one line for all its values when more than 5 differ, with the count and three examples, and the list stops at 30 lines, the last counting the rest: the same two files give 1 line of 102 characters and 1 of 131; the fixtures `qualifier_interleaved.csv` and `unknown_taps_many.csv` 1 line each. The pilot names at most 20 unknown taps.
    - µg/L spellings: 'Free Chlorine ugL', 'free_chlorine_ugl', 'Free Chlorine (Âµg/L)' (µ after an Excel round trip), 'Free Chlorine (ug per L)' and 'Free Chlorine µg L-1' were read as mg/L, so 450 and 380 were listed as above 10 mg/L and 8 and 5, the lowest residuals, fitted 1000 times too high; a units column 'mg Cl2/L', 'mgCl2/L' or 'mg/litre' dropped every row. Units are now matched on a compact form (lower case, camel case split, µ written one way, spaces, underscores, slashes and Cl2 allowed between the parts): the five headers give 0.45, 0.38, 0.008 and 0.005 with the µg/L warning, the three units read every row, and a log with readings above and below 10 mg/L and no unit converted warns that it may be in µg/L (`ugl_unnamed.csv`; `log_implausible.csv`'s 9999 and 450 now warn too).
    - An Excel date with no time in a column of timed datetimes (a sample whose time was not typed) was fitted at 00:00, model hour 0, while its CSV twin listed it as 'no time of day'. Such a 00:00 is now kept only at an online analyzer's tap (its midnight reading, as the review needed) and listed as 'no time of day' elsewhere, with a note (`excel_date_only.xlsx` gives its CSV twin's 2 rows and lists row 4 the same way; `analyzer_midnight.xlsx` still reads all 192 readings). The cell's number format was not used: openpyxl writes a format with a time for every datetime cell (the fixture's date-only cell has 'yyyy-mm-dd h:mm:ss' too), so in such a file the format does not tell a date typed alone from a midnight.
    - A comma title before a semicolon export with decimal commas ('Residuals report, March 2026'), and an Excel title row of two filled cells, were taken as the header and refused. A separator that passes the first test now gives way to one with a larger most common cell count when the lines up to that one's header do not fit the first (the comma split the title and every decimal comma in two); an Excel header is the first row with more filled cells than half the most common count, and the rows before it are named in the notes; a workbook sheet holds a table only with two or more rows of two filled cells. `log_comma_title_semicolon.csv` and `log_two_cell_title.xlsx` give their 4 rows; every earlier fixture and committed log is read as before (the reader's outputs on all 82 earlier cases compared before and after: the only change is the new µg/L warning on `log_implausible.csv`).
    - A two-column junction export ('Node ID,Elevation') was offered by 'Check a file' as a tap map with junction column 'Elevation'. A column that names a junction or node is never the tap: it is a junction list, as with three columns (`junctions_2col.csv`, `get_started_page_runs`).
    - On the bundled Net2 an uploaded log's times were put into the sample table as model hours (09:00 is hour 9) while the pilot with `--inp Net2` subtracts 8 h (hour 1), and the page did not say so. Lead decision 2 stands, so no number moves; with a log uploaded, the Net2 caption now says the log's times of day are read as model hours there, not converted, and that the pilot subtracts 8 h (`clock_conversion`, scenario D: hours 9 to 15, the caption; the demo-on page has no such words).
    - A tap ID with a superscript digit ('1²', from cp1252 bytes 0xB2, 0xB3, 0xB9) passed `str.isdigit()` and failed `int()`, so one cell stopped the whole log with a ValueError, in the app and the pilot. Only ASCII digits are matched by number now; such a tap is listed as not in the tap map, and a tap map holding one reads (`superscript_tap_log.csv`, `superscript_tap_map.csv`).
    - A time column holding a full datetime, as Access or SQL time fields export it ('1899-12-30 09:15:00'), dropped every row as 'no time of day'. Its time of day is read now (`time_column_epoch_date.csv`, its 4 rows).
    - The records. The review's records said 'on the final tree' for runs made before later edits: `residualmap/importers.py` was edited at 08:53:21, after the `post_task16_review` rerun (outputs written by 08:52:47) and after the browser check (08:41), and `residualmap/checks.py` at 09:11:16, after the full run that wrote the committed report (08:54 to 09:10); what changed was not kept. Those notes now say which tree each run was on. For this pass every file was copied before its first edit, except the export guide, whose one changed sentence is kept as a diff (both outside the repository). The sentence before this one was corrected after the full run (it first said every file was copied), and the two checks that read this journal, `numbers_trace_to_outputs` and `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text`, were rerun on it and pass. The browser check ran on the app's final code (no file the app runs was edited after it; this record's writer, the checks and the numbers registry were), and the four reruns (label `post_task16_final`) and the full checks ran after the last edit to any code file, in that order.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- None in the default numbers: the four committed reruns write 50 of 50 files byte-identical to the committed outputs after the first build (label `post_task16` in `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`), after the review's fixes (`post_task16_review`, which ran before a last edit to `residualmap/importers.py` at 08:53, so it was not the final tree, as its entry first said), and on the final tree after the final verification's fixes (`post_task16_final`), and the app's default demo is unchanged each time. ky4 and the initial-level structural variant were not rerun. The bundled examples never go through the new reader, and nothing new runs inside `simulate.load`. The committed example logs give `load_log` the same rows and columns as before, and 'pilot --inp Net3' on them writes files byte-identical to `outputs/pilot`.
- Behaviour changes outside the default numbers:
  - `load_log` reads more: other column names, separators, encodings, Excel, censored values. A blank reading is now dropped with its reason (before, it became a missing value in the fit); a tap listed twice in the tap map with two different junctions is refused (before, each of its samples was counted twice); a refused log stops the pilot with its message instead of a traceback; the pilot prints what it did not use. Found by the review and missing from the first build's list: a reading that is not a number ('n/a') is dropped with its reason (before, it became a missing value, as a blank did); a tap listed twice in the tap map with the same junction is counted once (before, its samples were counted twice); and a log whose slash dates all read two ways (03/04/2026) stops with the question (before, it was read month first without a word; the plan asks for the question).
  - since the review: a tap is an online analyzer by its cadence, so a diurnal grab-sampling study, which the first build thinned to one reading a day, keeps every reading again, as `load_log` kept it before task 16; a reading above 10 mg/L, or with a unit other than mg/L or µg/L in a units column, is listed instead of fitted; a log in µg/L is divided by 1000; a long-format export keeps only the chosen species' rows; a log with a free and a total column runs once its reading column is chosen; an Excel log's row numbers are its sheet's rows (the first build's pandas path skipped blank rows) and a text log's are its lines; the not-used list gives at most 20 runs of rows per reason; with the demo on, an uploaded file that does not start at midnight draws its demo samples at other model hours than the first build did.
  - the pilot converts every real log's times with the model's START CLOCKTIME, `--inp Net2` included (its 08:00 start shifts its samples' hours by 8); the app's bundled Net2 does not.
  - the readiness report's start-clock warning has new words, so `outputs/intake/readiness_bundled.json` was rewritten: Net2's warning is the only line that changed.
  - every app page has a new table and a plain table below the route, and Net2 a caption; the sidebar has new widgets with the demo off only.
  - since the final verification: a log with two reading columns that are not both of one named species, the species in brackets included, asks which is the reading (a log with 'Chlorine Residual' beside 'Free Chlorine' read the free column silently before); a long-format export whose analytes cannot pick the species, or a plain 'Result' beside an analyte-like column, is refused with the question (it was read whole); 'Chloramine (mg/L)' is refused in free mode; a detection limit, pressure or temperature column is no reading; units written without a slash are read; a log with readings above and below 10 mg/L warns of µg/L, `log_implausible.csv` included; a text with more than 2,000,050 lines is refused unread; an Excel 00:00 among timed datetimes away from an analyzer is listed as 'no time of day' (it was fitted at hour 0); a time column holding a full datetime gives its time; a reason that names a value is one line for its values when more than 5 differ, and the list stops at 30 lines; the pilot names at most 20 unknown taps; the bundled Net2 page with an uploaded log has one more sentence in its caption.
- Against the plan:
  - 'Both species' is a row-level reason for a long-format export (its rows of the other chlorine are listed as such); a log with a free and a total chlorine column is refused until its reading column is chosen, and then only that column is read (since the review; the first build refused it whole).
  - The column guesses are shown in selectboxes and used as they are, without a confirm click; the operator changes any that is wrong. Since the review the expander opens when a guess is not a template name or the log needs a choice.
  - Units: a reading column whose header names no unit, with no units column, is read as mg/L; only a reading above 10 mg/L, a median above 5 mg/L or (since the final verification) readings above and below 10 mg/L in one log catch a log in another unit.
  - Since the final verification, a long-format export whose analytes the reader does not recognise needs its analyte value chosen ('Analyte value', `analyte_value=`), with '(every row)' for a column that names something else; a lab's own codes beyond FCL2, TCL2, CL2-F and CL2-T are not known.
  - The Excel 00:00 rule is by cadence (kept at an online analyzer's tap), not by the cell's number format, which openpyxl writes with a time for every datetime; a grab sample truly taken at 00:00 and typed in Excel is listed as 'no time of day'.
  - The sample table's key holds a hash of the rows it is filled with (content, mapping and window), not of the file alone.
  - The conversion takes the start to the minute, as the readiness row shows it, and a sample's time to the minute; the flag stays unrounded. With the review's start of 0:00:20, the unrounded start would put a sample taken in the first 20 s of an hour into the hour before; the report shows 00:00 and the conversion follows it.
  - The sample table's hour column stays a model hour for a converted file (typed hours are never converted), with a caption; the maps' tags and the PDF show times of day.
  - 'Check a file' runs the readiness report as the main page does, so WNTR reads the model and EPANET runs its first hour and one timed 7-day run; 'no simulation' is read as no map and no grid.
  - The InfoWater Pro menu path is given with the words that it comes from a third-party guide and has not yet been checked against Autodesk's help; no check was made in this task.
  - The real-browser check stood in a stub for Chrome's save dialog, in headless Chromium, not the app pane.
  - A tap tagged as a zone inlet (a boundary condition, not data) is task 16c; the analyzer rule thins every analyzer the same way.
  - `--columns` in the pilot is a small step past the plan.
  - The task-19 form parser does not exist yet, so the templates parse with `load_log` and `load_plant` only.
  - Censored readings are shown and listed but not fitted, which loses information exactly at the low junctions; a censored likelihood is in Later.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.intake_exp samples` writes `outputs/intake/task16_samples.json` (a few seconds); `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.pilot --inp <file.inp> --samples <log.csv or .xlsx> --taps <tap_map.csv> [--date-format MDY] [--analyzer-hour 10] [--keep-every-reading] [--columns ...]`; the app's 'Get started' page is in the sidebar's page list. The checks of task 16 are part of the full run (`grab_log_review_cases` and `grab_log_final_review_cases` are quick); `clock_conversion`, `app_grab_log_upload`, `app_per_junction_table`, `get_started_page_runs`, `app_widget_order_unchanged` and `pilot_clock_and_log` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 16b, tap mapping by coordinates (built in part, stopped at its pre-registered bar)

**Asked:** (plan task 16b, category b; critique 3b, 4 and 5) let an operator give a tap's position instead of its junction: latitude and longitude from a phone or a GPS unit, or x and y in the model's own coordinates. Taps in latitude and longitude go to a local east-north plane and then through an affine fit to control taps (taps whose junction and position are both known): refused when the control taps lie almost on one line, marked unchecked with 3 of them, and each control tap's leave-one-out residual printed with 4 or more. A schematic drawing is refused with 'use junction IDs'. Each tap goes on the nearest pipe segment, vertices included, in metres; pipes of 16 in or more are left out unless the tap map's `on_main` column says yes; the suggested junction is the pipe's downstream end by the sign of the simulated mean flow between 07:00 and 17:00, never by drawing direction; a second candidate within 1.5 times the distance is shown and flagged, and a snap over 30 m is flagged. The output, `tap_map_suggested.csv`, is a suggestion to review: the app would use it only after a confirm checkbox, the pilot only after `--accept-snapped`. Six saved checks. The pre-registered bar: at least 0.98 of 2,000 taps on ky4 get the right junction at 5 m of radial RMS GPS noise; if it misses, stop and report.

**Outcome, in one paragraph (simulation only).** The bar missed, so the task stopped and nothing was tuned. On 2,000 taps on ky4's pipes under 16 in with 5 m of radial RMS GPS noise, 0.967 got the right junction, against the bar of 0.98, counted strictly. Counting a right junction shown as the flagged second choice too (open decision 11) gives 0.9775, which misses as well. The rule, the seeds and the counting were written in a scratch note outside the repository at 13:18, before any tap was snapped, and the run was scored once, at 13:30:47; part `ky4` of `outputs/intake/tap_snapping.json` is that run, byte for byte, and the saved check `tap_snapping_ky4` reruns it and compares. Open decision 11 was still open for Devansh when the run was scored (lead decision 7 says only that the bar stays as written), so the run counted strictly and reports the lenient share beside it. Since both readings miss, the decision left for Devansh is between a lower bar and a restated rule (for example, taps near an intersection treated as ambiguous) with its own pre-registered bar and one new scored run. What exists is the core, reading a tap map by coordinates, the georeference and the snapping, with its record and five of the six saved checks. Nothing an operator uses has changed: the app, the pilot and the Get started page do not offer tap mapping by coordinates, and its template is not shipped.

**Built** (its numbers are from simulation):
- `residualmap/importers.py`, a section for tap maps by coordinates (the standard library, numpy and WNTR):
  - `read_tap_coordinates`: a tap map with latitude and longitude, or x and y, columns (exact header names, or a mapping); a junction given with a position makes a control tap; every row not used is listed with its reason. `tap_map_kind(_of)` says 'coordinates' only for a map with a coordinate pair and either no junction column or a positioned row without a junction, so every tap map by ID keeps its path (since the review, with the mapping too).
  - `tap_geometry`: the pipes with both ends drawn, their segments and vertices in metres around the drawing's centre; a schematic drawing (task 15b's spread test), no coordinates or too few pipes drawn are refused with 'Use junction IDs instead'. `tap_hydraulics` runs the model once (7 days, water age) for each pipe's flow over the clock day window and each node's mean water age. `pipe_junctions` gives each pipe's downstream junction: by the sign of the mean flow, both ends listed when the flow reverses, the end with the older water when there is no flow, the other end when the downstream end is a tank or reservoir.
  - `snap_points` and `suggest_from`: the nearest eligible pipe, the second choice (the nearest pipe with another junction), flags in plain words; `suggest_tap_map` reads, checks, runs and suggests. `TapSuggestion.to_csv` writes the plan's columns.
  - `georeference`: WGS84 to earth-centred coordinates to a local east-north plane (`enu_from_latlon`), then a least-squares affine to the control taps; refused below 3 control taps or when they lie almost on one line (`control_spread`); unchecked with 3; leave-one-out residuals with 4 or more, flagged above 10 m, the largest named first.
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: an ellipsoidal Lambert conformal conic projection in an invented zone (Snyder's equations, sharing no code with the east-north step), `lcc_taps_20km.csv`, `lcc_taps_50km.csv`, `collinear_controls.csv`, `synthetic_urban_grid.inp` (metres, to scale, 271 pipes, a second 6 in main 12 m and 14 m beside two avenues, a 24 in transmission main 20 m beside a 4 in line), `ky4_half_reversed.inp`, Net3's tap files, the coordinates template (a fixture only) with a grab log at its taps, and, since the review, 23 more for the review's cases.
- `residualmap/intake_exp.py`: the `snapping` command and its parts, writing `outputs/intake/tap_snapping.json` (new).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task16b` group of five of the plan's six checks (`georef_enu_affine`, `tap_snapping_ky4`, `tap_snapping_urban_grid`, `tap_snapping_direction_invariant`, `tap_snapping_refuses_schematic`) and `tap_coordinates_review_cases`, added after the stop, for the built parts only. `pilot_suggested_tap_map` is not there, because the pilot's step is not built.
- `residualmap/numbers.py`: registry entries for this section's headline numbers.
- Not built, because of the stop: the pilot's `--taps` by coordinates and `--accept-snapped`, the app's suggestion table and sidebar confirm checkbox, the Get started page's entry, the template in `docs/templates/`, and the readiness row's wording (it still says tap mapping by coordinates is 'planned').

**Found** (simulation only):
1. The scored run (`tap_snapping_ky4`, part `ky4`). On ky4 (1156 pipes in feet, 3968 segments with vertices, 5 pipes of 16 in or more), 2,000 taps placed uniformly by length, given in the model's own x and y: at 5 m, 0.967 right junction, 0.9775 counting a right flagged second choice, right pipe 0.965, median snap 2.37 m; at 10 m, 0.9305 and 0.951; at 20 m, 0.8915 and 0.9135. The plan's October 18 probe of the rule, with other tap positions and noise draws, saw slightly higher shares at 5 m; its figures are in the plan, not in outputs/, so they are not quoted here.
2. Where the misses are (part `ky4_after_the_stop`, measured after the stop with nothing changed, not a rescoring). At 5 m, all 66 misses are on the wrong pipe. 40 of them have a true position within 5 m of an end node of the true pipe, where several pipes meet (88 of the 2,000 taps lie that close). 25 are flagged 'two pipes close', and 21 of those show the right junction as the second choice. A wrong junction with no flag at all: 41 of 2,000 taps (0.0205) at 5 m, 91 (0.0455) at 10 m and 150 (0.075) at 20 m. The flags mark some of the wrong suggestions, not all of them, and the suggestion's note now says so.
3. What 'right junction' means here. It is the product's own downstream rule applied to the tap's true pipe, so the ky4 and urban-grid scores measure the snapping (whether a pipe leading to the right junction was found), not whether the rule picks the junction whose water the tap gets. The rule is tested only for not depending on the drawing (finding 5).
4. The synthetic urban grid (`tap_snapping_urban_grid`, part `urban_grid`; invented, metres). 2,000 taps on the pipes under 16 in: 0.964 right junction at 5 m (0.976 counting a right flagged second choice), 0.9115 at 10 m and 0.851 at 20 m; the 326 taps on the dual mains' avenues 0.9571 at 5 m; no untagged tap is put on the transmission main at any noise, the 82 taps on the 4 in line beside it included; every ambiguous row lists two candidates; of the 100 taps tagged on_main, 0.97 are put on the main at 5 m.
5. Direction (`tap_snapping_direction_invariant`, part `direction`). On `ky4_half_reversed.inp`, 578 pipes drawn the other way, 0 of 2,000 assignments change, and the flow's sign flips on 575 of the 575 flipped pipes that carry flow.
6. The georeference (`georef_enu_affine`, part `georef`). With 3 control taps the maximum error over 400 test taps is 0.0072 m over 20 km and 0.1058 m over 50 km, so the 1 m bar passes; as scored, with the plane's tangent point at the taps' mean (finding 10 moved it to their median), it was 0.0071 m and 0.1011 m. It passes with well-spread control taps: the fixture places them by hand in a triangle across the zone, where the plan's probe drew them at random (its figures are in the plan, not in outputs/, so they are not quoted here). The direct affine from latitude and longitude, kept only in the record, has maximum errors of 25.3 m and 158.2 m. The projection matches Snyder's worked example (Clarke 1866) to 0.022 m, now in the record. Control taps along one street are refused. With 6 control taps and one moved 25 m, the leave-one-out check flags 4 of them: the moved one highest, at 25.0 m, but also 3 that are right, at 22.1, 16.8 and 11.7 m; the warning names the largest first.
7. Net3 (`tap_snapping_refuses_schematic`, part `schematic`). Taps by x and y or by latitude and longitude on Net3, and on Net3 without coordinates, get the plain refusal. No accuracy number exists for Net3.
8. The review (one review, 28 issues, each checked first; each real case is now a fixture, in part `review` of the record or the `review` and `transmission_main_variants` entries, and in `tap_coordinates_review_cases` or the check it belongs to). Fixed:
    - A model drawn in degrees of longitude and latitude, as some GIS exports write it, crashed with a division by zero, because the units' size came from a median rounded to 4 decimals; a drawing in miles got 1666.7 m per unit for 1609.3. The size now comes from the unrounded median (1609.344 m per unit; on the miles drawing every one of the 2,000 urban taps at 5 m gets the same junction as in metres), a drawing in degrees is refused in plain words, and `suggest_tap_map` turns any other error into a refusal that names it, never a traceback.
    - With both pairs of columns, latitude and longitude were always used, even blank; a map with x and y filled and the template's lat and lon left empty placed no tap. The pair filled on more rows is used now; on a tie, latitude and longitude, unless they have fewer than 3 control taps; a mapping chooses either pair; a note says which columns were not used.
    - The 16 in cut was a strict test on the model's own diameter, so a metric DN400 main (400 mm, 15.75 in) or a 16 in main given by an internal diameter of 15.0 in or more counted as a small pipe. On the urban grid with its main at 400 mm, the cut as first built put 3, 13 and 25 of the 2,000 untagged taps on the main at 5, 10 and 20 m; the cut is now 15.0 in on the model's diameter (0.9375 of 16 in), and the count is 0 at every noise, for 388.6 mm too. ky4 has no pipe between 15 and 16 in, so its scored run is unchanged. A 16 in main given by an internal diameter under 15.0 in is not cut: finding 10 flags the taps put on one.
    - A main left out by the cut that is nearer than the chosen pipe, or within 1.5 times its distance, is now flagged ('a main of 16 in or more is 1.0 m away: if the tap is on it, say on_main yes'); before, a tap 1 m from the main got an unflagged junction 21 m away.
    - Pipes drawn to a placeholder position such as 0,0 were snap targets, and the spread test did not see a few of them: with two nodes of the urban grid at 0,0, the review's probe put taps on those pipes. A pipe drawn at under half or over twice the model's usual drawn length per metre is now left out, with a warning naming it: 8 pipes there, and no tap on them. A model drawn in part (155 of its 271 pipes) now warns.
    - Thousands commas in x and y ('1,197') were read as decimal commas. A value that reads two ways is now not used, with its reason, unless the column settles it (values of 1,000 or more written plainly: thousands; another decimal comma: decimal). A latitude with W or E, or a longitude with N or S, is not used, with the reason 'are the two columns swapped?'; latitude and longitude columns that look swapped are refused as swapped.
    - A mapping that names a column the file does not have is refused, as `read_tap_map` refuses it. `tap_map_kind` takes the mapping, so a map by ID whose junction column is known only through the mapping stays a map by ID; unmapped, a column holding the model's junction IDs is named in a warning. A tap listed twice with two junctions is refused; listed twice with another position, it warns.
    - A position such as x = 1e200 overflowed and was told that every pipe near it was 16 in or more; a position more than 1,000 times the drawing's size from its centre is now not used, with the reason 'not near the model's drawing'.
    - The snap's distance matrix now goes in chunks of at most 2 million taps times segments, so its memory no longer grows with the model (the review's probe read its memory; those readings are not a record, so they are not quoted). The control taps' spread is measured on their convex hull, so `control_spread` takes well under a second for 4,000 control taps (the pair loop took seconds), and the largest distance and largest triangle use the same set; the leave-one-out step of `georeference` was still quadratic until finding 10. The east-north plane's centre takes the longitudes across the antimeridian: the 20 km zone moved to 180 degrees, its longitudes written from -180 to 180, had a maximum error of 71.1 m with 3 control taps when the plane's centre took the plain mean of the longitudes as written, and now matches the same zone at 100 W (0.0072 m since finding 10 put the plane's tangent point at the taps' median).
    - A long narrow zone with well-spread control taps was refused by the relative tests alone; it is refused now only when its control taps also lie within 50 m of their line. An 8 km by 300 m valley with its control taps at its corners and mid-sides, 3 m of GPS error on each, is accepted with a warning, with a maximum error of 2.75 m over 300 taps inside it. The refusal names only the tests that failed (it quoted both, as if both had failed, when only the triangle test had).
    - Not code: the record's `about` said saved checks rerun it when none did (now six do, five of the plan's six and `tap_coordinates_review_cases`, and it says so); the template sat in `docs/templates/`, where the app's help sends operators, while nothing reads it (moved out; `docs/templates/` holds task 16's four templates); docstrings described the app's checkbox and the pilot's flag as built (reworded); a code comment said lead decision 7 settled open decision 11 (it did not; corrected); `python -m residualmap.intake_exp`'s help did not list `snapping`.
    - Process: during the implementation's review, a review script ran the record from the repository's root, so EPANET's scratch files appeared there for a few seconds and a full run's `pilot_inp_is_content_hashed_and_private` failed with 'git status changed'. Every command of this pass ran from a folder outside the repository, the new checks run in the checks' scratch folder like the others, and the module's help says to run `snapping` from outside the repository.
9. Found in passing, not caused by this task: the committed report's `numbers_trace_to_outputs` entry was out of date for HEAD's own README and journal (its count of code and path spans was 2 short of the count on HEAD's text, probably because the lead's wording pass on the Get started page came after the last full run); the full run for this task refreshes it.
10. The final verification (9 issues, each reproduced first on the code as it stood; each case is now a fixture, in the `final` entries of part `review` and of part `georef`'s `review` entry, with the plastic mains among the `transmission_main_variants`, and in the check it belongs to). Fixed:
    - A 16 in plastic main given by its internal diameter is under the 15.0 in cut: on the urban grid with its main as AWWA C905 DR14 (378.8 mm, 14.913 in in the model) or PE100 DN400 SDR11 (327.4 mm, 12.89 in), 3, 13 and 25 of the 2,000 untagged taps were put on the main at 5, 10 and 20 m with no flag. The cut stays where it is, so an internal diameter of 15.0 in or more is cut and one below it is not; since this pass an untagged tap put on a pipe from 12.8 in (0.8 of 16 in) up to the cut is flagged ('a pipe of 14.9 in, under the 15.0 in cut for mains: if it is a main of 16 in or more given by its internal diameter, and the tap is not on it, the nearest smaller pipe is ...'). On both mains every such tap is flagged, and the pipe the flag names is the tap's true pipe for 3 of 3, 11 of 13 and 17 of 25.
    - One node at a no-data value corrupted every suggestion on an otherwise clean model: the drawing's centre was the mean of every drawn node, so a node at -3.4e38, or at any other huge value, took the precision out of every position, and one at nan or inf made every position not a number, with reasons and flags that were then false. Before anything is measured, a node whose position is not a number, or is farther from the drawing's median position than 100 times the width of its middle half, is now treated as not drawn, with a warning naming it and its pipes; the centre is the median position of the kept pipes' end nodes; a pipe with a vertex that is not a number is left out; and the no-pipe flag says what happened. With the urban grid's J0505 at -3.4e38 or at nan, the fixture's four taps get the same junctions and snaps as on the clean drawing. The final verification's model fuzz, rerun after the fix, found no case where every tap got one junction or none (a scratch run, not a record). ky4's parts of the record are unchanged byte for byte.
    - In a file that writes decimal commas (a column with a decimal comma, or a file separated by semicolons), '1.197' meant to be 1197 was read as 1.197, and the suggestion looked right. Such a value is now not used, with its reason, unless its column settles it ('1.503,5', or a value of 1,000 or more written plainly, settles a thousands separator; '503.25' a decimal point), the mirror of the review's rule for '1,197'.
    - The degrees refusal wanted x within 180 and y within 90 on every node, so a drawing in degrees with latitude as x, or with one placeholder node at 9999,9999, was accepted as to scale in units that are neither feet nor metres. Both are refused: the test takes both axes within 180, after nodes far outside the drawing are set aside.
    - The east-north plane's tangent point was the mean of every tap, typos included. It is now the taps' median position. With one typo tap in a small tap map (the control taps, one test tap and the typo, for each of the 400 test taps), the largest error over the twelve zone, control-count and typo cases was 13.245 m with the plane at the mean, as first built (50 km, 4 control taps, latitude -40.01 for 40.01), and is 0.133 m now. The georeference's own numbers moved a little (finding 6).
    - Control taps along one avenue (6 of them, 4 km long, 55.8 m across in RMS, 3 m of GPS error on each) were accepted for a 4 km square zone with a generic warning only: on the record's draw, 305 of the 400 test taps are placed more than 10 m off and 112 more than 30 m off, the largest 42.24 m. Each tap now gets a predicted error, the control taps' GPS error (taken as 5 m) carried to it through the fit by its leverage, which grows with its distance from them and across their line; a tap predicted above 10 m is flagged, and a warning names those taps. 348 of the 400 are flagged, and every tap placed more than 10 m off is among them.
    - Longitudes written from 0 to 360 (259.4 for 100.6 W) were refused as not degrees. They are read west of Greenwich now, with a note, and the template written that way gives the template's junctions.
    - Control taps all at one place, or at two, were refused as 'inf times longer than wide'. Control taps all within 50 m of each other are refused now as taken at about the same place (four within 4 m of each other were accepted before), and control taps at only two places are refused in those words.
    - The leave-one-out step refitted the affine once per control tap, so a sample-site list with a junction and a position on every row took time that grew with the square of its length (the verifier's timings, a run cost, not a record). The residuals are in closed form now, each control tap's residual over one minus its leverage; on the moved control tap's case they equal one refit per control tap to the record's nine decimals, and the saved check times 4,000 control taps through `georeference` under 2 s. Found while testing this: a control tap whose leverage is over 0.99 (without it, the others lie almost on one line) got a meaningless residual of hundreds of metres and a flag, which told the operator to check a right tap; it is now listed as a control tap that cannot be checked, with a warning, and not flagged.
    - Not code: findings 1, 6 and 8 no longer quote the plan's probe figures or the review's memory readings, none of which is in outputs/; the record's `about` and finding 8 say that six checks rerun the record (five of the plan's six and `tap_coordinates_review_cases`); this section and the CHANGELOG say which internal diameters the cut covers (15.0 in or more) and which the new flag covers (12.8 in up to the cut).

**Numbers** (`outputs/intake/tap_snapping.json`, `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`, `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`; simulated GPS noise on a public network and an invented grid, so this is simulation only, never a utility's own taps):
