**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The pre-registered bar missed: 0.967 against 0.98, and 0.9775 with a right flagged second choice counted. Not tuned, not restated. Devansh's decision.
- The georeference's 1 m bar passes with hand-placed, well-spread control taps, not random ones. With one wrong control tap, the leave-one-out check flags 3 right ones too.
- The flags do not catch every wrong suggestion: 41 of 2,000 taps at 5 m get a wrong junction with no flag.
- 'Right junction' is the product's own rule applied to the true pipe, so the direction rule is not scored against a truth.
- The rule after the review is not the scored rule in three ways: the cut is 15.0 in on the model's diameter, pipes drawn out of proportion are left out, and a main close by is flagged. None of them changes ky4: its part of the record equals the scored run byte for byte.
- Not built: the pilot's `--taps` by coordinates and `--accept-snapped`, the app's table and confirm checkbox, the Get started page's entry, the shipped template and `pilot_suggested_tap_map`. The readiness row still calls tap mapping by coordinates 'planned'.
- `fixtures.build_all()` with no names (as `python -m residualmap.fixtures --record` calls it) builds the new fixtures too, which adds a little time; the task-15 record does not change.
- After the final verification: the georeference's numbers moved with the plane's tangent point (0.1011 m as scored, 0.1058 m now, over 50 km); a nominal 14 in pipe, or a metric DN350, now draws the under-the-cut flag too; a 16 in main given by an internal diameter under 12.8 in (polyethylene with thicker walls than SDR11) is neither cut nor flagged; a node more than 100 times the width of the drawing's middle half from its middle is set aside even when it is real, with a warning; in a file that writes decimal commas, a value with exactly three digits after its dot that nothing in its column settles is not used, with its reason, where a person might read it fine; and the predicted error assumes 5 m of GPS error on each control tap, whatever the real error.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.intake_exp snapping` writes `outputs/intake/tap_snapping.json` (about 10 seconds); run it from a folder outside the repository, because EPANET writes scratch files into the working directory while it runs. The six task-16b checks are part of the full run; `tap_snapping_ky4` and `tap_snapping_direction_invariant` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 20, a no-install route: the hosted demo (built and checked on this Mac; deploying it is Devansh's step)

**Asked:** (plan task 20, category f; critique 22 and 25) a way to use ResidualMap without installing Python, so the Get started page, the export guides and the templates reach every town. The plan weighed three routes: a hosted Streamlit demo (picked), a packaged desktop app (later, if a utility wants to run real data itself) and the app in the browser on Pyodide (rejected for now: WNTR's water-quality path calls EPANET's C library, which Pyodide cannot load). Build a cloud mode that shows the example networks only, with a privacy notice; uploads off by default, and when turned on, limited, processed in memory and in temporary files, and kept in the visitor's session only; the chloramine demo straight to its reduced truth; EPANET's files in a per-run temporary directory in cloud mode; an explicit spawn context for the process pools; every requirement pinned; a deploy guide and a privacy note. The lead's decisions: pin every line (open decision 7), uploads off (8), no committed grids (10). The lead's notes, after looking at task 16 in a browser: name the main page 'ResidualMap' in the sidebar without moving `app.py`, make the Get started intro follow the mode, and find out whether Community Cloud honours or overrides `server.address = "localhost"`, keeping the cloud reachable and the laptop on localhost. Deploying, signing in and creating accounts are Devansh's.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** Cloud mode is built and checked headless and in a real browser on this Mac; nothing is deployed. With `RESIDUALMAP_MODE=cloud`, or from the hosted demo's own start, the Model radio offers only 'Example network' (Net3 and Net2), neither page has a file uploader, both open with the privacy notice, and the default demo gives the local app's map (42 of 92 flagged, 36 of 36 found, 1 false alarm) with no EPANET file left in the working directory. With uploads allowed, a Net3 upload gives the same map from a grid built in memory and leaves no file under `outputs/` or in the temporary directory; ky4 (959 junctions) is refused before its grid is built, and a file over 5 MB once it reaches the app, before it is read. Since the review of task 20, a model over 300 junctions is refused by its junction lines before WNTR parses it (a 32,400-junction model in a zip of about 1 MB had cost about half a minute and 2.5 GB of memory before it was refused), a zip whose model unpacks past 5 MB before it is unpacked, and a grab log or tap map over 5 MB before it is read; with uploads off, no text on either page tells a visitor to upload. The sidebar now names the pages 'ResidualMap' and 'Get started' in both modes, and every older check passes. The open question on `server.address` is not settled: the hosted start has its own settings file, which Streamlit 1.64.0 reads after the repository's on this Mac, but Community Cloud's documentation says that a subdirectory app's settings file must stay at the repository's root, so whether that file applies on the platform is unverified until the smoke test. The cold start and the behaviour on Community Cloud itself wait for Devansh's deploy and the read-only smoke test. The final verification then found that a model under both limits could still cost minutes and gigabytes, that an Excel grab log escaped the limit once unpacked, and that the check of the cloud texts read too little (findings 13 to 16). Since its fixes a model is also held to what it costs, its intake and every EPANET run on it run in a process whose folder holds EPANET's own scratch file, a workbook is held to 20 MB unpacked, and the check reads every string the pages show.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/cloud.py` (new; no Streamlit import at module level, so `simulate.py` and the grid's workers can read the mode):
  - `mode`, `is_cloud`, `uploads_allowed`, `grid_workers`: the variable first ('cloud' or 'local', any other value cloud), then a checkout under `/mount/src/`, else local mode; uploads in cloud mode only with `RESIDUALMAP_ALLOW_UPLOADS` set to a true word; 2 grid workers in cloud mode, the old default in local mode.
  - The texts the pages show in cloud mode, plain (no task numbers, paths or flags): the notice (the plan's words), the Get started intro by mode (the local one is the text before task 20, word for word), 'Check a file' turned off, typed samples, the reduced chloramine truth, the last line, the limits' refusals.
  - `run_pages`: `st.navigation` with the two pages named 'ResidualMap' and 'Get started', the chosen page run, and this run's temporary folder deleted afterwards, however the run ends; `inside_page`, so `app.py`, run as its own main page, skips it.
  - `scratch_dir`, `temporary_folder`, `session_cached`: a per-run folder for an upload under the system's temporary directory, at one path per browser session; 'Check a file''s folder under `outputs/private/` locally and the system's temporary directory in cloud mode; a session-only cache that keeps at most 3 results per kind.
- `app.py`: the entrypoint names the pages and runs the chosen one; in cloud mode, the examples without ky4, the Model radio without the upload (unless allowed), the notice, typed samples with the demo off, the reduced chloramine truth, the last line and the link's label; a cloud upload goes through `cloud_upload` (prepared in the per-run folder, its prepared text kept in the session and written back each run) and `prepare_in_session` (the grid built in memory with `build_grid_24h`), and the water age, the demo truth, the loss split and the grab log's reading go through `run_cached`, which calls the cached function as before or, for a visitor's upload in cloud mode, its undecorated body kept in the session. The link to Get started is an absolute path. Local mode runs the same code as before.
- `pages/1_Get_started.py`: the notice, the intro by mode, 'Check a file' turned off in cloud mode unless uploads are allowed, then the 5 MB limit and a temporary folder outside `outputs/`.
- `hosted/streamlit_app.py` and `hosted/.streamlit/config.toml` (new): the hosted start, always cloud mode, with `server.address = "0.0.0.0"` and usage statistics off in its own settings.
- `residualmap/simulate.py`: `_run_sim`, the default-prefix EPANET run of `nominal_scenario` and the default truth, in a temporary directory in cloud mode only. `residualmap/simgp.py`: the spawn context, and `grid_data` for `SimGP24`. `residualmap/chloramine.py`: the spawn context for the demo's EPANET-MSX worker.
- `requirements.txt`: every line pinned to the `.venv` version.
- `docs/deploy_streamlit_cloud.md` and `docs/privacy.md` (new).
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task20` group of five checks (below); `IT5_FILES` holds the new files, and the names check reads them before their commit.
- After the review of task 20: `residualmap/importers.py`, `prepare_inp` takes limits (`max_junctions`, `max_model_bytes`, and `workers` for the run cost; none by default, so every local path is unchanged): a model over the junction limit is refused by its `[JUNCTIONS]` lines (`scan_inp_ids`) before WNTR parses it, and a zip's model over the size limit by its declared size before it is unpacked, each recorded in `flags['over_limit']`; `run_cost` caps the assumed speedup at the worker count when fewer workers are given. `residualmap/cloud.py`: `intake_limits`, `limit_refusal`, `too_big` for an unpacked zip, the guide's cloud words (`GUIDE_CLOUD`, `guide_section`), the Demo toggle's help, the note under 'What this model accounts for' without folder names, a reworded reduced-truth warning and footer. `app.py` and `pages/1_Get_started.py` pass the limits, refuse with them, hold the grab log and the tap map to 5 MB, give every cloud uploader its own 5 MB limit (`max_upload_size`) and show the cloud words; `hosted/.streamlit/config.toml` adds `server.maxUploadSize = 5`. `residualmap/fixtures.py`: `cloud_grid_inp` and `cloud_grid_zip`, an invented n by n grid model, for the limit checks.
- After the final verification of task 20:
  - `residualmap/importers.py`: `prepare_inp` takes more limits, none by default: `max_links`, `max_tanks_reservoirs` and `max_model_lines` (data lines of every section but the title and the drawing's, `UNSIMULATED_SECTIONS`), counted by `scan_inp_ids` in the text before WNTR parses it; `min_pattern_step_s` and `min_rule_step_s` (the rule step only when the file has `[RULES]`), read from WNTR's load before EPANET runs the model (`_step_limits`); `max_seconds_per_run` and `max_run_minutes`, after the timed 7-day run (`_run_limits`). Each refusal is recorded in `flags['over_limit']`, and nothing is kept. `read_table` takes `max_unpacked` (`workbook_parts`: a workbook over it unpacked, or with a part over 1 MB packed more than 100 to 1, is refused before openpyxl opens it), and `read_grab_log` and `read_tap_map` pass it on.
  - `residualmap/cloud.py`: the limits (600 links, 300 tanks and reservoirs, 20,000 lines, a 15-minute pattern step, a 1-minute rule step, 3 s a run, a 10-minute grid, 60 s for the intake, 20 MB for a workbook) in `intake_limits` and `table_limits`, a plain refusal for each (`REFUSALS`), the notice's limits sentence; `run_in_folder` and `child_main`, which run an importable function in a Python process of its own whose working and temporary folder is the run's folder, stopping it and the processes it started at a time limit; `prepare_upload`, the intake in that process, stopped after 60 s; `upload_grid`, `upload_truth` and `upload_truth_reduced`, the upload's grid, demo truth and reduced truth for that process.
  - `app.py` and `pages/1_Get_started.py`: a cloud upload's intake goes through `prepare_upload`; on the main page its grid, water age, demo truth and loss split go through `in_folder` (`run_cached`'s `job`); a grab log, a tap map and a checked table are read with `TABLE_LIMITS` in cloud mode. Local mode calls everything as before.
  - `residualmap/fixtures.py`: `cloud_parallel_pipes_inp`, `cloud_pattern_step_inp`, `cloud_rules_inp`, `cloud_controls_inp` (the 17 by 17 grid with one costly thing added) and `shared_strings_bomb_xlsx` (written with fixed times, so its bytes do not depend on the clock).
  - `residualmap/checks.py`: `mode_detection_fails_safe`, `cloud_mode_examples_only` and `cloud_mode_uploads_leave_no_files` gain the cases below; no new check.

**Found:**
1. The sidebar's name. Streamlit's file-based pages name the main page after its file ('app'). With `st.navigation` the names are set; `app.py` stays the entrypoint and runs itself as the default page, skipping the navigation call then (a thread-local flag in `cloud.py`), so `streamlit run app.py`, the Get started page's address (`/Get_started`) and every older AppTest check are unchanged. In AppTest, `switch_page` runs a page's file directly, so its page registry shows the old names there; the names are asserted on main-page runs, and both pages were opened in a real browser from `app.py` and from the hosted start.
2. `server.address` on Community Cloud. Its documentation lists the settings it overrides in an app's config.toml, and `server.address` is not among them; a forum report shows the platform checking an app's health at `localhost:8501` and failing while the project's config.toml moved `server.port`, so the platform reads a project's server settings. Whether visitors' traffic would reach an app that listens on localhost only is not documented. So the hosted start has its own settings file in its own folder, which Streamlit 1.64.0's loader reads after the repository's when that start is the main script: on this Mac the hosted start reads `0.0.0.0` and `streamlit run app.py` still reads `localhost` (`hosted_start_settings`). Corrected by the review: that is the loader on this Mac, not the platform. Community Cloud's file-organization page says that when the entrypoint is in a subdirectory, the configuration file must stay at the repository's root, so whether the hosted file applies there is unverified. If it does not, the hosted app runs with the root's `localhost`; the platform's health check on `localhost:8501` suggests it may still be reachable, but only the smoke test settles it. The deploy guide now says how to read the address from the app's log, and `cloud_smoke.json` will record it.
3. Usage statistics. The same documentation lists `browser.gatherUsageStats` among the settings Community Cloud sets for every app, so the hosted demo sends Streamlit's usage statistics whatever the settings files say. `docs/privacy.md` says so.
4. Secrets. Streamlit puts a root-level secret into the environment only when it is text or a number, so `RESIDUALMAP_ALLOW_UPLOADS = true` would never reach the app; the deploy guide writes it `"true"`, and the mode also reads 'True'. Secrets are loaded after the server's settings, so a secret cannot set `server.address` either.
5. The chloramine demo in cloud mode flags 27 of 92 on Net3 against the reduced truth, where the local app with EPA's chemistry in EPANET-MSX flags 24 of 92: two different hidden truths, the first labelled as flattering the model.
6. A precaution, not a measurement: a container can report the host's core count, and the grid's default is one worker per core but one, so cloud mode uses 2 workers to keep the memory of a small container.
7. Pre-existing text the hosted demo showed that named repository paths: the note under 'What this model accounts for' (`outputs/chem`, `outputs/chloramine` and `docs/iteration3_journal.md`, from `capabilities.ACCOUNTS_FOR_NOTE`) on every main page, and Net2's caption '(README, Net2)'. First left for a wording pass; after the review, cloud mode shows the note without folder names (`cloud.ACCOUNTS_FOR_NOTE`) and the caption without its pointer, while local mode keeps both as they were. The chloramine banner's '(README and journal, task 12)' appears only when the mode is experimental, which it is not.
8. The review of task 20: the 300-junction limit came after the whole intake. `prepare_inp` loaded the model with WNTR, ran the one-hour test and a full 7-day timed run, and only then did the app read the junction count; 'Check a file' had no junction limit at all. On the review's own 32,400-junction grid (a run log, not committed), the intake took about 32 s and 2.5 GB of memory before the refusal, as a 4 MB `.inp` and as a zip of about 1 MB, which passes the 5 MB file limit; two such uploads at once could exhaust a container of under 3 GB. Fixed: the junctions are counted in the text before WNTR parses it, on both pages, and the same model (now `fixtures.cloud_grid_zip(180)`) is refused in well under a second.
9. The review: the 5 MB limit did not hold on three routes. The grab log and the tap map were read at any size; a zip within 5 MB could unpack to 200 MB (`importers.ZIP_MAX_TOTAL`), and the review's 4.5 MB zip held an 18.8 MB model; and the hosted settings left Streamlit's 200 MB upload limit, so the server took a 200 MB file into memory before the app refused it. Fixed: all three, and each cloud uploader shows the 5 MB limit itself. Streamlit's own limit is in binary megabytes, a little larger than the app's decimal ones, so a file just over 5 MB can reach the app and is refused there.
10. The review: with uploads off, the Get started page still told visitors to upload ('upload it as it is', 'turn the demo off and upload two files', 'Go to step 3 ... Check a file'), named `docs/templates/`, and the Demo toggle's help said 'Off = upload your grab log'. Fixed with cloud words for each; a check now reads every text and help on both pages, every guide section included, and finds none that tells a visitor to upload.
11. The review: the privacy note said the app writes nothing of a visitor's to disk and the footer said nothing is kept after the session. With the example networks, results at a visitor's settings (the dose, the demo scenario, the decay rates calibrated on their typed samples) go into the caches every visitor shares, and a new dose writes a grid file. None of it says who the visitor is, and the texts now say what is kept.
12. The review: the reduced-truth warning, the README and the deploy guide said EPANET-MSX needs a C compiler the server lacks. It runs uncompiled without one (`msx.run`), in minutes, and nothing was measured on Community Cloud; the texts now say that whether the server has a compiler and MSX's runtime is untested. In cloud mode the readiness report's run cost also assumed a speedup of 4 with 2 workers; it now assumes 2 there and says why.
13. The final verification: a model under both the 5 MB and the 300-junction limits could still be very expensive, because its cost is not set by its junctions. On the 17 by 17 grid (289 junctions), with parallel pipes up to 4.7 MB the intake took 127 s and 3.7 GB; with a 1-minute pattern step (EPANET cuts its hydraulic step to the pattern step, and `simulate.load` sets only the hydraulic step) one 7-day run took 38 s, the page started the 675-run grid on 2 workers with no confirm, since a model is 'large' only above 3,000 junctions, and a 10-second step ran past 400 s (the verification's run logs, not committed). EPANET also writes a scratch file of its own (named 'en' and six letters) to the working directory of the process that runs it, which on the server is the repository's checkout, and deletes it only when a run ends normally: a stopped run left 536 MB there. Before choosing the limits, the 17 by 17 grid was timed with one thing added at a time under the old limits (a scratch run log, not committed): 600 links 3.2 min for the grid, 900 links 11.7, a 15-minute pattern step 5.3, a 5-minute step 8.3, 300 tanks 4.2, 300 rules checked every second 188.7 minutes but every minute 3.6, and 5 MB of curves or of timed controls ran past 90 s, while 5 MB of patterns gave 24.7 minutes and 5 MB of demands 11.0. Fixed: the text limits refuse the parallel pipes and the 5 MB sections before WNTR parses them, the time steps are held before EPANET runs, and the timed run is held to its bounds, all in a process stopped after 60 s; every EPANET run on an upload runs in a process whose working folder is the run's own, so the scratch file goes with the folder, as a watcher in the saved check shows. The verification proposed refusing any pattern or rule step under an hour; that would refuse the 15-minute patterns of many real models and nearly every EPANET export, which writes a rule step of a few minutes, so the floors are 15 minutes and 1 minute, and the timed run and the 10-minute bound catch what they let through.
14. The final verification: an Excel grab log is a zip too, and the 5 MB limit held only for the file as sent. The verification's workbook, under half a megabyte, whose shared strings unpacked to 190 MB, was read in 32 s and 1 GB (openpyxl loads the shared strings whole, even in read-only mode), on the main page and in 'Check a file'. Fixed: in cloud mode a workbook is held to 20 MB unpacked, and no part over 1 MB may be packed more than 100 to 1 (the saved check's copy of that workbook, 466,377 bytes, written with fixed times, is refused); a real 20 MB sheet or shared-strings part, timed before choosing, read in 2 to 3 s and under 210 MB (a scratch run log).
15. The final verification: the check that no cloud text tells a visitor to upload matched only the review's exact phrases, and read only six kinds of element; it caught 1 of 7 plain rewordings. Fixed: it reads every string of every element (bodies, labels, help, options, headers, expander and button labels, table cells) and matches any mention of uploading, 'Check a file' or dropping a file, apart from the three texts that say uploading and checking are not offered here; it catches all 7 rewordings.
16. The final verification: the regressions bullet on the numbers scan's power gave the wrong count of integer changes no longer caught (below, corrected).

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The hosted demo's main file is `hosted/streamlit_app.py`, not `app.py` as the plan's step 3 said, so that it can carry its own settings (finding 2). Anyone who runs `streamlit run hosted/streamlit_app.py` on a laptop without `--server.address localhost` opens that copy to the network; the deploy guide says to add the flag.
- Not checked on Community Cloud itself: the address, the secrets in the environment, the `/mount/src/` checkout, the 2 workers, the memory, the cold start. The smoke test after the deploy is the first evidence.
- The hosted demo's chloramine map is made against a different hidden truth from the local app's (finding 5), and it flatters the model, as its warning says.
- Every run of the main page now runs the top of `app.py` twice (once as the entrypoint, once as the page), imports and constants only; the heavy work runs once.
- Uploads in the hosted demo are built and checked but off; turning them on is Devansh's decision, after `docs/privacy.md`.
- Whether the hosted settings file (address 0.0.0.0, the 5 MB upload limit) applies on Community Cloud is unverified (finding 2). If it does not, the browser's own 5 MB limit on each uploader and the app's check still hold the uploads, but the server takes a file of up to Streamlit's 200 MB before the app refuses it.
- The first `post_task20` rerun was started before the last edits to `cloud.py` and `app.py`; the label was rerun on the final tree, after the review's edits, with the same result, and again after the final verification's fixes.
- After the final verification, the hosted demo refuses some models the local version takes: patterns that change more often than every 15 minutes, more than 600 links, rules checked more often than every minute, and any model whose grid, by its own timed run on the server, would take more than 10 minutes there, so a slow or busy server refuses more. The floors are not the verification's suggested hour (finding 13).
- Each EPANET step on a cloud upload now starts a Python process of its own, which adds its start-up time to each. The example networks still run in the server's own process, so their EPANET scratch files are still written to the server's working directory while each run lasts; they hold no visitor's data.
- The limits bound the cost of one upload, not how many arrive: there is no rate limit, and the intake process's memory is not capped, since a cap that depends on the platform could not be tested on this Mac.
- Local mode has none of the new limits, by design (every default unchanged): on the laptop a workbook that unpacks to far more than its size is still read whole, up to the 200 MB a zip may unpack to.
- The numbers scan's power on older numbers fell a little (would a one-unit change in a traced number's last printed digit be caught; measured with the journal and README as committed before task 20, against the outputs as committed then and as they are now, section by section, a scratch comparison): on the outputs after the final verification's fixes, 6 integer changes are no longer caught (3 in task 15b, and 1 each in tasks 10 and 10b and the README's iteration-5 limitations) and 2 are newly caught (in task 12), so 4 fewer in all, and 1 fewer decimal (task 9's 3.10). They move because the values task 20 adds to the checks report and the `post_task20` rerun entry (pass counts, byte sizes, limits) land next to older counts such as 108, 114 and 601. Corrected after the final verification: this bullet had said 21 fewer integer changes, where on the outputs as they were then 21 were no longer caught and 1 was newly caught, so 20 fewer. The scan matches an integer at plus or minus 0.5, so its power on counts was low already; the registry is the exact check, and task 20's numbers table is now in it, but no row was added for these older counts.

How to run: `RESIDUALMAP_MODE=cloud streamlit run app.py` shows cloud mode on the laptop (add `RESIDUALMAP_ALLOW_UPLOADS=true` for the uploads), and `streamlit run hosted/streamlit_app.py --server.address localhost` runs the hosted start on the laptop. The five task-20 checks are part of the full run; `cloud_mode_examples_only` and `cloud_mode_uploads_leave_no_files` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 16c, zone extracts: per-inlet residuals and leave-sites-out validation

**Asked:** (plan task 16c, category b; critique 20, the large-utility contact's data path) a pressure zone cut from a larger system has several inlets, each with its own measured residual, and its water is already days old when it enters; today every entry point is dosed at the one dose. Give each inlet its residual: an inlet residuals file (`inlet_node_id,residual_mgL`), `pilot --inlets`, an optional upload in the app with the demo off, and inlet analyzers from task 16's tap map (role `inlet`), whose median over the window sets the inlet's residual and whose rows never enter the calibration. Each inlet dosed at r_i times the dose, r_i = residual_i / the largest, so the dose input means the highest residual entering the zone, its label saying so only then; ratios all 1 take today's code path and cache name. Say that water age starts at 0 at every inlet. And leave-sites-out validation (`pilot --holdout-sites k`), because a fixed compliance route has no rotating taps for the protocol's 'new taps' number. Pre-registered bars (written to the build notes before the scored run): ratios (1, 1) give the cached grid, sha256 equal; at one member, ratios (1.0, 0.5) equal C(1, 0) + 0.5 C(0, 1) within 5e-5 mg/L at the file's quality tolerance and 1e-6 mg/L at 1e-9. The leave-sites-out table has no bar.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** Built and both bars met. On Net3 (two reservoirs, River and Lake), one member at ratios (1.0, 0.5) equals the sum of its single-inlet runs within 1.36e-05 mg/L at the file's tolerance and 1.19e-07 mg/L at 1e-9, the second at the resolution of EPANET's single-precision results; a fresh 675-run grid at ratios (1, 1) has the cached grid's sha256. With the demo off, the app takes an inlet residuals file behind a checkbox, relabels the dose input 'Highest residual entering the zone' and starts it at the highest residual; the pilot takes `--inlets` and `--holdout-sites`. On a synthetic fixed route of 10 taps, leaving 3 sites out at a time gives a mean monthly RMSE of 0.154 mg/L and coverage90 0.900 at the held-out sites, against 0.114 and 0.867 at the route's own taps a month ahead, with each held-out-site fit trained on fewer samples. A review then found and fixed a silent case (an inlet tap with no usable reading in the app's window was left out without a word), a loose role rule, unit and species gaps in the inlet file, the training sizes of the held-out-site rows, and the untested chloramine path, now refused (findings 10 to 17). Everything here is simulation on a public network with invented readings.

**Built** (its numbers are from simulation on Net3, a public network):
- `residualmap/simulate.py`: `simulate_nominal_chlorine(..., inlet_ratios=None, quality_tolerance=None)`; `normalize_inlet_ratios` (each ratio a finite number of at least 0, rounded to 6 decimals, sorted; none, or every ratio 1, gives None, today's code path) and `check_inlet_ratios` (one ratio per entry point, nothing else, checked as given, before the all-ones shortcut). With None every line of the run is the committed one.
- `residualmap/simgp.py`: `grid_cache_path(..., inlet_ratios)` adds `_inlets_<inlet_tag>` (a sha1 over the normalized ratios and `chemistry.cache_tag` of the grid and condition) only when ratios are given; `simulator_grid_24h` and `build_grid_24h` pass them to every member and refuse ratios that do not name the model's entry points before any run; `SimGP24(..., inlet_ratios)` keeps them for the loss split.
- `residualmap/age.py`: `loss_split` runs its four runs at the model's ratios; its `source_water_share` is NaN then (the sources carry different doses).
- `residualmap/importers.py`, a zone-inlet section: `read_inlet_residuals` (two columns guessed from the header or named, any encoding and separator, decimal commas, a µg/L header divided by 1000; a residual that is not a number, one below detection included, refused), `inlet_taps_of` (a tap map's `role` column; a cell whose words include 'inlet' marks a zone inlet), `combine_inlet_residuals` (the file and the inlet taps; an inlet in both refused), `inlet_ratios_from` (every entry point needs a residual; a name that is no entry point refused; all 0 refused) and `INLET_ZONE_NOTE`. Since the review: `is_inlet_role` (the role grammar), `GrabLog.inlet_taps` (every tap marked as an inlet, with or without a usable reading), `GrabLog.inlet_unused`, `inlet_tap_residuals` (a declared inlet with no usable reading in the months used, or at a node that is no entry point, refused), the inlet file read for the disinfectant's species, with its notes, and `mgl_text` (findings 10 to 12). `read_grab_log` keeps an inlet tap's readings apart (`GrabLog.inlets`, never in the frame, never thinned), and `GrabLog.inlet_residuals(months)` gives each inlet's median. A tap map without a role column reads exactly as before. The readiness warning for more than one entry point now says how to give each inlet's residual and that water age starts at 0 at every entry point.
- `residualmap/pilot.py`: `--inlets` (without `--dose`, the highest residual is the dose; a `--dose` more than 10% off it warns; refused with `--plant` and with `--synthetic`), inlet taps from the tap map (the median over the whole log, one set of ratios for the run; since the review, a declared inlet with no usable reading is refused, and per-inlet residuals are refused in the chloramine mode), a second size gate for the grid at the ratios; `validate(..., inlet_ratios, holdout_sites)`; `--holdout-sites K`: within each held-out month its sites are split into groups of K in an order drawn from `default_rng(50000 + seed)`, and each group is predicted at its own hours by the same model refitted on the same months without any of the group's samples (rows `held_out_sites`, with `sites`, `site_groups`, `sites_per_group` and, since the review, `n_train_min` and `n_train_max`; no persistence baseline, since a held-out site has no reading of its own in its fit; the predictions gain a `validation` column). Outputs are named `_inlets` and `_sites<K>`. The model's constructors moved into one helper used by both fits, with the committed calls unchanged.
- `app.py`: the dose input is written into its place in the sidebar once the inlets are known (a container, the same widget; since the review every stop before then draws the plain dose input first, `stop_page`); 'My zone's inlets carry different residuals', a checkbox after the tap map uploader, offers 'Inlet residuals (CSV)' (since the review, in free-chlorine mode only); the inlets are checked against the model's entry points; with them the dose input reads 'Highest residual entering the zone' and starts at the highest residual, the grid, the model and the loss split use the ratios, an info box names each residual and where it came from, and the water-age caption and the PDF say that water age starts at 0 at every inlet. In cloud mode a grid made from a visitor's inlet residuals is kept in their session (`cloud.upload_grid` takes the ratios), on an example network too.
- `docs/templates/inlet_residuals_template.csv` (new). `residualmap/fixtures.py`: the zone-inlet fixtures (a tap map with AN-1 as Lake's inlet analyzer, River's residual in a file, both residuals, the same in µg/L under headers to guess, three files to refuse, and the example tap map with T10 as Lake's inlet tap), written by hand, never by the readers. `residualmap/intake_exp.py zone` writes `outputs/intake/zone_extract_Net3.json`.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task16c` group of six checks: the plan's four, `inlet_ratios_identity`, `inlet_ratios_superpose`, `holdout_sites_fixed_route` and `analyzer_is_boundary_not_data`, and `pilot_inlet_residuals` and `app_inlet_residuals`. The cache hygiene check (`repo_untouched`) now also fails on a grid at inlet ratios left in the cache, and the template joins the files held to 'no em or en dashes'. The review's cases extend five of them (no new check): `analyzer_is_boundary_not_data` (findings 10 to 12, from the record), `inlet_ratios_superpose` (finding 13), `holdout_sites_fixed_route` (finding 14), `pilot_inlet_residuals` (finding 15, and the counts quoted below) and `app_inlet_residuals` (findings 10, 15 and 16); the record joins the files the dashes and names checks read before it is committed.

**Found:**
1. The scored member's wall rate, 0.70 m/day, is the truth's default (`intake_exp.MEMBER`, the member task 15b called a grid member), not a point on the grid's kw axis (0.60 and 1.00 are its neighbours). The scored run stands as pre-registered; after it, the record adds the same test at a member on every free-chlorine grid (kb 0.40, kw 0.60, gamma 1): 8.96e-06 mg/L at the file's tolerance and 1.19e-07 at 1e-9, within the bars too. The saved check also builds the 75-run decay grid at ratios (1.0, 0.5) and finds that member's run in it exactly, so the grid's own code path applies the ratios.
2. The error at 1e-9, 1.19e-07 mg/L, is the spacing of single-precision numbers near 1 mg/L: EPANET's results file stores concentrations in single precision, so this is the floor of the test, not EPANET's mixing.
3. A ratio dict that named one of two inlets, at ratio 1, was read as 'every ratio 1', today's path, and run without a word. Found by the superposition check's refusal case before any scored use; the entry points are now checked on the ratios as given, in `simulate_nominal_chlorine` and the grid builds and, since the review, in `SimGP24` and `pilot.validate` too (finding 13).
4. The dose axis stays an exact offset in the grid by construction (the dose is applied after the runs as a shift in ln C); EPANET itself agrees: at fixed ratios a run at 1.1 times the dose is 1.1 times the run within 9.81e-06 mg/L at the file's tolerance (2.38e-07 at 1e-9).
5. How much an inlet's residual matters, at the scored member: with Lake's residual half River's, 30 of 92 junctions' daily means move by more than 0.1 mg/L (the largest by 0.578 mg/L), and 42 junctions instead of 36 have a daily minimum below 0.2 mg/L. In the app, with no samples, Net3 flags 41 of 92 with those residuals, against 36 of 92 with one dose.
6. The cost of a wrong inlet file: the committed example log is the synthetic Net3 pilot, whose hidden truth doses both reservoirs alike; run with Lake at half River's residual, the pilot's held-out RMSE rises from 0.077 to 0.0997 mg/L. Inlet residuals must be measured, not guessed.
7. Leave-sites-out on the fixed route: each held-out month's 10 sites fall into groups of 3, 3, 3 and 1, so 12 refits in all, and every one of the 30 held-out samples is predicted once by a fit that never saw its site. The held-out sites' RMSE, 0.154 mg/L (the mean of the 3 held-out months' RMSEs, as the pilot reports it, not one RMSE over the 30 samples), is higher than the route's own taps' month-ahead 0.114. The gap mixes two things: a held-out site is new to its fit, and each group's fit also trains on fewer samples, since its sites' earlier samples are dropped (21 of the month's 30 for a group of 3, 27 for the group of 1; finding 14). Both are under half of the network-mean baseline (0.346 and 0.322); coverage90 is 0.900 and 0.867; 5 of 7 readings below 0.2 mg/L are found in both, with 2 and 1 false alarms. On the committed log, whose rotating taps give its 'new_taps' rows, the flag adds its rows and changes no other row or prediction.
8. An inlet analyzer's 2,880 readings leave the samples the model is fitted on (33 left, against 63 when the tap map has no role column) and their median, 0.519 mg/L, is the fixture's own median; with River's 1.20 mg/L from a file, r_Lake is 0.4325.
9. The template is not offered on the Get started page: the page's saved check pins its downloads at five, so adding a sixth would change an existing check. The app's help names the two columns; the pilot's help names the template.
10. The review: a tap the tap map marked as a zone inlet whose readings all fell outside the app's window (the grab rows' last 3 months), or were all below detection, gave its inlet no residual, and the page went on with every inlet at the one dose, without a word, while its note said the readings set the inlet's residual; the pilot, reading the whole log, did use them. Fixed: every tap marked so is kept on the log, and `inlet_tap_residuals` refuses, in the app and the pilot, an inlet with no usable reading in the months used, saying where its readings are (the record's case: 3 readings in January to March against a window of April to June, refused in the app's window and used over the pilot's whole log). Inlet readings outside the window are listed as not used, and the note gives the count only.
11. The review: any role cell containing the word inlet marked a zone inlet, so 'not inlet', 'non-inlet' and 'downstream of inlet' took those taps out of the fit and then stopped the page with a refusal that named a junction. A role now marks an inlet only when it reads as one ('inlet', 'zone inlet', 'inlet analyzer', 'Inlet 2'); a role that mentions an inlet but marks none is read as a tap's, with a warning (the record's 3 such roles: every row fitted); a tap marked as an inlet at a node that is no entry point is refused, naming the tap and its role.
12. The review: the inlet residuals file ignored the disinfectant (a total chlorine column was read as free chlorine residuals), noted none of its decimal commas, and read a µg/L value written with a thousands separator ('1,200') as 1.2 µg/L, which printed as 0.00 mg/L and dosed that inlet at almost nothing. Now a column naming the other species is refused, as in the grab log; decimal commas and the µg/L conversion are noted, on the page and in the pilot; a µg/L residual under 10 µg/L, and any residual above 0 under a hundredth of the highest, is refused as a unit slip; and a residual under 0.1 mg/L prints with 3 significant figures.
13. The review: finding 3's check ran in the simulation and the grid builds only. `SimGP24` and `pilot.validate` normalized the ratios first, so there a dict of ones that left out an entry point still ran as today's path; both now check the ratios as given. No caller passed such a dict (the app's and the pilot's ratios cover every entry point).
14. The review: the held-out-sites rows gave n_train as the month's whole training set, 30, though each group's fit drops its sites' earlier samples. They now give the smallest group fit's training set, with `n_train_min` and `n_train_max` (21 and 27 here), and finding 7 says what that does to the comparison. A group size that leaves a fit no training sample (10 on this route of 10 taps, which ran as a fit on the prior alone and reported RMSEs) is now refused, and one at least the month's number of sites warns.
15. The review: the chloramine mode took inlet residuals with no saved check, and its dose axis lets the effective dose fall to 0.65 times the dose, a range made for the plant's fast organic demand, which a residual measured where water enters a zone has already passed. It is not offered there now: ticked in the chloramine mode, the box says so and offers no uploader, a tap map that marks an inlet stops the page, and the pilot refuses `--inlets` with `--disinfectant chloramine`, and inlet taps in that mode.
16. The review: the dose input was drawn only after the model check, so every stop between the sidebar and it left the page without its dose input, the earlier stops too (a cloud file over the size limit, a model WNTR cannot read, a model with no entry point), not only an error in the inlet residuals. Every such stop now draws the plain dose input first, as before task 16c.
17. The review, smaller: the record's summary and the checks' docstrings called the scored member a grid member; they now say its kw is off the grid's kw axis. The pilot printed read_grab_log's 'the median over the window' for an inlet tap whose median it takes over the whole log; it now says the whole log, the held-out months included. The readiness warning now says where in the app to give inlet residuals, the how-to guide names the tap map's role column, and the record is among the files the dashes and names checks read before it is committed.

**Numbers** (`outputs/intake/zone_extract_Net3.json`, `outputs/chem/checks_report.json`, `outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`; Net3, a public network, and invented readings, simulation only):

| check | result | bar |
|---|---|---|
| ratios (1, 1) (`inlet_ratios_identity`) | normalized to today's path, cache name `grid24_full_Net3.pkl`, one member's .inp byte for byte today's; a fresh 675-run grid's sha256 equals the cached grid's | sha256 equal (met) |
| superposition, scored member (`inlet_ratios_superpose`) | at ratios (1.0, 0.5), 2,208 junction-hours: 1.36e-05 mg/L at the file's tolerance, 1.19e-07 mg/L at 1e-9 | 5e-5 and 1e-6 (met) |
| superposition, a member on the grid, added after the scored run | 8.96e-06 and 1.19e-07 mg/L | within both |
| the dose axis at fixed ratios, 1.1 times the dose | 9.81e-06 and 2.38e-07 mg/L | none |
| Lake at half River's residual, the scored member | 30 of 92 junctions' daily means move by more than 0.1 mg/L; daily minimum below 0.2 mg/L at 42 junctions, against 36 | none |
| leave 3 sites out, fixed route of 10 taps (`holdout_sites_fixed_route`), means over the 3 held-out months | held-out sites: RMSE 0.154 mg/L, coverage90 0.900, 5 of 7 below found, 2 false alarms (30 samples); the route's own taps: 0.114, 0.867, 5 of 7, 1; each held-out-site fit on 21 or 27 samples, the month's fit on 30 | none |
| the committed synthetic log, with and without the flag | RMSE 0.077, coverage90 0.923, every row and prediction unchanged; its held-out sites RMSE 0.068 mg/L, coverage90 0.974 | unchanged |
| inlet analyzer (`analyzer_is_boundary_not_data`) | 2,880 readings, 0 in the samples fitted (33 left; 63 without the role column); Lake 0.519 mg/L, r_Lake 0.4325; 4 of 4 refusals in plain words; the review's cases: an inlet tap read only before the window, or only below detection, refused; 3 roles that mark no inlet read as taps; 3 more inlet files refused | rows absent |
| the pilot (`pilot_inlet_residuals`) | dose taken as 1.2 mg/L, the grid at the ratios built under its own name and removed; held-out RMSE 0.077 without the file, 0.0997 with Lake at half; an inlet tap leaves its 6 readings out (72 of the 78 samples fitted); 6 of 6 refusals before any grid, the chloramine mode's two among them | none |
| the app (`app_inlet_residuals`) | uploaders unchanged until the box is ticked; 'Highest residual entering the zone' at 1.2; Net3 with no samples flags 41 of 92, against 36 of 92; cloud mode: no box with uploads off, and with uploads on the same map with no file written under `outputs/`; since the review, a stop before the inlets (an inlet file missing Lake, an inlet tap read only before the window, a cloud file over the size limit) shows the plain dose input, and the chloramine mode offers no inlet upload | none |
| the saved checks, full run | 120 of 120 pass, the six task-16c checks among them: 119 in the committed report, and the names check (run with the private list set) in the git-ignored run log | all pass |
| the four committed reruns and the app's default demo (label `post_task16c`) | 50 of 50 files byte-identical; default demo 42 of 92 flagged, 36 of 36 found, 1 false alarm | unchanged |

**Regressions, recorded not hidden** (simulation only):
- `outputs/intake/readiness_bundled.json` changes: the two-inlet warning of Net3 and ky10 now says how to give each inlet's residual and that water age starts at 0 at every entry point, where it said a residual per inlet was planned. Text only; no number moves.
- A constant ratio per inlet is an approximation: inlet residuals vary through the day, and a zone's water is older than its age map shows, as the app and the PDF say when inlet residuals are given, and the readiness report for any model with more than one entry point (a single-inlet zone, or one run without inlet residuals, gets no such caveat on the page). The pilot takes an inlet tap's median over the whole log, one set of ratios for every held-out month, where the app takes the median over its 3-month window, so with inlet taps the pilot's held-out numbers use the held-out months' inlet readings too, as a boundary condition known when a map is made (as the plant log's temperature is).
- Every entry point needs a residual, or none: a zone with one unmeasured inlet cannot be run with the others' residuals.
- Per-inlet residuals are not built with a plant log (the temperature model's grids), nor for the hosted demo's typed samples (the box appears only where uploads do), nor, since the review, in the chloramine mode (finding 15: a dose range for measured inlet residuals, with its own check, is later work).
- Since the review, a tap the tap map marks as a zone inlet with no usable reading in the months used is refused, not ignored (finding 10): a log whose inlet readings stop before its grab samples' last 3 months needs an inlet residuals file instead. And the role grammar is narrow: a role such as 'zone A inlet' or 'inlet from the tank' marks no inlet, with a warning (finding 11).
- The held-out sites' numbers are on one synthetic route of 10 taps on one network, 30 samples; they show the method, not a utility's accuracy, and each held-out month costs the pilot one refit per group of sites.
- The dose input is now drawn after the samples section, in its old place in the sidebar. Before the review, every stop between the sidebar and the model check (an error in the inlet residuals, and also a cloud file over the size limit, a model WNTR cannot read, a model with no entry point) left the page without it; each now draws the plain dose input first (finding 16).
- The Get started page does not offer the inlet residuals template (finding 9).

How to run: `python -m residualmap.pilot --inp <zone.inp> --samples <log> --taps <tap map> --inlets <inlet residuals.csv>` and `--holdout-sites 3`; `python -m residualmap.intake_exp zone` (from a folder outside the repository) rewrites the record. The six task-16c checks are part of the full run; `inlet_ratios_superpose` and `analyzer_is_boundary_not_data` also run with `--quick`. The review's fixes were checked by rerunning the record (every scored number unchanged; only the summary, the analyzer's note, the held-out-site rows' training sizes and the review's cases moved) and the six checks, then the full run.

### Task 17, a GIS importer: a skeleton model from a mains layer

**Asked:** (plan task 17, category c; critique findings 2, 3, 8, 11 to 14 and 24) read a utility's GIS layer of its water mains, as a GeoPackage, a zipped shapefile, GeoJSON or a Google Earth KML or KMZ, all in memory, and build a skeleton EPANET model from it: join the line ends that meet, cut a line where another ends on it, give every node and pipe an ID EPANET accepts that does not depend on the order of the features, read the pipe sizes from text as utilities write them, take the lengths from the geometry in true units, and say plainly that the skeleton is not ready to run until its source, tanks, pumps, valves, controls and demand are given. Read a valve layer's normally closed valves as proposed rows for the later operator form, and WNTR's own GIS schema with its JSON file, for WNTR users. All choices on the Get started page, the prepared model handed to the main page as a third Model option. Eleven saved checks, one pre-registered bar: on four utility-style layers of ky4's mains, at least 1,151 of 1,156 pipes rebuilt at the 6 ft default, scored by endpoint-pair identity, one connected part and 99% of diameters, on every layer. One new package, pyshp 3.1.6. And the seven leftovers of task 16c's final verification, each as a fixture in its task-16c check. Why it matters: replies this week from people who work with small systems said those systems rarely have a hydraulic model, so a model built from the GIS map of their mains is the route for them.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** Built, and the bar is met on every layer, with no miss to spare: 1,151 of 1,156 ky4 pipes rebuilt at 6 ft, one connected part, and every rebuilt pipe's diameter within 1% (`outputs/intake/gis_roundtrip.json`). The five misses are the same on all four layers: the two pipes between J-702 and J-703, which ky4 draws closer together than the tolerance, and three pipes that pass 0.104, 0.117 and 0.601 ft from a node where other pipes end without being joined to it in ky4.inp, which a mains layer cannot tell from a tee. Lines drawn by hand in Google Earth, each end about 5 m off, rebuild 1,060 pipes at the 10 m default and 1,071 at 15 m (no bar). WNTR's GIS schema with its JSON, in files our own writer lays out as WNTR's to_gis does, rebuilds Net3, Net2 and ky4 with 0.0 difference in pressure, flow, tank head, water age and chlorine, and without the pattern workaround Net3's chlorine moves by up to 0.762 mg/L, so the test can fail; no file WNTR itself wrote was tested. Every result is converter self-consistency on public models converted to GIS by our own writers; no utility's own layer was read, and the public open-data layer the plan allowed was not downloaded (the lead's decision), so its check is skipped and says so.

**Sources.** Paper Lantern was not available in this session, so there is no Paper Lantern attribution. The formats follow their public specifications (the ESRI shapefile and dBASE table, the GeoPackage geometry header, ISO WKB, GeoJSON's RFC 7946, KML). Hazen-Williams C for new pipe is the middle of each range of the EPANET 2 Users Manual (Rossman 2000), Table 3.2, which names cast iron, concrete or concrete lined, galvanized iron, plastic, steel and vitrified clay only: putting ductile iron, asbestos cement and PCCP in the concrete-lined class (130) and HDPE, PE and PEX in the plastic class (145) is our own choice, not the table's. Web Mercator is converted by the spherical inverse that defines EPSG 3857.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/topology.py`, new, shared with the CAD importer to come: `build_network` joins line ends with scipy `cKDTree.query_pairs` and union-find, in float64 and in metres; splits again any node whose ends spread wider than twice the tolerance (complete linkage at the tolerance) and flags one wider than the tolerance; cuts a line where another line ends on its interior, at its nearest vertex only when that vertex is within the tolerance of every end that asked for the cut (since the review; else at the foot), with ends whose feet lie within the tolerance of each other sharing one cut only when its point is within the tolerance of each; cuts a closed line longer than twice the tolerance in two through a new node (since the review); drops any other piece whose two ends fall into one node and lists it; refuses a layer whose line ends crowd into a few squares of the tolerance before anything is joined (since the review); gives node IDs from the rounded position (N<x>_<y> in the layer's unit, or N<lat>_<lon> at 5 decimals) and pipe IDs from the layer's own ID field or a hash of the two node IDs and the length, every one cleaned for EPANET through a mapping table; and reports the connected parts, dead ends, ends joined, tees cut, nodes flagged and split, pieces dropped and lines drawn twice. `diagnostics` gives the same counts at half, one and twice the tolerance. `parse_diameter` and `bare_unit` read pipe sizes; `hazen_c` gives C from the material; `write_inp` writes the skeleton in GPM and feet with 12 significant digits. Never `wntr.gis.connect_lines`.
- `residualmap/gis_read.py`, new: the four readers, all in memory, through the importer's zip limits (a GeoPackage through an in-memory SQLite copy with a step budget, its WKB with Z, M and ZM and a MultiLineString's parts kept apart; a zipped shapefile through pyshp on the members' bytes, never a path, its unit from the .prj's last top-level UNIT, its encoding from the .cpg or latin-1 with a warning, null shapes and deleted records dropped with their attributes; GeoJSON and Esri JSON with a crs member honoured or longitude and latitude checked, and coordinates written with few decimals warned; KML and KMZ, WGS84 only, a document type declaration refused before parsing); longitude and latitude through task 16b's east-north plane; `guess_fields` for the size, material, install year, declared length, pipe ID, C and valve status; `valve_layer`, which finds the valves in the line layer's own points or in a separate layer of points beside it; `build_from_layer` and `build_gis`, which ask what the layer does not say (the coordinates' unit, the unit of bare sizes, whether a layer of a chosen unit is to scale, with an optional check against known lengths); `proposed_closed_valves`; `readiness`, the skeleton's report with the layer's rows first; and the WNTR-schema route (`wntr_schema_dict`, `wntr_schema_inp`).
- `pages/1_Get_started.py`: step 4, 'Build a model from a GIS layer', after step 3, so every widget before it keeps its place: the layer's sentence, the diagnostic table, warnings, the IDs changed for EPANET, proposed closed valves, the readiness report, 'Download the model (.inp)' and 'Open it on the main page', which keeps the model in the browser session (and in local mode writes it to `outputs/app_uploads/`) and opens the main page. The GIS sections of step 1 are no longer marked coming soon, and point to step 4.
- `app.py`: while a model prepared on the Get started page is in the session, the Model radio offers 'Model prepared on Get started'; the model goes through the same intake as an upload, and a skeleton stops at its readiness report with a sentence that says why. Without one, the radio is the one before this task.
- `residualmap/importers.py`: a GIS file's advice now names step 4 (the first sentences are unchanged). `residualmap/cloud.py`: three guide swaps for the GIS sections in the hosted demo with uploads off. `docs/how_to_export_your_model.md`: the GIS sections. `requirements.txt`: pyshp 3.1.6, installed into `.venv` from PyPI.
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: the GIS fixtures, written by the fixtures' own shapefile, dBASE, WKB and GeoPackage writers, GeoJSON and KML text, and the Lambert conformal conic inverse of task 16b; only `ky4_mains_pyshp.zip` is written by pyshp, the library that reads it, and is labelled same-library self-consistency. `residualmap/intake_exp.py gis` writes `outputs/intake/gis_roundtrip.json`.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task17` group of twelve checks, the plan's eleven (`gis_utility_style_rebuild`, `gis_kml_handdrawn`, `gis_spec_quirks`, `gis_snapping_invariance`, `gis_snapping_chain`, `gis_ids_deterministic`, `diameter_parsing`, `gis_readers_refuse_cleanly`, `gis_valve_status`, `gis_wntr_schema_roundtrip`, `gis_external_file`) and `get_started_gis_import`, a headless test of step 4 and the main page's third option, in local and cloud mode. The new files join the files the dashes and names checks read.
- Task 16c's leftovers, each with a fixture in its check: a tap marked as a zone inlet with an empty junction cell is refused naming the tap (it was dropped as an inlet without a word); an inlet tap's readings in a µg/L log are counted in the µg/L and decimal-comma notes, and one above 0 and under 10 µg/L is refused as a unit slip ('1,180' read as 1.18 µg/L); an inlet residuals file's second column is read only when it names chlorine or a residual, and a column that names pH, NH2Cl or monochloramine is refused; an inlet tap's daily value with a date and no time is kept, and a date that cannot be read is listed as the inlet's, so the refusal says why instead of 'the grab log has no reading of it'; the pilot's leave-sites-out refusal is a plain message before any fit, never a traceback; the pilot's readiness warning names its own way to give inlet residuals; and the CHANGELOG's per-inlet bullet says the app takes the median over its window and the pilot over the whole log.

**Found** (converter self-consistency on public models):
1. The bar is met with no miss to spare, and the five misses are the ones geometry cannot avoid. P-696 and P-625 join J-702 and J-703 into one node, as the plan measured; P-152, P-1126 and P-9 pass 0.104, 0.117 and 0.601 ft from a node where other pipes end (J-198, J-86 and J-836, measured in ky4.inp's own geometry and recorded since the review), but ky4.inp does not connect them there. In a mains layer such a crossing looks exactly like a tee, so the line is cut and joined (six of the 8 extra pipes, two for each), which is the right reading of a layer and the wrong one of this model. The other two extra pipes are P-625's halves: a loop between J-702 and J-703, whose two ends the tolerance puts in one node, it was dropped before the review and is now cut in two through a new node, so its length of main stays in the skeleton, as a loop on the merged node; P-696, shorter than the tolerance, is still dropped.
2. The tolerance table matters. At 1 ft and 3 ft the 4 ft undershoots stay apart (1,135 and 1,133 pipes, 7 connected parts); at 6 ft they join. For hand-drawn KML, 5 m leaves 138 parts and 658 pipes, the 10 m default 6 parts and 1,060 pipes, 15 m one part and 1,071, and 20 m starts to join ends that should stay apart (1,059). No one tolerance fits every layer, so the page always shows half, one and twice the chosen one.
3. The four layers give the same network: the same counts at every tolerance, the shapefile written by pyshp with 3 null shapes and 1 deleted record included, whose extra records leave every diameter aligned and add no pipe.
4. Union-find chains as critique 13 said: a 5 ft plus 5 ft chain joins three ends 10 ft apart (flagged), and a valve cluster of five ends 4 ft apart and a pump manifold of four ends 5 ft apart are split into 3 and 2 nodes. A two-link chain can never be wider than twice the tolerance, so the plan's chain is flagged, not split; the cluster and the manifold are split.
5. WNTR 1.5.0's `from_dict`, which `from_gis` goes through, reads a junction's pattern from 'pattern_name', and WNTR's GIS schema writes 'demand_pattern': without the copy, Net3's chlorine moves by up to 0.762 mg/L (mean 0.249). That much is read from WNTR 1.5.0's own code. The files the check reads are laid out by our own writer as WNTR's `to_gis` does (it needs geopandas, which is not installed here), and that writer keeps only each element's base and optional attributes, where `to_gis` keeps every plain column; so the route is tested on our emulation only, never on files WNTR itself wrote.
6. The first build of the ky4 fixture had 22 runs drawn through tees, not 43: the selection loop counted both pipes of a run. Found by counting the fixture's records before the scored run, and fixed there.
7. A skeleton's copy that 'Open it on the main page' writes to `outputs/app_uploads/` is removed again by the main page's intake, the rule for any model that cannot run; the session keeps the model, so nothing is lost. Net3 read from WNTR's GIS files runs on the main page and flags 42 of 92, as the example Net3 does.
8. The install year is read and shown but does not change C: no aging table was checked for this build, so C is the new-pipe value of the material, and no pipe's age reaches the model. The old-pipe factor cannot carry it either: `simulate.roughness_factor` scales wall decay by C alone, as 2 to the power of minus gamma times (C minus 130) over 30, with one gamma for the whole network, so it sees only the differences between materials. For cast iron those run opposite to aging: at its new-pipe C of 135 it gets less wall decay than ductile iron, asbestos cement or concrete at 130, where old unlined cast iron decays chlorine fastest. Carried to task 19, which measures the cost of each missing input. A layer with no material field gets one C everywhere (130), so task 15b's fixed-gamma rule applies.

**After the review** (converter self-consistency on public models; each issue was checked against the files, most with the reviewer's own scripts, before it was fixed; each fix of an issue has a fixture in a task-17 or task-16c check; the bar's three measures did not move):
- A coordinate that is not a number stopped the page: the Get started page called `build_from_layer` bare, and one vertex of NaN, infinity or 1e300 raised inside cKDTree (a GeoPackage in longitude and latitude whose lines were all empty raised IndexError). Every reader now leaves out a part with such coordinates, with a warning, and `build_from_layer` never raises: anything it did not foresee is a plain refusal. `bad_vertices.gpkg` builds its 24 good lines, on the page too.
- A layer in longitude and latitude with no coordinate system (a shapefile with no .prj) could only be read as feet, US survey feet or metres; each put the whole layer inside a foot, every piece was dropped, an empty model was offered for download, and joining took time and memory quadratic in the lines (the reviewer measured 15 s and 3.4 GB for 1,740 lines). The unit question now offers degrees and says when every coordinate lies in longitude and latitude ranges; a layer whose line ends crowd into a few squares of the joining distance (more than 200 ends in one, or 20 on average) is refused before anything is joined; and a layer that builds no pipe, or would lose most of its pieces, is refused. `lonlat_no_prj.zip`, 1,740 lines, is asked, refused as crowded within 5 s when read as feet, and one connected part in degrees. Longitude and latitude labelled EPSG:3857 is refused as crowded, and State Plane feet labelled EPSG:4326 is asked as mislabelled.
- Web Mercator was read as ground metres, and the readiness report said the lengths were in true units. It is now converted to longitude and latitude by its own spherical inverse (`mercator_3857.geojson`, and the same lines in a shapefile with ArcGIS's .prj), and any other Mercator is refused with what to export instead.
- A tee could disconnect the branch it was cut for: the cut moved to a vertex near the foot even when that vertex was farther than the joining distance from the branch end, and feet close together along a line took the middle one's cut. A cut now moves to a vertex only when every end that asked for it is within the joining distance, and close feet share one cut at their middle only when each end is within reach of it, else each gets its own. ky4's scored layers have two-point lines, so this moved no ky4 count.
- A loop main drawn as one closed line was dropped as a pipe from a node to itself. A closed piece longer than twice the joining distance is now cut in two through a new node, which keeps ky4's P-625 (Regressions).
- KML sizes are taken from a name or description only when most of them carry a unit, so paths their author numbered 'Pipe 2' give no size and it is asked, and an attribute table in a description (ArcGIS's Layer To KML) is read as fields, so its DIAMETER is found.
- Smaller: a layer of points over the feature cap beside the mains is skipped with a note instead of refusing the file; Esri JSON over the cap is refused (it was cut short without a word), a spatialReference given as its code alone is read, and features whose positions cannot be read are counted and warned; a zip's members are read with one opening of the zip, at most 100 shapefiles from one zip; a shapefile zip with a preview .kml is the shapefile; two shapefiles of one name keep their folders in their layer names, and the page picks a layer by position; the hosted demo reads a GIS zip within its upload limit unpacked and refuses a layer over its size before any line is joined; an install-year field with no year says so; a size labelled twice in nominal equivalents ('6" (150mm)') is one size, and a coupling of one size is that size, not a reducer; the Hazen-Williams, Install year and Lengths rows say what they rest on; the readiness report and the main page say where the missing parts can be added; the Get started intro names step 4; and an inlet file's second column that is only a unit or names a residual is read again. Two KML quirks that the reviewer's scripts showed beside the issues are read too, with no fixture of their own: a blank line before the XML declaration, and 'lon, lat' written with a space.
- Text: the motivation no longer counts replies or claims how common a missing model is; Found 5 and the WNTR-schema claims say the files were laid out by our own writer; Found 8 says what the code does with age; the departures from the plan are listed (Regressions); and the near-node distances behind the three crossing misses are now in the record.
- The task's report said the pre-registration was edited at 03:55; its file was written once, at 03:48, and not changed afterwards, so that claim is withdrawn (the bar it holds is the plan's own text). The four committed reruns were reproduced again on the final tree (label `post_task17_review`, below), since the first reproduction (`post_task17`) ran before the first build's last edits.


**After the final verification** (the final verifier confirmed fourteen issues after the fix pass, two of them major; each was reproduced first, with the verifier's own scripts where it had them, then fixed, and each fix has a fixture or a case in the new check `gis_final_verification`, part `final` of the record, or in `get_started_gis_import`; the bar's three measures and the scored record did not move):

*Asked:* fix what the verifier confirmed. The two major issues were reading costs: three KML patterns took time quadratic in a run of spaces or of '<' (a KMZ of about 5 KB within the hosted demo's 5 MB unpacked limit would have run for hours, holding the interpreter lock, so every session of the server stalls with it), and the coordinate system reader took time cubic in a run of spaces around a comma (28 s for a .prj zipped in under 1 KB). Both run before any limit of the hosted demo. The minor ones: a large layer of service lines (or points declared GEOMETRY) beside the mains refused the whole file; World Mercator named by its code alone was asked for its unit and then read as ground metres; latitude written first under EPSG:4326 built near the pole with wrong lengths and no warning; longitude and latitude under a State Plane .prj was refused as crowded with no control on the page to change the unit; several branch ends close together on a main drawn with dense vertices could be left in a node of their own; a loop main whose ends join through a third line's end was dropped; 'Line 3 in Oak St' gave 3 in; epoch milliseconds gave wrong install years; the KML document type guard looked at the start of the text only; a zipped GeoPackage got a refusal that contradicted itself; and the fuzzer's leftovers (a size of 400 nines read as inf and written into the model, '8"x8"x6"' read as 8 in, and four structural oddities that reached the page's generic guard as an AttributeError).

*Built:*
- `residualmap/gis_read.py`: every text a reader scans is read in one pass. A KML's coordinates are split on spaces with 'lon, lat' rejoined token by token, under a budget of points a file; tags are stripped and a description's attribute table parsed by scanning tag to tag, never by a pattern that can restart inside a run; a coordinate system definition's items are found by a scanner that skips spaces, commas and quoted names. A KML name or description is cut at 64 KB and a definition over 64 KB is not read. A document type or entity declaration is refused wherever it is, by an expat pass whose only handlers stop at it, before any entity is expanded.
- Layers over the feature cap are left unread whatever their type, with a note naming them, and a layer chosen by name among them is refused saying to export the mains alone. A zip holding one GeoPackage, GeoJSON or KML and no shapefile is read as that file; two such files are refused saying to unzip.
- A Mercator named by its code alone (World Mercator and eight other codes) is refused as one named by its definition is, before any unit question. A layer whose coordinates fit longitude and latitude only with their two numbers swapped, its median latitude beyond 75 degrees or most of it out of range, asks their order ('axes'; the page's 'Order of the coordinates'); swapped, it is range-tested again. A layer refused as crowded, or in longitude and latitude ranges while its file names a projected system, names degrees in the refusal, and the page offers 'Unit of the coordinates' with the file's own unit first.
- KML sizes take 'in' as inches only when no word follows it; install years are read by `year_of` (dates, epoch milliseconds, yyyymmdd, or a year standing alone in a text); a crs member whose properties are no object, Esri attributes or paths of the wrong type, and GeoPackage text that is not UTF-8 are read in plain words.
- `residualmap/topology.py`: a tee cut more than the tolerance from both ends of the line it cuts keeps its two piece ends and the ends that asked for it in one node when a wide node is split (complete linkage over units, `_split_units`); a piece longer than twice the tolerance whose two ends the snapping puts in one node is cut at its middle through a new node, as a closed line already was; a size that overflows a float is unreadable, a text over 500 characters is unreadable, and three sizes joined by x (quoted or not) are a reducer unless all equal.
- `residualmap/importers.py`: an inlet residuals file's second column named 'Cl', 'FAC', 'Free', 'FCl', 'Res' or 'Resid', or the unit 'mg_l', is read again, and a species it names is checked as any other.
- `residualmap/importers.py` also writes the Get started page's Excel template with fixed time stamps (`XLSX_FIXED_TIME`). openpyxl stamps the time of saving, to the second, in the workbook's properties and its zip entries, so the template download changed whenever the page's reruns crossed a second; task 20's check of the hosted demo's texts counts the distinct strings on both pages (1,128), and in this pass's first full run, on a loaded machine, it counted one more, a second media address of the template, so the numbers check failed on task 20's quoted count. With fixed stamps the count no longer depends on timing.
- `pages/1_Get_started.py`: the axes and unit selectboxes above; `residualmap/fixtures.py`: 28 fixtures (`FINAL_FIXTURES`); `residualmap/intake_exp.py`: part `final`; `residualmap/checks.py`: `gis_final_verification`, and three more cases in `get_started_gis_import`.

*Found:*
1. With the readers linear, each of the verifier's files builds or is refused within a second, process start included, and the hosted demo's page builds the 4.9 MB KMZ's 8 pipes within the check's 30 s bound, AppTest's own run included. The fixtures' KMZ is 5 KB on disk and its .prj zip under 1 KB.
2. Keeping every tee end with its cut cost the hand-drawn KML 3 pipes at the 10 m default and one connected part: there, many line ends that ask for a cut are junctions drawn a few metres off, whose cut lands within the tolerance of the cut line's own end, and the free split groups them better. So the rule keeps only true tees together, a cut more than the tolerance from both ends of its line; then every one of the verifier's 32 configurations of branches is one connected part, and the KML at 10 m is unchanged (1,060 pipes, 6 parts), with one pipe fewer at 5 m and at 15 m and two fewer parts at 5 m (Regressions). A narrower rule, re-splitting only when a branch end's node held no piece of the line it cut, was worse on both: such a node can hold a piece end of a piece that is then dropped.
3. A Total or TRC column of an inlet residuals file was read as free chlorine before task 17 (any second column was); now it is read as a residual and refused by the species check in free-chlorine mode, saying it is total chlorine (it is read in the chloramine mode).
4. The bare three-size texts '6x6x4' and '12x12x8 TEE' gave the first size before the review too (only the quoted '8"x8"x6"' changed with the coupling rule); all three are reducers now, flagged and given the commonest size with a warning.
