**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The skeleton cannot run: a mains layer carries no source, tank, pump, valve, control or demand, so the main page stops at its readiness report. Until the operator form (planned) is built, they can only be added to the downloaded .inp in EPANET, as the readiness report and the main page now say. Every junction is at 0 ft, which is harmless only while the model has no pressure-control valve.
- A line that passes within the tolerance of a node where other lines end is joined there, so a crossing drawn without a break at a node becomes a tee (three of ky4's pipes). The layer's sentence counts the cuts at a tee (it said 'lines cut', but a line can be cut more than once), and does not tell a tee from a crossing.
- C comes from the material only, at the new-pipe value, and the install year is shown and unused, so no pipe's age reaches the model (Found 8: the old-pipe factor scales wall decay by C alone, and new-pipe C puts cast iron above ductile iron, the opposite of aging; carried to task 19). The material classes for ductile iron, asbestos cement, PCCP and the plastics are ours, not the EPANET table's. A layer without a material field has one C everywhere.
- Departures from the plan, not disclosed before the review: a size flagged as a reducer, as two sizes or as unreadable is given the layer's commonest size, with a warning naming how many and why, where the plan said anything ambiguous is asked (only bare numbers whose range fits no unit are asked); and node IDs come from the rounded position only, not from facility IDs (hydrant or valve IDs) as the plan offered.
- Only the readers' own formats: GML and Esri file geodatabases are not read (each refused with what to export instead), KML is WGS84 only, and only a few EPSG codes are known by their unit; any other is asked.
- Existing checks changed with the page: `get_started_page_runs` now counts 2 sections marked coming soon, not 5 (the GIS ones are read now); `mode_detection_fails_safe` counts 7 guide swaps for the hosted demo, not 4; `cloud_mode_uploads_leave_no_files` expects the page's two uploaders (step 3's and step 4's, each at the demo's 5 MB) with uploads allowed, where it expected one; `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text` reads the new files too, and `requirements_pinned` counts 12 pinned lines, pyshp's added. Those four change their committed details; `cloud_mode_uploads_leave_no_files`'s is unchanged. The numbers script traces task 20's quoted 11 requirement lines and 4 guide swaps to today's report less task 17's pyshp line and its three GIS swaps.
- Task 16c's leftovers change behaviour where they were wrong: a tap marked as an inlet with no node ID, an inlet reading in µg/L under 10 µg/L and an inlet file's second column that is not a residual are now refused; an inlet tap's daily value with no time of day is now kept. No committed record changed. As the review left it, an inlet file of two columns whose second column names neither chlorine nor a residual nor is only a unit is refused, asking for residual_mgL: 'value', 'reading', 'result', 'mean', 'typical' and 'FCl' were read before task 17 and are refused now; 'mg/L', 'ppm' and 'flow-paced residual', refused by the first fix, are read again.
- The Get started page's intro now names its fourth step (since the review; it said three steps only, and was left so because a hosted-demo check pinned its text): `mode_detection_fails_safe` compares the local intro with the text before task 20 plus that sentence, and the hosted demo's intro with uploads allowed says it too (with uploads off there is no step 4, and the intro is unchanged).
- The review changed the scored record where its fixes reach ky4, though not the bar: the loop P-625 is cut in two through a new node, not dropped, so each scored layer builds two more pipes and one more node (1,159 pipes and 962 nodes), with two more extra pipes (8) and one piece dropped instead of two, at 3 ft too; 1,151 of 1,156, one part and every diameter within 1% are unchanged. The tee fixes moved no ky4 count; on the hand-drawn KML they moved the 5 m row and the cut and dropped counts, not the pipes rebuilt at 10, 15 or 20 m (the final verification moved the 5 and 15 m rows again, below). These are changes after the scored run, made to fix what the review found, not to meet the bar, which they do not touch.
- The hosted demo refuses a GIS layer over 600 lines (or 50 points a line) before it is joined, and reads a GIS zip within its upload limit once unpacked, so a layer the local version builds may be refused there.
- The final verification moved the hand-drawn KML's rows, not its default: keeping a true tee's branch end with its cut gives one pipe fewer at 5 m (658) and at 15 m (1,071), and two fewer connected parts at 5 m (138); at the 10 m default, 1,060 pipes and 6 parts as before (Found 2 after the final verification). At 5 m five ky4 pipes are newly missed and four newly rebuilt; at 15 m one is newly missed. These are changes made to fix what the verifier found, after the scored run; the scored record and the bar did not move.
- An inlet residuals file of two columns whose second column names neither chlorine nor a residual nor is only a unit is refused, asking for residual_mgL. Besides 'value', 'reading', 'result', 'mean' and 'typical', listed since the review, that refuses 'conc', 'concentration', 'average', 'avg', 'median', 'level', 'measured', 'dose', 'setpoint' and 'target', each read as free chlorine before task 17 (not disclosed until the final verification). 'Total' and 'TRC' are read as total chlorine now, so the free-chlorine mode refuses them where it read them as free chlorine before task 17. 'Cl', 'FAC', 'Free', 'FCl', 'Res', 'Resid' and 'mg_l', refused by the review's rule, are read again.
- Behaviour changed by the final verification's fixes: a layer over the feature cap is left unread with a note however many lines it holds, so when the mains themselves are over the cap and a smaller line layer is in the same file, the smaller one is built, with the note saying to export the mains alone; a document type declaration inside a description's CDATA no longer refuses a KML (only a real declaration does); three sizes joined by x ('6x6x4', '12x12x8 TEE', '8"x8"x6"') are reducers, given the commonest size with a warning, where they gave their first size; in a KML's name or description, 'in' followed by a word ('8 in main') no longer counts as inches, so such names are asked for a size; a layer whose median latitude is beyond 75 degrees is asked whether its coordinates are swapped, which a real network that far north must answer 'keep'; and the page offers the coordinates' unit for a layer whose file names a projected system but whose coordinates lie in longitude and latitude ranges, with the file's own unit first.
- Everything here is converter self-consistency on public models converted by our own writers. A real layer will have multipart lines, text such as '8 & 6', approximate positions and coordinate mix-ups the fixtures cannot cover; the external file was not used.

How to run: on the Get started page, step 4; or `python -m residualmap.gis_read <layer> --out skeleton.inp`. `python -m residualmap.intake_exp gis` (from a folder outside the repository) rewrites the record. The thirteen task-17 checks are part of the full run; ten of them also run with `--quick`.

### Task 18, a CAD importer: a skeleton model from a DXF drawing

**Asked:** (plan task 18, category d; critique findings 3c, 11 for CAD, 14 for DXF and 15) read a CAD drawing of the water mains, saved as DXF, and build a skeleton EPANET model from it with task 17's topology engine: model space only, ezdxf's recover mode for damaged files, lines, polylines with their bulge arcs, arcs and splines flattened, TEXT and MTEXT through their plain text, blocks exploded with their transforms so bound xrefs are read, Civil 3D proxies told to run AECTOACAD first; every (layer, linetype, colour) listed so abandoned and proposed mains can be left out; sizes from layer names or the nearest label (by the text's box, within 3 text heights), assigned before lines are split at tees and propagated along runs of nodes that join two pipes, with counts by source; units from the drawing, asked when they are 0 or implausible; a scale question; the tolerance table in feet on ky4 and ky10; DWG, DGN, OLE, IFC and backup files handled by content, the ODA File Converter offered as a link only; every CAD-built model labelled "topology and sizes only, needs the operator form". Eight saved checks, one pre-registered bar: on ky4's mains drawn as an R12 DXF by our own writer and read by ezdxf, at least 1,151 of 1,156 pipes at 6 ft by endpoint-pair identity, one connected part and 99% of diameters. One new package, ezdxf 1.4.4. The lead added: parse DXF text in linear time and give it the hosted demo's limits, since task 17's review had found patterns that took hours on hostile KML and coordinate system text. Why it matters: a town engineer's drawing is often the only map of the mains a small system has.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** Built, and the bar is met, with no pipe to spare: 1,151 of 1,156 ky4 pipes rebuilt at 6 ft, one connected part, and 1,150 of the 1,151 rebuilt diameters within 1% (`outputs/intake/cad_roundtrip.json`, part `rebuild`). The five missed pipes are task 17's five, a matter of ky4's geometry; the one wrong diameter is P-946, a short pipe whose label landed nearer another line, so it got the drawing's commonest size. The same drawing written by ezdxf as R2018 LWPOLYLINEs with MTEXT labels gives the same counts (same-library self-consistency). On the way, a measured hazard: ezdxf's default MTEXT reader takes time quadratic in a run of formatting codes (87.8 s for 800,000 characters on this Mac), and by that growth one MTEXT of the 4,800,000 characters a 5 MB upload holds would take about 53 minutes, an extrapolation, not a run (`outputs/intake/cad_mtext_timing.json`); the reader uses ezdxf's accurate parser and parses no text longer than 2,000 characters, and every hostile drawing tested is read or refused well inside its time bound. A review then found defects in the reader, the build and the page (among them a hosted-demo build with no bound on its labels, zone numbers in layer names read as pipe sizes, and inches declared on a drawing made in feet taken without a question) and claims in this section that did not hold; each was reproduced, fixed and given a drawing of its own, and the bar's three measures did not move (After the review, below). A final verification then confirmed six more issues, among them a handle repair from the review that could make a drawing hundreds of times larger, zone numbers still read as sizes beside a material word or a CAD abbreviation, and a label measure whose memory still grew with its pairs; each was reproduced and fixed, again without moving the bar's measures (After the final verification, below). This is converter self-consistency on public models drawn by our own writers; no real drawing was read.

**Sources.** Paper Lantern was not available in this session, so there is no Paper Lantern attribution. The drawings follow Autodesk's DXF reference for R12 and R2000 (group codes, the binary DXF layout, POLYLINE and VERTEX, TEXT alignment, MTEXT attachment points, INSERT and MINSERT, external xref blocks); AutoCAD's block rules (an entity on layer 0 inside a block takes the INSERT's layer, BYBLOCK linetype and colour take the INSERT's) and its insertion units (INSUNITS) and measurement setting are as AutoCAD documents them. ezdxf 1.4.4's own documentation gives recover mode, virtual entities and multi_insert; that recover mode does not read binary DXF, that virtual_entities gives only the first copy of a MINSERT and keeps layer 0, and that the fast MTEXT reader is quadratic, were found here (Found 3 to 5). Since the review: the DXF reference's VERTEX flag 16 (a spline frame control point, which AutoCAD does not draw), AutoCAD's documented initial value of INSUNITS for an imperial drawing (1, inches), and that ezdxf's recover mode keeps an undecodable byte as a lone surrogate unless asked otherwise and drops an ENTITIES section that never ends, found here with the reviewer's scripts. Hazen-Williams C is task 17's table.

**Built:**
- `residualmap/cad_read.py`, new. Reading (`read_drawing`, which never raises): the file is sniffed by its content; an ASCII DXF goes through ezdxf's recover mode, a binary DXF through ezdxf's binary tag loader and an audit. Model space only. LINE, LWPOLYLINE and 2D POLYLINE with their bulges (each arc as chords of at most 5 degrees, at most 72 a curve), 3D POLYLINE, ARC and SPLINE (evaluated at a bounded number of points), each taken from its own coordinate system to world coordinates. TEXT, ATTRIB and MTEXT with a box: a TEXT's from its insertion point (the box's corner) or the point its alignment names, an MTEXT's from its attachment point. INSERT blocks exploded through `virtual_entities`, every MINSERT copy through `multi_insert`, nested to 8 deep, with AutoCAD's layer 0 and BYBLOCK rules; external xrefs named, not read; ACAD_PROXY_ENTITY and AECC objects counted and given the AECTOACAD message. Bounds: no TEXT or MTEXT longer than 2,000 characters is parsed (an MTEXT goes through ezdxf's accurate parser, never its default one), and since the review the drawing's notes say how many were not; a block bomb is refused before anything is exploded, from one pass over the block definitions that counts what model space would explode to; a read stops, with a plain refusal, at 2,000,000 entities and 5,000,000 points (since the review a polyline's points are counted after its bulge arcs), while a MINSERT of more than 10,000 copies and a spline of more than 2,000 points are left out, each with a warning, and the read goes on (the hosted demo's limits are tighter).
- Building (`build_from_drawing`, which never raises): the layer styles listed with linetype and colour resolved as AutoCAD shows them, any left out, those on layers that are off or frozen left out at first; units from the insertion units, a unitless drawing, a unit no water network is drawn in, or a median line outside 20 to 5,000 ft in the stated unit asked, a unit that disagrees with the measurement setting flagged; sizes from the layer name (separators read as spaces, then task 17's parser, bare numbers read together by their range, a lone year passed over) or from the nearest label (a size with a unit, or a number marked with the diameter symbol, read together by their range), each label going to its nearest line by the distance from the line to its box, within 3 heights; a line whose layer names a size keeps it; then propagation along runs of nodes that join two pipes (a breadth-first search from every sized pipe at once), then the commonest size, or the size asked when there is none (since the review nothing is built until it is given); C from a material in the line's label, else in its layer name, else 130 (until the review, a label's material was read only on a line that took its size from the label; now also from a label of the layer's own size); task 17's `build_network`, and the table at 0.5, 1, 3, 6 and 12 ft; the location warning, the scale question and the known-lengths check; the skeleton .inp titled "CAD skeleton (ResidualMap): topology and sizes only, needs the operator form"; `readiness` adds the drawing's rows, among them 'What it is: topology and sizes only, needs the operator form' and 'What a drawing cannot give' (its scale if it is a schematic, demands, elevations, roughness, valve status, PRV settings, which crossings connect, pump direction, anything that changes with time).
- `pages/1_Get_started.py`: step 5, 'Build a model from a CAD drawing', after step 4, every widget of its own keyed, so no earlier widget moves: the drawing's sentence, its layer styles and the styles to leave out, the drawing's unit over its insertion units, the joining distance, the label reach, the questions, 'Topology and sizes only:' with the counts and sizes by source, the tolerance table, the warnings, the readiness report, 'Download the model (.inp)' and 'Open it on the main page' (task 17's hand-off, its phrase naming the drawing). Any other file gets what to send instead. The drawing is read once per file and kept in the browser session. The caption after step 1 no longer explains 'coming soon', since no section is marked so.
- `residualmap/cloud.py`: `cad_limits` (100,000 entities, ten times the GIS step's point limit, 1,000 copies of a MINSERT), `read_cad_upload` (the read in a process of its own through task 20's `run_in_folder`, stopped after 30 s, with a plain refusal), `cad_too_big` (the GIS step's 600 lines, or 50 points a line, before any line is joined; since the review also 6,000 texts), and two guide swaps for the CAD sections. Until the review only the read was bounded in the hosted demo: the build (labels, joining, the tolerance table) ran in the Streamlit process with no bound on its labels; it now runs in a process of its own too (`build_cad_upload`, stopped after 30 s). `app.py`: the main page's sentence for a skeleton names a CAD drawing too. `residualmap/importers.py`: the DXF advice names step 5 and no longer says a drawing importer is planned, and the binary DXF's no longer asks for a text DXF. `docs/how_to_export_your_model.md`: the AutoCAD or Civil 3D and MicroStation sections, no longer marked coming soon. `requirements.txt`: ezdxf 1.4.4, installed into `.venv` from PyPI (pip added nothing else).
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: every drawing written by hand, group code by group code, as R12 or R2000 ASCII DXF or R12 binary DXF, sharing no code with ezdxf; only `ky4_r2018_lwpolyline.dxf` is written by ezdxf, the library that reads it, and is labelled same-library self-consistency. `residualmap/intake_exp.py cad` writes `outputs/intake/cad_roundtrip.json`; `score_ky4` takes another network's manifest (ky10's), scoring by the same rule.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task18` group of ten checks, the plan's eight (`dxf_rebuilds_network`, `dxf_tolerance_table_feet`, `dxf_labels_and_propagation`, `dxf_blocks_and_proxies`, `dxf_layer_style_listing`, `dxf_units_and_mtext`, `dxf_bak_and_binary`, `dwg_dgn_ifc_get_guidance`), `cad_reads_in_linear_time` (the hostile drawings, timed) and `get_started_cad_import`, a headless test of step 5 and the hand-off in local and cloud mode. The new files join the files the dashes and names checks read; `residualmap/numbers.py` traces this section's headline numbers.

**Found** (converter self-consistency on public models):
1. The bar is met with no pipe to spare, as task 17's was: 1,151 of 1,156 pipes, one connected part. The five misses are task 17's five (P-1126, P-152, P-625, P-696 and P-9): two pipes between ky4 nodes closer together than the tolerance, three that pass within it of a node where other pipes end. A drawing cannot do better on them than a GIS layer, since both give lines only.
2. Two of the 557 labels went to another line, both on short pipes: P-941's label box overlaps P-943's line (0.000 ft) while its own line is 4.931 ft away, its longest segment 12.4 ft; P-946's label sits 4.049 ft from P-9 against 4.499 ft from its own line, its longest segment 14.2 ft. A label up to 2.5 heights (5 ft) off a pipe that short reaches the pipes that meet it at its ends. Each lost its label and got the commonest size, 8 in: right for P-941, wrong for P-946 (6 in), the one wrong diameter. 3 labels were within reach of two lines and went to the nearer. The page counts the sources (577 pipes from labels, 580 from layer names, 0 along runs, 2 at the commonest size) but cannot show which default was wrong.
3. ezdxf's default MTEXT reader (its 'fast' mode, used by `plain_text()` as the plan named it) is quadratic in a run of formatting codes: 400,000 characters of `\A1;` took 20.7 s and 800,000 took 87.8 s on this Mac, 4.23 times as long for twice the characters (`outputs/intake/cad_mtext_timing.json`, a timed record written since the review, when the first probe's seconds, which no file kept, were found quoted here; it varies from run to run and no check reruns it). By that growth one MTEXT of 4,800,000 characters, the hostile fixture's, which a 5 MB upload holds, would take about 53 minutes: an extrapolation, not a run. Its accurate parser, `plain_mtext`, took twice as long for twice the characters with alignment and paragraph codes, and somewhat more with stacking, height and font codes (3.12 to 3.33 times), so the reader also parses no text longer than 2,000 characters: each parse is bounded, and a drawing's texts cost time in proportion to their number. ezdxf's loader alone took at most about twice as long for twice the size on long texts, long layer names, long numbers and many entities.
4. ezdxf's recover mode reads ASCII DXF only; a binary DXF goes through its binary tag loader, then an audit. Both routes give ky4's skeleton byte for byte, and so does the drawing named `.bak`.
5. ezdxf's `virtual_entities` gives only a MINSERT's first copy (`multi_insert` gives them all) and keeps an entity on layer 0 inside a block on layer 0, where AutoCAD shows it on the INSERT's layer; the reader applies AutoCAD's rule, so a block of mains drawn on layer 0 and inserted on WATER-8IN takes that layer's size. All 8 lines of the block fixture (7 from blocks, 1 plain line beside them) were found where AutoCAD's documented block rules put them, exactly: the fixture's own transform, not a drawing checked in AutoCAD.
6. MTEXT does not exist in R12 (it came with R13), and ezdxf refuses an MTEXT without its AcDbMText subclass, so the hand-written R12 drawing's labels are all TEXT (a pre-registration amendment made before any reader code: four TEXT styles, one with the diameter symbol code). MTEXT labels are tested in the R2018 drawing and in `mtext_codes.dxf`.
7. Units: a unitless drawing and one in millimetres drawn in feet (its median line 1.554 ft) are asked, and metres declared on a drawing from an imperial template are flagged by its measurement setting. Without the measurement setting the same drawing is not flagged: read as metres its median line is 1,554 ft long, which is plausible, so feet read as metres is caught only through that setting.
8. The tolerance table: ky4's drawing joins into one part only from 6 ft, because 19 of its ends stop 4 ft short (1,133 pipes and 7 parts at 3 ft, 1,151 and 1 at 6 ft), and at 12 ft 5 real pipes collapse. ky10's drawing (no undershoots) rebuilds all 1,043 pipes in one part at 1 ft, and loses real pipes as the distance grows (1, 2 and 6 collapsed at 3, 6 and 12 ft); at 0.5 ft both break up (14 and 13 parts), since two ends of one node, each moved by up to 0.3 ft on each axis, can lie more than 0.5 ft apart. ky10's 18 node pairs drawn at one spot (the two ends of its pumps and PRVs) fall into one node at every distance: no drawing of pipes can separate them.
9. On ky10's drawing, where every size is a label, misplaced labels count for more. All 1,043 labels were read and assigned, 28 within reach of two lines, and 5 lines got two labels of different sizes; at 6 ft 1,030 pipes took their size from a label, 4 along runs of pipes and 7 the commonest size, and 1,029 of the 1,041 rebuilt pipes' diameters are within 1% (98.8%; part `tolerance`, `sizes_at_6ft`). That is below the 99% the ky4 bar asks. No bar was registered for ky10, whose table is recorded only, so it is reported here, not tuned: a drawing whose sizes are all labels, placed as these are, gets about one pipe in a hundred wrong.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The skeleton cannot run: a drawing of mains carries no source, tank, pump, valve, control or demand, so the main page stops at its readiness report, and every junction is at 0 ft, harmless only while the model has no pressure-control valve. Until the operator form (task 19) is built, they can only be added to the downloaded .inp in EPANET.
- A label goes to its nearest line, and on short pipes in crowded places that can be the wrong one (Found 2): on ky4 one diameter in 1,151 is wrong, and the page shows only the counts of sizes by source, not which pipe got a neighbour's label or the default. A label of another size on a line whose layer names a size is counted and warned, and the layer's size is kept, unless (since the review) at least half that layer's lines carry a label and most of those labels give another size, when the labels are used on its lines, with a warning.
- The writer centres each label along its line with the same 0.6-height character width the reader assumes for a label's box, so the box geometry the scored labels rest on is self-consistent, not tested against real fonts, whose widths differ; the two misplaced labels of Found 2 are on short pipes, where a box's length decides which line it reaches.
- The R12 fixtures carry `$INSUNITS` and `$MEASUREMENT`, which came after R12 (with R2000 and R14), so their unit results test the reader's logic, not a real R12 export: a drawing saved as R12 DXF by AutoCAD carries neither and is asked its unit. The export guide now says to save as R2000 or later, which keeps the unit.
- Crossing lines are never joined, and a line that passes within the tolerance of a node where other lines end is joined there (task 17's rule): a drawing cannot say which crossings connect.
- C is 130 on every pipe whose label and layer name carry no material (818 of the 1,159 pipes of the R12 drawing), so on many drawings the old-pipe factor will meet task 15b's uniform-C rule.
- Feet read as metres on a drawing with no measurement setting is not flagged (Found 7).
- On ky10's drawing, with every size a label, 98.8% of the rebuilt diameters are right (1,029 of 1,041), below the 99% the ky4 bar asks (Found 9); ky10 carries no bar, and nothing was changed to move it.
- Not read: DWG and DGN themselves (the app says what to export), external xrefs, paper space, circles, ellipses (an arc in a block scaled unevenly becomes one), mesh and polyface polylines, splines over 2,000 points, MINSERTs over 10,000 copies (1,000 in the hosted demo), texts over 2,000 characters. Each is counted or warned.
- Departures from the plan, disclosed: MTEXT goes through ezdxf's accurate parser, not `plain_text()`'s default (Found 3); the scored R12 drawing's labels are all TEXT (Found 6, an amendment made before any reader code); a bare number in a label counts as a size only when the diameter symbol marks it, and other numbers are passed over, not asked (addresses, stations and lengths are common in drawings); layer styles on layers that are off or frozen are left out at first, which the plan did not say; `insunits_wrong.dxf` is flagged through its measurement setting, not its median line, and the variant without the setting is recorded as not flagged.
- Existing checks changed with the page: `get_started_page_runs` now counts no section marked coming soon (it counted 2); `cloud_mode_uploads_leave_no_files` and `get_started_gis_import` expect the page's third uploader (step 5's, at the demo's 5 MB); `mode_detection_fails_safe` counts 9 guide swaps (it counted 7); `requirements_pinned` counts 13 pinned lines, ezdxf's added. The numbers script traces task 20's quoted 11 requirement lines and 4 guide swaps to today's report less what tasks 17 and 18 added.
- The hosted demo refuses a drawing over 600 lines (or 50 points a line), or since the review over 6,000 texts, on the styles chosen before any line is joined, as it does a GIS layer, and refuses one whose labels reach more than 2,000,000 pairs of a label and a segment, so a drawing the local version builds may be refused there.
- Since the final verification more drawings get a question about their layer names: a number beside CL, ST, PE, AC, WS or POLY (W_8_PE, W_8_AC), or right after ZONE, PHASE, SHEET, DIST or another numbering word, is asked, and so is a number its name cues when at least two of its layer's lines carry labels of another size and they outnumber those that agree. One stray label of another size still leaves the layer's size, only warned, as on ky4's drawing, where P-946's label lands on P-9 of W_12_DIP. The verification's fix asked for a question on any label that disagrees; one is not enough here, since that one label would have put a question on the bar's drawing. Those six abbreviations still give C from a layer name (ST as steel), which the verification did not ask about.
- Handles drawn twice, a handle 0 and a file cut short are repaired in ASCII DXF only. A binary DXF is read as it is: on `binary_dup_handles.dxf` ezdxf's audit keeps 5 of its 10 lines, and the reader warns that lines may be missing.
- The label measure's memory is now bounded, the topology engine's is not: on the verifier's drawing of separate short lines packed 1 to 2 ft apart, just under the pair limit (1.06 GB at its peak after the fix) about 0.64 GB of that peak is task 17's joining of their ends, which the hosted demo's 600-line limit keeps small there. The label measure counts its pairs before measuring them in every build now, not only under a limit: one more search of the trees.
- Everything here is converter self-consistency on public models drawn by our own writers. A real drawing will have firm-specific layer names, labels placed by hand, scale and unit mistakes and paper-space content the fixtures cannot cover; no real drawing was read.

**After the review** (converter self-consistency on public models; each issue was checked against the files, most with the reviewer's own scripts, before it was fixed; each code fix has a drawing of its own, written by the fixtures' writers, in the new check `dxf_review_cases` (part `review` of the record), in `cad_reads_in_linear_time` (part `hostile`) or in `get_started_cad_import`; the bar's three measures did not move, and of the record's earlier parts one count moved, below):
- The hosted demo's build had no bound on its labels: a label's reach is 3 text heights, nothing limited a text's height or the number of labels, every pair of a label and a segment within reach was held in memory at once, and the build ran in the Streamlit process. Now a text taller than 200 ft, or 10 times the labels' median height, sizes nothing (a title or a sheet's label, warned); the pairs within reach are counted before any is measured, and more than 20,000,000 (2,000,000 in the hosted demo) is a plain refusal; they are measured 200,000 at a time; and the hosted demo refuses a drawing over 6,000 texts on the styles chosen before any line is joined, and builds it in a process of its own, stopped after 30 s. The two hostile label drawings in the numbers table are built and refused within 5 s, local and hosted.
- A bare number in a layer name was read as a pipe size, over every label: WATER-ZONE-2, SHEET-12 and W-DIST-10 gave 2, 12 and 10 in. Now it is a size only beside a size word (DIA), a diameter mark or a material (W_8_PVC, so ky4's drawing is unchanged), and one with none of these is asked: not pipe sizes, or their unit. A layer whose name gives a size while at least half its lines carry a label, most of them of another size, uses the labels, with a warning, and the page can turn layer-name sizes off. `zone_layers.dxf` is asked, then sized from its 8 in labels each of those three ways.
- Inches declared on a drawing made in feet (INSUNITS 1, AutoCAD's initial value for an imperial drawing) were taken without a question: read in inches, ky4's drawing has a median line of 42.5 ft, plausible, and every length twelve times short. Inches are now asked whenever the median read in feet is plausible too, with both medians (42.5 and 509.7 ft); a drawing truly in inches whose median read in feet is implausible (7,200.0 ft) is taken in inches as before.
- Bytes invalid in the drawing's encoding (cp1252 in an R2018 file) became lone surrogates, and step 5 stopped with a traceback while drawing its table of layer styles. They now read as U+FFFD, on the page too.
- Entities with a handle another entity has were removed by ezdxf's audit without a word (half the mains of a chain of ten whose lines share handles in pairs), and a drawing whose handles are all 0 was refused as damaged. Such handles are now renumbered before ezdxf reads the file, with a note, and whatever an audit still removes is warned.
- A spline-fit POLYLINE kept its control frame's vertices, which AutoCAD does not draw (2,136.5 ft read for a curve of 688.0 ft). They are skipped.
- Step 5 answered its own question when no size could be read: its input started at 8 in, the skeleton was built at once, and the warning called 8 in 'the size you chose' while the summary and the readiness report said 'the commonest size'. The input is now empty and nothing is built until a size is given; then all three say 'the size you gave'. The Diameters row is now 'derived' whenever labels, matched to lines by distance, gave sizes, and 'from your file' only for layer names.
- Smaller: a frozen layer's texts sized mains, a frozen layer named in another case (old-mains for OLD-MAINS) was read as shown, and a block inserted on a frozen layer was read, though AutoCAD hides all three; each is left out at first now (the block's lines by a control of their own), and a style of texts only can be left out on the page. A file cut short read as having no line and was refused as having no mains in model space; it is now read up to where it stops, with a warning. A MINSERT's negative row count passed the block-bomb count as negative copies; a count below 1 counts as 1. The point limit counted a polyline's vertices, not the points its bulge arcs become; it counts those now, before flattening. AutoCAD's diameter sign U+2205 marks a size. A text over 2,000 characters is noted. A label's material gives C on a line whose layer names its size when the label gives the same size: on ky4's R2018 drawing one pipe more takes C from a material (555 of 1,159), the only count of the record's earlier parts to move. In the hosted demo a model or GIS file over the upload limit no longer hides the later steps.
- The docs: this section, the CHANGELOG and the README said the hosted demo was bounded when only its read was; said a read stops at four limits, when two leave an entity out with a warning; said C came from a label's material on every line; counted 8 lines from blocks where the fixture has 7 and a plain line; quoted the MTEXT timings from no record, and an hour as a finding where it was an extrapolation; and `cad_reads_in_linear_time` is a fixed time bound, not a test of growth. Each is corrected above. The checks report had been overwritten by a review's full run that failed only `repo_untouched`, because `.claude/scheduled_tasks.lock` changed while it ran; the full run below wrote it again, and the check counts here and in the README are now traced to it, so a stale report fails the numbers check. The README's task-15 bullet now says the importers are built.

**After the final verification** (six confirmed issues, each reproduced with the verifier's own scripts before it was fixed, each fix with a drawing of its own in the new check `dxf_final_verification` (part `final` of the record) or in `cad_reads_in_linear_time` (part `hostile`); the bar's three measures did not move, and of the record's earlier parts only the zone drawing's counts moved, since it gained two layers):

*Asked:* (the lead, from the final verifier's report) reproduce each confirmed issue, two major and four minor, fix it, minor ones too when cheap, add each case to the task's checks, run the full checks and confirm the defaults byte-identical.

*Built and fixed* (converter self-consistency on public models):
- The review's handle repair could make a drawing hundreds of times larger. It numbered each renumbered handle from the largest handle in the file, so one valid handle of 100,000 hex digits beside 3,000 handles 0 made every new handle that long: 0.415 MB of drawing became 300.4 MB of repaired bytes, and in the hosted demo's read process, which has a time limit but no memory limit, a 0.93 MB upload took 4.70 GB. A handle is now 1 to 16 hex digits, as a 64-bit number is: a longer or non-hex one is renumbered too and sets no new handle's length, and when the numbers above the largest handle would pass 16 digits the smallest free ones are used. `hostile_long_handle.dxf` (that drawing, with one handle at the 64-bit largest beside it) repairs to 0.773 times its size, its 3,001 bad handles renumbered and all 3,002 lines read.
- A layer name's number was still read as a size, with no question, whenever a material word was anywhere in the name, and the material table's words include CL, ST, PE, AC, WS and POLY, common CAD abbreviations (a centre line, a street, a polyline): PVC-ZONE-3, WATER-ZONE-2-PVC, W-MAIN-CL-ZONE-2 and seven more names each sized every line on their layer by a zone, phase, sheet or district number. On WATER-ZONE-2-PVC, 8 mains with 3 of them labelled 8 in were all built at 2 in, with only the generic warning, since the labels win only where at least half of a layer's lines carry a label. Now the cue must stand right beside the number (W_8_PVC, MAINS-8-DIA, 8PVC), a number right after a numbering word (ZONE, PHASE, SHEET, DIST and the like) is never cued, and those six abbreviations cue nothing: all ten names, and W_8_CL, are asked. A cued number is also asked when at least two of its layer's lines carry labels of another size and they outnumber those that agree (`disputed_layer.dxf`: W-2-PVC, with three mains labelled 8 in, is asked, and once called no size its pipes take 8 in from 3 labels and 5 along runs); one stray label keeps the layer's size, with a warning (W-8-PVC, and ky4's drawing, Regressions above). `zone_layers.dxf` gains PVC-ZONE-3 and WATER-ZONE-2-PVC (19 mains). The README now states the half-labelled condition of the labels-win rule.
- A UTF-8 byte order mark before the first group code (a file ezdxf itself reads) stopped the ASCII repair, which then saw no group code: ky4's drawing with no '0 EOF' read none of its 1,113 lines and was refused as having no mains in model space, and a drawing whose handles are drawn twice lost half its lines. The mark is now kept and both repairs run after it (`bom_ky4_no_eof.dxf`: 1,113 lines, warned as cut short; `bom_dup_handles.dxf`: 10 of 10). The CHANGELOG had said without qualification that such handles are renumbered before reading; it and the docs now say both repairs are for ASCII DXF only (Regressions above).
- In local mode the label measure's memory still grew with its pairs, up to the 20,000,000 a drawing may have: every pair of a label and a line within reach was kept, and each batch of 1,024 labels' hits was held whole (the verifier measured 5.17 GB for a drawing of separate lines just under the limit, and 1.31 GB for long polylines), while its docstring said memory did not grow. The segments are now searched in trees of at most 200,000, the labels are queried in batches whose pairs add up to at most 200,000, and only each label's nearest and next nearest line is kept, which is all the assignment returns. It gives the same answers, tie for tie, on ky4, ky10, the label cases and random drawings, with batches of 37 pairs too. On `label_pairs_near_limit.dxf` (1,900 polylines, 1,000 labels, 18,910,690 pairs) the assignment alone peaked at 1.49 GB before and 0.41 GB now, and the build adds 0.42 GB to the peak after its read, within the check's bound of 0.8 GB (a run log on this Mac; the record keeps what the build gave, not its memory).
- The point budget the review added for bulge arcs skipped a segment whose two ends compare equal, and two infinite ends do while their chord is nan, so an arc was drawn there all the same: one polyline of 160,000 vertices at infinity, in a 4.80 MB drawing under the upload limit, was charged one point a vertex and made 5,759,965 points before it was dropped as not finite. The count now uses the arc's own test, and a polyline with a vertex that is not finite is left out before it is flattened (`hostile_inf_bulges.dxf`: the drawing is charged 4 points, for its two plain mains).
- The numbers table said the review's reproduction (`post_task18_review`) ran on the final tree; four files changed after it started, none of them imported by the reruns. The row now says so, and `post_task18_final` was started after the last edit to any file the reruns or the checks read.

*Found:*
1. A question on any label that disagrees with a cued layer number, as the verifier's fix put it, would stop the bar's own drawing: on ky4's drawing one label, P-946's, lands on P-9 of W_12_DIP. The dispute rule therefore needs two lines, and one stray label stays a warning (Regressions above).
2. The label measure's peak no longer follows its pairs: on the verifier's drawing of separate lines, at a tenth of the pairs and at all of them, it peaked about as high (a run log), set by the batch and tree size, not the pairs.
3. What is left of that drawing's peak is task 17's joining of line ends (Regressions above), not the labels.
4. Only the byte order mark needed handling before the first group code: a file with blank lines there is not taken as a DXF at all (the sniff calls it plain text), so the reader never sees it.

How to run: on the Get started page, step 5; or `python -m residualmap.cad_read <drawing.dxf> --out skeleton.inp`. `python -m residualmap.intake_exp cad` (from a folder outside the repository) rewrites the record, and `python -m residualmap.intake_exp cad_timing` the MTEXT timing (about two minutes). The twelve task-18 checks are part of the full run; seven of them also run with `--quick`.

### Task 19, the operator form and the measured cost of each missing input (built in part, stopped at its pre-registered bar)

**Asked:** (plan task 19, category e; critique findings 1, 2, 3a, 16 and 17 as it applies to skeletons; the lead's note of 20 October on material-only C) a short form an operator fills in, one row per item (a source and the hydraulic grade it holds, tanks, pumps with their place and what they do, pump rules, closed boundary valves, PRVs and PSVs with their settings and elevations, check valves, elevations, the production, large users, a 24-hour pattern), and `apply_operator_form(skeleton, form)`, which turns a skeleton from a GIS layer (step 4) or a CAD drawing (step 5) into a model that runs, and fills the same gaps in an EPANET model; a GIS or CAD model never runs without a water source; the form as an upload on the Get started page, with the hand-off to the main page; a fidelity ladder run with the product's own SimGP24 on Net3, ky4 and ky10, each rung removing one more thing a map lacks, before and after calibration on 12 samples, its records under `outputs/intake/`; the readiness report's 'why we need this' sentences read from those records at run time, ky4 and ky10 side by side; seven saved checks, among them a pre-registered bar on ky4. The lead's note: material-only C cannot see a pipe's age, so either age C by install year with a cited relation, or say so plainly and measure it in the ladder. Also asked: fix task 17's step 4, which pre-filled the size of every pipe with 8 in and then called it 'the size you chose'.

**Outcome, in one paragraph (simulation on public models).** The bar missed, so the task stopped and nothing was tuned. The skeleton from ky4's clean drawing, joined within 1 ft and completed by a form built from the original (its rows give each of the 934 junction demands, which no operator has), was to match the original at every junction and hour of the scored day: water age within a median of 0.5 h and a 95th percentile of 2 h, chlorine within 0.01 and 0.03 mg/L, and the same junctions below 0.2 mg/L (Jaccard at least 0.95). The scored run gave a water-age median of 1.47 h and a 95th percentile of 22.58 h, and a chlorine 95th percentile of 0.0923 mg/L; the chlorine median (0.0015 mg/L) and the Jaccard (0.9989) met theirs (`outputs/intake/operator_form.json`, part `complete`, which `operator_form_completes_skeleton` reruns and compares). Everything the form gives was placed right (the reservoir's head, the four tanks, the two pumps at their split nodes, pump 1's rule as two controls, the pattern, each material's C, 934 large users), as the characterization after the stop shows. The cause, measured after the stop and not a rescoring (part `after_the_stop`): ky4 draws three junctions 0.104, 0.117 and 0.601 ft from a pipe they are not on (J-198, J-86 and J-836), and the drawing, joined within 1 ft, connects each to that pipe as a tee, which ky4 does not. The 1 ft was this build's own setting, fixed before the scored run (the plan named no joining distance), and task 17's committed record already listed these three junctions as joined into tees within 1 ft, so the miss could have been predicted from the record before the scored run. Joined within 0.1 ft instead, with no tee, the same form reproduces ky4 to within what the drawing's geometric lengths alone do (age median 0.0095 h and 95th percentile 0.28 h, chlorine 95th percentile 0.0009 mg/L, Jaccard 1.0). The form's other parts met their bars: ky10's five PRVs restored from the form give the original exactly (0.0 h and 0.0 mg/L), a pipe closed in a copy of ky10 for the test and left open, as a skeleton would have it, is closed again by one form row, which reproduces that copy exactly, a form with no source writes no model, and every bad row is refused with its reason. The ladder ran on all three networks and is committed. Because of the stop, the form is not offered in the app: the Get started page and the main page are as task 18 left them, except for step 4's fix. A review then found and fixed defects in the form that ky4's form does not reach (After the review, below), without moving the scored run's model.

**Sources.** Paper Lantern was not available in this session, so there is no Paper Lantern attribution. The form's units follow EPANET's (Rossman 2000, the EPANET 2 Users Manual) through WNTR 1.5.0's own conversions, so a value written in feet, psi, gallons per minute or horsepower is read exactly as WNTR reads the same value in an .inp; the pump rule and the clock rules are EPANET's simple controls as WNTR builds them; new-pipe C is the manual's Table 3.2 (task 17's table). No published residential demand pattern was checked for this build, so the form's default pattern, used only when the form gives none and labelled assumed, is the diurnal pattern the public models ky4 and ky10 both carry (the KYPIPE research database of distribution models, Jolly et al. 2014, bundled with WNTR). No relation between pipe age and C was checked either, so C is not aged by install year (the lead's second option).

**Built:**
- `residualmap/operator_form.py` (new):
  - `read_operator_form`: the form as CSV in any encoding or separator the grab-log reader takes, or an Excel sheet; one row per item with a `kind` column (source, tank, pump, pump_rule, closed_valve, check_valve, prv, psv, elevation, production, large_user, pattern, pipe_c); a row of an unknown kind, missing a value its kind needs or with a number that does not read is refused with its reason.
  - `apply_operator_form`: a location is a node ID or a point, x,y in the drawing's or layer's own coordinates or latitude,longitude for a layer in degrees (Google Earth), put on the nearest node within 50 ft; a pipe is an ID or a point on it. A source or tank turns its junction into a reservoir or tank; a pump, PRV or PSV either splits a node in two (where a drawing puts both of its ends at one point, as ky4 and ky10 do for every pump and valve), the pipe on its upstream side moved to the new node, or replaces a pipe; a closed valve closes its pipe, a check valve makes its pipe one in the given direction; a PRV's elevation goes to its downstream node (since the review, a PSV's to its upstream node); the production less the large users is spread by pipe length (`length_shares`); a pump rule becomes EPANET controls on a tank's level or the clock, and the pump starts off unless its tank starts below the on level; a pump with no performance gets a design point derived from the production, labelled assumed. A row that cannot be placed is refused with its reason, and no model is built until every row can be used; a pump the form adds with no rule, a form with no source and a form with no water use are blocking problems, and a model that cannot run is never written (since the review EPANET runs the completed model's first hour before it is written; before, a model EPANET refused could be).
  - The readiness text's measured changes (`why_sentences`, `pump_rule_cost` and the others; worded as costs until the review): each reads ky4's and ky10's numbers from the fidelity records when it is called (`FIDELITY_SOURCES`, as `capabilities.SOURCES`), and says it is a simulation on public models, not the operator's system. They are not shown anywhere yet, since the form is not offered.
- `residualmap/intake_exp.py`: the `fidelity` command (the ladder, its protocol written above its code before the scored runs), the `boundary` command (ky10's boundary valve, picked once) and the `form` command (the form's record and the characterization after the stop).
- `residualmap/fixtures.py`: `ky4_clean_r12.dxf`, `ky4_form_from_original.csv`, `ky4_form_no_source.csv`, `operator_form_bad_rows.csv`, `ky10_order_fixed.inp`, `ky10_prv_removed.inp`, `ky10_prv_form.csv`, `ky10_boundary_valve_closed.inp`, `ky10_closed_valve_form.csv` and the form's template, `operator_form_template.csv` (a fixture only, not shipped in `docs/templates/`, since nothing in the app reads it; task 16b's review moved its template out for the same reason).
- `residualmap/experiment.py`: ky10's hidden truth in `NET_TRUTH`, the default one said explicitly (kb 0.40 per day, kw 0.70 m per day, dose 1.2 mg/L), before the scored runs. No committed number moves.
- `residualmap/cad_read.py` and `residualmap/gis_read.py`: each skeleton pipe's material kept on the build, for the form's pipe_c rows (`c_class`, a class of the C table, until the review; since then `c_material`, the material itself, and on a GIS layer `c_from_field`, the pipes whose C the layer's own C field gives).
- `pages/1_Get_started.py` and `residualmap/gis_read.py`: task 17's bug. Step 4's 'Size of every pipe (in)' starts empty, as step 5's does since task 18's review, and nothing is built until a size is given; the warning then says 'the size you gave'.
- `residualmap/checks.py`: a `task19` group of eight, the plan's seven (`operator_form_completes_skeleton`, `operator_form_restores_prvs_ky10`, `operator_form_boundary_valve`, `no_source_never_runs`, `form_parser_refuses_bad_rows`, `fidelity_outputs_reproduce`, `readiness_quotes_trace_to_outputs`) and `get_started_gis_default_size_asked`; eleven since the review (After the review, below), thirteen since the final verification (After the final verification, below). `operator_form_completes_skeleton` asserts that the scored run reproduces, not the bar it missed.
- `residualmap/numbers.py`: registry entries for this section's headline numbers; and task 18's quoted check counts ('144 of 145') now count only the checks that existed after task 18, since they were tied to the whole current report and the first check task 19 added would have broken them (since the review by naming the groups that existed then, `numbers.groups_through`: the exclusion list of later groups would have broken on the next task's first check).
- Not built, because of the stop: the form on the Get started page, its hand-off to the main page (the dose and the inlets' residuals from the form's source rows, through task 16c's inlet ratios), the main page's import branch, the shipped template and the readiness report's measured-cost sentences on the page. The skeletons' readiness reports still say the form is not built yet.

**Found:**
1. The scored run (part `complete`). The clean drawing, every ky4 pipe through its exact vertices on a layer naming its size and a material (W_8_PVC, for example, for an 8 in pipe of ky4's C 150, and W_16_DIP for its C 140 ones), joined within 1 ft: 1,159 pipes and 962 nodes in one part (ky4 has 1,156 pipes and 964 nodes; each pump's two ends are one node), 3 tee cuts. Completed by the form's 946 rows: 959 junctions, 4 tanks, 1 reservoir, 2 pumps. Against ky4 with the skeleton's solver and quality options, at every junction and hour of the scored day: water age median 1.47 h, 95th percentile 22.58 h, largest 140.6 h; chlorine (kb 0.40 per day, kw 0.70 m per day, gamma 1, at ky4's truth dose of 2.0 mg/L) median 0.0015 mg/L, 95th percentile 0.0923 mg/L, largest 1.03 mg/L; junctions below 0.2 mg/L at the daily minimum 897 in the original and 898 completed, 897 in both (Jaccard 0.9989). Two of the five parts of the bar met, three missed. These are the scored run's numbers as its console gave them (part `scored_run`, copied); part `complete` is the same setup rerun after changes that do not touch what it measures (the skeleton's zero-demand entries cleared before the form's demands are added, only pumps the form adds needing a rule, WNTR's header comments left out), and the two agree to four decimals, the verdict part by part the same. A first attempt stopped before any score: its comparison could not map the three junctions the skeleton had joined to a passing main, whose nodes sit a fraction of a foot from ky4's, and it then took the nearest node within the joining distance; that is the run scored.
2. The cause (part `after_the_stop`, measured with nothing changed). Three junctions of ky4 lie 0.104 ft (J-198), 0.117 ft (J-86) and 0.601 ft (J-836) from a pipe they are not on, away from its ends; in the model they are not connected to it. A drawing cannot show the difference between touching and connected, and the topology engine, within 1 ft, splits that pipe and joins the junction to it. Joined within 0.1 ft, chosen after the scored run and just under the 0.104 ft of the nearest, the drawing gives ky4's 1,156 pipes with no tee, and the same form reproduces ky4 with a water-age median of 0.0095 h and 95th percentile of 0.28 h, a chlorine 95th percentile of 0.0009 mg/L and a Jaccard of 1.0: the same as ky4 itself with each pipe's length replaced by its drawn length (median 0.0095 h, 95th percentile 0.28 h). So everything the form gives is placed right, and the miss is the skeleton's topology at the build's 1 ft join, on a drawing whose geometry is exactly ky4's. The 1 ft was this build's own setting, fixed before the scored run: the plan named no joining distance, and the choice looked only at node-to-node spacing (ky4's closest two nodes are 2.2 ft apart), not at how close a node lies to a pipe it is not on. Task 17's committed record already lists these three junctions, at these distances, as joined into tees within 1 ft (`gis_roundtrip.json`, `near_node_misses`, which the README's task-17 bullet quotes), so the miss could have been predicted from the record before the scored run; what was found after the stop is only the measurement that the three tees are the whole of the miss. That bears on whether to restate the bar. At the CAD step's 6 ft default the same three junctions are within reach.
3. The reference is ky4 with the skeleton's options (a drawing and the form carry none): ky4's own file has 100 trials, an accuracy of 0.0001 and a diffusivity of 0.05, against the skeleton's 40, 0.001 and 1.0 (chlorine's own). On the original itself those options change water age by a median of 0.0001 h but chlorine by a median of 0.287 mg/L, so the diffusivity alone is a larger difference than any the form leaves; it is recorded, not barred. With chlorine's diffusivity, ky4 at the truth's dose has 897 of its 959 junctions below 0.2 mg/L at the daily minimum, which makes the bar's Jaccard part weak: nearly every junction is low in both. Every committed ky4 and ky10 chlorine result inherits the files' 0.05, the ladder's included: at it, ky4 at its truth has 376 of its 959 junctions below 0.2 mg/L at the daily minimum, against 897 at chlorine's own 1.0, and ky10 has 362 of its 920 against 611 (part `public_models`, measured in the review).
4. ky10's PRVs (part `prvs`, `operator_form_restores_prvs_ky10`). With each of its 5 PRVs an open pipe of 1 ft, water age moves by more than 2 h at 347 of its 920 junctions (largest 84.5 h, 95th percentile 16.4 h) and chlorine by up to 0.465 mg/L. The form's 5 prv rows (the pipe each replaces, its upstream node, its setting and its downstream node's elevation, from the file's text) give back the original's .inp as WNTR writes it, and 0.0 h and 0.0 mg/L of difference.
5. ky10's boundary valve (part `boundary` and `outputs/intake/boundary_valve_ky10.json`). The rule was written before the search: of ky10's 570 closable pipes, close each in turn; keep the closures that leave every junction fed (none newly holding water 144 h old or more on the scored day); pick the one that moves the scored day's mean age the most. 359 closures keep every junction fed, 211 do not; P-18 moves the mean age by 1.38 h on average (largest 46.2 h at one junction) and is pinned in `fixtures.py`. ky10 itself has P-18 open, so the test closes it in a copy of ky10 (`ky10_boundary_valve_closed.inp`, the closed original) and leaves it open in the model the form completes, as a skeleton would have it. Left open, the scored day's water age differs from the closed copy by more than 2 h at 272 junctions; one closed_valve row, the pipe given by the point halfway along it, closes it again and reproduces that copy exactly.
6. No source (part `no_source`). The skeleton alone stops at the intake every model goes through ('No reservoir, inflow junction or tank found'), with nothing kept; with the form less its source row (the four tanks are still there) the form stops with its own sentence and writes no model.
7. Bad rows (part `bad_rows`). Of the 7 rows, 6 are refused, each with its line and reason: an unknown kind, a PRV with no setting, a tank whose location is far off the network (more than the 50 ft a point may lie from a node), a rule for a pump no row gives, a pattern of 12 numbers, a large user whose use is no number. No model is written.
8. The ladder (`outputs/intake/fidelity_Net3.json`, `fidelity_ky4.json`, `fidelity_ky10.json`; the Numbers table). Each rung is a WNTR rewrite of the network, and the rewrite alone moves water age by 0.0 h on every network. The largest cost is not the same on every network, which is why the readiness text quotes ky4 and ky10 side by side. On ky4, losing every control takes the water-age error from 10.77 to 30.46 h, the false lows after 12 samples from 64.8 to 118.1, and the low spots found from 365.6 to 300.4 of 397.5. The step keeps each pump as the file starts it: ky4's pump 1, which runs on tank T-3's level, starts closed and so stays off all week. Before calibration its false lows go from 47.6 to 80.5, and after 12 samples they are higher still, as they are at every ky4 rung, the full model's included (30.2 before calibration, 78.9 after). On ky10, where 10 of its 13 pumps have no control to lose, the 3 that have one start open and so stay open all week, and the same step takes the error from 11.24 to 11.95 h and the false lows from 56.9 to 74.4. Demand spread by pipe length is the largest step on Net3 (9.36 to 13.47 h, false lows 3.6 to 11.5) and on ky10 (7.62 to 11.23 h, false lows 22.9 to 50.8), and the second largest on ky4 (8.14 to 10.77 h). One elevation everywhere changes nothing on Net3 and ky4, which have no pressure valve (their rungs r2 and r3 are the same), and on ky10, with its 5 PRVs, it leaves the error at 11.24 h but takes the false lows from 50.8 to 56.9. C 130 on every pipe moves water age little (9.53 to 9.36 h on Net3, 7.70 to 8.14 h on ky4, 7.34 to 7.62 h on ky10), but C acts on chlorine, through the old-pipe factor, so the step is judged there, as Found 9 judges material-only C (since the final verification; it had been judged on water age alone, as costing little): after 12 samples the daily-minimum chlorine error rises from 0.058 to 0.071 mg/L on Net3 (from 0.048 to 0.054 before calibration) and falls a little on ky4 (0.051 to 0.048) and ky10 (0.044 to 0.042); before calibration the false lows rise on ky4 (30.2 to 36.9) and ky10 (15.9 to 23.2), and after 12 samples they fall (78.9 to 55.4 on ky4, 25.2 to 22.9 on ky10). On Net3 that rise in chlorine error is about as large as material-only C's (0.058 to 0.074 mg/L, Found 9). Tanks 1.3 times as wide move the water-age error little (12.80 to 13.45 h, 30.46 to 30.84 h, 11.95 to 12.19 h), though on ky4 they add false lows after 12 samples (118.1 to 136.9). Losing ky10's PRVs takes its error from 12.19 to 12.63 h while its false lows fall from 75.5 to 67.1: the costs do not simply add, rung on rung, and each rung's cost is measured on top of the ones before it.
9. Material-only C (the lead's note; rung `c_material`). In a skeleton, C comes from the material alone, so the old-pipe factor, which scales wall decay by 2^(gamma (130 - C) / 30), sees no age: an old cast iron main gets cast iron's new-pipe 135, more than ductile iron's 130. Measured from the full model with each pipe's C replaced by the new-pipe value of the material its C suggests (145 for C of 145 or more, 130 above 130, 135 at 130 or less). Water age, which C moves only through the hydraulics, barely changes: 9.46 h against the full model's 9.53 h on Net3, 7.72 h against 7.70 h on ky4, 6.86 h against 7.34 h on ky10. Chlorine, where the factor acts, does: after 12 samples the daily-minimum error rises from 0.058 to 0.074 mg/L on Net3, from 0.051 to 0.054 on ky4 and from 0.044 to 0.046 on ky10, and before calibration ky10's false lows go from 15.9 to 24.6 and ky4's from 30.2 to 31.8, while after 12 samples the false lows barely move (3.4 to 3.8 on Net3, 78.9 to 77.6 on ky4, 25.2 to 24.4 on ky10). ky4 and ky10 understate the effect: their files set a relative diffusivity of 0.05 (Net3's is chlorine's own, 1.0), which leaves their wall reaction limited by mass transfer, so a wall rate 1.59 times higher (the factor of C 130 against 150) changes their junctions' daily minima by 0.041 mg/L on ky4 and 0.029 on ky10 at the 95th percentile at 0.05, against 0.090 and 0.069 at 1.0 (part `public_models`, measured in the review; ky4's median change is not smaller at 0.05, 0.021 against 0.016 mg/L, since at 1.0 most of its junctions are near zero). And on ky10 the step is the lead's case: its 391 pipes of C 100 get 135, old mains given a new-pipe C. So material-only C costs little in water age but not in chlorine, and most on Net3, the one network run at chlorine's own diffusivity (a different network too, so the diffusivity is not the only difference). These public models carry no install years, so none of this measures a real system's aging: there the model needs that system's own C for each material. The GIS readiness report already says that the install year is read and shown only, and does not change C; the form's pipe_c rows let an operator give the C they use for each material, and its warning says the factor cannot see age without them.
10. Step 4's default size (`get_started_gis_default_size_asked`). On a Google Earth path with no size anywhere, the input starts empty, the question is shown and nothing is built; once 8 in is given, the skeleton is built and its warning says 'the size you gave'.
11. Found while building, fixed before any record was kept: WNTR's .inp writer starts every file with three comment lines, the file it read and the time it wrote, so the ladder's rung files, their sha1 and the grids' cache names changed from run to run, and a saved check comparing them would have failed. Both writers now leave those lines out. The grids already built for ky4 and ky10 were moved to the names the fixed code gives (the comments change no simulation: Net3's fresh rerun in `fidelity_outputs_reproduce` and one ky10 rung rebuilt fresh give the committed sha256), and the records were written again from the cache.
