**Regressions, recorded not hidden** (simulation on public models):
- The pre-registered bar missed: water age 1.47 h and 22.58 h against 0.5 h and 2 h, chlorine 95th percentile 0.0923 against 0.03 mg/L. Not tuned, not restated; the joining distance of the characterization after the stop was chosen after the scored run and is no rescoring. The 1 ft join that made the tees was this build's own setting, chosen from node-to-node spacing alone (the plan named none), and task 17's committed record already listed the three junctions it joins into tees, so the miss could have been predicted before the scored run (Found 2). Devansh's decision: restate the bar (for example on a drawing whose dead ends do not touch other mains, or with the joining distance below the closest such dead end, written before a new scored run), or keep the miss.
- The test's reference is ky4 with the skeleton's solver and quality options, chosen before the scored run because a drawing carries none; ky4's own diffusivity changes its chlorine far more than the form does. With chlorine's diffusivity nearly every ky4 junction is low at the truth's dose, so the Jaccard part of the bar says little. And the test form carried every junction's demand (934 large-user rows, which no operator has), so the run shows where the form puts each thing, not what a short form gives: the realistic case, water use spread by pipe length, is the ladder's rung 2 (ky4's water-age error from 8.14 to 10.77 h).
- ky4's and ky10's files set a relative diffusivity of 0.05, and every committed ky4 and ky10 chlorine result inherits it, the ladder's included: their truths' low junctions (376 of ky4's 959 at the daily minimum) would be 897 at chlorine's own diffusivity, and their response to C is damped (Found 3 and 9).
- Not built: the form on the Get started page and the main page, its hand-off, the dose and inlet residuals from its source rows, the shipped template and the readiness sentences on the page. A map-built model still runs only after its source, tanks, pumps and water use are added in EPANET.
- The derived pump design point (from the production's peak hour and the lift to the highest tank, plus 10%) is crude and untested; demand by pipe length is a known weak point (the ladder's rung 2 is its cost); the default pattern is a public model's, not a standard's.
- A location given as a point snaps to the nearest node within 50 ft with no question. Since the review a pipe given as a point must lie 3 ft nearer to it than to any other pipe, so a point at a tee or between two close pipes is refused and the pipe's ID or a point along it is asked.
- Step 4 now asks the size of every pipe when none can be read, where it built at 8 in at once; a layer with no size stops there until it is given.
- The committed Net3 ladder run wrote its rung grids into `outputs/cache` (git-ignored; about 16 MB), where the saved check builds its own in a temporary folder; ky4's and ky10's rung grids are kept there for the sha256 check (about 0.4 GB together). They must be kept for `fidelity_cached_grids_sha256` to pass; since the review that check is in the run log only, so a cleared cache skips it without changing the committed report, and rebuilding the grids takes about 35 and 17 minutes.
- The readiness sentences were worded as costs: they called every step a cost, even one C everywhere, which lowers ky4's false lows after 12 samples (78.9 to 55.4), quoted water-age error for the C steps, which C moves only through the hydraulics, and did not say that each step is measured on top of the ones before it. The review found it; they now say 'measured change' and which way each number moved (After the review). They are still shown nowhere.
- A full run of the saved checks is longer: task 19's eight checks added about 2 minutes (the scored run's rerun about 50 s, the Net3 ladder rerun about 48 s, ky10's PRVs and boundary valve about 13 s each), and the review's additions about 35 s more (the public models' diffusivity runs in the scored run's check about 19 s, the command lines about 12 s, the cached grids about 3 s); a full run took 1,272 s before the review and 1,307 s after it (run logs on this Mac).
- What the saved checks exercise: ky4's form (pumps split at nodes, tank-level and 'always' rules, pipe_c by material, large users), ky10's PRVs in place of pipes and its closed valve, a form without a source, the bad rows, and since the review the review's cases (pipe_c by material, 'plastic' and 'all', a GIS layer's C field, pumps side by side, a PSV in place of a pipe, a part with no source, clock-hour rules at an 08:00 start, the production spread by pipe length, latitude,longitude points, the form applied again, the refusals) and the three command lines, and since the final verification its cases (a branch the form cuts off, check valves both ways, clock rules at 08:00 and 23:00 starts, elevation rows that disagree, ids, far points, the new value ranges, a model in degrees, wrong files) and a timed read of hostile headers. Built but untested still: the derived pump design point, a form given as an Excel sheet, points in a metric drawing's units, and the page and its hand-off, since the form is not offered.

**After the review** (29 issues, 14 in the form's behaviour and 15 in its docs, readiness text, command line, checks and record; each was checked against the code, the records or the docs before it was fixed, the code ones with the reviewers' own scripts. Each code fix has a case of its own in the new check `operator_form_review_cases`, part `review` of the record, on a small drawing and layer of the review's own (`form_review_r12.dxf`, `form_review_gap_r12.dxf`, `form_review_mains.geojson`), or in the new check `operator_form_command_line`. None of the cases is in ky4's form, so the scored run's completed model is the same byte for byte (`completed_sha1` in part `complete`), and no number of the record's earlier parts moved):

*Fixed in the form* (simulation on public models):
- pipe_c rows set C by the C table's class, not by the material: DIP, AC, PCCP, CICL and concrete share 'concrete or concrete lined', and PVC and HDPE share 'plastic', so a later row silently gave every pipe of its class its C (on the review's drawing, ductile iron given 140 and asbestos cement 100 all ended at 100), and the readiness row counted each row's assignments, not pipes. Each skeleton pipe now keeps its material (`topology.material_of`: DIP and ductile iron are one, AC another); a row gives the pipes of its material, 'plastic' the plastic pipes no PVC or polyethylene row gives, and 'all' every pipe no other row gives, whatever the rows' order; a second row for one material is refused with both lines; the readiness row counts pipes. On the review's drawing ductile iron keeps 140 and asbestos cement 100.
- Two pump, PRV or PSV rows at one node with the same suction pipe, the form's own way to enter duty and standby pumps drawn as one point, split the node twice: the second split moved whichever end the pipe still had, so one pump drew from a node with no other link and carried no flow all run, and where the main was drawn from the station to the plant the plant was cut off, both with ok. Rows side by side at one node now share its split, in parallel: on the review's drawing, whose well main is drawn that way, the always-on pump carries a mean of 385.7 gpm over 48 h and the well's main still reaches the well.
- A psv row in place of a pipe gave its elevation to its downstream node, where a PRV's setting acts; a PSV's acts upstream. It now goes upstream: on the review's drawing the PSV's upstream node is at 150 ft, the valve passes 0.0 gpm, its upstream pressure being below its setting, and the tank drains from 20.0 to 16.1 ft in 24 h, where with the elevation downstream the valve had kept filling the tank.
- The no-source rule looked at the whole model only, so a skeleton in two parts was completed from one source row, the production was spread onto the part no source feeds, and EPANET then failed. Each connected part with a junction and no source is now refused in words, with the parts' sizes and a node of the first.
- A clock-hour pump rule started its pump by midnight's state, not the model's START CLOCKTIME, and the form's pattern took midnight's multiplier at the model's first hour: an EPANET model starting at 08:00 with a '6-22' rule kept its pump off until 06:00 the next day. The rule's starting state and the pattern now follow the model's clock (the review's case starts the pump open, closes it at 22:00 and opens it at 06:00, and the pattern's first multiplier is the form's for 08:00), and a model whose own patterns step other than hourly is refused the form's water use, which it had been given with an hourly step for every pattern.
- Applying a form to a model the form had completed failed (its pattern was removed while its junctions used it) and was reported as a problem with the form's rows. The form's pattern now gets a name of its own, and an existing one is never removed.
- Values that passed every row check gave a model EPANET refuses, written with ok: a 3-point curve whose heads rise, a design point at 0 gpm or below 0 ft, a PRV of 0 in, a tank level below its bottom; a dose of -2 mg/L or '1,500' read as 1500 passed too, and a power of 0 failed inside WNTR with no line. Each is refused with its line now, and a PRV or PSV set below 0 psi too; and since what no row check sees can still stop EPANET, the completed model's first hour is now run before it is written (two PRVs side by side at one node, which EPANET refuses, are refused so in words).
- The model's text skipped task 15's intake: a duplicated ID was silently reduced to its later line and then disappeared from the completed text, and the command line crashed on a cp1252 or UTF-16 model and blamed a byte-order mark on the form's rows. The text is now decoded as task 15's intake decodes one (`importers.decode_text`) and scanned for IDs (`importers.scan_inp_ids`) before WNTR reads it, so a duplicated ID is refused in task 15's words; a form that is no table is refused in words on the command line, and the last guard no longer calls a model's failure a problem with the rows.
- Run time grew with rows times nodes, since WNTR rebuilds its name lists at every look-up: a form with a large user at every junction of a grid took 17.9 s at the review's largest size and 4.3 s at a smaller one. Names are now looked up in sets: 4.8 s and 1.7 s, most of it now WNTR's reading and writing of the model and EPANET's first hour (a run log on this Mac).
- The reader's notes ('Columns not read') never reached the result, a header with its unit in brackets ('Dose (mg/L)') was dropped without a word, an elevation row with no elevation was asked for 'the valve's elevation', and a PDF given as the form was told 'A grab log or tap map is a table'. The notes reach the result and the command line prints them with the warnings; units in brackets are read; each message names the form and the row's own kind.
- A pump row with no id is named PUMP1, and a later row with the id 'PUMP1' became PUMP1_2 and took the unnamed pump's place in the rule check, so the unnamed pump ran with no rule and nothing stopped it. Every pump the form adds is now checked by its row, an id equal to an unnamed pump's name is refused, and an unnamed pump's no-rule message says to give it an id. Two cases cover what the task's acceptance asked of the engine: a pump the form adds with no rule is blocked with the measured change on ky4 and ky10, and a PRV with no elevation is refused (neither is shown in the app, which does not take the form).
- Rows claiming one pipe (two PRVs in place of one pipe, a PRV and a closed valve on it) and a pump splitting a source's node (a well and its pump at one point) reached internal errors reported with no line. Each is refused with both lines; a PRV or PSV that would join a tank or a source straight away is refused (EPANET joins none); and the form says how to enter a well with its pump, the pump in place of the first pipe out of the well, which the review's case runs.
- A GIS pipe whose C came from the layer's own C field (85 or 70 on the review's layer, as from hydrant flow tests) was still given its material, so a pipe_c row overwrote the measurement, and the age warning said C came from the material alone when the field gave it. Such pipes keep their C now, the readiness row says how many kept it, and the warning counts only the pipes whose C is still the table's.
- On an EPANET model the snap distance was 50 model units, 50 degrees for a model in longitude and latitude (a closed valve was put on a pipe a degree away), and a point at or beside two pipes took whichever came first. A model whose coordinates read as degrees now takes 50 ft in degrees, and a pipe's point must lie 3 ft nearer to it than to any other pipe, else it is refused with both pipes named: a point at a tee is refused, one halfway along a pipe is not. On a layer in degrees a location written longitude first is refused with a hint to give the latitude first.

*Fixed in the command line:* the documented route, `operator_form SKELETON.inp FORM.csv` on a skeleton .inp written from a map, could not apply the form's material rows (an .inp keeps no materials, and the refusal said the file was not a skeleton), nor read points in a metric drawing's or a GIS layer's own coordinates, and no check ran it. `cad_read` and `gis_read` now take `--form`, which applies the form to the build itself, its materials and coordinates kept: on ky4's drawing and form it writes the scored run's completed model byte for byte. `operator_form MODEL.inp FORM.csv` stays for EPANET models, and its refusal of material rows says why and what to do instead. `operator_form_command_line` runs all three.

*Fixed in the readiness text:* the 'why we need this' sentences are now measured changes that say which way each number moved, quote chlorine error for the C steps, say when a step is on top of the ladder's earlier ones, and add to the material sentence that these public models carry no pipe ages (Regressions above); `readiness_quotes_trace_to_outputs` checks each direction against the numbers it prints.

*Fixed in the checks and the record:* `fidelity_outputs_reproduce` depended on the git-ignored ky4 and ky10 grids, and its skip on a machine without them would have changed the committed report's counts and failed the numbers check. Their sha256 is now `fidelity_cached_grids_sha256`'s, in the run log only, as the names check is, and the committed check keeps the Net3 rerun and every rung file's sha1. Task 18's and task 19's quoted check counts are counted over the groups that existed when each was written (`numbers.groups_through`): the exclusion list task 19 had added would have broken both on the next task's first check. Part `after_the_stop`'s key 'bar_met', which a reader could take for a pass of the bar, is now 'within_the_bar_values_post_hoc'. Part `public_models` records the public models' C values and diffusivity and the measurement of Found 3 and 9.

*Corrected in the docs* (this section, the CHANGELOG and the README): the 1 ft join was called pre-registered and its effect something found after the stop (Found 2 now says it was the build's own setting and that task 17's record had listed the three tees); the README did not say that the test form carried every junction's demand, nor that the reference was ky4 run with the skeleton's options; material-only C was said to cost little, judged on water age (Found 9 is restated on chlorine, both calibration states and the diffusivity); the boundary valve read as if ky10 had one the form restored (Found 5); the ladder's control-loss step did not say that it leaves ky4's pump 1 off all week and ky10's controlled pumps open (Found 8); the README's task-17 and task-18 bullets and the export guide still called the form planned or not built; the run time, the cached grids and the untested features were missing from the Regressions (above); and ky4's and ky10's diffusivity is now a README limitation.

**After the final verification** (16 issues a final verifier confirmed after the review's fixes, 12 in the form's code and 4 in its docs and checks; each was reproduced with the verifier's own scripts before it was fixed. Each code fix has a case of its own in the new check `operator_form_final_verification` (part `final` of the record) or `operator_form_reads_in_linear_time`, or in `operator_form_review_cases` and `fidelity_outputs_reproduce`. None of the cases is in ky4's form: the scored run's completed model is the same byte for byte (`completed_sha1`), parts `complete`, `after_the_stop`, `prvs`, `boundary`, `no_source`, `bad_rows` and `public_models` did not move, and in part `review` three messages changed, as their fixes below say, and two cases were added):

*Fixed in the form* (simulation on public models):
- The header reader took cubic time. The review's pattern for a unit in brackets ('Dose (mg/L)') had three runs of spaces competing for the same characters, so a header with an opening bracket, a run of spaces and no closing bracket stalled the reader: a header of 1.6 KB took 3.6 s, and the verifier's 6.5 KB form was still being read after 100 s, by itself and on every command line that takes a form (the grab-log reader underneath took no measurable time). The pattern is now linear (a bracket's contents hold no bracket, and nothing else competes for a space), and a header longer than 120 characters names no column. The 6.5 KB form, a header cell as long as the csv module takes and headers of nothing but brackets are each read within 5 s, in a process of its own, so a slow reader fails the check instead of stalling the run (`operator_form_reads_in_linear_time`).
- A branch the form itself cut off was completed. The review's rule counted connected parts over every link, open or not, so a branch the form closed off (a closed_valve row on its only pipe, a check valve or a pump pointing out of it, a pump whose rule is 'never') counted as fed: the production was spread onto it, EPANET's first hour only warned, the model was written with ok, and the main page's intake would have prepared it too, though EPANET reports the branch's junctions disconnected. A junction with water use now counts as fed only when a source's water can reach it over the links that can carry water: a link closed with nothing to open it carries none, a pump, a check valve, a PRV, a PSV or an FCV carries it one way, and a tank passes it on (a tank alone is still no source, in a part as in the whole model). A junction left over is refused in words that name the link that keeps water out and the rows that made it so, and a tank on its side is named as feeding it only until it is empty. The same rows where they cut nothing off still complete; a junction fed only through a pump that a rule turns off at times, with no tank on its side, is completed with a warning that EPANET reports it disconnected while the pump is off. Of the verifier's grammar fuzzer's 3,000 random forms, 34 had been completed with nodes EPANET reports disconnected within a day; after this fix 8 were, each a tank on the only path whose water stood above every source's head in a model with no pump, so it drained and never filled again. Such a tank is now refused too (with no pump, water rises no higher than the highest source's head), and 1 is left: a tank on the only path, low enough to fill, that fills to its maximum and then closes (below). The counts are copied into the record (part `final`, `grammar_fuzz`).
- Clock rules with minutes, which the reader took ('6:30-22:00'), wrote a control WNTR's own reader refuses: WNTR writes the time as a decimal hour (`AT CLOCKTIME 6.5`) and then cannot read it back, so the form blocked itself with a message that blamed its pumps, valves and tanks. The form takes whole hours only, and a rule with minutes is refused with its line ('06:00-22:00' is read); at 08:00 and 23:00 starts EPANET's pump status follows each whole-hour rule (`0-6`, `18-24`, `22-6` and `6-22`) at every model hour of two days.
- Messages named temporary files: where WNTR's reader failed, in the first hour or in the last guard, the operator was shown a path under the system's temporary folder (about a quarter of the last-guard messages in the verifier's fuzz of mutated models). A message now gives the error WNTR's reader wraps, when there is one (`(Error 213) invalid option value` for a half-hour rule written by hand), with no file named and no '%s' left over; the verifier's fuzz, rerun, quotes no path in any message.
- An id that starts with '[' ('[END]', '[P1]') was kept, and WNTR wrote it at the start of a data line, where WNTR's reader and EPANET's both read a section header: no model was written, but the operator was given an internal Python error with no line. Such an id is now renamed (END, P1), as an id with a space is, and the written model reads back whole.
- A point with an enormous coordinate reached an internal error: the KD-tree finds no neighbour once the distance overflows and returns an index one past the last node, and a little short of that the refusal quoted a distance of over a hundred digits. A point more than a billion feet (or model units) from the model's nodes is now refused as far off the network, with its line, before any distance is measured.
- Rows were taken with ok and then ignored or overridden without a word: a pattern row given to a model with water use of its own (the form's pattern shapes only the form's own water use), two elevation rows for one node (the later kept), an elevation row at a source's node (dropped), and an elevation row that disagrees with a PRV's elevation_ft at the node the PRV puts it on (the elevation row kept). Each is now refused with its line, and with the other row's line where there is one.
- The pipe_c readiness row counted pipes before PRVs, PSVs and pumps replace some of them (9 pipes given the row's C in a model left with 7); it now counts the pipes still in the model.
- pipe_c rows were applied to an EPANET model whose headloss formula is not Hazen-Williams, where a pipe's roughness is a roughness height (Darcy-Weisbach) or Manning's n, so the row's C of 110 became every pipe's roughness height, with ok. They are refused there now, with their lines.
- Values the row checks had missed: a head written `490 500` was read as `490500` ft (a space inside one number is no longer read as a thousands separator, nor is an underscore); a tank-level rule whose levels lie outside the tank's own range (on below 2 ft where the minimum is 5 ft never started its pump; off above 40 ft with the tank full at 30 ft never stopped it); and a tank's base at 646,000 ft. A rule's levels must now lie inside the tank's range, and an elevation or a head outside -1,500 to 20,000 ft is refused as a slip in the digits or the unit.
- On an EPANET model in degrees the snap was 50 ft taken as degrees of latitude in every direction, so east and west, where a degree of longitude is shorter, it was about a fifth short at the review's latitude, and its refusals overstated distances east and west by about a quarter. The model's coordinates and each point are now put on a tangent plane in feet first: a point 45 ft east or north of a junction is placed on it, and one 55 ft east or north is refused as 55 ft away.
- Wrong files got the main page's advice for a model upload: an EPANET model given as the form was told 'It is checked below and then run', and a drawing or a GIS layer to send its EPANET export instead; and the command line read any file as model text, so a zipped model printed the zip's compressed bytes and a drawing a syntax error. The form now says what to do in its own words, and the command line identifies the model file by its content, as task 15's intake does: a zipped model is read from the zip, a drawing or a GIS layer is pointed to cad_read or gis_read with --form, and anything else is refused in words.

*Fixed in the checks:* `operator_form_review_cases` never exercised two of the review's guards, so removing either left it passing: a GIS layer's own measured C kept against an 'all' row (the layer case gave only a 'cast iron' row, which reaches no pipe with a measured C), and a tank alone being no source in a part (the gap part had no tank). Both cases are added: under an 'all' row of 110, M1 and M2 keep 85 and 70, and the gap drawing with a tank in its far part is refused. No check guarded the review's look-ups in sets (a form with a large user at every junction of a grid had taken 17.9 s): `operator_form_reads_in_linear_time` counts how often WNTR's name lists are built, as often for 400 large users as for 25. `fidelity_outputs_reproduce`, in the committed report, read the git-ignored cache through `fidelity_reproduce_grids`, which unpickled every grid only to drop its sha256, so a grid cut short by an interrupted build failed a committed check; it now reads no grid, and a grid that cannot be read is reported, not raised, by the run-log check. As the verifier did, each fix was then put back one at a time, in memory only: the checks catch the three mutants that had survived and each mutant of this pass's fixes.

*Corrected in the docs:* Found 8 judged C 130 on every pipe on water age ('costs little by itself'), the reasoning the review had removed from Found 9 for material-only C; it is restated on chlorine error and both calibration states, as Found 9 is. The README's diffusivity limitation said that at 0.05 chlorine responds less to the wall rate, which holds at the 95th percentile only (at ky4's median junction the response is larger at 0.05); it now says so. The README's sentence that a part no source feeds is refused in plain words was not true of a part the form itself cut off, and is now.

*Left as it is, recorded:* the form now refuses forms it completed before (a branch it cuts off, a branch fed only by a tank it cuts off from every source, a tank no source can fill, a rule with minutes, a value outside the new ranges); in the verifier's fuzz of mutated forms most of these were mutations of its seed form, whose closed valve cuts off the branch that holds its tank. When a tank fills or empties is not checked: a tank on the only path to a branch closes when it is full or empty, and EPANET then cuts the branch off, which the first hour cannot see (the fuzzer's 1 form left), and a branch fed only through a pump on the clock with no tank is completed, with a warning. WNTR's own reader still raises internal errors on some malformed models (in its [PIPES] reader, for example), which the last guard reports as a case this tool does not handle; the form's own code raised none in the verifier's two fuzzers, rerun. The main page's intake (`importers.prepare_inp`) still counts connected parts over every link, so a model from elsewhere with a closed pipe that cuts junctions off is prepared as before: changing it would change a default path.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.intake_exp fidelity Net3 ky10 ky4` (Net3 a few minutes; ky10 about 17 minutes and ky4 about 35 on this Mac when their grids are built, most of it the grids, each record giving its grids' first build times; a few minutes from the cache), `... intake_exp boundary` (about 2 minutes, once) and `... intake_exp form` (a few minutes); run them from a folder outside the repository, since EPANET writes scratch files into the working directory. The form: `python -m residualmap.cad_read DRAWING.dxf --form FORM.csv --out completed.inp` (or `python -m residualmap.gis_read LAYER --form FORM.csv --out completed.inp`), which keeps each pipe's material and the drawing's or layer's coordinates, or `python -m residualmap.operator_form MODEL.inp FORM.csv --out completed.inp` for an EPANET model, where a location is a node ID or a point in the file's own coordinates and a pipe_c row can only give one C to every pipe. The thirteen task-19 checks are part of the full run; all but `no_source_never_runs`, `form_parser_refuses_bad_rows`, `readiness_quotes_trace_to_outputs`, `operator_form_review_cases`, `operator_form_final_verification` and `operator_form_reads_in_linear_time` are skipped by `--quick`.

### Task 21, the input and output contract, and the iteration-5 record

**Asked:** (plan task 21, category g; critique findings 23 and 24b) a plain-language page of every input and output, `docs/io_contract.md`, with formats, columns and units, brought up to date with tasks 15b, 16b, 16c and 20 (inlet residuals, `--holdout-sites`, `--date-format`, `--allow-large`, `--inlets`, the clock-time display and the cloud mode); its machine-readable twin, `residualmap/contract.py`, which the Get started page's template downloads and 'What files it takes' table read, and which a saved check holds to the code, the templates and the doc; a registry of iteration 5's headline numbers in `residualmap/numbers.py`; an 'Iteration 5 in one page' section and limitations bullets in the README; the journal's iteration-5 summary; two new saved checks, `io_contract_matches_code` and `iteration5_claims_labelled`, and three extended ones (`numbers_trace_to_outputs`, `no_dashes_in_iteration4_text`, `no_listed_names_in_tracked_files`); the WNTR bug of task 17 reproduced with real geopandas in a scratch folder before any public line, with a draft report for Devansh to file himself; and the Conrad facts of critique 23 checked. The lead added: fold the lead's own decisions and hand fixes into this record, and say plainly which tasks met their pre-registered bars and which stopped. No new package and no download. Two earlier attempts were cut off by network outages, the second after hours of work. The first left the contract, its doc, the two checks, the page's table and two small code edits; the second reviewed and finished them and wrote the registry, the wording pass and these sections. A third pass checked all of it against every acceptance criterion, fixed what it found (Found 6) and ran the saved checks until the report settled. A review of that pass, in three lenses, raised fifteen issues; a fourth pass checked each against the files, fixed the real ones and ran the saved checks again (Found 7). A final verifier then confirmed nine more issues after those fixes, and a fifth pass reproduced each with the verifier's scripts, fixed it and ran the saved checks again (After the final verification, below). That pass's last full run failed only `repo_untouched`, because git's view of the repository changed while it ran (the Claude Code session's lock file under `.claude/`, the one tracked file touched in that window, was rewritten), and with it `numbers_trace_to_outputs`, whose quoted check counts follow the report. A sixth pass, on October 25, ran the verifier's scripts again on a copy of the final tree, reran the four committed reruns on it and ran the saved checks again with nothing else writing to the repository. All of it is this task's own work.

**Outcome, in one paragraph.** Built, and its acceptance met but for one line, below: the contract and the code agree in both directions (every flag, column, template header, on-screen label, range, limit and committed output column the code has is in the contract, everything in the contract is in the code, the doc names all of it, and every table of the doc is the one the contract renders), every iteration-5 number in the README and this journal traces to a committed file under `outputs/` or is classified, every iteration-5 paragraph that quotes an importer number says that it is simulation or converter self-consistency, no em or en dash was added, and no utility on the private contact lists is named in any tracked file except the allowed Net3 provenance below. One acceptance line, that no utility is named anywhere in the repository, is not met: two tracked places still name a real utility. One is the Net3 provenance string in `residualmap/experiment.py` (`NET_DESC`, EPA's public description of its Net3 example), which the lead kept in task 14 as the names check's one allowed match and which is drawn into the titles of the committed Net3 map figures. The other is one line of iteration 3's prompt (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`), which names the target pilot, a name no private list holds, so the names check cannot see it. Task 14 found both and left them, the first by the lead's decision and the second for Devansh, since the file is his own prompt; both are left so here (Found 6 and 7). Two parts were not done. The WNTR bug was not reproduced with geopandas, because that needs a scratch install Devansh has not approved and this task allowed no new package; its code side and the half of it that needs no geopandas were reproduced instead (Found 4). The Conrad rubric's weights were not checked against the official page, so they are quoted nowhere (Found 5). Nothing a default path computes changed.

**Sources.** Paper Lantern was not available in this session, so there is no Paper Lantern attribution. For the WNTR bug, WNTR 1.5.0's own source as installed in `.venv` (`wntr/network/io.py`, `wntr/network/elements.py` and `wntr/gis/network.py`).

**Built:**
- `docs/io_contract.md` (new), in plain words: the units; 'What files it takes', the table the Get started page shows; the network model (an EPANET model, a GIS layer, a CAD drawing, and the files that are recognised but not read, each with what to send instead); grab samples (typed on the main page, a grab log with the roles its reader fills and the names it takes, the tap map, inlet residuals); the plant log; the operator form, as built and tested and not yet in the app; the templates; every input on the main page and on the Get started page with its range and default; the command lines that take a utility's files, flag by flag; the outputs (the readiness report, the prepared model, the main page's numbers, sections and tables, the PDF, the pilot's files and their columns, the folders it writes to); the hosted demo; the limits; and what is not built or not tested.
- `residualmap/contract.py` (new), the same as data: the units, the files table, each reader's roles with the template column for each and the columns it does not read, the operator form's kinds, columns and aliases, the templates and their headers, the Get started page's downloads, each command's flags, every labelled input, title and header of both pages (where it is, its kind, its label, its range and default), the main page's sections, metrics and table columns, the pilot's output columns, the folders it writes and the limits, each the code's own setting. Since the review it also renders the doc's tables (`doc_tables()`) and its sentences that quote a limit (`doc_sentences()`, the analyzer rule among them). `python -m residualmap.contract` prints the files table, checks the templates' headers and the doc's tables, and with `--write-tables` rewrites those tables.
- Read from the contract: the Get started page's template downloads and their labels, and its new 'What files it takes' table, which opens step 3. The table is shown where files can be given: on the laptop, and in the hosted demo only when its uploads are turned on (with them off the page stops before it, as before). It is a table, not a widget, so no widget moves. The main page's names that count as the templates' own, and the keys the pilot's `--columns` takes, come from the contract too; neither changes what the code does.
- `residualmap/checks.py`, a `task21` group of two checks:
  - `io_contract_matches_code`: the command lines (every flag and positional argument that each command's `main()` declares, read from the source, is in the contract, and nothing else is); the tables a utility gives (the grab log's roles are the keys of the column guess, the templates' headers are guessed into them for free and total chlorine, a long-format header gives the analyte and unit roles, the keys the reader looks up in a mapping are the pilot's `--columns` keys; the tap map's and the inlet file's roles likewise; the plant log's required columns are the ones the pilot tests, each one dropped is refused; the operator form's kinds, columns and aliases are the form's own); the templates (the folder holds exactly the contract's files, each with the contract's header); the labels (every labelled input, title and header of both pages, read from the source with every arm of a conditional and a run-time label written as {}, is in the contract and the reverse, with each constant range and default; the scanner fails on a Streamlit call it does not know, a label it cannot read and an input drawn on an object it cannot trace); the main page's sections, metrics and table columns; the committed pilot files' headers; each limit against its module's constant; the plant log (a semicolon export with decimal commas and a UTF-16 copy of the template read as the template, a blank or non-numeric temperature and a month not written YYYY-MM refused with the file's name); the doc (it names every flag, column, role, kind, alias, template and label; each of its tables is the one the contract renders, under its heading and in its order, with no other table, and each sentence that quotes a limit is the contract's word for word; every name it puts in backticks is the contract's, a check's, a file of the repository, a command line with its module's own flags, or one of a few literals); and, once, the page itself, rendered headless on the laptop, in the hosted demo with uploads on and with uploads off (not under `--quick`, which skips the app). Twenty-two negative controls, each a change to the contract, the doc or a module's constant, must each fail it, and thirteen small page sources must each be read or refused by the label scanner as listed.
  - `iteration5_claims_labelled`: every paragraph of the iteration-5 README and journal text, every table with its caption and every iteration-5 limitations bullet that quotes an importer number says 'simulation' or 'converter self-consistency' (or the record's 'same-library' or 'reader' self-consistency), and no sentence claims a validation outside simulation: a sentence with 'validated', 'validates', 'proven' or 'verified on' and no 'in simulation' fails, and so does one that claims it on utility, real or field data, unless the claim word is negated just before it. An importer number is a registry number whose file is under `outputs/intake/`, or any number in a paragraph that names such a file, other than a date, time, identifier, citation or run cost. Five negative controls must be caught, and four honest sentences must not be.

- `residualmap/pilot.py` (the review, Found 7): `load_plant` reads the plant log through `importers.read_table`, as a grab log is read, and refuses a blank or non-numeric temperature and a month not written YYYY-MM with the file's name; `main` prints that refusal as a plain message.

The claims check, run on it, found paragraphs in the sections of tasks 15, 15b, 16b, 16c, 17, 18 and 19, and one README bullet (ky4's and ky10's diffusivity), that quote an importer number with the label only in a neighbouring paragraph or in the numbers table's caption. Each now says it in its own words: 'converter self-consistency on public models' for tasks 15, 15b, 17 and 18, 'simulation' for tasks 16b, 16c and 19 and the bullet. In task 16's how-to-run line a hyphenated task name read as a number and was reworded. No number changed.
- The README's 'Iteration 5 in one page' and three limitations bullets; a CHANGELOG entry; this section, the lead's decisions and the iteration-5 summary.

**Found:**
1. The first attempt's partial work held up. Its contract and the code already agreed in both directions. Six things were fixed: the doc said that an InfoWater project database and EPANET's own `.net` project are recognised, and the code has no such kinds (a `.net` file is expected to be called unknown, with the advice to send the EPANET export, which the doc now says; no `.net` file was tested); the operator form's heading in the doc had a parenthesis inside a parenthesis; the doc named the PDF after the network where the app names it after the model file (Net3's PDF is `residualmap_Net3.inp.pdf`); the doc had no part on the hosted demo, which the plan asked for; the check never looked at the page, so a table dropped from the page would have passed (the page part was added); and the check built its negative controls twice.
2. What the contract covers, as the check counts it: 39 command-line flags and positional arguments over 5 commands (19 of them the pilot's); 41 labels on the main page (its inputs and the sidebar's headers; since the final verification its title is checked as the contract's title and its subheaders as its sections) and 42 on the Get started page (its inputs, title, headers and subheaders), with 12 and 4 distinct ranges; 5 templates; the grab log's 6 roles and 7 mapping keys; the operator form's 28 columns and 13 kinds. The Get started page shows the files table's 8 rows and 4 columns on the laptop and in the hosted demo with uploads on, under step 3's header and before its file checker, and not at all with uploads off. All 22 negative controls fail the check: a label missing either way, a range changed, a flag missing either way, a template header changed, a reader's role dropped, a limit changed, a required plant column dropped, a headline metric dropped, the doc missing a flag or a label or naming something the contract does not, and the nine the review added (Found 7). What it does not cover: the route table's column names (the code renames them, so they are listed, not checked), captions, markdown and expander titles (only labelled inputs, titles, headers and subheaders are read), and the 'what it is' wording, which a person checks, not the code (since Found 7 the doc's tables are rendered from the contract, so the two say the same thing; whether it is right is still a person's call).
3. The claims check finds 57 paragraphs, tables and bullets that quote an importer number, of 177 in scope, this task's own text included, and every one is labelled (`outputs/chem/checks_report.json`). Its idea of an importer number is a rule of thumb: a number that names no file and is not in the registry escapes it, and a paragraph that names a file and quotes only a setting is flagged (the reason for task 16's reworded line).
4. The WNTR bug (critique 24b), as far as it could go without geopandas. The code side is confirmed in the installed WNTR 1.5.0: for a junction without a `demand_timeseries_list`, `from_dict` reads its pattern from `pattern_name`, while a junction's dictionary, and so WNTR's own GIS layer of junctions, carries it as `demand_pattern`; `from_gis` keeps only plain values, so the list `demand_timeseries_list` is gone and the branch without it runs, finds no `pattern_name`, and adds the junction with no pattern, so it takes the network's default pattern. Reproduced without geopandas, in a scratch script that is not committed: Net3's dictionary from `to_dict`, with that list removed as `to_gis` removes it and rebuilt by `from_dict`, gives every junction the default pattern, so a junction with a pattern of its own loses it (its counts are not in `outputs/`, so they are not quoted). Task 17's committed negative control measures what this does to chlorine: without the copy of `demand_pattern` into `pattern_name`, Net3's chlorine moves by up to 0.762 mg/L (`outputs/intake/gis_roundtrip.json`, simulation on a public model). Not done: the round trip through WNTR's own `to_gis` and `from_gis` with real geopandas, shapely, pyogrio and pyproj in a scratch folder. That needs Devansh's approval. The plan frames the bug as an incomplete fix of a WNTR issue (`#434`, `PR #447`, merged November 2024); without the web this task could not check that, so the draft keeps it as the plan's reading. No public line until the bug is reproduced and filed.
   - Draft report, for Devansh to check and file himself after the geopandas reproduction (Claude does not post it). Title: 'from_gis drops junction demand patterns: from_dict reads pattern_name, the GIS layer has demand_pattern'. Body: in WNTR 1.5.0, `to_gis` writes each junction's pattern in the `demand_pattern` column and leaves out `demand_timeseries_list`, which is a list. `from_gis` passes the records to `from_dict`, whose junction branch without `demand_timeseries_list` reads `pattern_name` (`wntr/network/io.py:133-135`), which is absent, so each junction gets no pattern and runs on the network's default. Steps: load Net3, `to_gis`, `from_gis`, compare each junction's `demand_pattern` with the original's. Expected: the original patterns. A possible fix: in that branch, read `demand_pattern` when `pattern_name` is absent. It looks like an incomplete fix of `#434` (`PR #447`).
5. The Conrad facts (critique 23). None of the three is in the repository: the rubric's weights are quoted nowhere, nor the rural water association's count of mapped systems, nor any count of research agents (a search of every tracked file). So there is nothing to correct; the rule stands that the weights are checked against the official Conrad page before they are quoted anywhere. The named target pilot buys a large utility's chloraminated wholesale water, and the outreach script already leaves it out for that reason, so the link is known, not unverified; whether the chloramine mode brings it back into the outreach is open decision 12, for Devansh. Neither is named here.

The README's one-page table quoted the saved checks' counts with the committed report as their only source, while the report holds the checks that pass in it and the names check and the cached-grid check are in the git-ignored run log; the row now says where each is. The saved checks' own header listed neither the `task21` group nor the counts after this task. And the outcome said that no utility on the private lists is named, but one tracked line names a utility no list holds (the review then found that a second tracked place was left out, Found 7): iteration 3's prompt, `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`, names the target pilot. Task 14 found that line and left it for Devansh, and task 15 recorded it; it is his own prompt, so it is left for him again, and this record now says so plainly. The names check passes with each private list (the outreach lists and the list with the large-utility contact's organisation added). The WNTR scratch script above was rerun on this tree and gives the same result. The second attempt's last full run of the saved checks kept going through the outage and finished with every check passing but the external layer's, which is skipped, and with the committed report unchanged, so the report had settled before this pass's text edits.
7. The review of the third pass (correctness, numbers and honesty, and nothing else broken) raised fifteen issues. Each was checked against the files; all were real, two of them the same one, and each is fixed:
   - The doc's tables were written once by a scratch script and never compared with the contract, so a default, a range, a kind, a template header or an extra row could change in the doc, or a limit in the code and the contract together, and the check still passed: it looked only for each limit's value anywhere in the doc, and short values such as 3, 5 and 10 always match other text. The renderer is now `contract.doc_tables()`, and the check holds every table of the doc to it, in order and under its heading, with no other table allowed; the doc's sentences that quote a limit come from `contract.doc_sentences()` and must be in it word for word; and `python -m residualmap.contract --write-tables` rewrites the tables in place.
   - The doc's reverse test looked only at backticked names in lower case, so a mixed-case column such as `temp_F`, or a template that does not exist, passed. It now takes every backticked name, and one that is not the contract's, a check's, a file of the repository, a command line of one of its modules with that module's own flags, or one of a few literals (file extensions and `START CLOCKTIME`) fails.
   - The label scanner passed over inputs it did not know (a select slider, a date or time input, a link button), a label given through `**kwargs` and `st.sidebar.header`, and it put `st.sidebar.selectbox` on the main page. It now fails closed: a call on a Streamlit object whose method is on none of its lists, an input or heading without a label it can read, and an input drawn on an object it cannot trace all fail the check, and thirteen small page sources hold it to that. On the two pages it finds the same 41 and 42 labels as before.
   - The analyzer rule was wrong in the doc and the contract. A day keeps its reading nearest 10:00 only when one lies within 60 minutes of it, so a day without one keeps nothing, and a tap with 12 or more readings a day on 7 or more days is an analyzer at any step, an hourly series included. The rule is now one sentence rendered from the reader's own constants, which the Limits table lists.
   - The plant log, offered as a template on the Get started page, was read with a plain `read_csv`, outside the pilot's error handling: a semicolon export with decimal commas stopped the pilot with a traceback and a misleading message, a UTF-16 file with a decode error, and a blank temperature only later, under the grab log's name. This is older than task 21 (the reader dates from task 10). It is now read as a grab log is; a blank or non-numeric temperature and a month not written YYYY-MM are refused with the plant file's name; and the pilot prints the refusal as a plain message. On the committed example plant log and the template the frame is the same as before, value for value and type for type.
   - The claims check tested a paragraph, not a sentence, and only the word 'validated', so a sentence that said simulation and then claimed a validation on utility data passed, and so did 'validates'. It now works per sentence, with more claim words and a rule for claims on real data; its negative controls are caught and its honest sentences are not. The iteration-5 text passes it unchanged.
   - The contract check rendered the Get started page under `--quick`, which is meant to skip the app. Under `--quick` it now leaves the page alone; no check was added, so the check counts are unchanged.
   - The record missed a second tracked place that names a real utility: the Net3 provenance string in `residualmap/experiment.py`, which task 14 kept as the names check's one allowed match and which reaches the committed Net3 map figures' titles. The outcome, the README, the CHANGELOG, the regressions and open decision 5 now name both places and neither utility.
   - Wording. The doc's opening said everything the pages show is listed, and captions, expander titles and the optional modes' extra output columns are not. The README's and the doc's lines on vendor databases called the `.wtg` file a model database (it holds the project's settings, and the model is the `.wtg.sqlite` or `.wtg.mdb` beside it), left out that a GeoPackage, which is a SQLite database, is read, and said that EPANET's `.net` project is called unknown, which no test shows. The summary stated as a finding that most small systems have no hydraulic model, which is what several outreach replies said. The README said the other tasks met their bars, though task 21's no-names line is not met and task 20's smoke test after the deploy waits. The lead-decisions part left out the defaults the lead took on 18 October, among them the package installs. And this section described the private lists two ways.

**Regressions, recorded not hidden:**
- The WNTR bug was not reproduced with geopandas (Found 4), so the plan's condition for any public line about it is not met.
- The Conrad rubric's weights were not checked against the official page; they stay out of every text until they are.
- The contract check reads the code's syntax. A label written in a way it cannot follow fails it on purpose, so a later task that builds a label some new way has to teach the check or the contract; and what it does not read (captions, markdown, expander titles, the route table's columns) can still drift.
- The claims check is a rule of thumb (Found 3).
- Two tracked places still name a real utility (Found 6 and 7): the Net3 provenance string in `residualmap/experiment.py`, which the lead kept in task 14 and which is drawn into the committed Net3 figures' titles, and one line of iteration 3's prompt (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`), which names the target pilot. Changing either is Devansh's call; renaming the first changes committed figures.
- The plan expected 139 saved checks after this task; there are 160, because the reviews of tasks 15 to 19 added checks the plan did not count.
- A full run of the saved checks is a few seconds longer: the page part renders the Get started page three times (`--quick` skips that part).
- A behaviour change outside the defaults (Found 7): the pilot's plant log now refuses a month not written YYYY-MM (the old reader took '2026-01-01' as a month that matched no sample) and a blank or non-numeric temperature (which failed later, under the grab log's name). Since the final verification it also refuses a temperature outside 0 to 35 C and a row with a filled cell beyond its header, and it reads again a log whose rows leave out the optional cells, which the review's reader had refused (After the final verification).

**After the final verification** (nine issues a final verifier confirmed after the review's fixes, one major and eight minor: six in the contract and its check, two in the pilot's plant log and one in the claims check; each was reproduced with the verifier's own scripts before it was fixed. Each fix has cases of its own in `io_contract_matches_code` or `iteration5_claims_labelled`, so no saved check was added. No default path moved: on the fixed tree the four committed reruns write 50 of 50 files byte-identical, and the app's default demo still flags 42 of 92, finds 36 of 36 and has 1 false alarm, label `post_task21_final`):

*Fixed in the contract and its check:*
- The defaults the contract quoted in words were never compared with the code (the major issue). The pilot's `--window` default, its free-chlorine dose, the main page's chloramine pH, the GIS joining distance and the typed samples' mg/L column could each change with the check still passing, while the contract's docstring and this task's CHANGELOG entry said that a drifting default fails. Each quoted default now has an entry in `contract.DEFAULTS` (its module, constant, key and value) or `contract.CLI_DEFAULTS`, or is a column range in `contract.TYPED_SAMPLES_RANGES`, and every string that quotes it is rendered from that entry. The check compares each entry with the code: a module's constant or an item of it, a function's parameter default, a module-level name of the main page (its chloramine pH and Cl2:N defaults are now named there, `CA_PH0` and `CA_CL2N0`, with their old values), every add_argument default other than None, read from the five commands' sources in both directions, and the column ranges of the main page's data editor. So that no number slips past the entries, no string written in `residualmap/contract.py` may hold a number it does not render (labels, column names, the doc's headings, quoted names and 'step N' aside). The pilot's own threshold default is now read from `chemistry.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD_MGL`, which holds the same two values. The settings the doc's prose had written by hand (the run's length and quality step, the joining distances, the typed samples' columns, the plant log's temperature range and the maps' band) are now sentences the contract renders.
- The doc's table parser read only lines that start with '|'. GitHub's Markdown also renders a row without its end pipes, a row indented up to three spaces, a line with no pipe right under a table and a table without end pipes, and the check saw none of them. A table is now any block (lines between blank lines) with a '|' in any line, and each must be one the contract renders, whole; pandoc, reading the doc as GitHub's Markdown, finds the same 14 tables with the same rows. Run again on this fix, the verifier's fuzz of the doc found that a heading written right above a table was taken into the table and lost when the tables were rewritten: a heading is now a block of its own, and `--write-tables` refuses a table with a line that is no row instead of writing over it. A second sentence that quoted a limit with another value passed too, since only the rendered sentence had to be there; the prose may now hold no number outside the sentences the contract renders.
- The doc's single-quoted names were never compared. After a label was renamed in the code and the contract and the doc's tables were rewritten, the prose still quoted the old label; and renaming the sidebar's 'Upload my .inp' left the files table, which the Get started page shows operators, pointing at an option that no longer existed. Every phrase in single quotes in the doc's prose and in the contract's strings must now be a label (whole, without its closing parenthesis, or with a capital letter for each value written at run time), the main page's title, a section, metric or column, an option of the new `APP_OPTIONS_NAMED`, each read from its input's options in the source (the list, its appends and both arms of a conditional), or an example of the new `DOC_EXAMPLES`, each asked of the reader ('Sample Point' is guessed as the tap column, '<0.02' and 'ND' are read as censored, and the `--columns` example's keys are the pilot's); and every 'step N' must be a step of the Get started page.
- The main page's titles were not compared at all: its title could change and a title could be added in its sidebar, and Found 2, the doc's opening and the CHANGELOG said otherwise. The contract now holds the title, `APP_TITLE`, and the check holds every title of the main page to it and its subheaders to its sections, each where it is drawn.
- The label scanner still misread some sources without failing: a label name assigned twice was read from its first assignment, a function drawn in the sidebar through another function or a loop was filed under the main page, `getattr(st, 'button')` and a bound method were not seen, and a data editor or a metric added to the Get started page passed. Each is now refused: a name bound twice anywhere in the file has no value the check takes, a function that draws must be used by direct calls only (and be no method, decorated function or lambda), getattr on a Streamlit object and a Streamlit attribute used other than by a call fail, Streamlit must be imported as st, and the Get started page may draw no metric or data editor. Fifteen new page sources hold the scanner to this, thirteen refused and two read. The two real pages read as before.
- The old analyzer rule was still in the pilot's `--analyzer-hour` help, in the README's paragraph on the main page's samples and in the importers module's docstring: a tap with 12 or more readings a day, minutes apart, on 2 or more days, keeping the reading nearest 10:00. The code also thins an hourly series on 7 days, and keeps nothing on a day with no reading within 60 minutes of 10:00. And the `--allow-large` help wrote 3,000 into its text. The pilot's `--analyzer-hour` help is now the contract's (the hour, then `analyzer_rule()`), and its `--allow-large`, `--dose` and `--threshold` helps render their numbers from the code's constants. The README, the guide `docs/how_to_export_your_model.md` and the importers docstring were corrected, the docstring by the constants' names. The check now fails on an argparse help of the five commands that writes a default's or a limit's number into its text, and on any statement of the rule in the README, the pages, the command lines' sources and the docs that is not `analyzer_rule()` whole (it is there whole in 4 places).

*Fixed in the pilot:*
- The plant log took any number as a temperature. A log in Fahrenheit (68 to 75 in `temp_C`), or one with -40 or 1000, was read, and the pilot then loaded the grid and stopped inside its validation with the error under the grab log's name, the mislabel the review had fixed for a blank temperature. A temperature outside 0 to 35 C, Chemistry's own range (`pilot.PLANT_TEMP_C`, which the check holds to Chemistry at both ends), is now refused with the plant file's name and its rows, and when every one is above 35 the message says they look like Fahrenheit. Run end to end on Net3 with the verifier's inputs, the pilot now stops before the grid, with the plant file's name.
- The review's reader refused a plant log whose rows leave out the optional cells at their end, which `read_csv` had read, and when every row left them out it called the first data row the header. `importers.read_table` takes a new option, `header_names` (None by default, so a grab log and a tap map are read as before): a text whose first line holds month and temp_C takes that line as its header, and a short row is read with its missing cells blank, as `read_csv` read it. A row with a filled cell beyond the header is refused with its row number. The committed example plant log and the template give the same frame as `read_csv` did, value for value and type for type.

*Fixed in the claims check:* a negation anywhere in the three words before a claim word made it no claim, so a claim on real data passed in a sentence that opened with 'No doubt', 'Not surprisingly', 'Without doubt' or 'Nothing failed:', or one about every utility layer after 'No question:'; and since the real-data pattern consumed the first claim word of a sentence, a second claim in it (on utility data, after one in simulation) was never tested. A negation now counts only when it governs the claim word ('not', 'never' or 'no longer' just before it, "n't", or a negated subject just before its verb, such as 'nothing here is'), each claim word is tested on its own, the real-data rule looks at most six words ahead, twice, and it reads 'proved' and, in a claim on real data, 'confirmed' too. The verifier's six sentences and three more are caught (a second claim after a negated one, and one with each new word), eight honest sentences pass, and the rule is now linear in the sentence's length: a sentence of 153,636 characters takes well under a second, where the old rule took more than 10 s in the verifier's run. The iteration-5 text passes it unchanged.

*Corrected in the docs:* Found 2 now says how the main page's title is checked; the contract's docstring and the doc's opening say what the check holds, and the doc names the main page's title. The verifier's own scripts, run again on a copy of the fixed tree (with the lines that moved adapted), catch every case they had reported as passing. On October 25 they were run once more, on a copy of the final tree, with the same result: every contract case is caught and the unchanged control passes, the scanner refuses every source it had misread, the pilot stops on the Fahrenheit log under the plant file's name before the grid, the short plant rows are read as `read_csv` read them, the claims rule catches the verifier's sentences, and the fuzzers of the doc and the scanner find nothing.

| the final verification | result | bar |
|---|---|---|
| its changes to the code, the contract and the doc (`io_contract_matches_code`) | 25 of 25 fail the check, each with its own message; 15 of 15 page sources refused or read by the label scanner as listed | every one (met) |
| what the contract now holds to the code | 15 quoted defaults and settings, 5 add_argument defaults, 1 named option and 6 examples; the doc's 14 tables and 9 rendered sentences | every one, both ways (met) |
| the claims rule (`iteration5_claims_labelled`) | 9 of 9 claims caught and 8 of 8 honest sentences passed; a sentence of 153,636 characters read in under a second | every one (met) |
| the four committed reruns and the app's default demo (label `post_task21_final`) | 50 of 50 files byte-identical; default demo 42 of 92 flagged, 36 of 36 found, 1 false alarm | unchanged |

*Recorded, not hidden:* the plant log now refuses a temperature outside 0 to 35 C and a row with a filled cell beyond its header, and reads again a log whose rows leave out the optional cells (refused since the review), a behaviour change outside the defaults. The pilot's help for `--analyzer-hour` and `--allow-large` reads differently, with the same values. The contract check is stricter on purpose: a later task that draws a label some new way, writes a number into a string of the contract or into the doc's prose, or quotes a name in the doc must render it from the contract or teach the check. The new controls make a full run of the saved checks about 30 s longer.

How to run: `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.contract` prints the files table, checks the templates' headers and says whether the doc's tables, numbers and quoted names are the contract's, and with `--write-tables` rewrites the doc's tables from the contract; `.venv/bin/python -m residualmap.checks` runs both task-21 checks in the full run and in `--quick` (where the contract check leaves the page alone); the names check needs `RESIDUALMAP_NAMES_CSV` set to a private list, once per list.

### Lead decisions and hand fixes, iteration 5

The lead (Claude, running each task through its build, reviews and checks) made these decisions between tasks. They were in the CHANGELOG and the lead's notes; this part puts them in the record. Every number here is from a file under `outputs/`, and each is simulation on public models or self-consistency on files we wrote.

**How each task was run.** Each task went through its build, three review lenses (correctness, numbers and honesty, and nothing else broken), a fix pass, then a final adversarial attack on the fixes and a second fix pass when it found anything major. The final attack was added after task 15, where it found more real issues after the first fix pass, crash and freeze cases among them. Then the lead ran the quick checks, the names check against every contact list, a dash scan, a fuzz run or a look in a browser where it mattered, and committed. For task 15 the lead's fuzz run of mutated files raised nothing, ran slow on nothing and left no file behind; its counts are in the lead's notes, not in `outputs/`, so they are not quoted.

**Defaults the lead took on 18 October**, so the build could start (the plan's 'Lead decisions'; Devansh can overrule any of them):
- A grab sample's hour is truncated, as the code always did, not rounded as the pilot protocol says; the upload path says so.
- The bundled Net2 example keeps its committed numbers, with a caption that its file starts at 08:00, so its hours are model hours.
- The Darcy-Weisbach gate's values (water age moved by a median of at most 1 h and a 95th percentile of at most 4 h) were accepted by the lead and written down before task 15b's scored run; the plan had left them to Devansh (its open decision 3).
- Booster sources already in a file get a warning only.
- Task 20 moved up to follow task 16b.
- The three new packages (`openpyxl==3.1.5` with `et-xmlfile==2.0.0`, `pyshp==3.1.6` and `ezdxf==1.4.4`) were installed into `.venv` and pinned in `requirements.txt` as their tasks started. The lead read Devansh's request of 18 October for other input file methods as his approval; rule R8 asks for his approval before any install, so he may want to confirm it.
- Bars that looked likely to miss stayed as written; a miss meant stop and report.
- The external public mains layer was not downloaded; every fixture is generated locally.

**Missed bars, and how each was handled.** The rule was the same every time: a missed pre-registered bar means stop and report, never tune, and the miss stays on record.
- Task 15b's run-time estimate: the grid took 2.10 times the estimate in the scored run, against a bar of a factor of 2 (`outputs/intake/run_cost_timing.json`). The lead kept the miss on record, did not restate the formula after seeing the timings, kept only the parts that do not depend on timing in the saved check, and had the app call the speedup an assumption. Changing the estimate is open to Devansh.
- Task 16b, tap mapping by coordinates: 0.967 of ky4's taps got the right junction against a bar of 0.98 (`outputs/intake/tap_snapping.json`, simulated GPS noise). Committed as built and tested, and not wired into the app, the pilot or the Get started page. The lead's recommendation to Devansh: restate the rule (flag taps near intersections for the operator to confirm) with a new pre-registered bar and one scored run, rather than lower the bar.
- Task 19, the operator form: the scored run gave a water-age 95th percentile of 22.58 h against a bar of 2 h, and a chlorine 95th percentile of 0.0923 mg/L against 0.03 (`outputs/intake/operator_form.json`, simulation on ky4). The form engine is built and tested, and not yet in the app; its cause was measured after the stop and is not a rescoring.
- Two bars were met with nothing to spare, and were left as met, not rounded up: task 17 rebuilt 1,151 of 1,156 ky4 pipes against a bar of 1,151 (`outputs/intake/gis_roundtrip.json`), and task 18 did the same with 1,150 of the 1,151 rebuilt sizes right (`outputs/intake/cad_roundtrip.json`), both converter self-consistency on a public model.

**Wording passes.**
- After task 16 the lead looked at the Get started page in a browser and rewrote what operators read: task numbers, repository paths and a pilot flag came out, and the list of formats moved into an expander. Every later task was told the same: no task numbers, paths or flags in what the app shows. The main page was checked with the example log (39 samples used).
- Task 21's labelling pass (Built, above): paragraphs that quoted an importer number now say, in their own words, that it is simulation or converter self-consistency.

**Scope moved between tasks.**
- Task 20 (the hosted demo) moved up to follow task 16b, so a public demo link could exist sooner (lead decision 5). The lead looked at the hosted version on the laptop: the privacy notice, examples only, the sidebar named ResidualMap, the Get started text right for cloud mode, and the default demo flagging 42 of 92. Signing in to Community Cloud and deploying are Devansh's steps.
- The final attack on task 16c found minor issues only; they were moved to task 17 instead of a second fix pass, and all were fixed there.
- Task 17's review found that a skeleton's Hazen-Williams C comes from each pipe's material alone, so it cannot see a pipe's age. That went into task 19's plan as a lead note; task 19 says so plainly and measures it in its ladder.
- Task 18's review found task 17's wording bug on step 4 (a pipe size filled in for the operator and then called the size they chose). It was carried to task 19 and fixed there.
- The external public mains layer was never downloaded (lead decision 8): every fixture is generated locally, and its check is skipped and says so.
- The WNTR bug, found in task 17, was left to task 21 by the plan, and is still waiting on the geopandas reproduction (task 21, Found 4).

**What the outreach changed.** On 20 October several replies to the outreach said that most small systems have no hydraulic model at all. That made task 17's GIS route the most important way in, ahead of the vendor exports; Devansh asked that the people who said so be told.

**Outages.** Task 18's first review round was lost twice, once to a network failure and once to an expired login; both times the run was resumed from its cache, so the build was not repeated. Task 21's first two attempts were cut off by network outages, the second after hours of work; their work was reviewed and finished, not redone, and the second's last full run of the saved checks, which kept going, was used as it stood.

### Iteration 5 summary

**What was asked.** On 18 October Devansh asked for more ways to bring a town's own files into ResidualMap (a model from any software, GIS and CAD maps of the mains, the grab-sample log as the lab exports it) and for a way to use it without installing Python, so that it can reach as many towns as possible. Iteration 5 (tasks 15 to 21) built those input routes and a hosted demo, each with bars written down before the scored runs.

**Simulation only.** Everything here is simulation on public models: EPA's example networks and the KYPIPE research networks (ky4, ky10) bundled with WNTR, with simulated truths. The importers were tested on those public models written out as files by our own writers (converter self-consistency), and the grab-log reader on logs we wrote; no utility's own files and no real grab samples were used. No external file was downloaded.

**What was built, in plain words.**
- Any file can be given. ResidualMap says what a file is by what is inside it, not by its name, reads an EPANET model however a modelling package or Windows saved it, and tells the operator what to send instead of anything else (task 15).
- An imported model is checked against what the model assumes, fixed in a copy where it can be, and blocked with the reason where it cannot (task 15b).
- The grab log uploads as the lab or field system exports it, with a tap map, and a Get started page explains what to export from each modelling package and offers templates (task 16).
- A zone cut from a larger system can give each inlet its own measured residual, and the pilot can leave sites out to test the map where nobody sampled (task 16c).
- A town with no hydraulic model can start from the GIS layer of its mains (task 17) or a CAD drawing (task 18) and get a skeleton model of its pipes.
- A hosted demo shows the example networks in a browser with no install; real data stays on the laptop (task 20).
- Every input and output is written down in one page and one module that a check holds to the code (task 21).
- Built and tested, but not yet in the app: tap mapping by coordinates (task 16b) and the operator form that completes a skeleton model (task 19).

**Which tasks met their pre-registered bars, and which stopped.**

| task | what | pre-registered bar | result | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

**What was found that matters most.**
- Several outreach replies said that most small systems have no hydraulic model, so the GIS route of task 17 matters most; its skeleton has no source, tanks, pumps, valves or demand, and the operator form that adds them is not in the app yet, so for now those go in through EPANET.
- On the public models, the importers rebuild the network they were given almost exactly, and where they miss it is the drawing's geometry (pipes drawn closer than the joining distance), not the reader.
- Small things in a drawing cost a lot downstream: in task 19 three ky4 junctions drawn within 1 ft of a pipe they are not on became tees, and that alone moved the water age by hours at the 95th percentile.
- ky4's and ky10's files set a chlorine diffusivity of 0.05 instead of chlorine's own 1.0, and every committed ky4 and ky10 chlorine result inherits it (task 19's review).
- WNTR's GIS route drops junction demand patterns (task 17, task 21 Found 4); not yet reproduced with geopandas or filed.

**Regressions and missed bars, all in one list** (simulation on public models; each task section has the detail):
- Task 15b: the run-time estimate missed its bar (2.10 against 2); the speedup it assumes is called an assumption in the app.
- Task 16b: stopped at its bar (0.967 against 0.98); tap mapping by coordinates is not offered.
- Task 19: stopped at its bar (water age and the chlorine 95th percentile); the operator form is not offered, so a GIS or CAD skeleton still needs EPANET before it runs.
- Tasks 17 and 18: met with nothing to spare; a drawing with closer junctions would miss.
- Task 20: not deployed; its cold start and speed on Community Cloud are not measured.
- Task 21: the WNTR bug is not reproduced with geopandas; the rubric's weights are not checked; two tracked places still name a real utility, the Net3 provenance string in `residualmap/experiment.py` (kept by the lead in task 14, and in the committed Net3 figures' titles) and one line of iteration 3's prompt (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT.md`), both left for Devansh.
- Throughout: the readers are tested on files we wrote from public models, never on a utility's own exports; censored readings ('<0.02', 'ND') are shown, not fitted; native vendor databases are not read; Windows is untested.
- Not one of these moved a default number: every task ended with the four committed reruns writing their files byte-identical to the committed outputs, and the app's default demo unchanged (`outputs/chem/baseline_reproduction.json`).
